# Technical Notes

This is the native ARM port. It is not the older PowerPC-recompiled runtime.

- `native/src/` and `native/include/`: reconstructed game code with port changes.
- `native/platform/sdk/`: host replacements for console services.
- `native/platform/gx/`: bridge from the game's GX calls to `renderer/`.
- `native/platform/vita/`: input, audio, memory, packaging and player settings.
- `dependencies/vitaGL/`: recovered patched graphics implementation.

The selected player executable uses ThinLTO. Renderer and platform code must
agree on enum, char and wchar sizes; use the Makefile's ABI flags rather
than compiling individual files with guessed defaults.

The player uses `ux0:data/mscharged/`; extracted game data lives in `game/`.
Preserve `np-data/` and existing NAND/save files, plus `np-player.txt`.
The startup shader cache is build-specific. A cache from another renderer
is not a supported replacement. See [Install](../MANUAL-INSTALL.md) for exact paths.

`native/platform/vita/lab/` holds developer tools for scripted test runs and
diagnostics. They are not needed to install or play. Desktop or emulator
timing is not a measure of Vita performance.

## Runtime Switches

Lines in `ux0:data/mscharged/np-player.txt`. LiveArea Settings writes the ones
that have a row (see [Settings Screen](#settings-screen-performance-and-graphics));
add the others by hand. The first table holds the switches added for the
2026-10-23 release and the first 1.2 ones; [the 1.2 tables below it](#more-12-switches-by-area)
hold the rest, sorted by area. Switches marked "console A/B" have a default that
the Vita tests in [Console tests](CONSOLE-TESTS.md) decide.

| Line | Effect |
| --- | --- |
| `MSC_FX_LOWRES=0` | Draw glowing effect sprites at full size (default: Mega Strikes and heavy effects at a quarter of the pixels; `4`: a sixteenth; `always`: every effect, for tests) |
| `MSC_FX_LOWRES_SCENES=nis,replay` | Scene classes (`play`, `replay`, `nis` for cut-scenes, `other`, or `all`; Mega Strikes are always in) that use the effects pass from `MSC_FX_LOWRES_LEVEL` (default 4, as everywhere) with runs of at least `MSC_FX_LOWRES_MINVERTS` vertices (default 400). Default: none, so only Mega Strikes and a hot GPU engage it as before |
| `MSC_FX_LOWRES_MINPX=480000` | With `MSC_FX_LOWRES_SCENES`: a run must also cover at least this many estimated pixels (EFB, 640x448) to be drawn small, because each run costs about 0.36 Mpx for its composite and saves 0.75 of the pixels it covers. Default 0 (off) |
| `MSC_FX_LOWRES_ANY=1` | In the scenes named above, additive draws the game did not mark join the effects pass. Changes the picture visibly (hard-edged additive meshes are drawn at half size), for tests only |
| `MSC_FX_STAT=1` | Log, every 600 frames and per scene class, how often the effects pass ran and how many effect draws stayed at full size |
| `MSC_VGX_DEADDRAW=0` | Draw every draw (default: draws that provably change nothing are skipped: alpha 0 or colour 0 at every vertex, a konst alpha of 0) |
| `MSC_FX_THIN=0` | Never thin the Mega Strike haze (default: only at the smallest Mega Strike picture; `1`: always in Mega Strikes) |
| `MSC_FX_WARBLE=half` | Heat distortion (Sand Tomb and Lava Pit walk-outs and goal celebrations): copy the screen into a texture of half the size per side. Cheaper copy, but the whole picture is softer while the effect runs (default `full`) |
| `MSC_FX_GLOW=half` | Glow chain (`HighRange.cpp`): stop at the 160x112 picture and stretch it in the composite. The last pass, the only full-size quad of the chain (287 000 of its 470 000 four-texture fragments), and its copy are not made. Softer glow. Code default `full`; Vita plan `half` (`vita_plan.cpp` kQuality, ship26/int28; `=full` turns it off). Console A/B: R8, R9, R6 |
| `MSC_SHADOW_EVERY=n` | Replays and cut-scenes redraw each player's shadow picture every n-th frame, staggered by player (1 to 4), as open play does with 2. Code default `1` (the game's every frame; restored in int28 after live Vita 6423 proved `1` + `MSC_SHADOW_RES=144` runs 29.5 fps walk-out & 30.0 fps replays while eliminating DK fly-in shadow lag); PERFORMANCE (60) writes 2. Console A/B: R6 |
| `MSC_REPLAY_FARLOD=d` | In replays and cut-scenes a player further than d world units from the camera uses the open-play mesh instead of the full one (a change of mesh can be seen when a player crosses d). Code default 0 (off); `18` in the Vita plan (`vita_plan.cpp` kQuality, int28: raised from 10 to prevent DK mid-fly-in body LOD pop); PERFORMANCE (60) writes 12. The pitch is about 41 units long. Console A/B: R6 |
| `MSC_LOD_SIZE=0\|1\|2` | `0` | Screen-size level of detail for characters (np/lod.h, NpLod.cpp, DrawableCharacter.cpp; render thread only, nothing the simulation reads). The size is the projected diameter of a 2-unit sphere at the character on a 544-line screen. `1`: the game's lower mesh (CHAR_MODEL_LOW_POLY) chosen by that size, with hysteresis, per scene class (open play keeps the lower mesh; replays and cut-scenes use the full mesh near and the lower one far). `2`: also meshes built at load by an edge-collapse simplifier (np/lod_simplify.h) as an index buffer and display list over the SAME vertices (skin, UVs, animation untouched; seams and open edges locked; error under `MSC_LOD_PIXEL_ERR` screen lines at the size it is used), for the 12 characters the disc has no lower mesh for and for a second level below the lower mesh; characters under `MSC_LOD_CULL_PX` (2) are not drawn. `MSC_LOD_PX=a,b` (replay 120,58; cut-scene x1.08), `MSC_LOD_PX_PLAY=b` (58), `MSC_LOD_HYST` (0.15), `MSC_LOD_BUILD_PX1/2` (120, 58), `MSC_LOD_LOG=1`, `MSC_LOD_TRACE=1`, `MSC_LOD_INV=1`. No shader text changes. Console A/B: lod0, lod1, lod2 |
| `MSC_VGX_DLFLUSH_STADIUM=0` | Keep the geometry cache entries of a torn-down stadium for the full 600 frames (default: they are freed 240 frames after the stadium is destroyed unless called again; pictures and rebuilds are the same, 5 to 6 MB of static buffers fewer in the first seconds of the next match) |
| `MSC_VGX_DLLIVE=N` | Print what the geometry cache holds (lists, static buffers, decoded bytes, evictions) every N frames |
| `MSC_CROWD_REPLAY=1..4` | Under a non-gameplay camera (replays, cut-scenes, celebrations) the crowd sprites and the cluster picture refresh at least this many times less often. Default `1` (the game's every frame) in the code and the host harness, `3` in the Vita plan (`vita_plan.cpp` kSpeed, int26; was `2`, `=1` turns it off; console A/B `crowdreplay2`). **Visible** close to the crowd. Render-only: the proof by reading is at the call in `GameRenderTask.cpp` (no callback, no random number, slots rebuilt by the second visibility pass); the front end's sprites, whose capture does draw a random number, are never floored (their list has no cluster) |
| `MSC_CROWD_CLUSTER_LAZY=0\|1` | `1`: under a non-gameplay camera the cluster capture, and the steps that exist only for it, are left out in a stadium where no model samples the cluster's picture. The game replaces only four crowd textures (`_vice`, `_vicenight`, `_underground`, `_wastelands`), counted at load (`CrowdImpostors.cpp`). **Not the same picture (sweep24, 24 Nov, host GL, The Dump fb-dump 4400 frames, 960x544, two different builds, identical result): 28 of 48 pictures differ, 464,012 pixels, PSNR 41-56 dB, the far crowd rows at the top of the picture (max channel difference up to 147); the log says `cluster readers 0, cluster captured 0`, so the stadium counts as having no reader and something still shows the cluster's picture.** Default `0`; keep it off until that is explained |
| `MSC_MENU_LOD=0..3` | Menus. Default `0`, the game. `1`: the captains are captured from the match's low mesh (not Diddy Kong, whose packets the code hides by number), from the next load of a captain; flatter faces, and the low mesh's materials have never been seen in the menus. `2`: also their sprites refresh every second frame: half-rate captains, a blank sprite for one frame after a load. `3`: also the backdrop world is not drawn: an empty background, a test of where the time goes, not a mode to ship. Console A/B: `menulod1` to `menulod3` |
| `MSC_MENU_STILL=0\|1` | Menus. Default `0`, the game. `1`: once a screen's camera, world and set of captains have stood for 20 frames, the world's views (`eCLV_NoFog`..`eCLV_Unshadowed`) are copied once and the copy is drawn in view 9 in their place, with no change to anything the game runs: the views are only not sent, so no call that draws a random number runs more or less often (`np/stillback.h`, `StillBackdrop.cpp`). Any change of camera, world or captain is live on that same frame. A frame with warble is never held. The picture stays live until the renderer reports the copy; no room (copy size + 12 MiB free, the dynamic resolution's floor), a failed copy or a lost texture stays or goes live, three failures end it for the screen. A new picture size is one more copy under the old one. Memory: one RGBA8 render target at the picture's size, about 2 MiB at 960x544, 3 MiB at 1.2 per side. What stops moving: everything in the world's opaque layers (machinery, flags, world NPCs, the shadows of captains on the floor, held from the copy). What stays live: captains, their sprites and planar shadows, glow, particles, wipes, the 2D. Without the world's depth, sprites and particles are no longer hidden by world objects in front of them. Log: `MSC_STILL_LOG=1` |
| `MSC_PAUSE_STILL=0\|1` | The pause menu of a local match. Default `0`. `1`: as Galaxy's `PETARI_PAUSE_FREEZE`: after 8 frames of a steady camera views 0..35 are copied once into view 36 and left out. Only with `FrontEnd::m_bInPauseMenuState`, so not in a Mega Strike's frame lock (also `TASK_PAUSED`), and never in a game of more than one machine: `g_bAllowNetworkPausing` is off, so online has no pause menu, and its error menus stay as they are. Game-thread posing still runs: this saves the GPU and the send, not the game thread |
| `MSC_STADIUM_HEAVY=<ids>` | Stadium numbers by comma (`STAD_*`: Pipeline Central is `8`). Default empty. From the load of such a stadium to its end (`StadiumHeavy.cpp`, a `[heavy]` log line): crowd and decorative NPCs not drawn as with Stadium Crowd HIDE, shadows of replays and cut-scenes at least every 2nd frame, and, in a renderer that has `VgxHeavyStadium`, the floor of the picture at 0.70 per side, the effects pass in every scene class at a quarter of each side from 325 000 pixels, warble at half size. Drawing only: peers may differ. Recommended from the disc: `8` with `MSC_STADIUM_HEAVY_CROWD=0` (`docs/PIPELINE-CENTRAL-AUDIT.md`), the Vita build's default since 1.3 (`vita_plan.cpp` kSpeed, with `MSC_STADIUM_HEAVY_CROWD=0`). Console A/B: `pipeheavy0` (the profile off) |
| `MSC_STADIUM_HEAVY_CROWD=0` | Under `MSC_STADIUM_HEAVY`, keep the crowd and decorative NPCs drawn (code default `1`: the profile hides them; the Vita plan sets `0`, with `MSC_STADIUM_HEAVY=8`). The disc gives no reason to hide Pipeline Central's crowd (`docs/PIPELINE-CENTRAL-AUDIT.md`) |
| `MSC_STADIUM_CUT=0\|table\|<mask>\|<id>:<mask>,...` | Code default `0`; Vita plan `21336` (`vita_plan.cpp` kSpeed, ship26 + int28: bare mask `8+16+64+256+512+4096+16384`, with `MSC_STADIUM_CUT_FAR=32` in kQuality; bits 256+512 Dump sheen shader pre-baked in pack28; `=0` turns it off). The per-stadium BACKGROUND cut table (`np/stadiumcut.h`, `StadiumCut.cpp`; the row table `NpStadiumCutTableMask` is for The Dump and Lava Pit first, and what `MSC_BG30_CUTS` can share). Bits: 1 heat-shimmer particles not built (no pre-warble copy, offset pass or composite), 2 large haze sprites thinned, 4 crowd sprite refresh floor 3, 8 static drawables under `MSC_STADIUM_CUT_FAR` px (code default 24, Vita plan `32`) not drawn, 16 world NPCs not drawn, 64 The Dump's rain (template `fx_dump_rain`: every second streak not built, the rest at twice the alpha; census 1.9 to 1.5 Mpx at The Dump), 128 test; wave 25 (dumpgpu25, w8/dumpgpu25.md) at The Dump: 256 / 512 / 1024 the pitch material's (fragment program 1DEECD4F, class stadium only, `renderer/src/vgx_dumpmat25.h`) layer 1 / 2 / 3 not fetched, its mean colour instead (1024 is the light map: visible, test only), 2048 layers 1 and 2 not fetched in the far part of the pitch (window depth over `MSC_DUMPMAT_FARZ`, default 0.9926; `MSC_DUMPMAT_C1..3=r,g,b,a` override the constants, `MSC_DUMPMAT_LOG=1` lists the material's draws), 4096 layers 1..3 sampled bilinear (nearest mip, no anisotropy), 8192 the rain thinned 1 in 3 at 1.5 x alpha (gentler 64), 16384 the rain drawn as hull quads (`MSC_RAIN_HULL` forced on at The Dump: the same pixels). The Dump's `table` row is 16384 + 2048. Derived programs compile at first use: one more program per mode on the console. Render-only, never players, ball, shadows or HUD; each bit counts itself in the `[stadiumcut]` line. |
| `MSC_RAIN_HULL=0\|1\|2`, `MSC_RAIN_HULL_RECTS=<1..16>`, `MSC_RAIN_HULL_CLASS=<0..2>`, `MSC_RAIN_HULL_LOG=1\|2`, `MSC_RAIN_HULL_STRIPS=0\|1` (default 1: full-height strips), `MSC_RAIN_HULL_MARGIN=<lattice units>`, `MSC_RAIN_HULL_HASH=<template hash>`, `MSC_RAIN_HULL_BREAK=1` (test mutant) | Wave 11 (data16), `np/vgx_rainhull.h`, `ParticleSystem.cpp` model branch, host test `native/tools/rainhull`. The Dump's rain (template `fx_dump_rain`) is one quad of a texture that REPEATS (2.19 tiles of a 32 x 32 RGB5A3 texture holding four thin dashes, about 4 % of its texels), and a blended fragment of alpha 0 is still shaded. With it on, a drop is drawn as small quads (two triangles each) around the dashes of each tile the quad covers, the quad's own affine map, so every pixel inside keeps its texture coordinate. The rectangles are exact for a repeating, bilinearly filtered, mip-mapped texture (level L's footprint of a nonzero texel, wrapped; class c = levels 0..c); a drop is drawn from the class its size on the screen reaches (`NpTightClass`, the sprites' numbers), deeper than class 2 it stays the quad. `MSC_RAIN_HULL_RECTS` (default 8) bounds the rectangles per tile (merged by least added area), `MSC_RAIN_HULL_CLASS` (default 2) the deepest class, `=2` prints a `[rainhull]` statistics line (estimate, 640x448) and `MSC_RAIN_HULL_LOG=1` says why a model gets no hull. Render only: no simulation read, so it is not gated by LAN or online. Default in code `0`; the Vita plan default is in `vita_plan.cpp` (see `w8/data16.md` for the proof it rests on). Wave 12 (rainhull19): the crash (hull kept a packet copy whose material parameters were frame memory) is fixed: the hull holds geometry only and every drop copies the source frame model; default stays OFF. |
| `MSC_FX_MODEL_OFFVIEW=0\|1\|2` (`MSC_FX_MODEL_OFFVIEW_BREAK=1` is a test mutant) | nxrain25 (26 Nov), default `1` (exact, render only: nothing the simulation reads; `NpModelOvOff` in `np/fxmodelov.h`, host test `native/tools/modelov`). The model branch of `ParticleSystem::RenderAllParticles` (The Dump's `fx_dump_rain`, only while its hull table is built, i.e. `MSC_RAIN_HULL` or the stadium cut bit 16384) now leaves out a drop whose bounding sphere lies wholly beyond one side plane of the view's clip volume (clip x or y beyond +-w with `NpFxOffView`'s margin of 2 % of \|w\|; behind the camera counts, any such point has w < 0). The sphere: the drop's position, radius = the farthest vertex of any of the model's frames from its origin (`RainBuild`) x the Frobenius norm of `mCoord`'s 3 x 3 part x \|size\| x 1.25 + 0.05 (a rotation has 2-norm 1, so `mCoord * scale * rotationZ` cannot stretch a vector by more). The drop is still updated and `UpdateParticle` still runs; what is skipped is the two matrix products, `glAllocSetMatrix`, the model copy, the colour and raster-state sets and `AttachModel`. NEON's R1 was right that the cull belongs in the render and not in the update; its remark that `if (z < 0.0f) z = -z;` draws drops behind the camera is wrong (that line only picks the size class, clipping already discarded those). `2`: count only. `[fxmodelov]` line every 1200 draws (drops a draw, drops wholly off the view). Measured on the host (callgrind, The Dump live play, 40 pictures): 17.7 % of the rain drops culled, `RenderAllParticles` 14.22 -> 13.38 M Ir (this switch alone). Proof: five host GL pictures of live play (frames 2250..2990) and seven of the walk-out, goal and replay (1250..2300) are byte-identical with it on and off; the mutant changes all of them | `1` |
| `MSC_FX_RAIN_FAST=0\|1` | nxrain25 (26 Nov), default `1` (exact, render only). For a rain drop that is drawn as a hull (class >= 0, one-packet model) the model branch makes the drop's copy with `glModelDupNoStreams(pAnim->m_pModel)` directly instead of `GLVertexAnim::GetModel(frame)`, whose animated-stream patching was overwritten by the hull's streams at once; the `constantcolour` parameter offset and the raster-state word (the same four or five `glSetRasterState` field sets, each a per-bit loop, applied once to the source packet's word) are worked out once per call; the colour is written at that offset with one `memcpy`; `glModelSetMatrix` is left out (the packet's matrix is set in the same place). A copy made by `glModelDupNoStreams` SHARES the source model's streams array, so `GetModel(frame)` leaves the source streams on that frame for every drop that reads them later (a drop that is not drawn as a hull, the last call wins); `GLVertexAnim::PatchSharedStreams(frame)` makes the last left-out call's patch once after the loop, so the state is the same (the host test `native/tools/modelov` checks the sequence logic, MODELOV_BREAK=3 forgets it). Measured (callgrind, host, 40 pictures of live play): 900 instructions less per drop (`RenderAllParticles` 14.22 -> 11.90 M Ir with this switch alone, 11.50 with `MSC_FX_MODEL_OFFVIEW`); GL pictures byte-identical on and off | `1` |
| `MSC_VGX_SHADERTEXT_LOG=<file>` | Wave 11 (data16), `renderer/src/vgx_shadertext.h`, diagnostic, default off. Appends every distinct shader text given to the compiler (generated shaders and the back end's own programs) to the file with its two hashes (P: what the pack indexes and the `shader pack MISS` lines print; T: the `[shaderplay]` text hash). `tools/pack17/pack17_request.py` reads these files (named `<route>.texts`) with the console logs and shader lists to write the shader-pack top-up request; `tools/pack17/pack17_build.sh` is the owner's script that compiles it with his own libshacccg (never shipped, never copied). |
| `MSC_FX_HAZE=1..4` | Under `MSC_STADIUM_HEAVY` only. Default `2`. Long-lived large additive haze (`NpSceneHazeTemplate`: of all the disc's templates only Pipeline Central's Mist_rain fits) is drawn one sprite in n, with n times the alpha up to 255, chosen by pool slot. `1`: all drawn. No random number, no particle changes: peers may differ. Console A/B: `pipeheavy0`, `hazecache3heavy` |
| `MSC_FX_HAZE_WIDE=1` | Under `MSC_STADIUM_HEAVY` only. Default `0`. `MSC_FX_HAZE` also takes `NpSceneHazeTemplateWide`: additive, 5 to 12 units, living at least 3 s, from a fountain. On the disc: Crystal Canyon's caustics, Wastelands' ash, Storm Ship's wind, and Mist_rain. Drawing only: peers may differ. Console A/B: `hazewide` |
| `MSC_FLOOR_BAKE=<ids>\|all` | Default empty. Stadium numbers by comma (as `MSC_STADIUM_HEAVY`). The two reflective floor materials lose their moving reflection: its mean goes into the end colours of the diffuse's CMPR blocks at model load (`FloorBake.cpp`, `np/floorbake.h`, a `[floorbake]` log line), and those packets draw with one texture instead of three, plus the shadow lookup. No extra memory, no render target. First use in a stadium may compile one shader. Drawing only: peers may differ. Why not a real bake: `docs/PIPELINE-CENTRAL-AUDIT.md`. The Vita build's default is `7,8` (Lava Pit, Pipeline Central; `vita_plan.cpp` kSpeed); `none` names no stadium. Console A/B: `floorbake0` |
| `MSC_FLOOR_SHEEN=vertex` | With `MSC_FLOOR_BAKE` only. Default empty. Baked floors get the moving part of the reflection back: computed per vertex on the CPU from a table of the reflection (at most 32 x 32), sent as COLOR1 and added, signed, by a second TEV stage. No extra texture read. The mesh decides: a packet with an edge over 2/16 of the reflection's scale (Wastelands) takes a 16 x 16 lookup, one extra read. `lookup` forces that everywhere, for comparison. Such packets leave the display list and go through the pipe each frame. `[floorbake] ... sheen: <n> per vertex, <n> by lookup`. Drawing only: peers may differ. The Vita build's default is `vertex`; `none` turns it off. Console A/B: `floorsheen0` |
| `MSC_SHADOW_RES=96\|72` | Code default `144` (restored in int28; `96` missed `JointSize` 72x72 in `vgx_draw.cpp`, spawning an extra 739x743 RT + 67.7 ms solo-shadow hitch and severe shadow flicker; live Vita 6423 proved `144` is both crisper and faster on GPU). The side of the picture a player's shadow is drawn in; the texture is half of it. Read at start. Under 2 % of the fill by arithmetic |
| `MSC_FX_HAZE_CACHE=0\|2\|3\|4` | Default `0`. n: in open play the haze sprites (`NpSceneHazeTemplate`: Mist_rain; with `MSC_FX_HAZE_WIDE=1` also caustics, ash, storm wind) are drawn only every n-th frame, into a 160x112 RGBA8 picture (view `eCLV_GrabTexture`, before the world, so a small scene of its own), and that picture is added to every frame by one full-screen quad in `eCLV_WarbleBlend` (blend ONE, ONE, no scene change). On held frames the four corners of the screen are carried to the held picture through the plane at height `MSC_FX_HAZE_CACHE_Z` (default 5), so the haze stays on the world while the camera moves. Particles update and emit as ever; the held frames only leave out the attach (`np/amortize.h`, `Amortize.cpp`). Live: any other scene, warble, Mega Strike, a captain's shot, a camera jump over 96 px of 960x544 per frame. Visible: the haze no longer goes behind anything, and it is 1/16 of the pixels. Memory: 45 KiB, allocated only when on. Estimate from the disc audit, not a measurement: 5.9 screens of additive blend become 0.37/n + 1.0 screens. Console A/B: `hazecache3`, `hazecache3heavy`. Log: `MSC_AMORT_LOG=1` |
| `MSC_SHADOW_PLAY=2\|3\|4` | Default `2`, the game's. Interval of a visible player's shadow picture in open play. The shadow follows the player every frame; only its shape lags. Console A/B: `shadowplay3` |
| `MSC_PICTURE_HOLD=0\|1\|native\|full\|WxH` | Default `full` in the player build only when the file names neither SMOOTH 60 nor a frame rate: an empty np-player.txt is SMOOTH 60 (`MSC_LOCK60=1`, plan24 FIX-01; the plan sets that key first, so no hold and no fixed size), and a named 30 / 45 rate, `MSC_PICTURE_HOLD` itself or `MSC_FPS=60` keep the hold's defaults (vita_plan.cpp; code default off, so the host harness is unchanged); `0` is the older size ladder (no Settings row writes it since the 1.3 prune; the plan lets go of the hold by itself under 30 FPS, SMOOTH 60 MODE and a fixed size; the kit and a hand file can still set it). Not set by the plan when the player named a fixed size (`MSC_VITA_RENDER`, RESOLUTION), LOCKED 60, `MSC_FPS=30`, `MSC_DYNRES=off` or `MSC_VITA_MSAA`; `MSC_FX_GOV=1` is defaulted with it. The FULL picture policy for LIVE PLAY (`renderer/src/vgx_hold.h`, host test `renderer/tests/run_picture_hold_test.sh`, 65 checks, `BREAK=1..5`, with step-trace tests: steady 17.5 ms, 15/19 alternation, slow drift, spike trains, the table's stadiums). One size (960x544, edge smoothing on; samples per `MSC_PICTURE_HOLD_MSAA`, the next row, default 2 in the Vita build; `MSC_VGX_MSAA` is not used with the hold, logged). Steps: 1 effects target, 2 far shadows, 3 all shadows, 4 crowd refresh (these four opt-in), 5 and 6 the samples (4 to 2, 2 to 1), 7 to 9 the sizes. Measured over a window (48 frames over 0.92 of 16.7 ms, a late frame counts double, so one late frame never moves a step), a step down is taken at most every 180 frames (3 s) and takes 1 to 3 steps by how far over the mean is (table of estimated savings). Order by size only (each size step is made ahead and eased): the picture 0.90, 0.81, 0.73 per side (864x488, 776x440, 704x400; targets made before play (outside it, only with the trim floor of 16 MiB left free; the visible 4-present rehearsal of 11 Nov is off unless `MSC_PICTURE_HOLD_REHEARSE=1`; targets that were skipped or have gone are asked for again at each stadium change; the held size's own target is made again if the trim took it); about 8.0 MiB of video memory for the three; the present eases the sharpening over 20 pictures, `MSC_SHARPEN_EASE`, polish branch); the floor is the last size, nothing more is shed and the frame-rate controller is told there is no room (`VgxDynResRoom`). Opt-in, NOT in the default order (their engagement is not hidden in motion; the strips in w7/holdon show the shadow shape lagging a running figure): `MSC_PICTURE_HOLD_SHADOW=1` the far then all players' shadow pictures one frame less often (staggered over about 3 s, `VgxHoldShadowStepFor`), `MSC_PICTURE_HOLD_FX=1` the effects target one size smaller (wave 7 megafx4 order kept: when in the order it is step 1, the first taken on an overload and the last given back; host check 9b2), `MSC_PICTURE_HOLD_CROWD=1` the crowd refresh halved. A step is given back (last first) only when the mean plus its saving stays under 0.80 for 300 frames, 900 for a size step; a step taken again within 180 (size: 1200) frames of its return doubles its wait (forgiven one notch per quiet minute). Frozen (nothing measured, nothing moves, picture at the held size with no shedding after 30 presents, and from the cut in a scene the frame-rate policy holds at 30 (cadence or `MSC_FPS_REPLAY`, signalled through `VgxCadenceFull` bit 2 whatever `MSC_CADENCE_FULL` says), where no size is rehearsed either: a held scene's size never steps inside it) in menus, replays, cut-scenes, goal celebrations (Mega Strikes are not live play either: held at a steady 30, one sample, the full size); the cut back resumes the play's state with the measurement afresh. A match starts at 2 steps in a heavy stadium (`MSC_STADIUM_HEAVY`), or at the step the last match in that stadium spent most time in (per session, at most the shed steps), never at a size. `MSC_PICTURE_HOLD_STEP` (0.90), `_DOWN`, `_PIXSHARE`, `_MAXSTEP=0..9` (0: the size fixed, no shedding: kit run holdfixed), `_FORCE=n`, `_FORCE_AT=<present>:<step>` (pictures). Smooth 60 (`MSC_LOCK60`) never below 800x456 in PIXELS (the ladder draws the game's own 640x448 shape times a scale, so 728x512 holds the 364,800 pixels of 800x456): its level table is 1.50 (960x544, the steady 30's), 1.20 (768x544, the top of 60) and 1.13 (728x512, the bottom of 60); the old rungs 1.12 .. 0.50 (down to 448x320) and the multisampled rungs at the game's own size are out of the table and the presets are clamped to it (`lock60::PresetForFloor`). When 60 does not hold at the floor the frame-rate controller's relief gives a held 30 and, after 90 pictures of it, the picture goes to 960x544 (`lock60::Fallback30`: one entry per 30, leaves after 240 pictures back at 60). 18 Nov (floors26, F1 of fable-review25): EVERY other path obeys the Quality floor too. The ladders without the hold (FRAME RATE 30 FPS, DYNAMIC RESOLUTION off, `MSC_VITA_MSAA` named, `MSC_PICTURE_HOLD=0`, LOCKED 30, PERFORMANCE) get a four-rung Quality table (`floors::QualityTable`: 960x544 with 2x smoothing when the build has multisampling, then 1.38, 1.30 and 1.22 of the game's own size = 784x544, 426,496 pixels); the old table started at the game's 640x448 (286,720 pixels) and went down to 0.86, 0.70 (`MSC_DYNRES_SEVERE`) and 0.50 (Mega Strikes: 320x224). Every named floor (ordinary, 60, paced, SEVERE, heavy stadium, Mega Strike start, `MSC_DYNRES_MIN`/`_MIN60`) is an index into the table, so none can go lower; DYNAMIC RESOLUTION off sits at rung 0 (960x544). Where a picture does not fit at the bottom rung a 30 stays a 30 (no lower rate), a 60 falls back to 30 (existing controller). A fixed size a file names under 864x488 (the 1.0-1.2 PERFORMANCE file's 640x362, the old SMALLER / SMALLEST rows, a held `MSC_PICTURE_HOLD=native` 640x448) is raised to 864x488 by the Vita plan with a `[plan] MSC_PICTURE_FLOORS:` line; the RESOLUTION row now offers SMALLER 864X488 only. The hold's own kept targets (the held size at 1, 2 and 4 samples, the ladder's three) are pinned and never let go by the trim (r7: the Mega Strike pin budget of 16384 KiB left part of the 18.4 MiB unpinned, so the 704x400 target went at the first goal celebration with 21 MiB free and was made again in play, a hitch). At kick-off (the first live-play frame, `HoldCommit`) the free video memory decides what the match has: if less than `MSC_PICTURE_HOLD_RESERVE_KIB` (12288) would stay free, targets are let go NOW, 4x first, then the smallest ladder sizes, 2x last (`hold::DropForRoom`), and the ladder is the prefix of the sizes kept (`LadderMaxStep`); one line says what the match has and one for each target let go. Nothing is let go or made in the match. Host test 16d (`BREAK=14`, `15`); kit run `commit0`. | `MSC_PICTURE_HOLD_MSAA=0\|2\|4` | 1.3. Code default `0` (host harness unchanged); the Vita plan sets `2` whenever the hold is on (`MSC_VGX_MSAA=0` in the file gives 0, `MSC_VGX_MSAA=2` and the ladder's own `4` give 2, a named `MSC_PICTURE_HOLD_MSAA` wins; Settings ANTI-ALIASING: ON (DEFAULT) 2, OFF 0, 4X 4; wave 9 removed "2X ONLY", which gave the same 2 samples as ON, and the help line names the scenes that are always one sample). Needs `MSC_PICTURE_HOLD` (without it `MSC_VGX_MSAA` is the older ladder's rung). The held picture's samples: ONLY the main 3D scene target (the EFB) is multisampled (`vglFramebufferMultisample`, vitaGL patch 0011: its own GXM render target and DF32M depth buffer carry the samples, the hardware averages them into the single-sample colour texture when the scene is stored, so every copy, pass, shadow, effect target and the present see one resolved sample; no explicit resolve exists on the console). Order, all in `vgx_hold.h`: steps 5 and 6 sit after the shed steps and BEFORE any size step: `kAa1` 4 to 2 (only with 4), `kAa2` 2 to 1 (with 2 or 4); the size steps 7 to 9 are always one sample. Each sample count's target at the held size is made outside play after the ladder's (4 first, then 2, same room rule: made only when 12 MiB plus its own size are free), kept, pinned and shown for 4 presents once, like a size; a count whose target was not made or was let go for memory is taken out of the order (4 then goes straight to 1; the log says so), and a step is never entered into a target that is not ready. Down on the same evidence as the other steps (48 frames over 0.92 of the budget, at most one move per 3 s; the saving model is the pixel share times the cost of 1.3 (2x) / 1.9 (4x) against one sample, `Policy::aaCost`, an ESTIMATE: 4 to 2 about 3 ms and 2 to 1 about 1.9 ms at a 16 ms mean); back up one stop at a time on 300 frames of proof (the samples are the last thing returned after the sizes). Wave 9: the policy LEARNS what a step really cost (`Policy::learnAdd`: the mean just before the move down minus the mean 90 frames into it; for a move that took several steps the samples get what the model does not give the others), remembered per stadium (index < 32) and refused for a return unless the mean plus the measured cost times 1.15 stays under 0.80 of the budget (`vetoes` counts the refusals; the model's figure still applies when it is larger); the flap windows are 1800 frames (a shed step) and 2400 (a size), wider than gap + proof + hot window, so the doubling of the wait (`fails`/`block`) can engage (it could not before: the window equalled the gap). A learned cost relaxes 15 % per minute without a move down. Steady load aims at one AA change a match (host test 15a to 15d: guesses 1.1 to 2.5 against truths 1.1 to 2.5, at most 3 moves in 5 minutes; the reviewer's 1.3 guess against a 1.9 truth: one move, where the unmended policy made 55). The swap is a target change at the same size (the old picture is drawn scaled into the new target, one full-screen quad, a few ms estimated): there is no signal in the renderer for a stoppage, so a policy swap in play is accepted as it comes; the swaps at a cut are free of that (a replay, cut-scene or menu takes the top samples, the cut back takes the play's state). One sample in a Mega Strike (`d.mega`) and in the heavy stadium profile (`MSC_STADIUM_HEAVY`): the effects compositor reads the scene's depth, which `vglStrikersBindFramebufferDepth` cannot give for a multisampled buffer on the console (the host GL resolves depth, so the host draws the pass at 2x/4x and proves less than the console can do); at any other time an effects pass wanted during multisampling is idle on the console and its draws stay in the scene (the cost shows in the GPU time, so the policy gives the samples up). The soft edge filter stays on at the held size (`MSC_VGX_MSAA_EDGE` is implied by the hold). Video memory (colour + depth, ESTIMATE, 960x544): 1 sample 4080 KiB, 2 samples 6120 KiB, 4 samples 10200 KiB; the hold's ladder (864x488, 776x440, 704x400, one sample) 8161 KiB. Default (2): 4080 + 6120 + 8161 = 18361 KiB (hold alone 12241 KiB, +6120); 4x with the 2x stop 28561 KiB (+16320); the figures for other sizes are pixels x 8, 12 and 20 bytes. The log: `[hold] anti-aliasing (MSC_PICTURE_HOLD_MSAA=n)`, `[hold] picture size WxH 2x -> WxH` lines, `[hold] video memory reserved ... + multisampled held targets N KiB`, `[hold] no 4x render target ... made ahead`, and at start-up `[hold] memory self-check at WxH with 2x|4x: held + ladder + samples = N KiB reserved against F KiB free`, one line each for 2x and 4x with what fits (the fallback order: 4x dropped first, then 2x, then the ladder's sizes; each prints its own line when it happens). The match summary (`[hold] match summary`) and the ledger (`[match] #n end`) now print 5 s after the menus return (before: 30 s of menu pictures or the next stadium; the route that left the menu first never printed) and at process exit. Host test: `renderer/tests/run_picture_hold_test.sh` checks 13a to 13g (`BREAK=4`: a sample step into a target not made; `BREAK=5`: the samples never given up before a size). Not run on a console. Kit runs `aa0`, `aa2`, `aa4`, `aa800` |
| `MSC_SHADOW_FAR=0..3`, `MSC_SHADOW_FAR_PX=n` | Default `0`, `60`. A player whose projected size (screen lines of 544 of a unit sphere at his distance, `np/lod.h`) is under `MSC_SHADOW_FAR_PX` redraws his shadow picture that many frames less often than the game, in open play, replays and cut-scenes. The shadow's place follows him every frame (`RenderProjectedShadow`); only the shape lags, which at 60 screen lines a player is not seen. Estimate: one shadow redraw is about 9 draws plus a cell of the shadow atlas; a far player's interval 2 -> 3 saves 0.1-0.2 ms with 4-5 far players. Pure rule in `np/amortize.h` (`NpShadowExtraFrames`, host test `native/tools/amortize`). Render only |
| `MSC_AMORT_60=0\|1` | Default `0` (`1` only with LOCKED 60, `vita_plan.cpp` kLock60). `1`: while pictures run at 60 (`NpFpsPolicy` 2), the players' shadow pictures and the crowd sprites refresh every twice as many frames, so they keep the rate in time that they have at 30 instead of doubling with the frame rate. Effects are left alone: the PGO profile gives particle update 1.0 % and quad building 0.5 % of the counted blocks, against crowd 3.9 % and shadows 1.1 %, and each shadow picture is a scene. Not on a miss: under AUTO a CPU miss ends 60 at once, so an assist that waits for it never acts. Console A/B: `amort60` against `amort60ref` |
| `MSC_VIEW_LAYERS=0\|1` | Default `0`. `1`: the layer markers (`RLView::BeginRender`) compare `GLViewIdentity(view)` with the layer table. The two-stage pipeline sends shadows of the views, which never equal the table, so with `0` every forked frame is layer 0, class unknown: the census shows `~layer 00` only, and `MSC_DYNRES_FG`'s background/foreground split sees everything as foreground. In the kit's census runs. Proposed `1` after an A/B of `MSC_DYNRES_FG`, whose model has only ever run without the split With `MSC_BG30` on it is `1` whatever the file says (bg30c: the background prefix of a picture is found by these classes; on the console, with the pipeline, every class was 0 and the pair never engaged). |
| `MSC_AMORT_LOG=1` | Diagnostics, no change to the picture: every 300 frames of open play, how many pixels of 960x544 a held picture of that plane would be off by after 1, 2, 3 frames; and with `MSC_FX_HAZE_CACHE` the frames live, drawn and held |
| `MSC_SHADOW_PLANE=0\|1` | Default `1` since 25 Nov (nxr3b; host GL pictures `w8/nxr3b-pics/`: on Vice's goal sequence the shadows of banana peels are missing or broken up at z = 0 and whole with the switch on, 150 of 155 changed pixels darker, max 80/255, nothing else changes; det trace equal). `0` = as before. Planar shadows (stadium objects, hammers, power-ups) are drawn at the ini's Shadow Height (0.1) instead of at z = 0 in the pitch's own plane, where a strict depth test against the pitch decides pixel by pixel. The console gets that height through GX's Z-freeze from a culled quad (`RenderShadow.cpp`, `RLView::BeginPacket`); this is one matrix term, no draw, pass or shader, and unlike `MSC_VGX_ZFREEZE` it needs no reference tracking on every draw. The shadow moves up to about 1.5 EFB pixels on screen, since the Wii kept the mesh at z = 0 and took only the depth from the plane. Console A/B: `shadowplane0` (the old value). Render side only |
| `MSC_FX_HEAVY_PX=0` | Under `MSC_STADIUM_HEAVY` only. Default `1`: the effects pass goes by a run's pixels alone (`MSC_FX_LOWRES_MINPX`, at least 325000), at any picture size, because haze is few sprites of great size. `0`: the vertex floor and the wait for a smaller picture stay |
| `MSC_FX_TIGHT=0\|4\|6\|8` | Default `0`. Particle sprites are drawn as a convex polygon of at most that many corners around the part of the texture that can show, instead of the quad (`np/fxtight.h`). A polygon for each atlas frame, worked out the first time a texture is drawn (one texture a frame, a table of 4096 polygons, 147 KiB, only with the switch on; quads until then, the same picture). Corners sit on the writer's own texture lattice, so each pixel keeps the quad's coordinates. Only large billboards gain: the sampler reads mip levels, which smear, so a sprite gets the polygon of the deepest level its size on the screen can reach (`NpTightClass`, half a level of margin) and stays a quad below level 2. `4` keeps the quad list (no more vertices); `6`, `8` use a triangle list, 12 or 18 vertices for each sprite of a mesh whose texture gains from them. While the effects pass is in use additive sprites stay quads and go to it; while it is not, they are not marked for it. Off in Warble and white-texture draws, models, ground boards and orthographic views. Render only. Console A/B: `fxtight` |
| `MSC_FX_TIGHT_DROP=1` | Default `0`. A sprite whose largest possible contribution (vertex colour and alpha against the texture's largest alpha x brightness of any level) is under a quarter of an 8-bit step is not drawn: it cannot reach the picture. Render only |
| `MSC_FX_TIGHT_BLACK=1` | Default `0`. With `MSC_FX_TIGHT`: for additive sprites a texel with no brightness counts as empty too (the disc's 16 CMPR effect textures have no alpha at all). The picture's colour is the same; destination alpha is not (an additive sprite writes it), so this needs its own A/B |
| `MSC_FX_TIGHT_FLOOR=n` | Default `0`. **Not the same picture.** With `MSC_FX_TIGHT`: additive sprites' polygons may leave out texels whose alpha x brightness / 255 is under n. `fx_smoke02` (Pipeline's mist) has values 2.4, 4.8 ... 19.5: `3` keeps 0.46 of the quad at level 0 for 8 corners, against 0.77, and loses the 7 % of the texture's light that lies outside (arithmetic on the disc's texels; the sprite is also under an alpha of about 0.5). Faint edges of the haze go. Console A/B: `fxtightf` |
| `MSC_FX_TIGHT_AB=n` | Diagnostics, default `0`. With `MSC_FX_TIGHT`: polygons on for n frames, off for n, on a `[fxtight] tick` log line. For comparing the two within one run: emulator runs are not repeatable, the game keeps to the clock. **The pause menu does not work for this:** even a lossy `MSC_FX_TIGHT_FLOOR=8` leaves its backdrop unchanged in Vita3K, though sprites are still built, so a zero difference there proves nothing. It needs a scene that holds still and draws them |
| `MSC_FX_TIGHT_STAT=n` | Diagnostics, default `0`. Every n frames a `[fxtight]` line: sprites by class, faint ones, screens of billboards as quads and as drawn. Counted from the sprites' size (640x448, before the depth test): the estimate behind a kit run |
| `MSC_FX_COMPOSITE=0\|1` | 1.3. Code default `0`; the Vita plan's quality list sets `1` (host pictures, 11-12 Nov; no console run yet). The effects compositor (`vgx_fxcomp.h`, the "FX" section of `vgx_backend_gl.cpp`): in every scene the large additive draws and the large soft-alpha draws (SRC_ALPHA / 1-SRC_ALPHA, no depth or alpha write, of the particle, item and unclassified layers, never the HUD's) go into the one low-resolution accumulation target, and it is composited once. The target is half or a quarter of the picture per side, chosen per buffer so that the sprites' fill at that size is at most 2 screens (fill up to 8 screens: half, above: a quarter; measured every 8th buffer); a run goes by its pixels alone (at least 325000, as `MSC_FX_HEAVY_PX`) and the whole pass is left out when `fill/div^2 + 1/div^2 + 1 >= fill`. The target holds premultiplied colour and coverage when a run has soft draws (alpha factors of the blend set apart: `glBlendFuncSeparate`), composited with (ONE, 1-SRC_ALPHA); it is hidden behind the scene's depth as before (the EFB depth sampled at each target pixel's centre, so a silhouette edge can leak or lose up to `div - 1` picture pixels), scaled up bilinear. `MSC_FX_COMPOSITE_BUDGET=<hundredths of a screen>` (default `200`) is that 2-screen limit: `400` lets fills up to 16 screens keep the half-size target, `600` up to 24 (the Mega Strike cut-scene's thorn vines, 24 screens, lose their fine detail at a quarter; at half they cost 6). The size rule: a triangle of the draw covers at least `MSC_FX_COMPOSITE_TRIPX` pixels of 640x448 (default 900, a quad of about 42x42); smaller sprites end a run and are drawn at full size. `MSC_FX_COMPOSITE_LAYERS=<mask>` (default 520 = classes 3 and 9: the particle layers and the stadium's alpha-blended world layer, Storm Ship's rain) names the layers whose soft draws may join. With `MSC_FX_GOV=1` the first step of the governor is a target one step smaller (divisor doubled, at most 8; the game is told at most 4), before sprites thin and before any picture rung, and it is the last to return; this holds the old ladder and `MSC_LOCK60`'s governor alike. A multisampled picture (the rungs above the game's own size) cannot read its depth: the draws stay in the scene there, as without the switch. The older switches keep their meaning and win when set: `MSC_FX_LOWRES=0` is no pass at all, `MSC_FX_LOWRES_MAXH` caps the target's lines, `MSC_FX_LOWRES_RUNS`, `MSC_FX_LOWRES_MINPX`, `MSC_FX_TIGHT`, `MSC_FX_HAZE`, `MSC_FX_GLOW`, `MSC_STADIUM_HEAVY` (its divisor 4 stays the floor of the compositor's choice). Render only. No new shader: the pass uses the depth copy program and the picture quad program that were there; a (fragment shader, blend, target format) pair the patcher has not seen yet costs one patcher call (vitaGL) the first time, not a compile. Rules added 12 Nov (megafx3): (a) additive draws obey the size rule too and a thin one is left out (`MSC_FX_COMPOSITE_MINW`, default 12: mean altitude of the draw's triangles in pixels of 640x448, 3 pixels of a quarter-size target; `MSC_FX_COMPOSITE_ADDRULE=0` turns it off): a small or thin additive draw inside a run (Storm Ship's lightning arcs: 17-25 pixels wide) is moved behind the run's members (a stable partition of the buffer's commands, only when no texture or buffer is freed inside the run) and drawn at full size after the composite, in the scene, depth-tested as before (additive draws commute; its order against the run's soft draws changes). (b) A detailed texture (a side of 256 or more) shown at under 4x (`MSC_FX_COMPOSITE_MAGMAX`, hundredths) that is over 25% of the run's pixels (`MSC_FX_COMPOSITE_DETAILSHARE`) halves the divisor (the Mega Strike cut-scene's thorns: half, 7.6 screens drawn instead of 24). (c) The depth copy adds `MSC_FX_COMPOSITE_ZBIAS` millionths (default 500) to the sampled depth: a sprite lying on a surface (the floor glow of Storm Ship's lightning over its grating) passed the test only where the one sampled pixel happened to be a hole; 5000 and above lets glow show behind players. This is the text of the existing depth-copy program (not in the game's pack, compiled at first use, warmed with the Mega pass), no new program. (d) A soft draw of a texture under 64 on a side (`MSC_FX_COMPOSITE_SOFTMINTEX`) stays in the scene: Lava Pit's sky haze is a 32x64 gradient and the 8-bit premultiplied target bands it. Diagnostics: `MSC_VGX_FXTRACE=<frame,frame..>` prints each draw's pixels, triangles, width, texture and whether it is a member; `MSC_FX_COMPOSITE_SKIPTEX=<ids>`. Host test `renderer/tests/run_fxcomp_test.sh` (size rule, divisor, cost, governor order, with a deliberately broken rule that must fail)  Wave 7 (megafx4, host pictures only): (a) `MSC_FX_COMPOSITE_BOX=0|1` (default 0, host-measured but not proven in motion): the depth copy and the composite are made inside the rectangle the run's draws land in (their own bounding boxes through their matrices, each cut to its scissor, padded `3*div+2` pixels, even numbers, as sub-viewports; a run with a draw nobody can measure or a triangle behind the eye keeps the whole picture; not used above 85% of it); the pass's cost test counts that share instead of 1 screen for the composite. (b) `MSC_FX_COMPOSITE_DITHER=0|1` (default 0: at 1 level it changed no pixel on 17 frames of each of three stadiums or the Mega route, and did not cure Lava Pit's sky haze, `_AMP` hundredths of a level, 100): the composite goes through a fixed program built at start-up with the depth program (NOT the game's pack: two small programs, plain and blur; a new (fragment program, blend, target) pair costs one patcher call on the console the first time, warmed at start-up) that adds a 2x2 ordered pattern (-3/8..+3/8 of a level) before the 8-bit store; `MSC_FX_COMPOSITE_SOFT=0|1` (default 0: it needs the run rectangle, and its third-size target is a new target size made in play): a buffer whose runs are all very large sprites (mean triangle over `MSC_FX_COMPOSITE_SOFTPX`=6000 px, not a Mega Strike, not detailed) takes a third (a sixth after a governor step) and the blur variant (four diagonal bilinear reads). (c) `MSC_FX_COMPOSITE_EDGE=0|1|2` (default 0, the old single read at a pixel boundary, whose pick flips with float rounding): 1 the farthest of four depths in the footprint (taps in pixel interiors: whiter ring at the Mega ball's edge but ragged leaks onto its rim; brighter, blobbier floor glow on Storm Ship), 2 the nearest (a dark fringe, worse). (d) `MSC_FX_COMPOSITE_MSAA=0|1` (default 1, host only): at 2x/4x MSAA the host resolves colour and depth when the pass's target is bound, the sprites go into the single-sampled target against the resolved depth and the composite quad is drawn into the multisampled EFB (after the resolve of the scene so far, before the final one); on the console vitaGL cannot read a multisampled depth buffer (`vglStrikersBindFramebufferDepth` returns 0), so it stays idle there: NOT done for the Vita. (e) `MSC_VGX_CROWDTRIM_PROJ=0|1` (code default 0; the Vita plan sets 1 since gpueff18, 23 Nov, with `MSC_VGX_CROWDTRIM_WELD` / `_SKEW`: the differences are the same isolated threshold pixels as theirs, Vice celebration crowd fragments about -43 %; earlier note: celebration crowd fill 13.0/16.6/11.7/7.0/21.6 -> 11.6/16.5/10.9/7.0/20.7 screens, but 5 of 25 frames differ by up to 11 levels in under 100 pixels, unexplained): crowd trim also takes a projective texgen whose q row is (0,0,1,0) or (0,0,0,1) (q = 1): the goal celebration's crowd draw was never trimmed. (f) the hold policy's shed order has the effects target first (see `MSC_PICTURE_HOLD`). Census `[fxcensus]` prints the overhead of the depth copies and composites. |
| `MSC_FX_CELEB_MINPX=N` | 1.3. Code default `0`; the Vita plan's quality list sets `900000`. With the compositor on, in cut-scene and replay scenes (goal celebration, walk-out) a run of effects must cover at least N estimated pixels (EFB) to get its own reduced pass; smaller runs are drawn in place. Each run costs a depth copy, a composite and two scene changes, which in a celebration was 19, 56 and 20 runs on The Dump (host census, 960x544). `0` is the old behaviour. Open play is not touched. Host estimate; no console run yet. |
| `MSC_MEGA_SHARP=0\|1\|2` | 1.3 (w10 megaball; int34b). Code default `0`; **`1` in the Vita plan's quality list (`vita_plan.cpp` `kQuality`)**. Needs `MSC_FX_COMPOSITE=1`. `1` = the rules below in the Mega minigame (locked `960x544 1x` under `MSC_LOCK60_HELD_FULL=1` with `MSC_MEGA_BALLS=1` + `MSC_BALL_FINS=2`), and in the captain's launch and result cut-scenes (presentation functions `Megastrike*`, the game's `VgxMegaCutHint`) only the nearest-depth copy / `MSC_MEGA_BALLS` upsampling, keeping them as cut-scenes (`768x544`, 224-line FX cap, `MSC_HELD_GOV=1` active: real-Vita A/B `6512` vs `6513` eliminated all 5 mid-Mega-Strike resolution bounces); `2` = the cut-scenes also count as Mega scenes (`VgxMegaSceneHint`): held size, one sample, uncapped effects target, and no `MSC_HELD_GOV`. The effects compositor works so that the ball keeps its own edge: the reduced target is not capped by `MSC_FX_LOWRES_MAXH` (a half of the picture, not 224 lines) and its depth copy takes the NEAREST of the four depths of a texel (`MSC_FX_COMPOSITE_EDGE=2`), so a texel that touches the ball hides the glow behind it and the ball's full-size edge is not overwritten in 3 pixel steps. Cost: a thin ring round the ball without glow (about one reduced texel), the pass at 960x544 has 1.47 times the pixels of the 224-line one (host estimate; the governor `MSC_FX_GOV_MEGA` thins first). Render only: the game reads nothing back (NP_DET_TRACE identical). The uncapped target is a pass object of its own size, made at its first use in a Mega scene (not ahead: no mechanism for pool targets; the video memory is at the edge): `[megasharp] effects target WxH first used` logs it with the free memory. Log line `[megasharp] nearest-depth copy in use` prints at the first effect run that used the rules and every 600th. No shader text changed (shader pack unaffected). `0` is the 1.3 behaviour. |
| `MSC_MEGA_BALLS=0\|1` | 1.3 (megaballs27, 18 Nov). Code default `0`; the Vita plan's quality list sets `1`. Needs `MSC_MEGA_SHARP>=1` (and so `MSC_FX_COMPOSITE=1`). The balls that fly in during a Mega Strike (the Mega Save, and the shots of the captain's launch cut-scene) carry lightning and halo sprites of 7-13 million pixels a picture (game pixels), which the compositor draws at a quarter of the picture per side; MSC_MEGA_SHARP's nearest-depth copy hid that light over every reduced texel touching a ball, a dark ring in 4-pixel steps that read as a pixelated ball. With the switch, in the buffers MSC_MEGA_SHARP acts in: the depth copy reads the picture's depth at the texel centre, and the composite does nearest-depth upsampling: it reads the picture's depth at the pixel and at the four texel centres round it; where all agree within `MSC_MEGA_BALLS_EPS` (millionths, default 2000) it keeps the filtered light, at an edge it takes the light of the texel whose depth is nearest the pixel's. The balls' outline (and hands', characters') is then the picture's full-size one, the glow reaches it, and light in front of the ball stays on it. Sprite fill unchanged (the lightning stays a quarter size); the composite reads 5 depth and 2 colour texels a pixel against 1 (cost line `[megaballs]`: runs, composites, screens each). Host 960x544 crops: w8/megaballs27-pics/. Console A/B: kit run megaballs0. Render only. |
| `MSC_BALL_FINS=0\|1\|2` | 1.3 (mball24, 24 Nov; int34). Code default `1`; **`2` in the Vita plan (`kQuality`)**. `2` = `1` plus a soft edge: the texture's alpha is baked as a steep smooth ramp (about a screen pixel or two) and its draws go in two passes (back end `Draw`): the core (alpha above 0.9) as before, then the same draw blended (SRC_ALPHA, INV_SRC_ALPHA, no depth write, depth test LESS) for the rim, the two passes differing only in the alpha-reference uniforms of the same program (no new shader text; on the console one more patched fragment-program blend variant of that program, built at its first draw, or ahead by `MSC_CUT_LEARN` bit 2). Polygon edges of the spikes stay one-sample steps. The white spikes round a Mega Strike ball (the launch cut-scene's held ball and the shots) are one 32x32 CMPR texture with on/off alpha (texel hash `a43cbd668e5f274b`), alpha-tested and drawn 5 to 10 times larger than its texels: a staircase contour that no picture size or effects-pass setting changes (the ball's colour map is 256x256; the HD pack leaves the charge glow out). That one texture is upscaled at upload to 128x128 (alpha low-passed twice, Catmull-Rom, colour bled outwards, own mip chain, 87 KiB): the spikes get a smooth contour at the same place. Nothing else is touched. `MSC_BALL_FINS_LOG=1` logs the hash of every 32x32 CMPR upload. `0` = the game's texture as before. |

The picture program of the mode in force (the plan's `combined`, or `smooth`) gets an edge variant: a pixel the luma test calls an edge follows the edge four reads each way (1, 2, 4, 8 texels, on the line half a texel toward the steeper side), and is taken that far across (FXAA 3.11's idea, written from the published idea); pixels with no edge cost what the plain picture costs, an edge pixel costs about 13 reads more. The HUD stays sharp: the pass is used only when no 2D layer draw (layer 37 and up) is left in the picture, i.e. the HUD run of `MSC_VGX_HUDNATIVE` is drawn on the display after the picture pass, or there is no HUD (a picture with a 2D draw left in it, and multisampled pictures, keep the ordinary picture). A GPU gate skips it while the picture's GPU mean is over 95 % of its budget and brings it back under 85 % after 20 pictures (`vgx_ppaa.h`, test `renderer/tests/ppaa.cpp`). The programs are runtime-compiled (picture shaders are not in the pack) and are built at start-up by `MSC_PIC_PREWARM`; if one does not build, the ordinary picture is used. `[ppaa]` lines at class changes, gate moves and every 300 presents. Host: with `MSC_VGX_SCREEN` the extra variant of `MSC_VITA_UPSCALE_EXTRA` is made without the pass, so one run gives the before and after of the same picture. Crops: w8/aa24-pics. |

Reclaim is the rungs' depth only (about 11.6 MiB if all four 2x and 1x rungs of the ladder exist together: 3944+3456+3000+1500 KiB; the older 14.4 / 13 MiB figures counted rungs that do not coexist and the held blocks that stay); colour textures, pass objects (`MSC_VGX_NODEPTH_PASS` takes theirs) and the display are not shared. Same shaders. Console A/B: `fxmaxh960` against `mega960`. The Vita default is `224` (vita_plan.cpp) |
| `MSC_DYNRES_LEVEL=n` | Hold the dynamic resolution at level n (0 to 12) for tests |
| `MSC_DYNRES_FG=1` | Foreground first: before the whole scene shrinks (levels 6-7), the ladder takes background-only steps (`MSC_DYNRES_FG_STEPS`, default `lc`: `l` texture LOD of the stadium, crowd and sky draws, only when `MSC_FG_LODBIAS` is set; `c` crowd impostors refreshed half as often) and gives the whole scene back first when the load falls. Players, goalies, the ball, items and the HUD are never touched by a step. On by default in the Vita build ([1.2 defaults](#12-defaults-the-best-experience-is-on); `=0` turns it off), off in the desktop harness. `MSC_DYNRES_FG_STEP=k` with `MSC_DYNRES_LEVEL` holds k steps; `MSC_FG_LODBIAS=n` raw vitaGL LOD-bias units of the `l` step (1 to 8, default 0 = the step is not offered; the unit is believed to be 1/8 mip, unmeasured: a console test starts with 4) |
| `MSC_VGX_FGCENSUS=1` | Diagnostics: per window (`MSC_VGX_FGCENSUS_WINDOW`, default 600 frames) and scene class, the draws, triangles, pixels, texel fetches and distinct texture bytes of every draw class (players, items, effects, HUD, stadium, crowd, sky, far scenery); `MSC_VGX_FGCENSUS_ORDER=<frame>` lists the layer runs of a frame in draw order |
| `MSC_VGX_FGCENSUS=2` | Diagnostics, 1.3. As `1`, and: frames under the game's Mega Strike hint are their own scene (`mega`), every class is also split into `.add` (additive), `.blend` (other blending) and `.opaque`, with `ALL.add` / `ALL.blend` / `ALL.opaque` totals, and per scene the ten textures of blended frame-buffer draws that cover the most pixels (`tex <id> <w>x<h> <class> <blend> [marked]`). Pixels are screen area at the game's own size before the depth test: multiply by 1.82 for 960x544 |
| `MSC_VGX_OCCCENSUS=all\|every:N\|f1,f2` (+ `_SELFTEST`, `_TOP`) | census19, diagnostic, HOST GL ONLY, default off, changes nothing drawn (pictures identical, det trace identical). Depth-tested fill: each blended draw of the main picture is drawn once more into a count texture (same shaders and depth state, ONE+ONE) and the passing fragments are summed; `[occ]` lines in screens. Needs `MSC_VGX_SHCENSUS=1` for the area column (renderer/src/vgx_occcensus19.h) |
| `MSC_VGX_CENSUS_COPY_MIB=<MiB>` | 1.3, Vita default `8`, `0` no limit. The CPU copies of static geometry that `MSC_VGX_FGCENSUS` and `MSC_VGX_SHCENSUS` keep. Without a limit they ended Pipeline Central's census run in `operator new` at the walk-out in Vita3K. Past it a buffer is not read by the census (`[vgx] census:` line) |
| `MSC_VITA_ANISO=3` | Anisotropic filtering of trilinear textures (no Settings row since the 1.3 prune). On by default in the Vita build with the value `2` (`vita_plan.cpp` kQuality since 11 Nov: the Galaxy console runs preferred it; `3` is the earlier choice and still works), `0` turns it off. Not yet measured on a console: vitasdk's `MIPMAP_POINT` / `MIPMAP_LINEAR` are believed to be the anisotropic modes |
| `MSC_VITA_UPSCALE=off` | Plain scaling to the screen, no sharpening (the code's default is `smooth`: bilinear plus a light unsharp mask, amount `MSC_VITA_SHARPEN`, default 0.5, and 0.7-0.8 in Mega Strike scenes; the Vita plan sets `combined` since 11 Nov). `sharp`, `sharp+` and `linear` remain |
| `MSC_FPS_MEGA=auto` | Let Mega Strikes try 60 like other scenes (default: they stay at 30 with the full-size picture) |
| `MSC_VITA_QUEUE=off` | No GPU queue limit (default `auto`: at most 2 unfinished frames on the GPU, 1 while the GPU needs 78 % or more of the frame time) |
| `MSC_FPS=30` | A steady 30 fps (Settings > Frame Rate: 30 FPS). The default is `auto`: 60 where every CPU core and the GPU hold it |
| `MSC_GPU_BUSY_FIX=0\|1` | 1.3, default `1` (Vita build with vitaGL 0030). The GPU time the dynamic resolution, LOCKED 60 (step down and step up) and the auto queue depth read leaves out the display scene's wait for a display buffer. rt.c counts a scene's busy time as done minus the later of its submission and the previous scene's end, and the display's scene is begun with the back buffer's sync object as its fragment sync (gxm.c scene_reset), so near 60 fps with the producer ahead of the display busy_us follows the frame period with an idle GPU (Galaxy's ladder stepped down for nothing on its console; MSC_DYNRES_BLIND does not cover it, it acts only when no timed frame arrives). vitaGL `0030-rt-display-scene-time.patch` appends `display_us` to `vglRtGpuFrame`; `renderer/src/vgx_gpubusy.h` subtracts it less `MSC_GPU_BUSY_BLIT_US` (default 2000, the real upscale or copy). A frame of one scene (the display scene is the whole picture) keeps its raw time. The `[rt]` and diagnostic lines stay raw. Whether the trap bites here is unproven: no Strikers console log separates the cases (`[rt]` busy ~ the period with a light scene, or `MSC_VITA_DISPLAY_BUFFERS=2`, would show it). Render only. Log `[vgx-vita] GPU busy time:`. Tests: `renderer/tests/run_gpubusy_test.sh` (a trace with busy == period and 7 ms of 3D scenes: raw calls every frame over budget, corrected none and shows step-up headroom; a true overload stays one; `BREAK=1..2` fail). The host mirror `vgx::workload::GpuFrame` now has every field of the library's struct (it lacked `pixel_us` and `tiling_us`: the library wrote past it). Console kit run `gpubusy0` |
| `MSC_FPS_BETWEEN=pace` | 1.3 (Settings > Frame Rate: UP TO 60). Default now `pace` through `vita_plan.cpp` `kBetweenDefault` (a plan default; `MSC_FPS_BETWEEN=30` or the 30 FPS choice turns it off; not under `MSC_LOCK60`): AUTO alone went back to 60 only below 80 % of a refresh for 2 s after a cool-down of 6 s to 4 min, so a scene needing 14-26 ms a frame held 30 with 38-70 pictures a second available, the shape of Galaxy's old pacing rule. Kit run `between30` is the A/B. Before 1.3 default `30`, off: under AUTO, a match whose busiest stage stays under `MSC_FPS_BETWEEN_ENTER` (26.7 ms, about 37 pictures a second) for a second runs free (swap mode 3, no display gate, frames in flight + 1) instead of waiting out a held 30; over `MSC_FPS_BETWEEN_LEAVE` (30 ms) on average for 20 frames it goes back to 30, with a cool-down of 6 s doubled per failed try. 60 is decided as before; replays, Mega Strikes, menus and every LAN or online session stay as without the switch. The renderer keeps the 30 budget. Model: `native/tools/fps/pace_mid_model.cpp`; no console measurement yet |
| `MSC_PRESENT_SYNC=0\|1\|2` | 1.3: each picture on the vblank of its frame's start plus an adaptive latency (the Galaxy port's PRESENT_SYNC). `0` off, `1` the `[present]` line only, `2` hold pictures. Default: `2` while `MSC_FPS_BETWEEN=pace` is in force, `1` otherwise; policy 30 and LAN sessions are only measured. `MSC_PRESENT_SYNC_MIN`/`_MAX`/`_LAT` (1/6/4 vblanks), `MSC_PRESENT_SYNC_RAISE` (4 late of the last 32) |
| `MSC_VITA_CLOCK_POLICY=max` | 1.3 Vita default, as Galaxy (r99): 1 s into the process the ARM clock is measured; below 470 MHz one 500 request, refused then 444; 470 or more (a PSVshellPlus profile at about 500) gets no ARM request at all. GPU 222, bus 222 and xbar 166 (the maxima) are always asked. If the plugin later lets go (the report falls under 400), the watch asks 500 first, then 444. Clock speed cannot change a result (fixed 50 Hz steps), it only changes how many steps fit in a frame's time. Test `test_clock_policy.cpp` |
| `MSC_VITA_CLOCK_POLICY=measure` | The ARM clock is measured first; 470 MHz or more (a clock plugin) gets no ARM request, anything else asks for 444 as before. `fixed`: 1.2, 444 with no measurement. `max`: one ordinary 500 request, then one 444 if it is refused and the core measures under 470. `MSC_VITA_CPU_MHZ=111\|166\|222\|333\|444` wins over all of these, unchanged. A clock left to a plugin is put back to 444 only when the power API's report falls below 400 from 400 or more at the start: under PSVshellPlus it reads 333 from the start with the core at 498, and that is left alone. A `clocks:` line in `log.txt` has requested, returned, reported and measured values |
| `MSC_FPS_HYST=1` | The 1.1 AUTO thresholds (default `2`: down after 4 frames over 92 % of a refresh, up after 2 s within 80 %, a longer wait after every failed try of the scene). | `MSC_FPS_SCENE_PAUSE=0` | The 1.1 scene identity of AUTO: pausing or opening the home-button menu counts as a new scene and resets the retry back-off and the hold of a replay (default `1`: the pause bits are ignored) |
| `MSC_FPS_REPLAY=30` | Replays, goal celebrations and cut-scenes always at 30 (like Mega Strikes). Default `fit`: AUTO decides, but a replay that did not hold a tried 60 stays at 30 until it ends, and the renderer may draw it smaller (to 0.70, `MSC_DYNRES_PACED_MIN`) to keep the 30 even; `auto`: as in 1.1 |
| `MSC_LAT_ANCHOR=0` | The 1.1 position of the 30 fps cadence anchor (after the frames-in-flight gate's own refresh; default `1`: before it). `MSC_LAT_RETRY=0`: a gate that switched itself off stays off (default: it tries again after 900, 1800, 3600, 7200 frames) |
| `MSC_DYNRES_RULES=1` | The 1.1 rules for the larger pictures (default `2`: a full second of per-frame headroom, two frames over 92 % send it back and lock the larger sizes for 20 s and more, no change within 90 presents of the last, none within 5 s of a 30/60 change). Values: `MSC_DYNRES_UP_ABOVE` (0.80), `MSC_DYNRES_DOWN_ABOVE` (0.92), `MSC_DYNRES_UP_SECS` (1.0), `MSC_DYNRES_GAP` (90), `MSC_DYNRES_AUTO_LOCK` (5 s) |
| `MSC_DYNRES_UNDO=0\|1` | `1` (Vita player build; host harness `0`) | Every step down of the picture size in an ordinary scene is judged on its own measured GPU frames (the mean of the 24 frames before it against the next 30, with the model's expected saving as the bar, at least 3 %). A step that saved nothing (and did not make the scene heavier) is undone, the level it left becomes the floor for that scene's block time, and the log says `no gain, undone` or `kept`. Stalls cannot end the check: it counts measured frames. `MSC_DYNRES_UNDO_FRAMES` (30, 8 to 300). `MSC_DYNRES_FAKE=a,b` simulates the GPU time on the host (tool `run_dynres_undo_test.sh`) |
| `MSC_DYNRES_SEVERE=0\|1` | `1` (Vita player build; desktop `0`) | The GPU's mean time at 1.8 times the frame budget (`MSC_DYNRES_SEVERE_RATIO`) for 3 s (`MSC_DYNRES_SEVERE_SECS`) of its own measured time in play opens a deeper floor, 0.70 per side (`MSC_DYNRES_SEVERE_MIN`), under the ordinary 0.86 at 30; the ordinary ladder then steps down at once, and `MSC_DYNRES_UNDO` judges the step. It closes after 180 frames predicted to fit at the ordinary floor. Not in Mega Strikes, replays and cut-scenes (their own floors), not after a step found to buy nothing, not when `MSC_DYNRES_MIN` is set by hand. The frame-rate controller's prediction keeps the ordinary floor. Render only. From Galaxy's `GXV_AUTOQ_SEVERE`; the ladder here never waited for a quiet moment, so what could hold such a scene is the floor. Log: `dynamic resolution: severe overload` |
| `MSC_DYNRES_LATEHOLD=<ms>` | `0` (off) in the code; `2000` in the Vita plan (`vita_plan.cpp` kQuality; `=0` turns it off) | 1.3 (borrowed from strikersVita's dynamic scale, which drops to a second, smaller target for 2 s after any late present). A frame whose own GPU time is over the budget (`MSC_DYNRES_LATEAT`, percent, default `100`) asks for a step down at once instead of waiting for the smoothed time to pass 90 %: as many rungs as that frame's time says it needs to fit in 82 %, at most `MSC_DYNRES_LATESTEP` (default `2`), one at least; at a larger picture (anti-aliasing rungs) it is the existing go-back-to-the-game's-size rule, taken on one frame instead of two. Every late frame restarts a hold of `<ms>` of clock time in which no step goes up. The queued frames measured after a step (4) only renew the hold. Only the GPU's own time counts, so a frame late for the CPU asks for nothing (strikersVita's rule reacts to any late present). At 60 only unless `MSC_DYNRES_LATE30=1`: at 30 the GPU queue keeps two frames (`MSC_VITA_QUEUE_FIXED30`), which hides a single late frame; at 60 the auto queue goes to one frame once the GPU needs 78 % of the budget. Not in Mega Strikes, paced scenes, below a step found to buy nothing, or with a forced level. Trace model (`renderer/tools/dynres_sim.cpp`, synthetic traces shaped to the console logs): at 60 with a one-frame queue, late frames 0.51 -> 0.21 % (open play) and 0.43 -> 0.23 % (Galaxy-shaped) for about 8 % fewer pixels; at 30 with two frames queued, no change. Render only. Log: `dynamic resolution: ... a late frame (MSC_DYNRES_LATEHOLD)` |

Test: `run_vgx_arena_test.sh` |
| `MSC_VGX_ARENA_SOFT=<0/1>` | `1` (default on: a refused block costs a few draws for a frame, never the program) | Growth of a frame buffer when the C heap has no block for it. The vertex arena past its floor tries the step, then the bare size, and when neither fits the draws that do not fit that frame are dropped (one `[arena] vertex arena could not grow` line, a count in the `[arena]` census line) and the next frame tries again; the other frame vectors (indices, uniforms, mesh uploads) ask again every 2 ms for up to 1.5 s with one `[vgx]` line before the program ends with the allocator's FATAL line; a record chunk (`vgx_rec.cpp`) waits for the player to hand chunks back for up to 3 s. 1.1 and 1.2 wrote through the null block (a guest data abort, C2-12828-1). Not read by the simulation. `0` = the old way. Test: `run_vgx_arena_test.sh` (`vgx_arena_soft`). `MSC_VGX_ARENA_SIM=<KiB>` (test only) refuses any growth to that size or more |

Nothing is made: 32 MiB move from the C heap (peak 82.4 to 83.8 MiB in 6027/6033/6124, free 8 to 12 MiB) to the system's free main memory (33 to 38 MiB, so 1 to 6 MiB would be left). It does NOT make `wrcopy`'s DMA possible: a USER_RW block is a cached address at 0x88400000 and above (the MEM1/MEM2 arenas, 6122.log:58-60); the rule needs both addresses below 0x81000000 (only the uncached and CDRAM pools are, 0x62200000 and 0x70100000). With N > 0 every `glDeleteTextures` / `glDeleteFramebuffers` waits N presents (3 is Galaxy's value; `vglSwapBuffers` deletes it then), and a delete of a texture a scene drew into within 4 presents (stamped at scene begin, guard on only) or of the colour of the bound framebuffer is reported once per call site and kind: `[free-guard] FREE WHILE REFERENCED texture 17 (...) by 0x8123abcd` (resolve with `arm-vita-eabi-addr2line -e <image>.elf`), and a `[free-guard] N deletes held back` line every 300 presents, with the peak sizes of the collector lists of one picture (of 16384 and 128: `** COLLECTOR LIST OVERFLOW **`; vitaGL's `mark_as_dirty` has no bounds check outside DEBUG_GC, a longer list writes into the next picture's list and those blocks are freed one swap early). With N the free guard of 0034 runs in referenced-only mode: a `glDeleteTextures` / `glDeleteFramebuffers` call that names a block a scene drew into within the last four presents (or the colour of the bound framebuffer) is held N presents and reported once per site (`[free-guard] FREE WHILE REFERENCED`); a call that names none is immediate, exactly as with no guard, so a run that never makes such a delete is unchanged (the 0034 tour found none). `MSC_VGX_FREE_GUARD=N` (every delete held) wins when both are set. | `MSC_DYNRES=off` | No dynamic resolution: the game's own picture size everywhere (also turns the GPU timers off) |
| `MSC_DYNRES_MAX=1.0` | Do not draw larger than the game's own size when the GPU has time to spare (default up to 1.2 per side) |
| `MSC_VGX_MSAA=2` or `4` | A rung of the dynamic-resolution ladder (the Vita build sets `4`; `0` turns it off; host default 0): when the GPU has time to spare for a full second, draw the scene with 4x then 2x (or 2x) multisampling at the game's own size instead of the larger, supersampled sizes. Needs the vitaGL per-framebuffer MSAA patch (`native/platform/vita/vitagl/0011-*.patch`); without it the switch does nothing. The SMOOTH filter then leaves out its edge smoothing (`MSC_VGX_MSAA_EDGE=1` keeps it) |
| `MSC_VGX_MSAA_COST=1.3,1.9` | The GPU cost of 2x and 4x against one sample that the dynamic-resolution model assumes (a guess, for console tuning) |
| `MSC_DYNRES_MIN=0.93` | The smallest picture in menus and matches (default 0.86 per side; 0.75 to 1.0) |
| `MSC_MEGA_RENDER=off` | Mega Strike scenes use the ordinary floor (default: they may go down to 50 % if the GPU is still short) |
| `MSC_MEGA_RENDER=WxH` | One fixed internal size during Mega Strike scenes, e.g. `448x320` (160x120 to 960x544). Ignored when `MSC_VITA_RENDER` is set |
| `MSC_VITA_RT_TIMERS=0` | GPU timers off (the plan sets `2` in the player build and `3` in a lab build unless `MSC_DYNRES=0`) (the dynamic resolution then keeps the game's size, apart from the Mega Strike start) (bit 1 = the CPU timers). vitaGL 0065 (nxrain25, counters only, costs nothing without bit 1): `begin` in the `[rt]` line's `CPU scene change` also holds the depth and render target creation before `sceGxmBeginScene`; with bit 1 the line now ends with ` \| begin_disp X (max Y) begin_fbo X (max Y)`, ms a frame and the longest single call since start, of the two `sceGxmBeginScene` calls alone (display target, framebuffer object target). Not run on a Vita |
| `MSC_FX_THIN=2` | Stronger thinning (up to alpha 24, fading out by 48), for console A/B tests |
| `MSC_FX_ZERO=0` | Also rasterise fully transparent particles |
| `MSC_FX_OFFVIEW=0\|1\|2\|3` | gx25 (25 Nov, from the Galaxy port's `GXV_HOTPROF_PTCLCULL`). Code default `0`; Vita plan `1` (int26: exact off-view sprite cull). Render only: nothing the simulation reads. A sprite (the mesh writers in `main.cpp` `BuildParticleQuads`) whose four corners all lie beyond the same side plane (clip x or y beyond +w or -w, with a margin of 2 % of the corner's |w|) of the view the mesh is attached to is not written: the mesh is made as short as what is written (`NpFxTrimPacket`, the packet's vertex count and the flush range), and sprites that are collapsed (zero alpha, `MSC_FX_ZERO`) are left out the same way. Every particle is still updated and counted, in the same order (`UpdateParticle`, the thinning, the governor's census, `NpFxTightParticle`): only the vertex write is skipped. A mesh that would be empty keeps one collapsed sprite, so its packet and the runs of effects draws around it stay. Sprite systems only (not the warble layer, not models, not rain). `2`: count only, `3`: count and compare the matrices with the game's `glViewProjectPoint` (`[fxoffview]` line every 600 pictures: sprites, collapsed, off the view, kept, checks, mismatches). The renderer's effects pass takes a run of marked draws by its vertex count (`MSC_FX_LOWRES_MINVERTS`) and the compositor by pixels, so a cut run may change which pass a draw goes to: the proof is picture equality on the host GL back end, not the glnull frame hash (which hashes the vertices) | `0` |
