| `MSC_VITA_UPSCALE=combined` | 1.3. The Vita plan sets it (`vita_plan.cpp` kSpeed); the code's own default stays `smooth` (host harness, lab builds without the plan); `=smooth` returns to the old look. Not timed on a console. SMOOTH's look in one pass from FAST's three fetches, at coordinates the vertex shader computed, no dependent read, fragment arithmetic in half: `d = centre - mean of the two half-texel diagonal fetches` is both the unsharp detail and, with their difference, the edge measure; one gain takes the sharpening away at edges. Real compiler, primary instructions: SMOOTH 89 (six reads), its plain form 32 (five), FAST 9 (three), `combined` 15 (three). A fit to the Galaxy console figures (0.9 / 5.3 / 8.1 ms for plain stretch / sharpen / sharpen + edge at 960x544: 0.68 ms a read, 0.037 ms an instruction) gives 2.6 ms at most, FAST 2.4; those reads were dependent, these are not, so about 2 is likely. An estimate, not a measurement. Against SMOOTH's output on 9 real frames: 45.3 dB, gradient 1.000 (FAST 41.4 dB, 1.035). Not in the shader pack until it is regenerated: one cold compile. Kit run `upcombined` |
| `MSC_VITA_UPSCALE=cas` | 1.3, default off. Contrast-adaptive sharpening, five fetches, no dependent read; 28 instructions, 53 with its edge step (`MSC_VITA_EDGE_AA=0` leaves that out). Dearer than `combined` and no closer to SMOOTH. Kit run `upcas` |
| `MSC_VGX_HUDNATIVE=1` | 1.3, default off; set to `1` only with LOCKED 60 (`MSC_LOCK60`, itself off by default), which lists it among its supporting defaults (`vita_plan.cpp` kLock60). The tail of a frame's 2D layers (FrontEnd and after, `vgx_hudplan.h`, test `native/tools/render/run_hudplan_test.sh`) is drawn on the display at its own size over the upscaled scene, in the display's scene: no new scene, and each HUD pixel costs about 1.8 of its old self at 640x448 (counted, not timed). The run stops at any copy that reads the EFB, a pass, an effect draw, shader work, destination-alpha blending. It no longer tests against the 3D scene's depth, which also brings back the score digits the scene hid. `MSC_VGX_HUDNATIVE_LAYER=<eCLV>` moves the first layer. Off with `MSC_VITA_UPSCALE=off` and `MSC_VITA_MSAA`. Settings > Native HUD. Kit run `hudnative`. `[split] held N finished M` line every 600 presents (split17: held must equal finished; a buffer that ends with the display still standing in is finished by the back end and counted as leaked) |
| `MSC_VGX_HUDCULL=0\|1` | mark25 (25 Nov), code default `1` (on, a bug fix; `0` = as before), render only. In the HUD run of `MSC_VGX_HUDNATIVE` the display stands in for the EFB with the viewport of a framebuffer object (negative height), but vitaGL's `change_cull_mode` (misc.c) picks the cull mode of the DISPLAY (`is_rendering_display` adds 1); the two tables differ by exactly the inversion the opposite `y_scale` needs, so a draw with culling on was culled on the wrong side in the run. The score digits (`GLS_Culling 0`) never noticed; the player indicators, glow and ball-carrier arrow (`Indicators.cpp`, `glSetDefaultState` = cull on) vanished on the console and not on the host GL (the host has no split present, so no HUD run). With it on the run asks for the opposite face (`ApplyRenderState`, `g_hud.on`); no-cull and CULL_ALL draws are untouched. Proof: `renderer/tests/run_hud_cull_model_test.sh` (vitaGL's own `change_cull_mode` / `glViewport` over a model calibrated on vitaGL's display and FBO cases; `EXTRA=-DFIX0` and `EXTRA=-DBREAK1` must fail). Console: `MSC_VGX_HUDCULL=0` brings the missing indicators back; `MSC_VGX_HUDNATIVE=0` shows them too |
| `MSC_SCORE_NODEPTH=1` | 1.3. Code default off; `1` in the Vita plan (`vita_plan.cpp` kSpeed). The score digits neither test nor write depth. Tested (the default) they vanish wherever the scene's depth lies in front of them; written, they sit in the depth buffer under what follows |
| `MSC_VGX_PASSES_WIPE=<n>`, `MSC_VGX_PASSES_HUD=1` | Diagnostics. The first n frames that clear depth alone and the 12 after each go through `MSC_VGX_PASSES` (frame numbers differ from run to run); the 2D layers listed draw by draw. The second also gives the layer number in a forked send, where `LayerDrawClass` otherwise sees a shadow view: layer and class are 0 on the Vita (`[fillcensus] layer=0 cls=0`) |
| `MSC_VITA_SHOT_HOLD=1` | Vita3K only. The listed presents stay on the screen 4 s for a capture from outside; the emulator's guest memory does not hold the display, so a read-back is stale |
| `MSC_VITA_UPSCALE=fast` | 1.3, default off (`smooth` stays the default). Bilinear plus an unsharp mask from three fetches: the centre and two samples half a texel off along one diagonal, all at coordinates the vertex shader computed, with no per-pixel clamp and no edge smoothing. SMOOTH reads six times per screen pixel at computed coordinates, and the native compiler counted about 89 instructions for it (29 without edge smoothing) over 522 240 screen pixels. The rectangle gives up one texel per side so that no fetch leaves the display copy (a 0.3 % zoom). Sharpness along the axes is SMOOTH's; along one diagonal it is weaker. The shader is not in the pack: the first start compiles it once (needs `libshacccg`), or falls back to the blit. Not measured on a Vita |
| `MSC_VGX_LODBIAS=0..-8` | 1.3, default `0`. LOD bias of every mipmapped texture that is not a render target, in sceGxm's raw units of 1/8 of a level (`vgx_picture_math.h`, `BindTexture`; Settings > Texture Detail: `-2`, `-4`). The picture is mapped for 640 wide and shown on 960, so a small negative bias shows detail the screen can carry. Risks: shimmer in motion and more texture reads; the cost has not been measured on a console. Adds to the dynamic resolution's background step. Read once at start. |
| `MSC_VGX_LODBIAS_RUNG=<percent>` | 1.3, Vita default `60` (0 = off). The texture LOD bias follows the size the scene is drawn at: percent/100 x log2 of the geometric mean of (render width / picture width, render height / picture height), in 1/8 levels, 0 at the screen's size, never below -31 (`RungLodBiasRaw`, `vgx_picture_math.h`). Mipmapped textures of 3D draws only: not render targets, not the 2D layers (layer 37 up), not passes or draws on the display. Applied through the per-texture bias cache of `MSC_VGX_LODBIAS`, so a size change costs one `glTexParameteri` per texture at its next bind and nothing per draw; vitaGL writes it into the texture's control word at once (no upload). The sum with `MSC_VGX_LODBIAS` and the background step is clamped to the 6-bit field (before: a wrap was possible at -32). Not shader text. 60 is Galaxy's console choice (`GXV_RUNG_LODBIAS=60`: 100 shimmers); 100 gives the exact native level. Pictures were not possible: the emulator's matches drift apart in time between runs. Console A/B: `lodrung0` against default |
| `MSC_VITA_SHARPEN_SCALE=<k>` | 1.3, Vita default `1.0` (0 = the old rule by the ladder's scale). The sharpening of smooth / fast / cas / combined is k x sqrt(stretch - 1), at most `MSC_VITA_SHARPEN_CAP` (0.8), where stretch is the geometric mean of screen / picture size on both axes (0.29 at 848x480 with k 0.8, 0.46 at 720x408; the old rule gave 0.5 at every fixed size). A picture within 3 % of the screen's size is copied by the blit: no picture shader, no sharpening, and no `MSC_VGX_HUDNATIVE` split (the HUD is native already). Uniform value only, the picture programs are unchanged. Fit on host-sim frames (native frame box-reduced, then `combined`): the amount nearest the native picture by PSNR is flat between 0.2 and 0.5 for stretch 1.1 to 1.35; gradient energy against native, today / this at k 0.8: 0.91 / 0.87 (848x480), 0.87 / 0.86 (720x408), 0.86 / 0.86 (640x448); k 1.0 sits between. The blit is a plain bilinear stretch; the HUD edge ring is bounded by the shader's own edge term and the 0.8 cap |

With `MSC_VITA_ANISO=3|2`, a texture whose larger side is under this keeps trilinear filtering (vitaGL 0031, `vglSetAnisotropicMinSize`; judged when the filter is set, from the texture's own size words: nothing per draw). Not shader text. Which of filter value 2 or 3 is the 4x mode is not proven: Galaxy's console runs used 2 (`GXV_AUTOQ_ANISO=1`), Strikers has used 3 |
| `MSC_VITA_ANISO_SCOPE=0\|1` | Wave 24 (tile24), code default `0`, Vita plan under `MSC_LOCK60` (LOCKED 60) `1` (int31: Quality 30 keeps full blended AF). With `MSC_VITA_ANISO` on: `1` keeps the anisotropic filter only on draws that are opaque and write depth (pitch, stands, ramps); a draw with a blend (effects, glow, particles, crowd) samples the same texture trilinear. Per texture, by its descriptor's minification bits (vitaGL 0057 `vglTexAnisoOff`); a texture used by both kinds of draw changes each time the kind changes (counted in `texParams`). Host model: `MSC_VGX_HOST_ANISO=<n>` (host GL, not the console) puts the host GL's anisotropic filter n on every mipmapped texture with the same scope rule. |
| `MSC_VGX_TILED_RT=0\|1\|2` | Wave 24 (tile24), default `0`, console only (the host does not model tiling, so no host picture proves it). `1`: pass targets and EFB-copy textures (RGBA8, 32 texels or more each way) are `SCE_GXM_COLOR_SURFACE_TILED` surfaces with `sceGxmTextureInitTiled` textures (vitaGL 0057 `vglTexMakeTiledTarget`: storage of ceil(w/32)*32 x ceil(h/32)*32 texels, zeroed like the empty linear one). The GPU stores and reloads 32 x 32 tiles into contiguous memory instead of 32 rows a pitch apart. The EFB, the display, the screenshot and every target the CPU reads stay linear; `ReadTexture` and vitaGL `glReadPixels` refuse a tiled target. `2`: only targets whose width and height are multiples of 32 (no padded storage: the careful first step). A draw that writes depth is never cut. `ZERO_COUNT=1` prints `[zerodraw]` every 300 pictures: draws, blended draws, and how many are provably no-ops. |

| `MSC_VITA_CLOCK_KEEP=1\|0` | 1.3, default `1`. A thread of the lowest priority reads the four clocks every 2 s. One reported below its value at the start is asked for again, at most 3 times until it recovers; `clocks: GPU reports ...` and `clocks: GPU is back at ...` go to the basic record. A plugin's lower clock is the starting value and is left alone; a plugin's ARM clock is not touched. clockfix17: a 500 the game asked for and got (report at least 444, the baseline is what the getter said, e.g. 494) is kept too, by `RestoreArm` (500, then 444 if refused). |
| `MSC_FREEZE_BUTTON=1\|0` | galaxyport18, default `1` in the player build, `0` in lab builds (a test script that holds L + R + Select must not end a lab run). A thread of the lowest priority (191, 16 KiB, `msc_freezebutton`) reads the pad itself every 100 ms (`sceCtrlPeekBufferPositive`, not the game's pad path); L + R + Select held for 3 s without a break ends the game: see "The freeze button" below. Ignores the first 20 s of the process and any time the settings screen is open. `0` starts no thread |
| `MSC_VITA_CLOCK_MWATCH=1\|0` | clockfix17, default `1` (quiet builds too) | Only when nothing was asked of the ARM clock (the core measured 470 or more at 1 s: a plugin's profile) AND the getter reported under 400 (the PSVshellPlus case: 333 with the core at 498), where the old watch cannot see anything. The `np_clock_measure` thread (lowest user priority 191, below every game, render and audio thread; wakes every 30 s; one 12 ms `MeasureMHz` spin, no more often) feeds each reading to `MeasuredLook`: two readings under 430 in a row, then `RestoreArm` (500, then 444 if refused), at most 3 times a run; then it ends (quiet builds) or goes on logging. A reading cut short by a busier core only reads low and costs a harmless request (a lock answers 0). Log (logging builds only): `clocks: measured N MHz twice; requested 500 rc ...`. `0`: no requests. Not the simulation. Test `test_clock_policy.cpp` (R1 checks) |

The reason: `MSC_VITA_VBUF_CACHED=1` measured 13.6 -> 17.4 ms back-end CPU on 23 Nov, and one write-back syscall per draw (up to ~330 a picture) is the suspect, not the bytes. Host proof `renderer/tests/run_stream_scene_test.sh` (the real arena text and macros against a fake cache: every written byte clean after the submit, one write-back per scene with new bytes and none per draw, slices not reused before the fence, 13 mutants plus placement checks of `scene_end`). How frame data is copied INTO the uncached pools (stream buffers, the four uniform ring copies; copies under 192 bytes stay `sceClibMemcpy`): `1` two aligned 32 byte NEON stores per 64 bytes, `2` one 16 register `vstm` per 128 bytes, `3` `sceDmacMemcpy` for 8 KiB and more. `auto` runs a start-up benchmark on a 252 KiB uncached block (verifying every variant byte for byte against the source, a mismatch disqualifies it) and takes the fastest if it beats the plain copy by 15 %; `bench` only logs it. `[wrcopy]` lines at start-up and `[wrcopy] copies` counters. The bytes written are the same, so the picture is unchanged (host: `renderer/tests`-free proof under qemu-arm, `w8/be24/wrcopy_qemu_test.c`: byte-exact for every dst/src alignment and length, a mutant fails). Kit runs `wrcopyauto`, `wrcopybench`. |

With `MSC_VITA_WRCOPY=3` (what `auto` chose on the console, 6121 / 6122) a copy the DMA engine does not take went to `sceClibMemcpy` (0.34-0.41 GB/s into the uncached pools; 768 byte copies 408-410 MB/s). The DMA engine takes only copies of 8 KiB+ with **both** addresses below 0x81000000, and the executable sits at 0x81000000 (log: C heap 0x8286d8a8), so a source in the C heap (every `std::vector` of vertices and indices, the uniform buffers) is never DMA'd: in play mode 3 was plain copies. Now those copies use mode 1's 64 byte NEON stores (768 B: 768-780 MB/s, 252 KiB: 599-623 MB/s, MEASURED start-up benchmark of 6121 / 6122); DMA stays for what it is allowed to take. The same bytes (host test `renderer/tests/run_wrcopy_pick_test.sh`: 712,800 cases of size, alignment, address class and DMA error against an oracle, ASan/UBSan, 20 mutants; the start-up `[wrcopy]` benchmark still compares every variant incl. mode 3 byte for byte). `auto` now says mode 1 when the benchmark's own large copy was not taken by the DMA engine. Console lines: `[wrcopy] benchmark addresses` (source / destination, DMA TAKEN or NOT) and, beside `[lane]`, `[wrcopy] per picture` (copies, bytes, DMA copies, 8 KiB+ copies refused for their address, DMA errors). The gain (ESTIMATE -1.0 to -1.7 ms a picture on core 1) is not measured on the console; the small-copy column of the benchmark is noisy (the same sceClibMemcpy path read 413 and 799 MB/s in two runs). |
| (`MSC_VITA_WRCOPY_FB` order and counters) | nxr6a (NEON round 6 #6, verified): `WriteCopySetup()` benchmarked the variants BEFORE it read `MSC_VITA_WRCOPY_FB`, so the `auto` benchmark always ran mode 3 with the fallback ON and an `FB=0` run could pick a mode on numbers it does not have; `FB` is now set first (and with FB=0 a not-DMA mode 3 is `0`, not `1`). The `[wrcopy]` counters: `vgl_wr_copy` counted `[0]/[1]` before the dispatch, so the FB=0 plain fallbacks counted as NEON and every DMA copy twice; the variant now counts the branch it takes (`[0]/[1]` a copy whose bulk is NEON stores, `[2]/[3]` a copy made wholly by `sceClibMemcpy`, `[4]/[5]` DMA) and the log line says so. Bytes written unchanged. Host: `run_wrcopy_pick_test.sh` (new branch-counter check in every case, 4 new mutants) | - | exact (counters and start-up order only) | `[wrcopy] per picture` line: the three groups add up to all copies of the picture |
| `MSC_VITA_FBO_KEEP=0\|1` | nxgl25, vitaGL patch 0062, code default `0`, Vita plan `1` in `kQuality` (int26/int26b, console 6250/6405: RT destroys 26->1 and peak CDRAM dropped 6.0 MiB; fixes Q30 RT-destroy churn too). `glFramebufferTexture2D(GL_COLOR_ATTACHMENT0, 0)` read the size of texture slot 0 into the object before it looked at the id, and always dropped the render target, so the next attach of the same size re-made it (`sceGxmCreateRenderTarget`, 46-55 ms a picture-size step in 6122) and, through the garbled size, the depth surface too. With `1` the detach only lets go of the texture: width, height, render target and depth surface stay; a later attach of a texture of the same width and height (the samples are the object's own) takes them back, a different size makes them again; `glDeleteFramebuffers` frees them as always; a scene on an object with no colour texture is refused (`gxm.c` scene_reset, it read a null pointer). Host proof `renderer/tests/run_fbo_keep_test.sh` (the real attach and delete text, 800 random sequences with the switch on and off, ledger of made and freed objects, ASan/UBSan, 19 mutants). Console: `[fbkeep]` line, `[hitch]` `rt` figures on size steps, `[vgx-vita] vitaGL pools now` (VRAM free). Independent of and additive to `MSC_VGX_RT_POOL`. nxr6b (NEON round 6 finding 3, both claims RIGHT): gxm.c `scene_reset` refuses an object with no colour texture whether or not `MSC_VITA_ROBUST` is on; the kept detach sets `dirty_framebuffer` when the object is the one in use. |
| `MSC_VITA_FBO_DUALRT=0\|1\|2` | nxr4b, vitaGL patch 0063, default `0` (OFF: the driver memory is not measured and whether the GPU orders two render targets' writes to one colour buffer cannot be shown off the console). `vglFramebufferDualRT(fb, 1)`: the object keeps a second `SceGxmRenderTarget` (scene slots only: `sceGxmBeginScene` gets the colour and depth surfaces of the object either way, gxm.c scene_reset) and the pictures of odd frame number begin their scenes on it, so two pictures in flight never share the 8 scene slots (`MAX_SCENES_PER_FRAME`) of one target. `1`: the EFB (both targets are made together, at the first scene); `2`: also a pass object from its 5th scene in a picture. What libgxm does when a target's scenes are used up is NOT proved: the tree has no sceGxm header or text; vitaGL's own comments only say that the count is the most a target takes, and the 0033 census text says a further scene "waits for the GPU" (an assumption: no console log ever carried a `[scenes]` line, and 8 logs show 0 BeginScene errors). The `8 of 8` of the fly-in is not shown to be the EFB: host fs.csv has `efbScenes` 4 there and a pass object can reach 8 only because `MSC_VGX_FBO_USES` caps it at 8. Host proof `renderer/tests/run_dual_rt_test.sh` (the real texts of the make, pick, drop sites, attach and delete; a GPU model with scene slots; the surfaces every scene is begun with are the same on and off in 162,191 scenes; every target made is freed exactly once; with the pair on 5..8 scenes a picture block 0 times against 59 in 60 pictures with one target; with 3 pictures in flight the pair does not help: 58 against 59; failed make falls back; ASan/UBSan; mutants). Console: `[dualrt]` line beside `[scenes]` (second targets made, driver memory KiB from `sceGxmGetRenderTargetMemSize`, scenes begun on them, failed makes), `[rt]` CPU `scene change ... begin B` ms, `[scenes]`, `[hitch]` `be`. Companion A/B (existing switches): `MSC_VITA_DISPLAY_SCENES=3` first, `MSC_VGX_FBO_USES=4`. If the pair flickers, the colour buffer itself would need the pair (+1.42 MiB). |

A dynamic draw of 8192 bytes or more of vertices (not the crowd, triangles only) whose bytes were seen in an earlier picture (XXH3 128 of the vertex bytes, found by a 192 byte sample) draws from a buffer that holds exactly those bytes, through `vglDrawElementsDirect` with the stream address shifted back by `vtxFirst * stride`; `UploadStream` then leaves those ranges out of the stream copy (`glBufferData(NULL)` + `glBufferSubData` per gap). The six player draws are half a MiB of the 1 MiB a picture writes into uncached RAM. `=2` keeps a copy of each cached range and compares every hit (`mismatch` must stay 0; the host proof, on the console only a short check); `MSC_VGX_BREAK=128` is the mutant (a hit needs only the sample). `[dyncache]` lines: considered, hits, KiB saved, mismatch, evicted. | `MSC_VGX_TEXSWIZZLE=<side>` | 1.3, default `0` off; `1` means 32. swz20: patch 0020 now takes only 2 and 4 byte texels of 8 to 1024 a side (Galaxy's limits, its swizzled upload has run on the console without a fallback) and checks `sceGxmTextureInitSwizzled` on a scratch descriptor before committing. Uncompressed power-of-two textures of at least that many texels a side end their upload in the swizzled layout (vitaGL patch 0020), the layout DXT textures always had. Same texels. One transfer per level, waited for, so a slot uploaded before (a movie plane, anything that changes) stays linear. **Textures with mip levels stay linear** unless `MSC_VGX_TEXSWIZZLE_MIPS=1`: the 1.3 console run showed a wrong HUD icon and nobody found why; the one thing in the patch that rests on a guess is the place of the smaller levels of a swizzled uncompressed texture (vitaGL builds such chains for DXT only; levels of 1 to 4 texels also go through a transfer with a swizzled destination), so that is what is left out first. Unproven as the cause: the kit run `texswizzle` decides. `[texlayout]` every 128 uploads. Of the game's texels about 90 % are CMPR and already swizzled (upload logs of two match routes); this reaches RGB5A3, C8 and the CMPR textures with transparent texels, which `MSC_VGX_CMPRA` off decodes to RGBA8. Kit run `texswizzle`. |
| `MSC_VITA_PB_MIB=<4..64>` | 1.3, default `16` (vitaGL's). sceGxm's parameter buffer; the video memory reserve follows it (buffer + 2 MiB; `MSC_VITA_CDRAM_RESERVE_MIB` still wins). | `MSC_VITA_MSAA_GUARD=0` | 1.3, default on. | `MSC_VGX_CMPRA=0\|1` | 1.3, default `0`. CMPR textures with transparent texels, which DXT1 cannot show and which are otherwise decoded to RGBA8 (32 bits per texel), go as DXT5 (8 bits). Alpha is the same texel by texel. The middle colour of a three-colour block is not a DXT5 entry: its texels alternate between the two inner entries, at most a sixth of the block's colour range away, with the right mean. Non-power-of-two textures are still decoded. The texture budget then holds more **`2` (1.3, new): the same, but a texture goes as DXT5 only when no texel of its authored levels moves by more than `MSC_VGX_CMPRA_TOL` (default 12) steps of 255 in a colour channel (`CmprDxt5MaxError`, host test `run_texture_compact_test.sh`); `0` there means only textures whose three-colour blocks never show their middle colour. The others stay RGBA8.** The statistics line says how many were left decoded and the largest error taken. Proposed default: `2` once the console A/B (`cmpra`) shows the crowd and cut-out edges clean; `1` stays what it was. |
| `MSC_VGX_LA88=0\|1\|2\|3` | 1.1 switch, documented in 1.3 / int29. Code default `0`, Vita plan `2` (console-verified 6447: self-test exact, saves 1.4-5 MiB CDRAM). `1`: non-mipmapped GX I4, I8, IA4 and IA8 are uploaded as luminance-alpha (2 bytes a texel, vitaGL's A8L8, sampled as (L,L,L,A)) instead of RGBA8 (4). **`2` (1.3): I4 and I8, whose luminance and alpha are the same number, as one byte (`TF_I8`, vitaGL's `GL_INTENSITY` = U8_RRRR, sampled as (I,I,I,I)); IA4 and IA8 as with `1`.** The same four numbers per texel as the RGBA8 decode, so the same pictures; host test `run_texture_tests.sh` (exhaustive channel oracle, deliberate break). On the harness's match route 80 % of the bytes uploaded as RGBA8 are I8 textures (92 MB of 116 MB over 4,600 frames). Proposed default: `2` once the console A/B (`lai8`) shows fonts, HUD digits and shadows unchanged; not measured on a console (vitaGL's `GL_INTENSITY` upload is read byte by byte through its callbacks, a few ms for a 512 x 256 texture, at load). Statistics: `texture uploads: n as one byte a texel` **`3` (wave 24, tile24, console only: needs the back end that makes its own mip chain): the mipmapped I4, I8, IA4 and IA8 textures too go as level 0 in U8_RRRR / A8L8 and vitaGL generates the chain (sceGxmTransferDownscale per channel, the same engine as the RGBA8 chain; not provable on the host, so `3` stays off until the console A/B `la883` against `la882`). Since wave 24 a texture whose mipmap flag is set but that is sampled without levels (`!wantMips`) also takes the `1`/`2` forms (same texels, one level). A start-up self-test (`[vgx] MSC_VGX_LA88 self-test: ... exact`) decodes synthetic I4/I8/IA4/IA8 both ways and turns the path off on any mismatch. Statistics: `texture census (tile24) <fmt>`: mipmapped uploads and the bytes as RGBA8 / LA88 / one byte, live now.** |

Coordinates, depth, fog, the alpha test's value, uniforms and the output stay float. `2`: also `half4` colour varyings. Other forms below |
| `MSC_VGX_TEX2D=0\|1` | 1.3 / int32, code default `0`, **`1` in the Vita plan** (`vita_plan.cpp` kSpeed, backed by shader pack31+; real-Vita 6474d with LEANVARY+FS_PROJ: Vice `55.7 -> 58.2` eff_fps, `92.4% -> 97.0%` at 60 Hz, `-1.03 ms` GPU). | `MSC_VGX_WARM_HEAP_MIB=<MiB>` | 1.3, wave 10: no default any more (it was `44`); set explicitly it keeps its old meaning (`> 0`: stop at that much C heap in use, `0`: never stop, ignores the floor below). The default rule is `MSC_VGX_WARM_FLOOR_MIB`. The old default stopped the warm-up at picture 0 on every console build from r7 on: the game leaves 48-52 MiB of the 96 MiB heap in use before the first picture. Old text: In the warm-up of the first frames, with the C heap in use at this figure or above, no more shaders are compiled: they compile one per frame when a draw needs them (their first draws are skipped), and the next start goes on from the cache. Why: in Vita3K with the real compiler a cold vertex shader left about 145 KiB in the C heap and a fragment shader about 10, after vitaGL had freed all it owns; a start that compiled the whole list (`MSC_VGX_TEX2D=1`, 157 vertex shaders) reached 58 MiB of 64 and ended in `operator new` at the title screen. A pack hit does not grow the heap, so a start with the pack does not reach it. This does not bound compiles on first use: in Vita3K the same start then got past the menus and ended in `operator new` at the match's walk-out; the second start, with the cache, played the tour through. Log: `MSC_VGX_WARM_HEAP_MIB:` note |
| `MSC_VGX_WARM_FLOOR_MIB=<MiB>` | 1.3 wave 10, default `24`, `0` off | | The warm-up (picture 1) compiles no further shader when less than this much of the C heap is free (heap size `NpPlatformHeapTotalKiB` minus use `NpPlatformHeapUsedKiB`); a host build has no accessors and never stops. Log: `MSC_VGX_WARM_FLOOR_MIB:` note, the `[warm]` line (lowest free heap, stops). Decision: `renderer/src/vgx_warmspread.h` Stop(), test `renderer/tests/run_warm_spread_test.sh` (BREAK=1,2). |
| `MSC_WARM_CAP_MARKED=0\|1` | | `1` | nxr6b / int34. `MSC_WARM_CAP` (400 in code, 600 in the Vita plan) no longer cuts a MARKED program (one a match drew: the list's first `listFront`, `[match]` "programs marked as the matches' own"), raising the cap up to `ProgramLimit() - 64` (836 on Vita, or `cap + 512` when `ProgramLimit() == 0`), only when the cap is above 0. Before int34 the raise was capped at `cap + 128` (528 total), which left 258-294 marked programs on `pack32` / live Vita installs unwarmed at boot and caused first-minute match shader stutter; raising to `ProgramLimit() - 64` warms all marked match programs (~786-822) in ~0.35-0.40 s while leaving >= 64 free slots before `EvictPrograms()`. The `[warm]` line ends with "raised the cap by N". |
| `MSC_SCRIPT_FLUSH_BLOCK=0\|1` | | `0` (harness builds) | nxr6b. The route's `[script]` / `log` lines and `BootMatchWatch` called `ScriptFlush()` = `VitaLog::FlushBlocking()` on core 0: it waits for the sink's io lock behind the writer thread (priority 191, inside `sceIoWrite`) and writes itself, three times at the first kick-off. Now mid-run it is `NpVitaLogFlushAsync()` (stdio into the sink, the writer thread woken, no wait for the card). The exit command, `HarnessExit` and every crash / freeze path (`VitaLog::Flush` / `FlushBlocking`) are unchanged. `1` = the old blocking flush. Harness builds only; no effect on a player's build. Line `[script-flush]` at the exit. Unit test `native/platform/vita/tests/run_log_async_test.sh` (3 mutants). |
| `MSC_AUDIO_PREWARM=0\|1` | | `0` | nxr6b (NEON round 6 section 2, fix 2B). `1`: at `GS_KICKOFF` of the first kick-off the gameplay music handle is created and prepared (`PrepareTrackedSound(18, ...)`: the dvd 2 x 1 KiB and 2 x 27 KiB reads of the whistle frame move into the kick-off wait) and `GS_GAMEPLAY` starts it (`StartTrackedSound`, the call the NIS cue uses). NOT exact, hence OFF: host det trace equal (7f8ee01a5edb), but the cue / source choices are drawn on `nlDefaultSeed` (the render stream the crowd and cameras share) about 13 frames earlier, and 3,314 of 4,445 frame hashes after picture 1132 differ from the base (fs.csv, host glnull). The music also starts at the whistle itself instead of when the reads came back. NEON's "bit-identical pixels" is wrong. Lines `[audio-prewarm]` at both points. |
| `MSC_WARM_CAP=<n>` | | `600` in the Vita plan, `400` in code | Picture-1 boot warm-up base program cap (`vgx_backend_gl.cpp` `WarmPlanBuffer`), unconditionally clamped to `ProgramLimit() - 64` (836 on Vita). Set to `600` in `vita_plan.cpp` `kQuality` so menu / VS character-select programs warm even on a fresh list while `MSC_WARM_CAP_MARKED=1` raises dynamically up to `836` to cover all marked match programs without crowding `ProgramLimit()=900` (`EvictPrograms()`). |
| `MSC_WARM_MATCH=0\|1` | | `1` in the Vita plan, `0` in code | Wave 10. Picture 1 makes only the first `MSC_WARM_CAP` (400; 600 in the Vita plan, raised by `MSC_WARM_CAP_MARKED=1` up to `ProgramLimit() - 64`) programs of the warm-up list (the marked, match-own ones first, `MSC_VGX_WARM_ORDER`) with the shaders they use and the variants of those programs; the rest of the list is left to first use (never the whole list: the pack holds ~3,000 shaders, vitaGL has 1,024 program slots, the patcher pools are small, a link is one call). Behind the boot loading screen, blocking. `0`: the whole list in picture 1 (held only by the heap rule). Log: `[warm] match warm-up` line (programs asked / linked, left to first use, variants, ms, lowest free heap); the `[match] start` line says how many programs the warm-up really linked (the old "linked first by this start" was the list order, not links). |

Render only: no simulation state, the draw lists are unchanged. Log: `[popin] match #n` at the match's end (window pictures, draws made whole, links made now with ms, programs still missing). Logic: `renderer/src/vgx_popin.h`, test `renderer/tests/popin.cpp`. |
| `MSC_WARM_BLOCK=0\|1` | | `1` in the Vita plan, `0` in code | Wave 10. A draw that is not a plain main-scene colour draw (render-target pass, source of an EFB copy, depth-only: `async::Skippable`) is never left out for a shader or program that is not ready: its compile and link are made now, without the one-compile-a-frame rule. A skipped draw that feeds a one-time copy leaves the copy wrong for good (Dolphin: Mii heads missing; the goalie texture in a replay close-up). Counters: draws left out for a program not ready, main-scene and feeding a copy / off-screen target, and dead programs: `[match] end`, `[hitch] window`, `sk a/b` in a `[hitch]` line. |
| `[hitch] ... pt v/f/rt pf` | wave 10, console only | | Cost of the patcher's vertex and fragment program creates and of `sceGxmCreateRenderTarget` in the picture (calls/ms), and the cumulative patch failures (`vgl_shader_patch_failures`); per match in `[match] #n patcher`. vitaGL patch 0035 (`vglPatchTimingTake`); the host has none. |
| `MSC_VGX_TEX16=0\|1\|2` | 1.3, default `0`. A decoded texture whose texels all fit goes as RGB565, RGBA5551 or RGBA4444, decided from the texels (RGB565 sources always; RGB5A3, palettes and I4 when they fit): half the memory and half the fetch traffic. `1` textures without mipmaps, `2` also mipmapped ones (565 and 4444 only; their generated levels round to the same bits). The GPU expands 5 bits to c/31 rather than to the 8-bit replica: an output can differ by one level at most. The `texture uploads by GX format` lines of the statistics report what is held per format **1.3, new: `MSC_VGX_TEX16_MIN=<texels>` (default 16384, 128 x 128): smaller textures (HUD pieces, icons, glyph pages, most faces) stay 32 bits whatever their texels say; they hold few bytes. `2` is not exact: a mipmapped texture's smaller levels are made by the GPU in the 16-bit format and round to 5 bits (up to 8 steps of 255), so the proposed default is `1` (textures without mip levels: the game's textures are almost all of those), where the only difference is the c / 31 expansion against the replicated 8-bit value (under half a step).** On the match route of the harness (`MSC_VGX_STATS`) almost no bytes are of a kind `1` reaches: RGB5A3 0.9 MB, C8 2 MB, of 25.7 MB live; `LA88=2` and `CMPRA` are where the bytes are. |
| `MSC_HDTEX_FACES=1` | 1.3. Default `0`: the blink's eye frames (`<c>_eye0`, `1`, `2`) stay the game's own whatever the pack holds (filtered at load by the pack's names, so a built pack works). The closed frames show for one or two pictures, so they arrive late and over budget and an HD open eye flicks to an original closed one; the Galaxy port kept its eye textures for a like reason. `1`: replace them as 1.1 and 1.2 did |
| `MSC_VGX_FOGVTX=0\|1\|2` | 1.3, default `0`. Fog from the eye-space depth (a varying) instead of a division by the pixel's depth. `1`: only where it equals the old form to float rounding; `2`: also where it is within 2^-11 (an eighth of an 8-bit step) of it. Changes the text of fogged shaders (new keys), so the pack misses. int32 bound `v_fz` to hardware `FOGC` (`VGL_TYPE_FOG`) without bumping `kHostShaderAbi`, so dev units that previously tested `MSC_VGX_FOGVTX=1` must clear cached `v_fz` entries (bump `kHostShaderAbi` if ever promoted to a shipped default). Form below |
| `MSC_VGX_FOGNOOP=0\|1` (`2` test only) | nxgpu25, default `0` (code and plan). `1`: a fog the registers make a no-op is not in the fragment key: BP 0xEE (A) is +-0, C (BP 0xF1) is >= 0 and the type is 2, 4 or 5 (types 6 and 7 map 0 to 1/256 and keep their fog), so the factor is exactly 0 and `mix(c, fogColour, 0) = c` bit for bit (`renderer/src/vgx_fognoop.h`; host test `renderer/tests/run_fognoop_test.sh`: 2.1 million cases against the generated fog code, mutants 1, 2, 3, 5 caught). BP 0xEE is not a key register, so the bit rides in `RsExt`, `ResolveStateLegacy`'s `keysDirty` and `KeyInputs` like `ZKeyBit`. A key without the fog is a plain fog-free key, but the shader pack and the warm-up list of the package hold the fogged text: with the switch on every inert-fog draw would meet a shader the pack may not have. MEASURED on the host (Vita plan, `MSC_VGX_FOGNOOP_LOG=1`): The Dump, Lava Pit, Pipeline Central and Sand Tomb fog most draws with real parameters (A 4e-5 to 4.3e-4, B' 1.000065, the factor at the far plane 2.5 to 25.7, so the fog saturates), Vice, the crep, replay and Mega Strike routes no fog at all: no draw anywhere has A = 0, so the switch changes nothing and stays `0`. `2` makes every fog count as inert: the proof's deliberate break (53 of 86 Dump frames differ). |
| `MSC_VGX_FOGNOOP_LOG=1` | nxgpu25, diagnostics. Every 600 frames: the draws, those with a fog type, those whose fog the registers make inert, the distinct fragment shaders of each, and each distinct fog set-up (A, C, B', the factor at the far plane, draws). No effect on drawing. |
| `MSC_VGX_RTWHY=1` | nxgpu25, diagnostics (needs `MSC_VGX_RT=1`; on the host also `MSC_VGX_RT_PRESENTS=1`). Every 2000 EFB scenes: how many store their depth and what the first reader after the scene is (a depth copy, an effects run, or a depth-tested draw with its function and write flag). No effect on drawing. |
| `MSC_VGX_PALMERGE=0\|1\|2` | 1.3, default `2` (auto). Palette runs: adjacent static skinned draws that differ only in their matrix palette (players and their passes) are one draw, from a combined buffer made once per run and found again in later frames; same pixels (proof below). `1`: on, a record's 60-row vertex shader is made when first needed (not in the 1.3 pack: a compile in play). `2`: on only with 60-row keys already in the shader warm-up list (the pack's list plus what the console compiled and kept), never a compile in play; while the list has none it is off and keeps nothing, so **with the 1.3 pack the default does nothing until the pack is regenerated with those keys** (`native/tools/seed/shl.py pal60`, or keys recorded with `=1`). `MSC_VGX_PALMERGE_ROWS=30\|60` (default `60`): the rows a record may have (`30` needs no new shader but merges almost nothing: the members use rows up to 27). `MSC_VGX_PALMERGE_MB` (default `3`; at `6` the stadium route reached 87-89 MiB of the 96 MiB C heap against 80.5 MiB without merging, at `3` 78 MiB for about 63 % of the saving: pack14 proof, 6 Nov): the CPU copies of decoded vertices kept for gathering (a list over it keeps none); the combined buffers may take half as much. `MSC_VGX_PALMERGE_STATS=1`: a `[palmerge]` line every 600 frames; `MSC_VGX_PALMERGE_CHECK=1` (diagnostic) compares every found buffer and palette with a fresh gather. Form below | Wave 8: the kept-vertex cap is `MSC_VGX_PALMERGE_MB` / `MSC_VGX_SKINMERGE_MB` (code default 3 MiB each; host tests used 8 / 4; peak kept vertices 7.2 MB in a replay window, 4.3 MB in open play), diagnostics `MSC_VGX_PALMERGE_KEEPONLY` and `MSC_VGX_PALMERGE_CLSMASK` / `MSC_VGX_SKINMERGE_CLSMASK`. The 60-row twins need pack 17 (key list `w8/pack17/pack17-merged-keys.shl`).
| `MSC_VGX_SKINMERGE=0\|1` | 1.3 (wave 7), **code default `0`, not in the Vita plan until the crowd-band pixels are explained** (console A/B run `pmwave8`). Runs of GPU-skinned static draws (replays, cut-scenes, celebrations: `MSC_SKIN_GPU`) that differ only in the bone block are one record: the run's vertices are the members' in draw order with each member's four bone-row bytes moved up by 3 x (the bones the members before it hold), its bone block the members' blocks one after the other (at most 32 bones, the palette the shader declares), its indices the members' in order. The vertex shader is the members' own 32-bone form, so **no new shader key: pack 16 serves it** (`MSC_VGX_SKINPAL=1` turns it off). Machinery shared with `MSC_VGX_PALMERGE` (`vgx_draw.cpp` "palette runs", `PmCandidate` skin branch); the list cache keeps the decoded vertices of skinned lists (`MSC_VGX_SKINMERGE_MB`, code default 3, **Vita plan 4**, counted apart from the palette ones; the combined buffers may take half of both caps). Host: pictures identical (4,652 runs compared by the CHECK mode, 0 differ). Draw records (census dump): replay 85 -> 57 player records (379 -> 351), celebration 42 -> 30 (370 -> 358), pre-match cut-scene 16 -> 12. Not measured on a console (front-end gather cost, C heap, GPU memory for the combined buffers). `MSC_VGX_PALMERGE_STATS=1` also prints `skin runs` and why a skinned draw was not a candidate. Wave 8 found why wave 7 saw 3 to 57 scattered crowd pixels move with both merges on and a big cap: see `MSC_VGX_CROWDTRIM_LRU` |
| `MSC_VGX_PALREGROUP=0\|1\|2` | 1.3 (wave 8), **code default `0`, not in the Vita plan until the crowd-band pixels are explained** (console A/B run `pmwave8`). Palette and skin runs of the same material **anywhere in a stretch** of player draws, not only next to each other. A stretch is the consecutive draw records that are all run candidates, in one class / layer / fx and all drawn opaque with depth: blend off, depth test on, depth write on, colour written, **alpha not written** (`PmOrderFree`: a record that writes alpha keeps its place, the destination alpha would be the last fragment's, not the nearest). For such records the draw order cannot change a pixel except where two fragments have exactly the same depth (the later one wins with LEQUAL): every pixel ends with the nearest fragment of the stretch whatever the order, and a fragment that fails the alpha test writes nothing. When the stretch ends its records are grouped by material (shaders, layout, textures, render state, every uniform block but the palette compared **by content**: the blocks are shared only when consecutive, so equal ones further apart have other offsets) in the order of each group's first record, and every group becomes runs of at most 60 palette rows (32 bones) made as before; one record per run replaces the stretch's records (`PmWinFlush`: the tail of the command list is rebuilt, nothing is inserted in front of earlier commands). `=1` only for the main picture (viewport at least 256 x 192 EFB pixels): `=2` also for the small pictures of the passes (the crowd figures' 64 x 64 cells, the shadows), where the host moved 1 to 2 isolated pixels of the crowd bands by up to 50 levels in 5 of 25 frames (a figure's parts at equal depth). `MSC_VGX_PALMERGE_STATS=1` adds `[pmwin]` lines (records in / out, why a record did not join, different materials, the runs a 30 / 60 / 90 / 120 row palette would make). Needs `MSC_VGX_PALMERGE` (the 60-row twins, pack 17) and / or `MSC_VGX_SKINMERGE`. What it cannot join: records whose konst colours differ (the TEV block: 24 of 87 candidate records in the open-play stretch census) and those past 60 rows |
| `MSC_VGX_PALCOMPACT=0\|1` | 1.3 (wave 8), **code default `0`, not in the Vita plan until the crowd-band pixels are explained** (console A/B run `pmwave8`). A palette run member takes only the matrices its vertices name (3 rows each) instead of every row up to the highest it names: a part that names matrices 0, 6 and 9 of the ten a packet may load holds 9 rows of a run's palette, not 30. The set is found once per list when it is decoded (`Seg::posMtxMask`, a row that is no multiple of 3 or not below 30 leaves it unknown: the old span), a run's member has its vertices' matrix numbers renumbered in order after the members before it (`PmGather`) and its palette block copied matrix by matrix (`PmClose`); the same rows are read by the same arithmetic, the shader is the same. Open-play stretch: 18.5 draws saved by adjacent runs, 20.2 with the compaction. `MSC_VGX_PALMERGE_CHECK=1` compares every run's palette rows against the members' |
| `MSC_VGX_CROWDTRIM_LRU=0\|1` | 1.3 (wave 8), code default `1` (a fix of `MSC_VGX_CROWDTRIM`, no plan entry, A/B `MSC_VGX_CROWDTRIM_LRU=0`). The crowd trim measures the box of every figure picture from CPU copies of the static vertex buffers it keeps in a 2 MiB LRU (`MSC_VGX_CROWDTRIM_COPYKB`), and cuts a crowd quad only where its figure was measured; an unmeasured figure's quads are drawn whole, and a cut and a whole quad differ by 1 to 3 levels at a few pixels (the old unexplained cut-vs-whole difference of `MSC_VGX_CROWDTRIM`). The figures' parts are palette / skin run members, so the **combined buffers are new uploads that the figures draw**: a new copy was entered as "never used", so the next upload pushed it out before its first pass measured it, and evicting a copy also threw away the boxes already measured from it; only a copy budget of 8 MiB or more kept the pictures equal to the unmerged ones. Now: a new copy counts as used (plain LRU), evicting a copy keeps its boxes (they go with the buffer), a draw whose boxes are cached needs no copy, a combined buffer may be copied up to 1 MiB (a list's limit stays 128 KiB), the box cache holds 512 entries (256). This was the cause of the 3 to 57 scattered crowd-band pixels wave 7 saw with `PALMERGE` and `SKINMERGE` both on and 24 MB caps (not memory addresses, not a sprite sort tie: `MSC_VGX_PALMERGE_KEEPONLY=1`, lists kept but no run made, was identical; `MSC_VGX_CROWDTRIM=0` identical; `COPYKB=8192` identical). The base picture itself is independent of the budget in the censused frames |
| `MSC_VGX_SBATCH=0\|1` | 1.3 (wave 6), default `0`. Static batches: adjacent rigid background draws (stadium, crowd, sky, far classes) of one material (shaders, layout, textures, render state, class / layer) that differ only in geometry, model-view and the texture matrices of position / normal texture generations are drawn as one record from a batch buffer: each object's vertices stored once, moved into a common frame (R = MV_A^-1 MV_i, camera-independent for rigid objects), one record per stretch of objects consecutive in the buffer. Every member is checked every frame against its own matrices (`MSC_VGX_SBATCH_TOL`, relative 1e-5 at its bounds: about 0.01 pixel); one that moved is drawn alone that frame. No shader change (pack unaffected). Host match60 (Vita plan, native size): wide-play windows 297-323 -> 275-294 records a frame (stadium 88-100 -> 68-83); pictures against knobs: 8 of 25 frames differ by 1-5 pixels, 1 level, plus single edge pixels in 3 frames (triangle-edge coverage); largest member eye-space error 9e-4 units. `MSC_VGX_SBATCH_MB` (default 6): decoded vertices kept in the C heap for building (rigid lists are kept at promotion in any class; when full, copies of lists never seen as a candidate go; a candidate whose copy went has its list decoded again once, 8 a frame); `MSC_VGX_SBATCH_BUFMB` (default 8): batch buffers. `MSC_VGX_SBATCH_STATS=1`: an `[sbatch]` line every 600 frames; `MSC_VGX_SBATCH_CHECK=1` the largest eye-space error of batched members. `MSC_VGX_SBATCH_ORDER=1` (default `0`): an opaque candidate is first moved back to the last earlier record of its material in the same stretch of draws when it provably commutes with every record it passes (disjoint pixel rectangles, or both opaque depth writers whose window depth ranges are 2^-15 apart: no depth tie); same pixels by construction. Form below |
| `MSC_VGX_FS_TRIM=0\|1` | 1.3, default `0` in the code, `1` in the Vita plan (`vita_plan.cpp` kSpeed). Fragment text without the TEV stage clamps the ranges prove idle, and ordered alpha tests without `floor`; the final output clamp (colour and alpha) is always kept (r86 follow-up: the Galaxy port's console showed static on shadows and decals where it was dropped). Same pixels; every fragment text changes, so the pack misses (pack15 holds the forms). Form below |
| `MSC_VGX_TEV_CANON=0\|1\|2` | 1.3, default `0` in the code, `2` in the Vita plan (`vita_plan.cpp` kSpeed, 12 Nov, with shader pack 16; `=0` is served by the pack's plain forms, no compile). Fragment text from the ranges the key proves (implies the FS_TRIM analysis): operands whose range is one number are that number and fold through later stages, `mix` of a zero or equal operand is a product or the operand, no add of zero, no scale of one, a stage part nobody reads is not computed (compare stages keep their text). `2` adds one comparison of `fin.a` against a threshold built from the references for alpha tests that are lower bounds (GREATER / GEQUAL, or ALWAYS beside one): same decision except for an alpha within float rounding of half a step; no colour moves. Measured with the console's compiler on the 291 distinct fragment texts of the 1.3 catalogue: mode 1 text is 15-35 % shorter but the instruction count is unchanged (7,545 -> 7,569: the compiler folds the same algebra), mode 2 saves 2.7 instructions on each of the 106 changed shaders (D352 20 -> 15, 54A7 30 -> 25) and 1.2 % of the census-weighted instructions (the hot unlit-texture shaders are at 7 and 11 instructions in every mode). Changes shader text: pack 15 misses (267 of the 271 live pack-15 texts change); **shader pack 16 holds both forms** (`/home/claude/work/final/pack16`, not in git: see docs/HANDOFF-1.3-WAVE7.md). `renderer/tests/run_tev_canon_test.sh` (BREAK=1, BREAK=2 must fail) |
| `MSC_VGX_HSR_PROVEN=0\|1` | 1.3 / int29, code default `0`, Vita plan `1` (console-verified 6447). A draw blended by its source alpha (add; SRC_ALPHA or ONE against INV_SRC_ALPHA or ZERO) whose final alpha is proven 1 from the numbers in its own u_tev block (`AlphaRangeDyn`: interval arithmetic, konst and registers as held, texels and vertex colours 0..1) is drawn without the blend, so the tile GPU keeps hidden-surface removal for it. Exact. No text change, only the draw's blend function; extends `BlendCut` family (`BINALPHA`, `BLENDNODISCARD`, `DISCARD_BLEND`) at the draw. Key-level proofs (constant alpha, alpha range against the references) hit 0 of 553 catalogue variants, so only the draw-level one is in |
| `MSC_VGX_FINCLAMP=0\|1\|2\|3` | 1.3, default `0` in the code, `3` in the Vita plan (`vita_plan.cpp` kSpeed, 12 Nov, with shader pack 16). `3` keeps only the clamp side the value ranges leave open (colour and alpha judged apart), on every draw, blended or not; the proof is `renderer/tests/run_tev_canon_test.sh`, the console A/B is `finclamp` (look at shadows, decals, effects, because the Galaxy console once drew blended shadows as static with the clamp gone). The output clamp `fin = clamp(...)` that FS_TRIM and TEV_CANON keep is dropped, colour and alpha apart, where the ranges prove the value inside 0..1 (needs FS_TRIM or TEV_CANON on). `1`: only for a draw that is not blended (key bit `FSK_NOFC`, set in `BuildFsKey` from BP 0x41, which is in the key memos: an unblended write is saturated by the output conversion); `2`: every draw (`FSP_NOFC`). The Galaxy port's console drew blended shadows and decals as static with the clamp gone (r86): **2 is a console A/B (look at shadows, decals, effects), 1 is the safer half**. With the console compiler the clamp is 2 instructions: 7 -> 5 (the hottest text, tex x vertex colour), 11 -> 9, 20 -> 16. In the 11/5 tour census 92 % of the shaded fragments are in blended draws, so `1` takes 1.9 % off the weighted instructions and `2` 26 %. Changes text: pack 15 misses (see w6/canon2-pack.md). Console compiler, census-weighted (11/5 tour): `2` -26.1 % instructions, `1` -1.9 %, with TEV_CANON=2 `2` -27.3 %. Test: `run_tev_canon_test.sh` MODE=fc compares the raw (unsaturated) output of the new text with the clamped old one: 0 differing over all 291 catalogue texts; no deliberate break is possible on those keys, every one ends in stage clamps |
| `MSC_VGX_LEANVARY=0\|1` | 1.3 / int32, code default `0`, **`1` in the Vita plan** (`vita_plan.cpp` kSpeed, backed by shader pack31+; real-Vita 6474d with TEX2D+FS_PROJ: `-1.03 ms` GPU, zero picture change). The vertex shader writes `v_col0` only if the fragment shader of the draw declares it (no stage reads vertex colour 0): `VsKey::tg[7].pad[0]` bit 2, set from the fragment key built just before (`g_fsNoCol0`), as VS_TRIM does for colour 1. Same picture. Texture varyings are already lean (`TEX2D`, `numTexGens` counts read coordinates only), indirect coordinates per vertex (Galaxy `IND_VS`) do not apply: 3 of 366 catalogue keys have an indirect stage, none in the census. |
| `MSC_VGX_HSR_FACTS=0\|1`, `MSC_VGX_HSR_COUNT=1` | 1.3, default `0`. `FACTS` adds to HSR_PROVEN's interval proof: a texture whose texels are all opaque (checked at upload: RGBA8 / DXT1 / LA88 / I8 contents, RGB565) has alpha 1, and a vertex colour made from the material and ambient registers (no per-vertex source, no lights) is the number the light block holds; swapped lanes keep the unit range. Exact, draw level, no text change. `COUNT`: `[hsrcount]` line every 20000 draws: blended draws, those the key's numbers prove (alpha 1) and those the facts prove, alpha tests that cannot fail (a discard the text does not need: not removed, it would be a key change), triangles |
| `MSC_VGX_VS_TRIM=0\|1\|2` | 1.3, default `0` in the code, `1` in the Vita plan (`vita_plan.cpp` kSpeed). `1`: vertex text without light attenuation terms that are exactly 1, without a lower clamp that cannot act, without colour 1 where the fragment shader does not read it. Same results. `2` adds far lights with one direction for all vertices: NOT exact, up to about one 8-bit level. Form below |
| `MSC_VGX_VS_NOBRANCH=0\|1` | Wave 13 (G5), default `0` in the code, `1` in the Vita plan (`vita_plan.cpp` kSpeed). Since 22 Nov the per-light part (light direction guard, spot gate) is a separate switch `MSC_VGX_VS_NOBRANCH_LIGHT` (default `0`, needs this one): with it a program of 5 light blocks compiled to 85 temporary registers (Sony's compiler; the branch forms: at most 52 over the pack list) and Vita3K cannot translate more than 80 (`array index must be < 20`, shader 4efa3e4a: the Dump gate failed at boot); the texts below that are per-light are therefore only with `_LIGHT=1`. Vertex text without data-dependent `if`: `if (nl > 0.0) n = n / nl;` (also the light direction's [LIGHT only] and the texgen's) is `n / (nl + step(nl, 0.0))` (exactly nl for nl > 0, exactly 1 for a zero length), the palette and texgen-row clamps `if (pi > 61) pi = 61;` are `min(floor(w + 0.5), 61.0)` before the int cast, `pi > 29 ? 93 : 64 + pi` is `64 + min(pi, 29)`, the spot gate `dot(n, ld) >= 0.0 ? x : 0.0` is `step(0.0, dot(n, ld)) * x` (only with PALIDX=1, the default). Same values for every finite input (NaN inputs, and an index past 2^31, which the cast does not define, are the exceptions). Keys unchanged, text changed: with it on, 124 of the 137 vertex programs of the shipped pack list are new texts (pack miss until re-recorded). Proof `renderer/tests/run_shader_text_test.sh` (Mesa, bit patterns of gl_Position and all varyings over random, zero, 1e-30, 1e25 vectors and zero / 1e-25 / 1e20 matrices; `BREAK=1..3` are wrong texts that must fail). Kill switch `=0`. |
| `MSC_VGX_FS_PROJ=0\|1\|2` | Wave 13 (G7) / int32, code default `0`, **`1` in the Vita plan** (`vita_plan.cpp` kSpeed, backed by shader pack31+; real-Vita 6474d with TEX2D+LEANVARY: Vice `55.7 -> 58.2` eff_fps, `-1.03 ms` GPU). A projective texture coordinate that only plain (not indirect) stages read is read by `texture2DProj(sampler, vec3)` instead of `v_tex.xy / (z == 0.0 ? 1.0 : z)` into a vec2. `1` keeps the z == 0 rule (`z + step(abs(z), 0.0)` as the third component), `2` passes the varying as it is (z == 0 reads at infinity: NOT the same, the proof's mutant). Not bit-identical (the divide moves into the texture unit: on Mesa 1 level in a few of 100 000 channels); real hardware gain confirmed in `6474d`. Proof / numbers: `run_shader_text_test.sh MODE=fs`. |
| `MSC_VGX_FSCENSUS=n`, `MSC_VGX_FSCENSUS_WINDOW=f` | Diagnostics, default off. Every n-th frame each draw is rasterized on the CPU through a model of the tile GPU's hidden-surface stage; every `f` frames (1800) cumulative `[fscensus]` lines per shader text. `native/tools/gxpcount/rank.py` multiplies them by the instructions of the compiled GXP |
| `MSC_HDTEX=0` | Ignore the HD texture pack (`ux0:data/mscharged/hdtex.pack`, when it is there); `MSC_HDTEX_BUDGET=MiB` caps its graphics memory (default 8) |
| `MSC_VGX_KEEP_TARGETS=0`, `MSC_VGX_KEEP_SIZES=0` | Do not keep the render targets and pass objects of recently used picture sizes |
| `MSC_SKIN_GPU_MORPH=0` | Skin faces with active expressions on the CPU as before |
| `MSC_SKIN_LANE=1` | Off by default: the CPU skinning that is left (cut-scenes and menus with `MSC_SKIN_GPU=0`) runs on a helper thread beside the game thread. With the default GPU skinning almost nothing is CPU-skinned any more, so it has nothing to do. Waits are bounded: a lane thread that does not run for 12 ms is replaced by the game thread, a full queue is skinned inline. `MSC_VITA_PRIO_LANE=<prio>` sets the lane thread's priority; `MSC_SKIN_PROF=1` (host) prints the game thread's CPU time per frame and the skinning share of it |
| `MSC_RULE_NOMEGA=1` | Rule option, off by default: no Mega Strikes (a captain who charges a shot to the end takes an ordinary shot; the Mega Strike meter never starts). Friendly matches, cups and LAN matches; the Strikers 101 tutorial and the challenges keep their Mega Strikes, and ranked online ignores it. It changes the match, so it is part of the LAN rule fingerprint: both Vitas must use the same setting or they do not find each other |
| `MSC_DOF=0` | Depth of field off in cut-scenes (host default `1`, as the game; the Vita build sets `lazy`): the cut-scene trigger is ignored and the half-size capture of the frame that feeds the effect is not made either. `lazy`: the same picture as the game, with the capture made only on frames that use it. `1` is the game's own capture every frame |
| `MSC_LANGUAGE=es` or `fr` | The language of the menus and texts (default English). The USA disc carries the North American Spanish and French text tables and fonts; spoken audio is the same in every language. 1.3: the port's own texts follow it as well (see "Version 1.3: languages, versions, one VPK") |

### 1.2 Defaults: The Best Experience Is On

For 1.2, what helps performance and graphics is on by default, for a player who never opens the Settings screen; if testing finds a
major problem, the default is changed back afterwards. The Vita build (`native/platform/vita/src/vita_plan.cpp`, "quality and timing switches") sets the
keys below when neither `env.txt` nor `np-player.txt` names them, and logs `[plan] quality and timing switches on by default (...): KEY=VALUE ...` at
start. Naming a key in the file wins, whatever the value; the host harness does not run `vita_plan.cpp`, so its own defaults (and the picture comparisons
made with them) are unchanged. The console A/B in `tools/console-1.2` turns each item off.

| Item | Key and default | To turn it off | Notes and risk |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Foreground first | `MSC_DYNRES_FG=1` | `MSC_DYNRES_FG=0` (no Settings row since the 1.3 prune) | The crowd-refresh step comes before the whole scene shrinks; acts only when the GPU is short. Needs the dynamic resolution |
| Shadow-volume quad cut | `MSC_VGX_SHQUAD=1` | `0` | Exact (pictures equal on the host in the shared gate's `exactopts` run). Keeps up to 6 MiB of CPU copies of small static buffers (`MSC_VGX_SHQUAD_COPYKB`) and a little ledger work per frame |
| Shadow quad cut in open play | `MSC_VGX_SHQUAD_LIVE=0\|1\|2`, `MSC_VGX_LEDGERPAL=1` | `0` (Vita plan: `SHQUAD_LIVE=2` heavy stadiums only, `LEDGERPAL=1`) | With `MSC_SHADOW_LIVE=flat` the flat shadows are alpha-plane ellipses drawn through the position-matrix palette and resolved by a full-screen blended quad; the scan that cuts the quad was skipped in live play and could not measure palette draws. `1` scans all stadiums, `2` (int26b default) scans heavy stadiums only (The Dump / Lava Pit / Sand Tomb where GPU is tight) and skips on non-heavy stadiums like Vice (saving `1.39 ms` CPU / `4.03 ms` wall on Core 1, measured in 6247 `[beph]` and 6407 sampler). Exact like `MSC_VGX_SHQUAD`: host pictures equal; mutant `MSC_VGX_BREAK=64` must differ. Counter: `[fill] MSC_VGX_LEDGERPAL: n palette draws measured`. About 86% of the quad's pixels cut at The Dump (host). |
| Depth of field | `MSC_DOF=lazy` | `MSC_DOF=1` (the game's own capture; no Settings choice since the 1.3 prune). `MSC_DOF=0` (Settings: Depth Of Field OFF) removes the blur | Same picture as the game, without the capture no quad reads |
| GPU queue depth | `MSC_VITA_QUEUE_FIXED30=1` | `0` | Depth 2 is held while the frame rate is held at 30 (Galaxy's fixed-depth fix; mode `1`). Costs up to a frame of input delay in a scene whose GPU time exceeds the budget; acts only under a 30 cap. Not tried on a console |
| Frame-clock priority | `MSC_VITA_PRIO_VBLANK=0x70` | `0` or `off` (the timer role's priority) | The `vi-retrace` thread runs ahead of the mixer and the audio output (abs 112). A performance risk only |
| Game thread alone on core 0 | `MSC_VITA_CORE0_ALONE=1` | `0` or `off` | Library threads that may run on core 0 and elsewhere are moved to cores 1-2 at presents 5, 30, 120 and every 600th; threads pinned to core 0 alone stay. A thread the system refuses to move is logged `refused`. Not tried on a console |
| Pacing by vblank count | `MSC_PACE_VBLANK=1` | `0` or `off` | Animation pacing uses the display's own count of the vblanks a picture was held when it agrees with the frame clock to within half an original frame; the clock decides otherwise |
| Distant textures | `MSC_VITA_ANISO=2` (`3` before 11 Nov; both read as the first choice of the Settings row) | `0` | Anisotropic minification of trilinear textures. **Risk beyond speed**: the two filter values are believed, not proven, to be the anisotropic modes; textures that look wrong or a frame-time cost are the thing to look for. no Settings row since the 1.3 prune |
| Bounded slip | `MSC_SLIP=20` | `0` | A late frame advances the game by at most 20 % less than it took (a steady 83 % at worst), instead of asking the next frame for more simulation steps. Single player only: `NpLanActive()` (a LAN, Wi-Fi or online session) turns it off by itself. The simulation steps are the same; only how many are made per second of wall clock changes. No Settings row |
| Rigid merge limit | `MSC_VGX_SOFTXFORM=256` | `128` (the 1.1 value; **`0` is no CPU transform at all**) | Meshes up to 256 vertices are transformed on the CPU and merged: 4.4 % fewer draw calls on the host, with rounding-class pixel changes. Lists with draws of 129 to 256 vertices are no longer promoted to static buffers on first sight (see `MSC_VGX_PROMOSMALL`), so the CPU and the stream carry a little more of them. No Settings row |
| Anti-aliasing | `MSC_VGX_MSAA=4` (4x, then 2x) | `0` (Settings: Anti-aliasing OFF); `2` for 2x only | A ladder rung, not a standing cost: the scene target is multisampled only at the rungs that replace the 1.06 / 1.12 / 1.2 sizes, which the ladder reaches after a full second of GPU room and leaves after two frames over 92 % of the budget, and only with 32 MiB of video memory free. Never in Mega Strikes, replays held at 30 or cut-scenes the policy holds at 30. The cost model (1.3 / 1.9) is a guess; the effects pass that reads the scene depth is not used while the target is multisampled, and SMOOTH leaves its edge smoothing out. **Not set** when the picture size is fixed (`MSC_VITA_RENDER`: the samples would be on all the time), with `MSC_DYNRES` off, or with the old `MSC_VITA_MSAA`; the log says so. Needs the vitaGL per-framebuffer patch (0011), which the release libraries have |

Kept off on purpose: `MSC_VGX_GXMCACHE` (the sceGxm state cache; emulator aborts are under investigation), `MSC_VGX_FBO_SAMPLES`, `MSC_REDUCED_MOTION` (a comfort
choice), `MSC_LAN_INPUT_DELAY` (changes how multiplayer feels), and the crowd, clear-cut and skin-lane options that are still being built.

Settings rows follow the defaults: Anti-aliasing and Depth Of Field have the on state as their first choice (it writes
nothing), and an OFF choice that writes the key with the value above. A file that names the build's own value (`MSC_DYNRES_FG=1`, `MSC_VGX_MSAA=4`,
`MSC_DOF=lazy`, `MSC_VITA_ANISO=3`) reads as the first choice and the next save drops the line. A reread of the settings file takes the defaults back and decides them again
(`VitaPlan::ForgetDefaults`), so a fixed picture size chosen in the Settings screen also removes the anti-aliasing default in the same session.

The release libraries are built with the `gsco` recipe (`dependencies/Makefile.vitadeps` `VGL_EXTRA_OPTS`): `-DINDICES_SPEEDHACK` and `-DSAMPLERS_SPEEDHACK`
(made safe by patch 0014) and the compiled-in, run-time-off state cache. `tools/build-deps.py` uses that recipe unless `VGL_EXTRA_OPTS` is set (an empty value builds the plain
`stk` library).

### Keys the Vita plan sets that have no other row (12 Nov, kept in step by `tools/check-plan-docs.py`)

`python3 tools/check-plan-docs.py` reads every key `vita_plan.cpp` gives a value to (the `kSpeed`, `kQuality`, `kLock60`, `kPadOnly`, `kLab` and `kPlayer` tables, the plain `setenv`
calls and the overlap switches of `np_overlap.cpp`) and fails when a key has no row, when no row states the plan's value, or when a row of its own still calls it off. It also lists the
code fallback (what the host harness and any build without the plan read): the plan is the player's default, the code fallback is what a harness picture comparison runs with, and the two
differ on purpose for almost every key here. These keys had no row of their own or no statement of the plan's value:

| Key | Value in the plan | Applies | What it does | Off |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `MSC_FPS_BETWEEN_ENTER` | `20` | every Vita build | Free-run gate: the busiest stage under 20 ms (1.2 refreshes) for a second lets AUTO run free between 30 and 60 (code `26.7`) | `26.7` |
| `MSC_FPS_BETWEEN_LEAVE` | `25` | every Vita build | Free-run gate: over 25 ms for `MSC_FPS_BETWEEN_OUT` frames ends the free run (code `30`) | `30` |
| `MSC_FPS_BETWEEN_COOL` | `45` | every Vita build | Free-run gate: seconds before the next try after a way out (code `6`); doubled for a try that failed | `6` |
| `MSC_FPS_BETWEEN_COOLMAX` | `240` | every Vita build | Free-run gate: the longest cool-down, seconds (code `96`) | `96` |
| `MSC_FPS_BETWEEN_FAIL` | `90` | every Vita build | Free-run gate: a try that ended within 90 s was a failed one (code `30`) | `30` |
| `MSC_FPS_BETWEEN_OUT` | `60` | every Vita build | Free-run gate: frames over the leave limit that end a free run (code `20`) | `20` |
| `MSC_DYNRES_PACED_UP_MS` | `24` | every Vita build | The dynamic-resolution ladder judges a free-running pace by 24 ms, not the 30 budget of 33.4 ms (steps up under 80 %, down over 90 % of it): the headroom of the 30 budget is not spent on 2x anti-aliasing and a larger picture until a free run gives 38-45 pictures a second. Not compiled for the Vita yet | `0` |
| `MSC_LAT_DEPTH_PACED` | `2` | every Vita build | An aligned free run keeps 4 frames in flight, not 3 (59.2 pictures a second against 52.3 at a GPU of 1.0 refresh, about 4 ms more button delay; model only) | `1` |
| `MSC_TAP_CARRY` | `1` | every Vita build | A frame that ran no simulation step (one in six at 60 pictures against the 50 Hz simulation, and in a free run) reads the pad only at its end (the menu pad set), which took the latched samples (a tap down and up since the last read) away from the step that would have seen them: a press shorter than a refresh was lost to play. The tap is now carried to the next read once (`np_controls.h` `TapCarry`, mutants in `test_feel.cpp`). Single player only (no carry in a LAN or online session). Held 30 and LOCKED 30 never have a frame without a step, and read the pad once per step from the cached sample of the retrace (4 samples latched, 250 ms gap rule): no lost tap found there. Console A/B: `tapcarry0` | `0` |
| `MSC_SMOOTH_CLOCK` | `1` | every Vita build | Under a held cadence (30 or LOCKED 60) in live single-player play, a frame advances the game by its picture's predicted show-to-show time instead of its start-to-start time (`np_show_clock.h`, `np_smooth.cpp`). Every picture is drawn from the blended snapshot (every bone, position, object, the camera's aim) for the moment its frame began; frame starts move by a refresh against the display gate's cadence (late-wait anchor, back-pressure wait) while the 2-deep queue keeps the holds even, so a picture shown 2 refreshes carried 1 or 3 refreshes of motion (r10 [present], evenly held windows: 8 % d1-tour, 41 % perf13-tour, r9 3-7 %). The clock predicts each picture's show vblank with the gate's rule, `S = max(stamp + L, S_prev + n)` (L: p80 of the display's ready-after counts), re-anchored on the display's report of the newest shown picture, in whole vblanks; only after 8 pictures held exactly n (else the frame's own time, so heavy stretches are as before). The debt clock takes the show time as its input; the steps, the pad read and the simulation are unchanged (only how many steps a picture carries). Host model `tools/fps/smooth_unit.cpp` (r10 statistics: pictures off by more than half a refresh 7.8 -> 0.0 % steady, 33 -> 0 % start jitter, 22 -> 15 % rough; mutants 1-4). Never in a LAN or online session, never in a free run (`MSC_FPS_BETWEEN=pace` aligns pictures to their frame start instead). `[smooth]` line (`MSC_SMOOTH_LOG`, plan 1): motion against hold per layer, blend histogram, run/ball/camera evenness, clock counters. Console A/B: `smooth0` | `0` |
| `MSC_LAT_DRAIN` | `1` | every Vita build | The frames-in-flight gate stands aside for 150 frames after a frame over two refreshes (a load, a cut, a long present); at a held 30 a backlog made by that long present stood for five seconds (the Galaxy port's console run: 200 ms of input lag at a perfect cadence). With the switch, a held 30 standing aside still waits one refresh a frame while more than depth + 1 pictures are on their way (`np_fps_fif.h`; mutants in `fif_drain_unit.cpp`). Never in a free run | `0` |
| `MSC_PAD_LATE` | `1` | every Vita build | The late latch (wave 6, np_feel.h `PadLate`): under a held 30 (the clean 30, the 30 fallback, a Mega Strike or Mega Save, whose touch screen is read in the same call) the pad is read up to 12 ms later, into the slack the picture would have waited for its refresh anyway; given back at the first late picture. Single player only; never in a free run or a LAN or online session. Host model: 5.6 ms (light) to 14.7 ms (heavy) of press-to-picture at 30, no fps or hold-mix change by construction. Console A/B: `padlate0` | `0` |
| `MSC_AI_BUDGET` | `off` | player build | No AI script is skipped for time and nothing is drawn from the default random seed (what the network match and the oracle runs rely on); `retail`, `slow:K` or `ops:R` bring back the original real-time rule | `MSC_AI_BUDGET=retail` |
| `MSC_AI_NOCLOCK=1` | ON: the Vita plan sets `MSC_AI_NOCLOCK=1` since r17 (probe18: every host proof holds; kit price17a / probe18b are its off side); code `0` | With `MSC_AI_BUDGET=off` (the player plan's) the AI scripts' profiling clock (`gAIProfilingClock`, read twice around every script execution: about 80 reads a picture, each a system call on the console; estimate 0.06-0.54 ms) returns 0. Its only readers are `gScriptFrameTime` (read by `CheckScriptTimeBudget` only when the budget is above 0; the budget is -1), the unread peaks, and `MSC_AI_STATS`; the statistics a bare `MSC_AI_BUDGET=off` turns on stay off unless `MSC_AI_STATS=1` asks for them. Not applied in network or recorded games (`NpAiBudgetSetup` is not called there). Host proof (w8/probe18.md): det trace identical on match60, The Dump and Lava Pit (`NP_DET_PIN_SEED=1234abcd`), the BREAK seed differs, the negative control (`MSC_AI_NOCLOCK_BREAK=1`: budget 1.0, the clock counts) changes the trace; frame hashes are identical when the harness's virtual clock is not advanced by clock reads (`MSC_VIRTUAL_TICK_NS=0`; with the default 1 us a read the 80 fewer reads shift the virtual time and a time-driven picture detail differs from picture ~1160: the gt10 finding, a harness artifact). The `[aibudget]` line at exit counts the reads. |
| `MSC_TASK_EXEC_SKIP=0\|1` | Vita plan `1`, code `0` | gxp17 (23 Nov). `nlTaskManager::RunAllTasks` (nlTask.cpp) read the clock after each task for `mExecutionTime`, a field nothing reads (about 18 reads a picture, each a system call of about 0.7 us on the Vita): 1 does not make the read. The read that gives a task its time step is untouched. `2` is the host proof mutant (also skips that one), never set in play. Proof: det trace and back-end hashes equal on the Dump and Vice goal routes at `MSC_VIRTUAL_TICK_NS=0` (with a tick, every clock read moves the harness's virtual time, so the read count is part of a host run's clock). The cpu22 rows below are `1` in the plan from the same change. |
| `MSC_CPU22_INVLAZY=0/1/2` | Vita plan `1` from gxp17 (host proof on two more routes, verify mode 0 mismatches), code `0` (the console runs `cpu22v-v` / `cpu22v-d` still worth reading: `[cpu22] invlazy mismatches 0`) | `SetGameObjectShadowViewMatrix` (GameObjectLighting.cpp) inverted the 4x4 view matrix every call (about 18 a picture in the tour) although `ApplyGameObjectShadowLighting` reads `gShadowInverseViewMatrix` rarely. 1 = the matrix is copied and the same `nlInvertMatrix` runs at the first read; 2 = both ways, a pending copy is compared with the eager result at every Set and at the read, `[cpu22] invlazy mismatches N`. Host proof (w8/cpu22.md): det trace and frame hashes identical to 0 on Vice and The Dump (6500 pictures, `NP_DET_PIN_SEED=1234abcd`) with 1 and 2; BREAK seed `1234abce` differs; mutant `MSC_CPU22_BREAK=1` (the lazy inverse perturbed) gives about 100k mismatches in mode 2. The glnull host never reads the inverse (0 reads), so the mutant does not change the trace in mode 1: only mode 2 proves it on the host. Saving (callgrind, all three switches together, 60 pictures of open play, ovl.env): 65k host instructions a picture on Vice, 38k on The Dump, about 0.3 / 0.2 ms on the Vita at 4.8 ns an instruction (an ESTIMATE; the three are not split). |
| `MSC_CPU22_POWERUP=0/1/2` | Vita plan `1` from gxp17, code `0` | `DrawablePowerup::Render` (DrawablePowerup.cpp) looked the stadium drawable `NP_POWERUP_HASH(idx)` up every picture (AVL tree, then the drawable map; 150 lookups). 1 = a 150-entry table filled on first use and dropped when `World::AddDrawableObject` / `RemoveDrawableObject`, the World or BasicStadium constructor or destructor, or `fn_8027876C` bump `NpCpu22DrawEpochV`, or when the stadium pointer changes; only the game thread uses it. 2 = the real lookup answers and is compared. Host proof: identical det and frame hashes on both routes with 1 and 2, 0 mismatches in about 776k calls (99.97 % served from the table); mutant `MSC_CPU22_BREAK=2` gives about 98k mismatches in mode 2 (mode 1 trace unchanged: the powerups are hidden in these routes). Saving: see MSC_CPU22_INVLAZY (the three together). |
| `MSC_CPU22_REPLAY=0/1/2` | Vita plan `1` from gxp17, code `0` | `GetReplayExcitement` (ReplayManager.cpp) walked the replay ring (up to 2000 frames) twice with `Replay::TimeOfLastOccurence`. 1 = `Replay::TimeOfLastOccurence2` makes one walk with two accumulators (same traversal); 2 = the two walks answer and the bits are compared. Host proof: det and frame hashes identical with 1 and 2 on both routes, 0 mismatches in 15000 calls; mutant `MSC_CPU22_BREAK=3` (another mask) gives 1178 mismatches in mode 2 (mode 1 trace unchanged: the excitement is cosmetic in these routes). Saving: see MSC_CPU22_INVLAZY (the three together). |
| `MSC_CPU24_PART=0/1/2` | Vita plan `1`, code `0` | `EvaluateParticleColour` (ParticleSystem.cpp), run for every drawn particle every picture, called `floorf` through a double round trip, divided `timeElapsed / lifeSpan` again although `UpdateAllParticles` had just stored the same float in `timeFraction`, and multiplied and clamped three channels by colour scales that are 1.0f. `1`: the keyframe index is `(int)frame` when `frame >= 0` (the truncation of a non-negative float is its floor; a negative or NaN `frame` keeps `floor`), `timeFraction` is used when `lifeSpan > 0` (spawn: both 0, so `0 / lifeSpan` is the stored 0), and the scale step is skipped while all three tweak values are exactly 1.0f (`c * 1.0f = c`, inside 0..255). `2` verify: the original answers and the fast colour is compared (`[cpu24] part: checked N mismatches M`, M must be 0). Proofs (w8/cpu24.md): unit test `tools/gamefast/run_cpu24_test.py host|arm` (7.87 M random and awkward cases: negative, NaN, huge, lifeSpan 0 and below, keyframe edges, scales 1.0f and others; x86 and the VitaSDK GCC `-O2 -mcpu=cortex-a9 -mfpu=neon` under qemu-arm: 0 differences, `CPU24_BREAK=1` mutant caught, 61,561 differences); host routes with `1` and `2`: det trace and every frame hash equal, 714 k colours verified on the Vice tour, 0 mismatches; `MSC_CPU24_BREAK=1` (red channel +1 for 1 colour in 64) changes the frame hashes and is counted in mode 2. Saving (callgrind, 100 pictures of open play, ESTIMATE for the Vita): 81 `floorf` calls and one divide a picture there, more in effects-heavy scenes (celebrations, Mega Strike) |

A notification only invalidates, so a span that includes alignment padding can at worst make a cache look once more. Host: 21,284 -> 13,084 notifications per 100 pictures of open play (the writers 12,300 -> 4,100 calls; `np_gx_notify_write` inclusive -16.0 k host instructions a picture; the Vita pays the barriers, which an instruction count does not show: ESTIMATE 0.1 ms a picture at 1.6 us a round).
