### 1.3 Proposed Defaults

The owner's standing rule for 1.3 as for 1.2: what helps performance or graphics is on for a player who never opens Settings, and one goes back off only if testing
finds a major issue (a crash, a fault, a desync risk, a clearly broken picture or a measured slowdown). The switches below passed the host checks of the 6 Nov 2026
defaults wave and are **proposed** as defaults. The change itself is the top commit of `agent/defaults` ("1.3 defaults: turn the proposed switches on"), kept apart so that it can
be applied or held as a unit after the console session; until then they are off, as the rows of each switch say. That commit puts them in `vita_plan.cpp` (`kQuality`, logged in
`[plan] quality and timing switches on by default`), made the ON choice the first of each Settings row (the 1.3 prune removed the rows named "no row" below), and turns the console kit's runs into "off" A/B runs. Today the kit's
run `defaults-all` turns every one of them on together, against `d1`. Each is render-only, or disc I/O and thread placement only: none changes a game tick, a random number or a
sync record (the online agent's 45-switch lockstep run of the armhf harness, 5,061 identical steps, covered every game-side one). None has been timed on a console.
Evidence: `reports/defaults.md` of that wave (host32 GL pictures of match60 at both sizes, together and by group, a whole-route run and all 16 stadiums with every one on).

| Item | Proposed key and value | Settings row (kit run after the change) | Picture |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Tight particle sprites | `MSC_FX_TIGHT=8`, `MSC_FX_TIGHT_DROP=1` | `0` (`fxtight0`) | Same pixels (polygons on the texel lattice; dropped sprites cannot reach a pixel). Forms in the 1.3 pack |
| Fill governor | `MSC_FX_GOV=1`, `MSC_FX_GOV_MEGA=1` | no row since the 1.3 prune (`fxgov0`, `fxgov0960`, `fxgovmega0960`) | Only under overload: large sprites one in two, then one in four, the rest brighter by as much, by pool slot; the size ladder waits at most about a second |
| Lighter glow | `MSC_FX_GLOW=half` | no row since the 1.3 prune (`fxglowfull`) | Softer glow in menus, replays and cut-scenes |
| Match shadows | `MSC_SHADOW_PLAY=3` | no row since the 1.3 prune (`shadowplay2`) | The shadow's shape lags a frame more; its place does not |
| Amortising at 60 | `MSC_AMORT_60=1` | `0` (`amort60off` against `amort60ref`) | At 60, shadows and crowd refresh in time as at 30 |
| Planar shadow height | `MSC_SHADOW_PLANE=1` | `0` (`shadowplane0`) | A fix: stadium-object and item shadows stop flickering into the pitch |
| Lazy crowd cluster | `MSC_CROWD_CLUSTER_LAZY=1` | `0` (`clusterlazy0`) | NOT the same picture (sweep24: far crowd differs at The Dump); stays off |
| Layer marks under the pipeline | `MSC_VIEW_LAYERS=1` | `0` (`viewlayers0`) | The size ladder's background steps can act |
| Still backdrops | `MSC_MENU_STILL=1`, `MSC_PAUSE_STILL=1` | no row since the 1.3 prune (`menustill0`) | A settled menu world stops moving; captains, glow and menus stay live |
| Compact textures | `MSC_VGX_LA88=2`, `MSC_VGX_CMPRA=2`, `MSC_VGX_TEX16=1` | no row since the 1.3 prune (`texfull`, `cmpra0`, `tex16off`) | LA88=2 the same texels; CMPRA=2 within 12/255; TEX16=1 under half a step |
| Disc pre-open | `MSC_DVD_PREOPEN=1` | `0` (`preopen0`) | None (same bytes) |
| Loader at 60 | `MSC_LOAD_FPS=60` | `0` (`loadfps0`) | Loading screen at a picture per refresh; solo only |
| Sweeps off the frame | `MSC_VITA_SWEEP_BG=1` | `0` (`sweepbg0`) | None |
| Fourth core | `MSC_VITA_CORE3=1`, `MSC_VITA_CORE3_SEND=1` | no row since the 1.3 prune (`core3off`) | None; acts only where the start-up probe proves core 3 (CapUnlocker) |
| GPU queue event wait | `MSC_VITA_QUEUE_WAIT=event` | `poll` (`queuewait0`) | None; same queue rules |

Kept off, with the reason: `MSC_VGX_GXMCACHE` (emulator aborts); `MSC_VGX_TEXSWIZZLE` (its first console run drew a wrong HUD icon, and the host cannot see the
swizzled layout); `MSC_VITA_USSE_CDRAM` (8 MiB of video memory the anti-aliasing rungs and the HD pack need, for an unmeasured gain); `MSC_VGX_PALBUCKET` (measured on the
harness: 57 % more program switches for 5 % fewer ring bytes, and new vertex forms outside the pack); `MSC_VGX_FS_HALF`, `MSC_VGX_Q1`, `MSC_VGX_FOGVTX`
(shader text or keys outside the pack: a cold compile on the console keeps about 145 KiB of heap per vertex shader; `MSC_VGX_TEX2D`, `MSC_VGX_LEANVARY`, `MSC_VGX_FS_PROJ` moved to `kSpeed` defaults in `int32` backed by `pack31+`, note Mesa desktop link still rejects `TEX2D=1` on colour texgens); `MSC_FX_HAZE_CACHE` and `MSC_FX_TIGHT_FLOOR` (lossy,
and on Pipeline Central, the only stadium with the haze they target, the default heavy-stadium profile already halves the mist and sends it to the reduced effects pass);
`MSC_FX_TIGHT_BLACK` (changes destination alpha); `MSC_VITA_CORE_GC` (puts the collector and mixer on core 1 with the frame send and the GL back end; console only);
`MSC_POSE_CULL=2` (not exact for effects that start off the screen, and the pose is read by the simulation); `MSC_REPLAY_FARLOD=12` (the host pictures of a Mega Strike cut-scene show a sidekick close to the camera drawn with the open-play mesh, with a dark seam on the shoulder: the distance is not the one the picture shows; it stays in PERFORMANCE (60)); `MSC_VGX_HUDNATIVE`, `MSC_VGX_LODBIAS`, `MSC_VITA_SHAPE`
(picture trade-offs never seen on a console: Settings rows); `MSC_MENU_LOD`, `MSC_FX_WARBLE=half`, `MSC_SHADOW_RES` (visible losses for small gains); `MSC_SKIN_LANE`,
`MSC_VGX_CROWDSORT`, `MSC_VGX_CROWDPREZ` (still being built or expected to lose); `MSC_VGX_PROMOSOLO`, `MSC_VGX_DL_TINY`, `MSC_FX_LOWRES_RUNS`, `MSC_VGX_SOFTXFORM` levels
(tuning values with no evidence for any value; the kit's `softx128`, `softx64`, `promosolo8`, `dltiny64`, `fxruns2` decide).

### Galaxy 14 Nov 2026 comparison (wave 11, galaxy16): ad hoc radio switches

The Galaxy port's multiplayer line reviewed the ad hoc radio it had taken from this port (its commits 6c8227c8, f4265a5e on `radiostub`, 13 Nov; host evidence and review only:
Galaxy's multiplayer has not run on a console yet). The decisions are in `native/platform/sdk/net/np_galaxy16.h` (host test `tools/net/galaxy16_test.cpp`, 6 mutants caught);
`vlink_vita_radio.cpp` only calls them. Every default below is the 1.2 behaviour; a console run is needed before any is turned on (kit line `radiofix15`).

| Switch | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `MSC_LAN_RADIO_FIX` | 0 | Bits: 1 the four teardown calls that fail when the cell is already gone (PdpDelete, AdhocctlDisconnect, AdhocctlTerm, AdhocTerm) are tried 3 times, then logged and the close goes on (1.2 retried for ever and the radio stayed CLOSING until the game restarted); 2 a PdpRecv error that is not a time-out ends the link only after 200 in a row (about a second); 4 a datagram is not dropped for a source port other than 19021 (a byte-swapped port would have dropped every frame); 8 log lines for the first console night (own MAC, PdpCreate result, NetCheck result, first PdpRecv error, first odd source port, whether each of the first 8 senders is in the peer list). 15 = all. |
| `MSC_LAN_GROUP` | 1 | 0: the NetCheck dialog starts without the group name (the fallback choice of 1.2). |
| `MSC_LAN_TIMEOUT_MS` | 60000 | The NetCheck dialog's own time-out (0..3600000). |
| `MSC_NET_KEEP_STACK` | 1 | (mpfix18) 1: after a Nearby session sceNet, sceNetCtl and the NET module stay up (the pool is not freed). HTTPS (`np_https_sys.cpp` `g_netUp`) and the online socket cache "the stack is up"; in 1.2 a Nearby session that had been the first to start sceNet ended it with `sceNetTerm`, and the Online menu, leaderboards and the connection test then failed (resolver and socket errors) until the game was restarted. 0 = the 1.2 teardown. Host test `tools/net/mpfix18_test.cpp`; console check: Nearby match, leave, Connection Test. |
| `MSC_MP_KEEP_AWAKE` | 1 | (awake19) 0: no power tick. 1: `np_vita_lan_frame` (`vlink_vita.cpp`, once per picture) feeds `NpKeepAwake::Gate` (`np_keep_awake.h`): while `NpNetActive() \|\| NpLanActive() \|\| NpOnlineFlowActive()` (a LAN or online session, lobby, queue or invite wait) it calls `sceKernelPowerTick` at most once a second by `sceKernelGetProcessTimeWide`; logs `[mp] keep awake on` / `off`. Outside a session: no clock read, no call (test `test_keep_awake.cpp`). |
| `MSC_NET_KEEPAWAKE_MS` | 0 | (wake14) The stock Vita Wi-Fi uses power save and the first datagram after a quiet spell takes ~100 ms (median) to ~200 ms (p95) instead of ~7 ms. `N` (10..5000): while a peer link (a console we sent a unicast to in the last 10 s) carries nothing for `N` ms, `vlink.cpp` (`LinkImpl::KeepAwakeLocked`, run from every pump of the link, so once per frame on the game thread) sends it one 24-byte header-only frame (destination port 0, no payload). The receiving link counts it (`keepAwakeReceived`) and drops it before any socket: it never reaches the game, the lockstep or its digest, and a build without this switch drops it as a datagram for a port nobody has bound. Both consoles run the same code, so each direction is covered by its own sender. Not on the ad hoc radio (Nearby), not while the link is lost or while the keep-awake idle limit of `MSC_MP_KEEP_AWAKE` has paused (10 minutes untouched outside a match), not under test impairment. Not sent to the online service (its HTTPS polls are unchanged); on an internet session it rides the peer path (direct or TURN relay) like any datagram of the link. Extra traffic: at most `1000/N` frames a second per peer (20/s at 50 ms), 24 bytes each plus the medium's headers: about 1 KB/s on Wi-Fi, about 1.3 KB/s encrypted over the internet path; only in the quiet spells, none in a match that sends every 20 ms. Battery: the radio stays out of power save while it runs, which is the point; not measured (console run `wakegap` of the wave-14 notes). Test: `tools/net/wake14_test.cpp` + `wake14_mutants.sh` (in `run_tests.sh`). |
| `MSC_LINK_GAPLOG` | 0 | (wake14, diagnostic) `N` ms: every datagram of a peer that ends a quiet spell of at least `N` ms (each direction; a received keep-awake filler counts as an arrival, so the log of the partner console shows when this console's radio really sent) logs `[vlink] gap tx|rx <ms> ms phase=<st<session state> [sim|nosim] sc<last scene>>`, and a cumulative table per phase (count, longest, keep-awake frames sent) every 30 s of link time and when the link goes down. `sim`/`nosim`: a simulation tick ran in the last 100 ms or not (a cut-scene, a replay, a load). |
| `MSC_NET_TEXT_ASCII` | 1 | (netclient13) 1: a JSON answer of the online service (`np_json.h` `Json::Parse` for the signaling client, `np_online_json.cpp` `JsonParse` for the DWC layer) that holds a string or key with a byte outside 0x20..0x7E after unescaping (a line feed, UTF-8, a control byte) has each such byte turned into `?` (a UTF-8 sequence becomes one `?`) and is still parsed (`ScrubAscii`; not refused, because the admin / maintenance message of `/v1/info` and `503 service_disabled` is free text that may hold an en dash); and every name or sentence copied into a fixed field (`np_text_safe.h` `SafeCopy`: room members, relay credentials, friends rows, error texts) has such bytes turned into `?`, so no peer nickname can put a line feed in the log. 0: the earlier behaviour (bytes >= 0x20 pass). Counters `TextCounters()`; fuzz: `native/tools/net/fuzz_net_test.cpp`. |
| `MSC_TURN_VERIFY` | 0 | (netclient13, console only) 1: a TURN success answer to a request that carried MESSAGE-INTEGRITY must carry a valid one itself (HMAC-SHA1 under the long-term key, RFC 5389 15.4, `np_turn.cpp` `VerifyIntegrity`); otherwise it is dropped (`TurnStats.integrityRefused`) and the request retransmits. 0: only the transaction id and the server address are checked, as before. Off until a console relay run shows the service's TURN server signs every answer. |
| `MSC_FRIENDS_IDLE_STOP_S`, `MSC_FRIENDS_MAX_REQS`, `MSC_FRIENDS_MIN_GAP_S` | 0, 0, 0 | (netclient13, O4, console only) Galaxy's friends-list pacing on Strikers' held poll (`np_friends_policy.h`, `np_dwc_friend.cpp` `StartNext`): no new friends request after N seconds (Galaxy 180) without a button / stick / touch (`NpVitaInputActivity`) or after N requests (Galaxy 20) since the last one; a button press starts it again at once; the minimum gap lengthens the gap after an answer (Galaxy 30 s). Logs `[friends] refresh paused ...` / `resumed`. All 0 = exactly the earlier poll. `NpFriends::RefreshNow()` (a hand refresh, Galaxy's START) exists but nothing calls it: the retail friend screens have no START hook. |
| `MSC_NET_CENSUS` | 0 | (netclient13, O9, diagnostic) 1: `[net] sockets open N (online udp U, lan udp L, https S, epoll E; A opened, B closed so far) at <where>` when the online screens are left (`np_online_flow.cpp`) and at game exit (`np_vita_main.cpp`); `np_net_census.h` counts the two UDP sockets where they are made and closed and reads the HTTPS client's count (`np_https_socket_stats`, `np_https_epolls_live`). Run it before blaming the game for the vitacompanion FTP wedge. Host test: `native/tools/net/run_census_test.sh`. |
| `MSC_NET_POW` | 1 | (gxnet14, from the Galaxy port) 1: a register that the service answers with `428 pow_required` (`{bits, challenge}`, only when the service has REGISTER_POW_BITS > 0, default off) is solved (`np_pow.h`, slices of 6 ms per poll in `np_online_http.cpp` `SolveSlice`) and sent again with `{"pow":{challenge, nonce}}`; at most 3 rounds (the price rises per address); more than 17 bits (`NpPow::kMaxBits`, about 2 s on a Vita, ESTIMATE) is not ground: the player reads "very busy". No extra request while the service has it off. 0: the 428 is an ordinary refusal. Host: `tools/net/pow_test.cpp` (against the service's reference nonces), `tools/dwc/http_test.cpp` `TestPow` (rising 9..13 bits against the fake). |
| `MSC_NET_EDGE_BUSY` | 1 | (gxnet14, Galaxy quota32) 1: an answer that says 429 and is not the service's JSON error (Cloudflare's own page, "1027 Worker exceeded free tier daily request limit") is "busy": the signaling client (`np_signal_http.cpp`) and the DWC layer (`np_online_http.cpp` `Classify`) fail a decisive call at once with the allowance sentence and remember it for 30 s (`np_service_busy.h`), instead of retrying three times against a spent quota. A 5xx page keeps its retries. 0: the older retries. Host: `online_test.cpp --held`, `http_test.cpp` `TestEdgeBusy`. |
| `MSC_NET_PACE_HINTS` | 0 | (gxnet14, console only) 1: obey `nextWaitSec` (the hold to ask for next, 1..55) and `nextPollMs` (250..60000, the pause after an answer that was not held) in the service's poll answers (server13 "Pacing hints"; the room poll in `np_signal_http.cpp`, the quick / ranked queue in `np_dwc_match.cpp`; the friends list already holds 45 s as the service says). Out-of-range numbers are ignored. ESTIMATE (`scripts/estimate-requests.mjs`): queue 293 -> 133, invite room 440 -> 353 requests per player-hour. Needs the service of package s31 (older services do not send them: no effect). |

Without a switch (a branch that only runs after a failure): `WifiOpen` (`vlink_vita.cpp`) returns false when the 1 MiB net pool cannot be allocated instead of passing a null block to `sceNetInit` (Galaxy lanfix, f3030944).

### Galaxy r35-r41: what was not ported

`GXV_TEXBAKE` has no target here: particles are per-emitter meshes with their texture coordinates already written on the CPU (`ParticleSystem::RenderAllParticles`). On the Pipeline Central route (`MSC_VGX_MERGECENSUS`, emulator) no same-key pair differs in the texture matrices alone, and 3-5 pairs a frame differ in model-view and texture matrices together; 35-38 pairs differ in the palette block. `GXV_BE_FAST`: its source was not at hand, and `MSC_VGX_UNIDUP`, `MSC_VGX_GXM_DIRECT` and the backend's state filters cover the same ground. Galaxy's two crash classes: this game has no loading thread (`OSCreateThread` is used only by the network code; file callbacks run from `nlServiceFileSystem` on the game thread) and shader warm-up draws (`CMD_VARIANT`) run only inside `Execute`, on the one render thread.

### Galaxy r57-r86: what was ported in 1.3

The Galaxy port (same renderer family: its `platform/vgx` is this `renderer/src`) measured these on the same Vita up to r76. The full item-by-item comparison is in the
1.3 agent reports `galaxy-parity.md`, `galaxy-parity-r82.md` and `galaxy-parity-r86.md`; what they added here:

| Switch | Default | Effect and risk |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `MSC_DYNRES_NEWSTADIUM=0\|1` | `1` | Galaxy r73's `GXV_AUTOQ_NEWSTAGE`. When the game tears a stadium down (`DestroyStadium` -> `VgxStadiumChanged`, also on the way to and from the menus' world) the size ladder starts again at the game's own size, with the usual up-step pace (`upHold` 45), no "no gain" floor, a fresh severe-overload count and no late-frame hold (`MSC_DYNRES_LATEHOLD`). Before, a heavy stadium's (or the GPU-heavy menus') low level and doubled up-hold carried into the next match, which then climbed one level per 1.5-3 s or more. What a Mega Strike learned (`megaNoGain`) is kept. Render only: the picture size of a match's first seconds, nothing the game reads; it does nothing with a fixed size, `MSC_DYNRES=0`, `MSC_DYNRES_LEVEL` or `MSC_DYNRES_CYCLE`. Console kit run `newstadium0` |
| `MSC_VGX_UNI_SHADOW=0\|1` | `1` | Galaxy r57's `GXV_UNI_SHADOW` (on there since r57). A program's plain F32 vec4 uniforms (`u_proj`, `u_mv`, `u_tm`, `u_ptm`, `u_pal`, `u_lt`, `u_tev`, `u_bone`) are written straight into vitaGL's per-program default uniform buffers at the addresses `vglGetUniformShadow` (vitaGL patch 0024) gave at link, with one `vglUniformShadowWritten` per draw for the flags `glUniform*` would have set (the program's changed flag of each stage, which also keeps patch 0018's uniform reuse from naming an older copy, and the stage's dirty flag). Same bytes in the same buffers: the same picture by construction. It saves vitaGL's per-call work (location decode, a libSceGxm type query, the generic array copy) on every block a draw gives after `MSC_VGX_UNIDUP`'s filter. A uniform kept in another type (half precision under `MSC_VGX_FS_HALF`), with an element offset or larger than vitaGL says belongs to it keeps `glUniform*`; a vitaGL without the patch (weak symbols) keeps it for all. `MSC_VGX_GLSTATS` counts `uniShadowWrites`. Test: `renderer/tests/run_vitagl_uniform_shadow_test.sh` (the patch and `vgl_fill_uniform_data` / `vglSetUniformData` / `upload_uniforms` taken from the source; three deliberate breaks fail). Console kit run `unishadow0`. Not measured on a Vita here |
| `MSC_HEAP_GUARD=0\|1\|2` | `0` | Galaxy r68's `GXV_HEAP_GUARD`, a diagnostic. `1`: long-lived port storage registered with `np::HeapGuard` (`native/include/np/host_alloc.h`) is checked at every stadium destroy, and any of it inside a game arena is logged (`[heapguard]`). `2` (host builds with `<execinfo.h>`): also a census of every operator new the game thread makes from the game's heaps, by call stack, printed at exit (`[heapcensus]`); `native/tools/heapguard/census.py <run.log> <executable>` symbolises it and names port code among the callers. Neither touches the game's heaps |
| `MSC_VGX_VP_EXACT=0\|1` | `1` | Galaxy r82's exact-halves `glViewport` (vitaGL patch 0025, `vglSetViewportExact`). vitaGL halved a viewport with an integer shift, so a viewport of odd width or height was mapped one pixel short from its corner: a 1-px dark line along the far edge of what it draws. The ladder's picture sizes are multiples of 8, but the game's viewports and copy rectangles scale with them: in the host harness at level 7 (0.86 per side, the usual floor at 30) 11.8 of 16.8 viewports a frame are odd, at the game's own size none (`viewportOdd`, `MSC_VGX_GLSTATS`). With the patch the halves are floats: OpenGL's rule, the same numbers for even sizes. `0` gives the shift back (console A/B `vpexact0`). `MSC_VGX_VP_TRUNC=1` (host builds only) draws odd viewports as the console did before the patch, to see the line on the host. Tests: `renderer/tests/run_vitagl_viewport_test.sh` (vitaGL's own `glViewport` over a model of `sceGxmSetViewport`, every size 1-600, framebuffer and display; `BREAK=1` fails) |
| `MSC_VGX_PX_NOOP=0\|1` | `1` | Galaxy r77's `GXV_PX_NOOP`. A draw whose state lets it write nothing (no colour: mask off or blend ZERO/ONE; no alpha: mask off or that blend without the constant-alpha pass; no depth: depth writes need the test) is left out. This back end never uses stencil, so the picture is the same by construction (`pxNoop`). None on the match route; kept for scenes that have them |
| `MSC_VGX_HUNT=<mask or names>`, `MSC_VGX_HUNT_WIN=<pictures>` | unset (off); lab and logging builds only, never in a player plan | 1.3 (wave 9) GPU hunt for the first console runs. Drops whole classes of GPU work so a console window shows where the GPU time goes; the picture is wrong on purpose. Names (comma list) or a number: `crowd`=1 `far`=2 `pitch`=4 `stadium`=8 `chars`=16 `pshadow`=32 (shadow pictures; zero in the Vita plan at The Dump, where the shadows are the volumes) `svol`=64 (shadow volumes and their darkening quad) `fx`=128 `passes`=256 `copies`=512 `hud`=1024 `upscale`=2048 `atest`=4096 `blend`=8192 `flat`=16384 (every fragment program replaced by one opaque single-texture program) `pixel`=32768 (scissor 1x1: no fill) `upskip`=65536 (final picture pass not drawn) `rain`=131072 (gpuscene20, class-free: additive draws whose first texture is 32 x 32 texels or smaller, the Dump rain; it reads no class, so it acts on the console). Live: `ab hunt <mask>` writes `np_live_ab[5]` (-1 = the environment value). Every window (default 120 pictures that drew something) prints one `[hunt]` line with the draws, passes or copies dropped per picture and `DID NOTHING` for an arm that dropped none. Unset, the pictures and the `NP_DET_TRACE` trace are byte-identical. |
| `MSC_VGX_PX_SCISSOR=0\|1` | `1` | Galaxy r77's `GXV_PX_SCISSOR`. On vitaGL each change of an enabled scissor draws a full-target mask quad (the host route makes 12 scissor calls and 17 updates a frame). A triangle draw cannot put a fragment outside its own viewport, so when the viewport lies inside both the new scissor and the one in force (or none), the scissor is left as it is (`pxScissor`). Lines and points (wide ones reach past the viewport) and the display HUD path always set their scissor. On the host match route at level 7 with `MSC_VGX_RT=1` it acts on 0.1 of 15.8 scissor calls a frame: this game's scissors are mostly smaller than their viewports. Exact under OpenGL's clipping rule (host picture check); the console must clip to the viewport as well, which the Galaxy port's console runs with this cut on indicate but do not prove for this game: console kit run `pxscissor0` |
| `MSC_VGX_PX_DEADCLEAR=0\|1` | `1` | Galaxy r77's `GXV_PX_DEADCLEAR`. With `MSC_VGX_RT=1` (Vita default), an EFB clear whose depth plane nothing reads afterwards (the RT scan's liveness, asked before and after the command in progress) is made without it, and a clear left with no plane is not made (`pxDeadDepth`, `pxDeadWhole`; 0.09 depth planes a frame on the host match route). The depth left in place is never read: host check with `MSC_VGX_RT=1 MSC_VGX_RTSIM=1` |
| `MSC_DYNRES_BLIND=0\|1` | `1` | Galaxy r86 (its console: the GPU timers stopped reporting under a heavy overload, the ladder never stepped down, 25 fps at 960 for 25 s). The same blind spot is here: the ladder acts only when a new, fully timed GPU frame arrives (`vglRtLastGpuFrame`), and vitaGL gives a scene no notification once its watcher is 48 scenes behind (`rt.c`, `RT_SLOTS - 16`) and publishes a frame with an untimed scene as untimed. With the GPU queue at depth 2 three frames can be in flight, so a frame of 17 or more scenes (the 1.0/1.1 logs' menus; the emulator counts 7-12.6 in play, up to 16.7) goes untimed exactly when the GPU is the bottleneck, and the host's queue-depth rule loses its input with it. After `MSC_DYNRES_BLIND_AFTER` (6) pictures without a timed frame, a picture longer than `MSC_DYNRES_BLIND_LATE` (125) percent of the budget whose busiest CPU lane (the per-core probe `NpFpsPlatformProbe`, time-following work left out) stays under `MSC_DYNRES_BLIND_CPU` (90) percent is fed to the ladder with the GPU taken as busy for the whole picture; other blind pictures are frames counted without a measurement (a step's check still ends), and nothing goes up until the timers are back. A late picture with a busy or unmeasured CPU lane feeds nothing. Not in a Mega Strike's own levels (their frame-interval fallback answers a missing time already), with a forced or cycling level, or simulated times. The probe is read only while blind. Render only. Log: `no timed GPU frame for N pictures`, `GPU timers back after ...`, level changes `late pictures with the GPU timers silent (MSC_DYNRES_BLIND)`. Tests: `renderer/tests/run_dynres_blind_test.sh` (the verdicts, and `dynres_sim --blind-test`: on the console's timer model with 17 scenes a frame a 25 s stretch at 1.6x the budget is 60.5 % late pictures at 30 without the guard and 14.1 % with it, 18.3 -> 0.3 % at 60; where the timers report, identical; `BREAK=1` fails). Console kit runs `blind0`, `blindpipe0` |
| `MSC_DYNRES_HOLD30=<frames>` | `4` | Galaxy r84's hold30. At 30 (not in a Mega Strike) the ladder's step on the smoothed GPU time (over 90 % of the budget) also needs a leaky count of measured frames over 90 % to reach `<frames>`: one spike lifts the mean by a quarter of itself and alone stepped the picture down, although the queue of two frames absorbs it, and at a mean between 72 and 90 % the ladder never climbed back. Three frames in a row over the budget, `MSC_DYNRES_LATEHOLD` and the 60 budget are unchanged; `0` is the old rule. Trace model (`dynres_sim --hold30=4`, synthetic): one-frame spikes at 65 % load 0.7 level changes a minute -> 0, open play at 85 % mean picture 0.837 -> 0.869 of the pixels with the same late frames, the Galaxy-shaped trace 0.04 -> 0.06 % late. Render only. Console kit run `hold30off` |
| `MSC_VITA_CORE_SIM=<cores>` (alias `MSC_SIMT_CORE`), `MSC_VITA_PRIO_SIM=<n>` (alias `MSC_SIMT_PRIO`) | by `MSC_SIMT` level | **pace18 (plan-simthread17 stage 2d), only with MSC_SIMT 1 or 2** (nothing changes at 0 or when the switch was refused at start-up). Role `kSim` of `vita_threads.cpp` places `np_sim`: digits 0-2, a set like `12` = cores 1 or 2, never 3 (refused with a log line); the reader is `CoreMask(kSim)`, `MSC_SIMT_CORE` is the alias (`MSC_VITA_CORE_SIM` wins). Defaults: `MSC_SIMT=1` the game thread's own core (it waits, the core is idle), priority as the thread has it; `MSC_SIMT=2` core 2 at priority `0x10000100-17` (one more urgent than the job workers 144, below audio 128). Also only under `MSC_SIMT=2`: the frame-send role's default is core 1 (it was core 2; plan C already sets 1) and the job pool's workers sit on cores 0 and 1 (`np_jobs.cpp` WorkerCores; `MSC_JOBS_CORES` wins). The thread is registered with the per-core probe, so `[lane]` and the controller count its CPU on its core. **Thread / core map** (core 0 / 1 / 2; the fourth core only with `MSC_VITA_CORE3` and the plug-in): `MSC_SIMT=0` (today, 444 and 500 alike): game + step / render, gxm queue, frame send (plan C), worker 1 / audio, mixer, helpers, collector, player, worker 0. `MSC_SIMT=1`: step on `np_sim` on core 0 while the game thread waits, everything else as at 0. `MSC_SIMT=2`: game thread (Begin Frame, window, rest of the picture), worker 0 / render, gxm queue, frame send, worker 1 / `np_sim`, audio, mixer, helpers, collector. With the fourth core proven audio, mixer, disc and save workers and the log writer (and the send worker with `MSC_VITA_CORE3_SEND`) go to core 3 and `np_sim` has core 2 almost alone. 500 MHz changes the speed, not the placement. |
| `MSC_VITA_CORE3_WATCH=0` | on whenever core 3 is in use | pace18 (after VitAdhoc's finding that a thread on the system core can lose its share during play): thread `np_core3watch` (only when the probe proved the core): every 2 s a 3 ms spell on core 3 at a priority above its residents; two checks in a row under 50 % of the spell's time (one does not; a good check clears the count) move every thread on the system core back to cores 1-2, mark the core unusable for the session (later `PinCurrentThread` calls take the plan's core) and print one `[vita] fourth core lost its share` line. Decision `NpVitaCore3::Watch` (np_vita_core3.h), tests `platform/vita/tests/test_core3.cpp` (mutants `NP_CORE3WATCH_BREAK=1..4` fail). Without the fourth core nothing runs. |
| `MSC_VITA_CORE3_MIXER=1` | off | c3g18 (Galaxy's CORE3 breakdown, 23 Nov): under the fourth-core rules the mixer stays on its plan core (it shares a lock with the game thread; preempted on core 3 while holding it, the game waits); `1` lets it go to core 3. The log writer (file I/O, Galaxy's console-freeze pattern) is never moved there under the rules (`MSC_VITA_CORE3_RULES=0` gives the 1.3 placement back for an A/B). |
| `MSC_VITA_CORE3_MARKER=0` | on whenever core 3 is in use | c3g18 (Galaxy's recommendation 8): `ux0:data/mscharged/core3-launch.txt` is written when the probe proves the core and removed by the watch after 120 s; a launch that finds it removes it and runs on three cores (`[vita] fourth core: not available ... the launch marker`), so a launch that froze the console is followed by one without the core, and the one after tries again. |
| `np_simpace18.h` (no key) | with `MSC_SIMT` 1 or 2 | pace18: the pacing cost model when the step is on `np_sim`. `Auto::Predict` (60 Hz and free-run cost) and the game clock's picture cost (`CapSteps`) price the picture as `base + max(window, step) + overhead` with `MSC_SIMT=2` (overhead = join wait less (step - window), smoothed), not `window + step`; `MSC_SIMT=0` and `1` with `np_sim` on the game core give the old numbers bit for bit (`tools/fps/simpace18_unit.cpp`, mutants `NP_SIMPACE_BREAK=1..5` fail). `[head]` lines gain `sim thread (MSC_SIMT) busy ms p50/p95/max, join wait ms p95/max, joins that waited N of M` and `[simt]` gains the join wait p95 and the sim thread's busy per kick (np_sim wall time). |
| `MSC_VITA_CORE3_RETRY=0..3` | `2` | Galaxy r83 (its probe once measured a 3 % share and the run went on three cores). Here the fourth-core probe (`MSC_VITA_CORE3`, `MSC_VITA_CORE3_SEND`) is a yes/no that decides the whole run, and one thread not scheduled on the system core within 200 ms said "not available" with the plug-in installed. The answers a moment's load can give (did not finish in time, did not report, ran elsewhere with the system mask) are asked again up to `<n>` times after 50 ms, each with twice the time (at most 400 ms; the default costs at most 1.1 s of boot, only then); a refused mask, no thread, a refused start or a mask without the system core are final, so a console without the plug-in answers at once. Each try reports into its own slot (a late thread of an earlier try cannot overwrite a later answer). Log: `[vita] fourth core: ... try N`. Test: `native/platform/vita/tests/test_core3.cpp` (`-DNP_CORE3_RETRY_BREAK` fails) |
| `MSC_VITA_CORE3_RULES=0` | on whenever `MSC_VITA_CORE3=1` | T8 (22 Nov; Galaxy's `CORE3.md` and the MH3 report: the system's network stack lives on core 3, a busy thread there stalled online login, pings 4.5-8.4 s; Galaxy's stage-load freezes point at its disc worker on core 3). The rules are the behaviour of `MSC_VITA_CORE3=1` (`np_vita_core3.h` `Rules`, `FilterMask`): only short or blocking threads on core 3 (audio output, the mixer, the log writer, a job lane that parks); the disc and save workers stay on their plan core (`MSC_VITA_CORE3_DISC=1` gives them the core); the frame-send worker, the vitaGL collector and any library thread are refused it (`[vita] core 3 rules:` line); no thread on the core spins (a `MSC_JOBS_CORES` lane with a 3 sleeps at once, `[jobs]` line); the 3 ms spell of `np_core3watch` is skipped while online; an online or LAN session or the online screens (`NpLanActive() \|\| NpOnlineFlowActive()`, read every 8 frames) move every listed thread to cores 1-2, back 3 s after the session; threads are created with the mask (`np_thread_create`, `msc_audio_out`); a thread with core 3 in its mask that nothing asked for is moved in the thread sweep. `=0` gives the 1.3 placement back for an A/B. Default off as a whole (the key is off); console-only question |
| `MSC_VITA_CORE3_DISC=1` | `0` | T8, with `MSC_VITA_CORE3=1`: the disc and save workers (`dvd-io`, `dvd-open`, `dvd-keep`, `nand-io`) on core 3 as in 1.3. Off: `vita_plan.cpp` does not write `NP_VITA_CORE_IO=3`, and a hand-written `NP_VITA_CORE_IO=3` is filtered too (Galaxy's leading suspect for stage-load freezes: a low-priority thread in long `sceIo` calls on a core the system shares) |
| `MSC_VITA_CORE3_AUDIO_PRIO=<n>` | `128` | T8, core 3 rules on: the most urgent priority a thread may have while it is on core 3 (64..191 or `0x10000100 +- offset`). | `MSC_VITA_CORE3_WORK=<bits>` | `3` (Vita plan since c3def18: `MSC_VITA_CORE3_WORK=3` with `MSC_VITA_CORE3=1`; code `0`) | core3x15 (22 Nov), real work on the system core, only with a proven core (the probe runs for it; unproven, lost or held: nothing changes, not even a log line). Bit 1: the job pool (`np_jobs.cpp`: `MSC_POSE_JOBS`, `MSC_PHYS_POSE`, `MSC_SNAP_PAR`, `MSC_CROWD_POS_PAR`) gets one more lane on core 3 (workers 2, 1, 3; it parks at once; only a call with work for every lane uses it; not when `MSC_JOBS_CORES` names the cores or `MSC_JOBS` is 1). Bit 2: the front end's decode worker (`MSC_VGX_FEPAR`) runs on core 3 instead of core 1 and parks at once (it took time from the record player there). Both obey the T8 rules (off while an online session is up, the 2 s watch moves them away if the core loses its share) and a lateness rule: a core-3 lane or decode worker that keeps the caller waiting more than 1500 us (`MSC_VITA_CORE3_SLOW_US`, jobs only) on 3 calls in a window of 48 is given up for the session (`[jobs] core 3 lane given up`, `[fepar] the decode worker on the system core kept the player waiting`). Simulation results do not depend on it (same jobs, same chunks; det trace seed `1234abcd` equal with it on and off on the host with a fake core 3, `NP_CORE3_FAKE=1`, `MSC_VGX_FEPAR_FAKE3=1`). Host mutants: `NP_CORE3LANE_BREAK=1..4`, `NP_CORE3WORK_BREAK` (test_core3.cpp), `MSC_VGX_FEPAR_BREAK=5`. Default off: needs the owner's CapUnlocker and a console A/B (w8/core3x15.md has the run lines). |
| `MSC_VGX_VGL_MASKKEEP=0\|1` | `1` | Galaxy r84's `GXV_VGL_MASKKEEP` (its vitaGL patch 0018 MASK_KEEP, on by default on its console; here vitaGL patch 0026, `vglSetMaskKeep`, written again from the description). vitaGL keeps the scissor in the mask bit of the depth/stencil surface and `update_scissor_test` draws it from nothing: a full-target quad that clears the bit and the quad of the region (or of the whole target) that sets it. Under `STORE_DEPTH_STENCIL` that happens at the start of every framebuffer-object scene, at every return to the display and after every scissor or attribute restore, although the surface usually holds that mask already (drawn there, stored at the scene's end, loaded at this one's start). The two quads are deterministic, so each surface (by its depth memory, up to 16) keeps a record of the mask it holds, and a rebuild with the same inputs (scissor on or off, region, target size, display or not) is not drawn; every state set and restore around the quads is made as before. The record is dropped for new surface memory (`init_depth_stencil_buffer_ms`), a scene that does not load the surface (it starts from the background mask) or does not store it (the memory then keeps an older mask, or part of this one after a partial render), a clear that writes stencil, and table overflow. Exact under the same assumption vitaGL already makes for the display (its scenes do not redraw the mask at all): the mask bit survives a store and a load, and only mask-update programs write it. Counters: `[rt] ... scissor mask rebuilds N/frame drawn, M/frame kept` (Vita, with the `[rt]` line); `scissorKept` in `MSC_VGX_GLSTATS` is a host model of the same rule. Test: `renderer/tests/run_vitagl_maskkeep_test.sh` (vitaGL's own `update_scissor_test` and records over a model of the mask bit with hostile partial renders, stencil clears and reused memory; the mask must equal the scissor after every rebuild with the switch on and off; `BREAK=1`, `2`, `3` (no-load, no-store, stencil-clear rules removed) fail). `0` draws every rebuild (console A/B `maskkeep0`) |

Strikers' own MASK_KEEP (0026, `MSC_VGX_VGL_MASKKEEP`) keeps a record of the mask a surface holds and so leaves out a rebuild only when the scene LOADS and STORES that surface; a scene that does not load it (the RT depth hint, most of a frame's scenes) begins from the background mask, which is clear, and with the scissor test off `update_scissor_test` draws one full-target quad to set the mask everywhere (`[rt] ... scissor mask rebuilds ... drawn`). The background mask is a bit of the depth/stencil surface the scene is begun with (`vglDepthStencilSurfaceSetBackgroundMask`): set before `sceGxmBeginScene` the surface starts with the mask set and the quad is left out (a draw is 25-35 us of GPU time on the Galaxy console, plus its fill). With the scissor test ON the background stays clear and only the region quad is drawn as before; every unloaded scene sets the value explicitly (so a surface that had it set is clear again when the switch is 0 or the scissor is on). Bit 0 (`1`): framebuffer-object scenes; bit 1 (`2`): the display's scene (the picture to the screen, one full-target quad every picture at 960x544: the case with less console evidence, default off); `3` both; `0` every quad drawn. One caution carried over from Galaxy: the quad is drawn under the draw state at that moment (depth test and write); the shortcut equals it where the quad would not have been rejected by the depth test, which Galaxy's console runs with the same vitaGL support. Exact by the host model test `renderer/tests/run_vitagl_maskkeep_test.sh` (mask equal to the scissor after every update with MASK_BG modes 0-3 and MASK_KEEP on and off; BREAK=4..6 caught). | `MSC_VGX_VGL_MEMFREE=0\|1\|2` | `1` | The Galaxy port's free-video-memory fix (console 9-10 Nov: every EFB copy asked vitaGL how much video memory was free, `vglMemFree` answered with `sceClibMspaceMallocStats`, which walks every block of the heap, and the back end went 17 -> 34 ms a picture over 15 stage visits; their patch 0031 keeps a running figure, 2 us a question; here vitaGL patch 0028, written again from the description). Strikers asks in `DynVramFreeKib` (renderer/src/vgx_backend_gl.cpp): the cached value is refreshed every 30th CALL (not frame: `TrimPool`, `DynamicResolution`, the cut record and `StillFrame` each call once a frame, so a walk about every 8 frames, more with the EFB swap) and on every FRESH read, `DynRoomFor` (the ladder's step-up test, evaluated every frame while the ladder is below the game's size and has headroom) and the Mega warm-up; each fresh read walks both pools (video memory and main RAM) in `vgx_vram_free_kib`. Also `LogPools` every 600 presents and the NVX glGetIntegerv token (not used). Now `vgl_malloc`, `vgl_calloc`, `vgl_memalign`, `vgl_realloc` and `vgl_free` keep per pool the usable bytes and the live block count (atomics: the collector thread frees); the free figure is `free_when_empty - (usable + overhead * blocks)`, a few loads. The per-block overhead and the empty figure are measured at pool creation with probe blocks (malloc and memalign of 1 B to 64 KiB, each must add its usable size plus one constant to `current_in_use`, and the pool must return to its start figure): a pool whose allocator fails the probes (a statistics call counting requests; none, as in Vita3K) keeps the walk, logged as `free figure kept on the heap walk`. `0`: the walk as before. `2`: every answer is the WALKED figure and is compared with the counters (`vglGetMemFreeStats`; a check across another thread's allocation is skipped, not compared); the `[mem] free-figure questions` line shows checks, differences and the worst difference. Exact by construction and by the host model test `renderer/tests/run_vitagl_memfree_test.sh` (300000 random operations, every answer equal to the walk; three threads; allocators the probes must refuse; BREAK=1..3 caught); on the console the probes and `=2` are what prove it for Sony's allocator. Kit runs `memfree2` (tier 1) and `memfree0`. |
| `MSC_VGL_GC_PAUSE_MS=0..5000` | `16` | vitaGL patch 0036: the wait after a failed GPU allocation, bounded and counted. When a request is refused by every pool (video memory, main-memory pool, physically contiguous, budget), patch 0005 finished the GPU, ran a collector cycle and called `sceKernelDelayThread(1000000)`, then recursed up to `FRAME_PURGE_FREQ` (4) levels and slept one more second at each level on the way out: a frozen picture of 1 to 4 s, on the render thread, in a game that no console log has shown it in yet (0 FAILED in the pool tables of r11) but whose memory use grows (warm-up, pre-made goal-scene targets, hold targets). Now (`utils/gc_retry.h`, plain C over a table of operations): the GPU is finished once (`sceGxmFinish`, or `glFinish` for CPU-side blocks), then up to four collector cycles each acknowledged (the collector frees the retired texture / buffer / target blocks of one bucket, only after the GPU finish, exactly as before: no block is freed earlier than in 0005) with a try after each; only if that gave nothing a timed wait of at most this many milliseconds in 2 ms steps with a try after each (memory another thread gives back); a path with no collector running yet waits the same bounded time. `0`: no wait; `1000`: about the old second (the kit OFF run `gcpause1000`). The renderer already lets idle pooled pass objects go on a failed target allocation (`TrimPool` follow-up above); vitaGL has no texture cache here (`HAVE_TEX_CACHE` is not defined) and a deletion is never done from inside the allocator (a target deleted in the frame that drew into it crashed r9). Counters (`vglGetGcRecoveryStats`) in every `[mem]` checkpoint and report: `GPU memory recoveries N (resolved, failed, with a timed wait, collector not ready, collector cycles); X ms stopped in all, Y ms of it waiting, longest Z ms; K KiB asked`, a `[mem] NOTE` line when N moved since the last report, and one `[vitaGL] GPU memory recovery #n` line for each of the first eight (then one in 64). N = 0 in a console log means the pools never ran dry. Host test `renderer/tests/run_vitagl_gcretry_test.sh` (a model GPU that is busy until finished and a collector that must not run on a busy GPU, memory that appears after k cycles or after some ms of waiting, a clock only the waits advance; BREAK=1..3 caught). The emulator proof forces the path with the test knob `MSC_VGL_GC_TEST_EVERY=N` / `MSC_VGL_GC_TEST_FAILS=K` (default off; vitaGL `vglSetGcTestInject`: every N-th allocation of 64 KiB or more is treated as refused by every pool and the first K tries of its recovery are refused too, so K above 4 reaches the timed wait). A tiny pool (`MSC_VITA_CDRAM_RESERVE_MIB=80`, `MSC_VITA_VGL_RAM_MIB=56`) was tried first and crashed the game on its own (a NULL write at match start, before any recovery was counted: the game does not survive a pool that is simply too small, which says nothing about the retry), so it is not the proof: Vita3K has an instant `sceGxmFinish` and shows only that the path runs, the counters, and no crash; the real stop on a console is for the kit run `gcpause1000` against d1. |

| `MSC_VGX_PASSHALF=0\|1` | `0` (proposed defaults: `1`) | Galaxy r84's `GXV_PASSHALF=2` ("bloom blur passes only at half scale", on by default there). A blur step of an offscreen chain is drawn at half the render scale: a pass outside an atlas, of at most a quarter of the EFB, whose draws (up to the copy that ends it) sample nothing but colour pictures the frame copies. In this game that is the glow chain (`HighRange.cpp`) without its last, full-size pass: the 320x224, 160x112 and 80x56 steps down and the 160x112 and 320x224 steps up (EFB units), five single-quad passes; their copies make textures of half the size per side, sampled through the same normalised coordinates. `MSC_FX_GLOW=half` removes the other one (the 640x448 pass), so the two stack. Fill saved per frame with the chain (estimate from the sizes): about 250 000 pixels at 960x544, 138 000 at 640x448, against 522 000 for `MSC_FX_GLOW=half` at 960x544. A pass the size ladder already draws at half or less is left alone; one that would come out under 8x8 is not halved. `BeginPass` and the pass memo size passes through the same `PassScaleOf`. **Changes the picture**: host pictures (match60) differ only where the glow is, 2-25 % of the pixels, at most 83 levels at a glow edge in a Mega Strike cut-scene, not told apart side by side; menu frames 1000/1500/1800 identical. Counter `passesHalved` (2.2 a frame over the route). Same shaders. Console A/B: `passhalf` against d1 (menus and Mega Strikes: GPU ms, glow by eye) |
| `MSC_VGX_PASSDOWN=0\|1` | `0`; `1` in the Vita plan (`vita_plan.cpp` kQuality, 12 Nov) | A filter pass whose copy halves it (the glow chain: six passes at 320x224, 160x112, 80x56, 160x112, 320x224, 640x448 EFB units, each copied to a texture of half the size per side by a 2x2 mean) is drawn at half the scale straight into the copy's destination texture, which keeps the size the copy would have made. Eligible (front end `DownPasses`, after the atlas step, so atlas shadows and crowd cells are untouched): not an atlas pass, at most 4 draws of one quad, every sampled texture a copy of this frame and none the destination, the copy exactly 2:1 of the pass rectangle with an identity conversion, or the alpha broadcast of GX format A8 (the glow chain: then every draw must write alpha only, no depth test, no blend or one/zero, and the back end draws the pass with the colour planes enabled as well: the four-texture add's colour stage mirrors its alpha stage and its textures hold alpha in all planes, so colour equals alpha); and (back end `PassScaleOf`) at least 8x8 pixels after halving and `MSC_VGX_PASSDIRECT` on, else the pass and copy run as before. The copy and its scene are not made; `PassCmd::down`, counter `passesDown` (`MSC_VGX_GLSTATS`), the dump marks the pass. Pass memo, dynamic-resolution pass scale and the memo's destination size follow the same `PassScaleOf`. With `MSC_VGX_PASSHALF` also on, PASSDOWN passes are not halved again. **Changes the picture**: the 2x2 mean of four pixels (each four bilinear taps) becomes one pixel of four taps at the destination centre; host pictures (match60, p2b-base against p2b-down, 25 frames, 960x544): 5 frames differ, at most 9 levels (Mega cut-scene frame 2280: 14.6 % of pixels changed, mean 1.6 levels, 623 pixels over 5; goal frames at most 5 and 3), looked at side by side: no visible difference (crops in `/home/claude/s13/w6/passes2/`); synthetic bound `renderer/tests/passdown_error.py`: at most 3 levels on soft glow (about 0.7 % of pixels over 2), 5 on hard bars, mean 0.2-0.7. Saves 6 scenes a frame where the chain is drawn (not in menus, where the pass memo already skips it). Same shaders (no pack change). Console A/B: `passdown` against d1 (replays, celebrations, Mega Strike: GPU ms, glow by eye) |
