| `MSC_VGX_DISCARD_BLEND=0\|1` | `1` on the Vita (plan), `0` in the code (22 Nov sgx16: 61 host pictures and 2,097,152 blend/z cases identical; shader pack re-recorded with it on) | Galaxy r99's `GXV_DISCARD_BLEND` (on there; its r99 defaults table, read from the eboot). An alpha-tested draw that is blended so that a source of (0, 0, 0, 0) leaves the target as it is (GX subtract, or a destination factor of 1, 1 - source alpha or 1 - source colour after the pixel format's mapping, blend not turned off by the source 1 / destination 0 rule), that writes no depth (no z update, or no z compare) and no constant destination alpha gets the key bit `FSK_NODISCARD`: its text keeps the test as `bool vgx_ok = ...` and ends `gl_FragColor = vgx_ok ? vec4(fin) : vec4(0.0)`, with no `discard`. On the SGX a shader with discard makes a punch-through object; without it the draw is an ordinary translucent one. It widens `MSC_VGX_BLENDNODISCARD` (which drops tests that only reject alpha 0) to every test. Exact by the blend's algebra (0 * s + d * 1 = d, colour and alpha); `BinAlphaCut` (`MSC_VGX_BINALPHA`) leaves such draws blended. **Shader text changes for those keys: the pack must be seeded with the switch on (new keys, new texts) before it can be a default**; until then each such program is a cold compile on the console. Log: `[vgx] MSC_VGX_DISCARD_BLEND: fragment program N without discard` (first 64). Test: `renderer/tests/run_discard_blend_test.sh` (every GX blend / z / destination-alpha combination drawn as `BuildRenderState` maps it, in 8-bit fixed point: a zero source never changes the target where the rule takes the draw; the text keeps the discarding text's test expression; `BREAK=1` fails) |
| `MSC_VGX_ASYNC_SHADERS=0\|1` | `1` on the Vita (console A/B `async0`, forced misses `asyncmiss`); `0` elsewhere | Galaxy r101's `GXV_ASYNC_SHADERS` (written again from its eboot's strings; its source after r66 does not exist). A shader the running game meets for the first time after the warm-up frames (`CMD_SHADER`) whose compiled form is in neither the package's pack, the card's cache nor the seed (`vglShaderCacheProbe`) is not compiled on the render thread: a worker thread compiles it into vitaGL's cache (`vglCompileShaderToCache`, vitaGL patch 0027: translated, compiled and written, never registered with the shader patcher; vitaGL's compiler lock, which `glCompileShader` now also takes, keeps one compile at a time). A draw whose shader is not ready: a colour draw of the main scene (not a render-target pass, not the effects target, colour or alpha write on, no copy that reads the EFB into a texture later in the same frame's commands; clear-only, pass, fill and atlas copies do not count) waits at most `MSC_VGX_ASYNC_SHADERS_MAXWAIT_MS` (default `2`, 0..100) and is left out of that frame if the shader is still not ready; every other draw (passes, sources of EFB copies, depth-only draws, warm-ups, pass memos, crowd runs) takes the blocking path: a queued job is taken back and compiled on the render thread as in 1.2, a job the worker is compiling is waited for. A skippable draw whose job is older than 400 ms takes the blocking path too, so nothing is missing for longer than that. When the worker is done the render thread's `glCompileShader` loads the cache file. The warm-up frames (the first 8, the warm-up list) compile on the render thread as before. With the pack every shader is found: no job, no skip, the same vitaGL calls (host pictures identical, `MSC_VGX_ASYNC_SHADERS_SIM=-1`). Render only: no simulation path, no RNG, no input timing. The worker runs on cores 1-2 at the lowest user priority (0xBF); stack `MSC_VGX_ASYNC_SHADERS_STACK_KB` (default `4096`, the render thread's: the Cg compiler runs on it). Without patch 0027 it is off and says so. Log: `[vgx] async shaders: on, ...`, `[vgx] async shaders: worker on cores 1-2 at the lowest priority (0xBF)`, and every 5 s with activity `[vgx] async-shaders-5s: compiled N shaders in the background (failed; ms of compile, longest), draws skipped, longest wait, queue; blocking path taken (must not wait) + (waited too long), waited for the worker's own compile, taken back; new shaders found in the cache (probes ms)`. Not ported: Galaxy's `GXV_ASYNC_SMALL_VERTS` (its meaning is not recoverable from strings) and the per-stage package warm-up `GXV_STAGEWARM_PKG` (this port's warm-up list is global and fully linked at start-up from the package's pack; there is no per-stadium unlinked set to compile in a load). Host test: `renderer/tests/run_async_shaders_test.sh` (the rules, the queue with a real worker, and the real GL back end over the stub GL with `MSC_VGX_ASYNC_SHADERS_SIM=<ms>`: a host-only stand-in for the cache and the worker; `BREAK=1..5` fail; clean under ThreadSanitizer). On the host route most frames end with an EFB copy, so most misses take the blocking path (match60 with every shader missing: 97 misses, 2 draws skipped, 50 blocking); what the worker saves there is only the time between the shader's arrival and its first draw `MSC_VGX_ASYNC_SHADERS_HEAP_MIB=<n>` (hardware audit, default `8`, `0` = no check): the worker compiles only while the C heap has `n` MiB free (`mallinfo` against `_newlib_heap_size_user`); below that its job fails and the draw takes the render thread's own compile, because `vglMalloc` falls back to vitaGL's pools when the heap is full and that allocator is not thread-safe against the render thread. The emulator never shows the real compiler's memory. |
| `MSC_VITA_CORE3_SHADERS=0\|1` | `0` | REFUSED, no effect (r16): the shader compile worker never runs on the fourth core (Galaxy r101's `GXV_CORE3_SHADERS` powered its console off); `EnvOneShaders()` is false, the key does not start the probe, the worker stays on cores 1-2 at the lowest user priority. Kept so an old env file is not an error |
| `MSC_VGX_ASYNC_HEAPONLY=0\|1` | `1` on the Vita (plan), `0` in the code (wave 14) | Galaxy asyncsh31 (`vgx_worker_alloc.h`). The shader compile worker's `vglMalloc` / `vglCalloc` / `vglMemalign` / `vglRealloc` calls (the translator, vitaShaRK and libshacccg allocate through them) take newlib's heap only. vitaGL tries newlib first and then its RAM / PHYCONT / budget / VRAM pools, which the render thread uses without a lock: a compile that reached them while the render thread allocated from the same pool could corrupt it, and `MSC_VGX_ASYNC_SHADERS_HEAP_MIB`'s room check is a snapshot. A block newlib cannot give is NULL: the compile fails, the job is marked failed and the render thread compiles the shader itself (the old path). Other threads get the original call. Link: `-Wl,--wrap=vglMalloc,vglCalloc,vglMemalign,vglRealloc` (`vita.mk`), wrappers in `renderer/vita/vgx_host_vita.cpp`, worker id published by `VgxHostAsyncWorkerPlace`. Host proof: `renderer/tests/run_worker_alloc_test.sh` (fake allocators, two threads; `BREAK=1` mutant caught). Never linked or run on the Vita by its author. Console check: `[vgx] async-shaders-heap:` line (blocks from the C heap, refused, foreign reallocs). `=0` in env.txt: the originals |
| `MSC_VGX_MIDFLUSH=<mode*10000+n>` | wave 14, default `0` (off), Vita only | Galaxy midflush31 (`vgx_midflush.h`). `sceGxmMidSceneFlush` inside the EFB scene hands the GPU the vertex (TA) work recorded so far, so it runs while the render thread still records instead of in series with the scene's pixel phase. Exact by construction (same draws, order, state; the pixel phase still starts at EndScene); the cost is the call and the state sceGxm forgets (the host call `VgxHostMidSceneFlush` waits for texture transfers, keeps the default uniform buffers, zeroes vitaGL's `vgl_gxc` / `vgl_gxs_valid` caches; the next draw takes the scene-start path and gives every uniform block again). Modes: `n` flush after every n-th draw; `10000+n` after n draws at the next program change (forced after max(8, n/2) more); `30000+k` k flushes in the largest scene, chunks from the previous picture's largest EFB scene (>= 24 draws). Gain only where the GPU idles while the render thread records (replays, Vice play); none where the GPU is the limit (The Dump). The GPU timers' busy time falls by the part moved before EndScene: compare frame gap, not `[gpu scenes]`. Log: `[vgx] midflush:` every 5 s. Host proof: `renderer/tests/run_midflush_test.sh` (schedule; mutant caught); the flush itself is never run off the Vita |
| `MSC_AB=<KEY>,<a>,<b>[,<window s>][;<KEY2>,...]` | wave 15 (ab15, after Galaxy's ab27.h), default unset (diagnostic only), up to 6 keys | The in-run A/B harness: ONE console run flips a switch a, b, a, b ... in 6 s windows (a block per key is a (b a) x `MSC_AB_REPEATS` = 4 by default, every b has an a on both sides; `MSC_AB_START_S` 10 s of play first, `MSC_AB_SETTLE_S` 1 s cut out after each flip), so heat, battery and card drift cancel inside the run. The env-file form has no ':' (the test build refuses it); the host also takes `KEY:a,b[:w]`. Only keys in the registry (`renderer/src/vgx_ab.h` kKnobs) can be flipped, each audited as read at every use (a VGX_KNOB word re-worked out at the flip, an EnvInt read per call, or MIDFLUSH and the vitaGL setters VP_EXACT / VGL_MASKKEEP / VGL_MASKBG / VGL_FRAGMEMO / VGL_UNIREUSE made by the render thread at its next scene boundary, and SHADOW_EVERY / REPLAY_FARLOD / FS_REFRESH_PREFETCH answered to the game's own getters); every other key is REFUSED with its reason (`MSC_AB=list` prints both lists). Never flippable: anything that changes simulation state or time, memory layout, threads, a value read once at boot, shader text, a cache's key. Log: `[ab]` lines at the start and at each flip; the `[lane]` line (needs the probe, switched on by MSC_AB itself on the host) is cut at every phase change and ends `ab KEY=v a|b wN` (`ab-settle`, `ab-pre`, `ab-done` are skipped by the reader). Read: `tools/console-1.2/abpair.py --inrun LOG` (b against the a windows beside it, bootstrap over the differences; a neighbour with a draw count 15 % different is another scene and is left out). Unset: one relaxed load in EnvInt, nothing in the knob read path; equal arms read what the environment gave (host test `renderer/tests/run_ab15_test.sh`, mutants 1-3 caught; NP_DET_TRACE seed 1234abcd identical, notes ab15.md) |
| `MSC_EXIT_TRACE=0\|1`, `MSC_EXIT_WATCHDOG_S=<s>` | exit17 (test runs only: the harness's Finish, i.e. a script's `exit`, MSC_MAX_FRAMES, the lab's time limit; a player never runs it), defaults `1` and `30` (0 = watchdog off) | | The end of a scripted run names its steps and has a watchdog. Each step of `Finish()` (script flush, frame-send join, watch/hash lines, exit hooks, statistics, memory report, frame timing, the `[plat]` line, back-end quiesce, final flush) writes `[exit] step N: <name>` straight into the log file (past the sink's 250 ms buffer), so the last such line of a log is the step that did not finish. A watchdog thread (priority 64 on the Vita) gives ONE step `MSC_EXIT_WATCHDOG_S` seconds, then writes `[plat] EXIT STUCK in step <name> after N s (step k): leaving the process` and leaves with exit code 3; the console runner (session.sh) ends the wait at once and calls the run stalled. Why: the 23 Nov console runs d1, discardblend0, heaponly0 and tlsverify1 printed `[script] f=N exit` and then nothing (no final `[watch]`, no reports, no `[plat]` line) for 90 s; with the marks the next such run says where. Code `platform/harness/src/np_exit_watch.h`, host test `platform/harness/tests/run_exit_watch_test.sh` (+3 mutants) and `run_tests.sh` (hung join: step named, exit 3). The player's own quit now ends with `VitaLog::FlushBlocking()` (bounded waits) instead of `Flush()`, which skipped the write while the log writer thread was inside its own |
| `MSC_VGX_TEX_CEILING=<0/1/2>`, `MSC_VGX_TEX_CEILING_KIB=<n>` | wave 14, default `0` (off), diagnostic: the picture is wrong on purpose | Prices texture fetch. Uploads of RGBA8 textures (both sides >= 8, level 0 >= KIB, default 16) are replaced: `1` by one 4x4 average-colour texel block (no mips), `2` by half width and height (level 0 dropped, or a 2x2 box filter when there are no authored mips). Compare GPU scene ms and fps against the baseline at The Dump; the log line is `[vgx] texceiling:`. Not a feature; never default on. |
| `MSC_VGX_TEX_COMPRESS=<0\|1\|ceiling>` (+ `_PSNR` 32, `_PSNR_A` 36, `_MIN` 4096, `_READY` 8, `_BLOCKS` 600, `_APPLIES` 2, `_HOLD_MB` 12, `_MAXERR` 40, `_HEAP_MIB` 24, `_CACHE` stem, `_CACHE_MB` 16) | wave 15, default `0` (off; visible change: the console A/B decides) | Galaxy's `GXV_TEX_COMPRESS`, cut down (`vgx_texcomp.h`, test `run_texcomp_test.sh`). Uncompressed RGBA8 textures (both sides power of two and >= 16, >= 64x64 texels) that the scene draws only in perspective (never orthographic, HUD class, front-end layer, indirect or Z-texture shader) are re-uploaded under the same id as DXT1 (opaque) or DXT5 after `_READY` pictures: 1/8 or 1/4 of the bytes per fetched texel and that much memory back. Quality gate per texture, on levels 0 and 1: RGB PSNR >= 32 dB, no texel off by more than `_MAXERR` (40) in any channel, binary alpha exact, smooth alpha PSNR >= 36 dB, else it stays RGBA8. Long thin textures (aspect over 4:1) are a class of their own and keep the whole chain. Copies of the levels are held only while the free heap is above `_HEAP_MIB` (24) and within `_HOLD_MB`; beyond that a texture is counted as left for the cap and retried when room returns (ceiling mode keeps only the average colour). A replacement the driver refuses (no GPU memory) is not retried this run (`REFUSED UPLOADS`). A torn card index is rewritten before any append; vetoes are written at the end of the picture. The encoder is sliced (`_BLOCKS` 4x4 blocks a picture, render thread, end of picture; no worker thread). Verdicts and payloads are kept on the card in the game's own data folder (`ux0:data/mscharged/texcomp.idx/.dat`, made on the console from the player's own data; version in the header, crc and payload hash checked, torn tail ignored, vetoes for good, `_CACHE=0` none), so later runs only read them. A texture seen in 2D after its replacement is vetoed on the card for the next run. `ceiling` is the diagnostic: the same textures by the same rules become one 4x4 average-colour block (no card, no gate, WRONG PICTURE): the GPU time it saves is the most the switch can save. Log line `[texcomp]` (eligible MiB by class, replaced MiB -> MiB, encode ms). Not with `MSC_VGX_TEX_CEILING`; needs S3TC DXT1+DXT5. |
| `MSC_VITA_CLOCK_DEFER=0\|1\|<ms>` | `1` (1000 ms) | Galaxy r99's `GXV_CLOCK_DEFER` (on there). The start-up clock requests and their measurement (`MSC_VITA_CLOCK_POLICY`, default `max`) are made by a thread above the game's priority 1 s into the process instead of at the very start: the Galaxy port's console, with the same PSVshellPlus profile, measured the core before the plugin's profile held the new process, so the policy asked for 500 (refused) and then 444. `0`: at the start, as before; `2..5000`: that many ms. The `fixed` policy is never deferred; an explicit `MSC_VITA_CPU_MHZ` measures nothing and is not deferred by default, but an explicit `MSC_VITA_CLOCK_DEFER` with it is honoured (clockfix17: `clock444` / `clock444d` use `MSC_VITA_CLOCK_DEFER=1` so the 444 lands after the PSVshellPlus profile; the profile of the test title must not lock 500 during them). The watch, measure and keep threads start from the same place, after the requests. Not the game's simulation (only when the clock is asked for). Log: `clocks: requested 1000 ms into the process (MSC_VITA_CLOCK_DEFER; ...)` after the `clocks:` line. The `[clock] boot:` line reads the clock at the process start, before the requests: it is not the clock after them (read the `clocks: the ARM core measures` 30 s line). Test: `native/platform/vita/tests/test_clock_policy.cpp` (`-DNP_CLOCK_DEFER_BREAK` fails). Console A/B `clockdefer0` |
| `MSC_VITA_STOCK_SIM=1` | off (test only) | Galaxy's `GXV_STOCK_SIM`: exactly one 444 MHz request whatever the core measures (even over a plugin's 500), and the fourth core off (`MSC_VITA_CORE3`, `_SEND` and `_SHADERS` forced to 0 in `ApplyPlan`, before the plan places the mixer and the file workers, and again in `SetUpCpu`; `vita_plan.cpp` `PlaceAudioAndFiles` returns early under the sim; clockfix17), for an A/B on a console that has CapUnlocker and a PSVshellPlus profile. Still deferred 1 s. `MSC_VITA_CPU_MHZ` (a lower clock) wins over it. The `clocks:` line says `MSC_VITA_STOCK_SIM`. Not the simulation. Test `test_clock_policy.cpp` |
| `MSC_GSPLIT=0\|1\|3` | `0` | A diagnostic of logging builds (`np/gsplit.h`, `platform/compat/np_gsplit.cpp`). Phase markers at the task, simulation-step, render-task and frame-send level make one id current on the game thread; the time between two switches goes to the phase that was current (self time). Every 300 pictures one `[gsplit]` line: wall ms per picture of each phase (sim by part: ai, npc, physics, pose; render task by part: characters, stadium, crowd positions / sprites / draw, hud, shadow, fx; particles update / render; end frame, send, skin matrices; audio; task runner; outside the task list), the waits (swap, frame-send join, hand-over to the renderer, pacing: blocked, not CPU) and `sum S of CPU C`, where C is the thread CPU time. A 1 ms sampler thread on another core counts the phase id it sees (`sampler N: name pct ...`); `3` runs without the sampler. Two more lines per window (v2): `[gsplit-phys]` (ms per simulation STEP: collision pair walk, near callbacks, narrow phase by geom-class pair with calls a step, contact joints, net and ball, PreUpdate / PostUpdate by body class, island search, the quick step's preparation / Jacobian / right-hand side / SOR solver / integration; counts a step: geoms, pairs, callbacks, contacts, islands, rows; a census of geoms by class, dynamic / static) and `[gsplit-ai]` (ms per step: team, game and fielder script machines, intercept times, team AI, action, head, animation, controller, goalie, ball; events by part; per picture: begin frame, front end, blend and character draw with character counts). The hand-over waits of the renderer's semaphores count as blocked time. Off: one load and one branch per marker; on: a clock read per marker (tens a picture, about 150 a picture with the v2 physics and AI markers: compare the frame time with the tour). Does not touch the simulation (no random number, no input, no state read). Host proof: `NP_DET_TRACE` and frame hashes equal with it on and off. frame24 (17 Nov): in the forked send path (plan C, `glplatSendFrameForked`) the draw-done / pacing wait of `glxSwapPost` and the 30 fps late wait are now booked as `wait swap` / `wait pace` (they were booked as end-frame `send`, which read 12-20 ms a picture on the console at a held 30 although its CPU is about 2 ms). Diagnostic only: no behaviour change. |
| `MSC_SNAP_PAR=0\|1`, `MSC_CROWD_POS_PAR=0\|1` | `1` on the Vita (plan; `0` elsewhere, and no lanes on the host without `MSC_JOBS=3`) | Wave 9. Job sites on the helper cores (`include/port/parallel.h`, `platform/vita/src/np_port_parallel.cpp` over the job pool `np_jobs.cpp`). `MSC_SNAP_PAR`: `RenderSnapshot::Grab` (end of every simulation step) and `::Blend` (every picture) split the ten characters and the 150 power-ups over the lanes; this code came with 1.2 but the Vita build never defined `NP_HAVE_PARALLEL`, so it never ran on the console (the Makefile now defines it for `TARGET=vita`). `MSC_CROWD_POS_PAR`: `ImpostorManager::UpdatePositions` computes the crowd sprites' view matrices one crowd character per item. Every item writes only its own object; first-use allocations and the one-time lookups (Hammer Bro prop joints, effects texturing) stay on the game thread; a worker takes the caller's FPSCR before its chunks. The simulation's own state is not touched (the grab only copies it); results are bit-identical. Log: `[snappar] MSC_SNAP_PAR=1 (3 lane(s))`, every 1800 parallel pictures `[snappar] snapshot blends on the lanes` / `crowd views`, and the pool's `[jobs]` lines. Host proof: frame hashes and `NP_DET_TRACE` identical with 0 and 1 (`MSC_JOBS=3`) over 30000 pictures at Vice (10174 simulation steps, goals and replays included) and the whole The Dump route (7258 pictures, 2587 steps), the worker lanes taking about a third of the items; `MSC_SNAP_PAR_BREAK=1` / `MSC_CROWD_POS_PAR_BREAK=1` (the worker lanes use a wrong blend factor / up vector) change the hashes. Console A/B `snappar0` (both off) against `gsplit1`. Host measurement switches in the same build (off: one compare per task and per random draw): `MSC_SIMT_PROBE=1` (per task: changes of the simulation checksum and of the network seed, random draws by generator and caller, table in `MSC_SIMT_PROBE_OUT`), `MSC_SIMT_PERTURB=skip\|twice` with `MSC_SIMT_PERTURB_TASKS` (the named drawing tasks skipped on odd pictures / run twice in play: the det trace shows whether the simulation reads what they write; `src/Game/NpSimThread.cpp`, findings in w8/simthread.md). |
| `MSC_VITA_THREAD_CPU=0\|1` | `0` | Galaxy r99's `GXV_THREAD_CPU`, a diagnostic for logging builds. With the 600-frame log line, the CPU time of every live thread since the last such line, summed by core mask: `[threads] frame N: S s, CPU ms by core mask: core 0 only A (of it the game thread G), 1 B, 2 C, 1-2 D, 3 E, other F`, then up to 8 `may run on core 0: "name" mask 0x...: ms` lines for the other threads whose mask allows core 0 (or is the system's default). One `sceKernelGetThreadInfo` per live thread a line, on the game thread |

Galaxy r84 items not ported as new switches (report `galaxy-parity-r84b.md`): its "shadow-volume fill scissoring" is `MSC_VGX_SHQUAD=1` here already (the quad that resolves the shadow volumes is cut to up to six rectangles around the volumes' projected triangles; on the match60 route 83-97 % of its pixels are left out, about 250 000 EFB pixels a frame in open play; `MSC_VGX_SHQUAD=2` is the plain-scissor form). `GXV_EDGE_AA_HUDCUT` has no counterpart: the Vita's present (`MSC_VITA_UPSCALE=combined`) folds its edge term into the same three-fetch pass, so there is no separate smoothing pass to cut, and the present does not know the HUD's rectangles; `MSC_VGX_HUDNATIVE=1` (kit `hudnative`) is the route to an unfiltered HUD.

The storage rule behind Galaxy's r67 crash: on the game thread the global operator new is the game's allocator (`NL/nlMemory.cpp`). In Galaxy that took port-side lists
into a scene heap that was freed under them. Strikers' game never frees an arena (`StandardAllocator` and `VirtualAllocator` live for the run; nothing else is ever made
current), so that crash cannot happen here as such, but port storage on the game thread still lands in arenas sized for the game and moves the game heap's layout with
render-side work. Long-lived port containers that grow on the game thread use `np::HostVector` / `np::HostString` / `NpHostAllocScope` (`np/host_alloc.h`).
The census (`MSC_HEAP_GUARD=2`, host harness, match route, frame send on the game thread: the plan S worst case) found 5,831 game-thread operator new calls in the
whole route, 5,643 of them port code and none of them the renderer (its record chunks and arenas use malloc). Moved to the host heap in 1.3: the AX mail queue
(`ax_mix.cpp`, about a thousand 512-byte deque nodes a match), the latency samples (`np_latency.cpp`, `MSC_LAT`), the EFB-copy retire list (`gx_copytrack.cpp`),
everything the input-script harness keeps or builds per frame (`np_harness*.cpp`; its calls into the game's scene manager stay on the game heap through
`NpGameAllocScope`), and, from the online audit, the ranking rows (`np_dwc_rank.cpp`), the result retry queue (`np_online_queue.cpp`) and the ranked match's
ticket, room and match strings (`np_dwc_match.cpp`, registered with the guard). Tests: `native/tools/heapguard/run_heapguard_test.sh` (the real `nlMemory.cpp`
and `MemAlloc.cpp`; `BREAK=1` fails).

### 1.3 Game-Thread Switches

Strikers had the Galaxy port's "logic overlap" before Galaxy did: in core plan C (`MSC_FS_PIPE=1`, the default) the game thread builds frame N+1 while frame N is sent
on the worker and decoded on the record player, in double-buffered views, packet trees and frame arena, and it joins only at the next fork (`glplatSendFrameForked`). The join
ends when the record player has read frame N (`rec::Present`, which guest data held by reference needs), not when the back end or the GPU has drawn it. Every state
change and teardown waits for the send (the `state` and `unload` gates). So these switches add no new overlap; they remove work the frame does not use. All are read once, have no
Settings row, are on in the Vita build and off in the host harness unless named, and run only on the render path, which already runs a different number of times per
simulation tick on each peer. None draws a random number. No gain has been measured.

| Line | Default | Effect and risk |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `MSC_FS_SNAPONCE=0\|1` | `1` | The send's copy of each packet (parameters, stream list, skin matrices) was made at the attach and again at the fork, where everything was overwritten. It is now made at the fork; the attach takes only the packet. Same bytes for the send (`native/tools/overlap/run_tests.sh` runs the real `glRenderList.cpp`) |
| `MSC_ODE_BROADPHASE=0\|1\|2` | `1` | The pair walk of `dxSimpleSpace::collide`, the hottest loop of the PGO list (about 7,000 pairs a physics tick at about 45 instructions and up to six FPSCR transfers each, about 75 reach the callback). `1`: before the walk the X and Y extents of the enabled geoms go into flat arrays as integers that order exactly as the floats do, and four pairs at a time are skipped by integer compares (`native/src/ode/np_broadphase.h`). Only pairs `collideAABBs` would reject at its bounds test are skipped; the others take the real `collideAABBs`, in the same order. No float arithmetic, so neither NEON's flush-to-zero nor the FPSCR can change a decision. A NaN or denormal extent, a dirty geom or under 8 geoms: the plain walk for that call. A callback that moves, adds, removes or switches a geom bumps `npGeomEpoch` and the rest of the call is the plain walk from that pair, on the live list. `0` plain walk. `2` plain walk plus a check of every skip against the float test, one `ode broadphase:` line each 600 calls (`wrong=` must be 0, `left=` counts calls that left the flat walk). Tests: `native/tools/gamefast/run_broadphase_test.py` (real ODE sources, NEON and C paths under qemu-arm), `tools/det/stepcmp.py` on `NP_DET_TRACE` of a match. Estimate, not measured: 0.3-0.5 ms a physics tick on the Cortex-A9 |
| `MSC_POSE_FAST=0\|1` | `1` | Same bits as the plain code, pose evaluation is read by the simulation (`GetJointPosition`). (a) `BuildNodeMatrices`: a bone with no around-Z weight skipped the sine, cosine and blend at t = 0, keeping the normalisation (`nlQuatRenormalize`; -0, denormal, huge or non-finite components take the plain blend). (b) `nlRecipSqrt`: one integer range test in place of three VFP compares. (c) `SAnimDecodeRot16` and `Rot12` in NEON: a 16-bit integer times 2^-15 is exact, no rounding and nothing near the flush range. Test: `native/tools/gamefast/run_pose_fast_test.py` (qemu-arm, FPSCR.FZ clear and set; every Rot16 lane value, every 24-bit half of a Rot12 key, keys ending a page). Not done on purpose: NEON blending of quaternions in the pose. On this core it gains little and needs a denormal guard on every operand and result (sums keep their order), and the pose is simulation. Estimate, not measured: 0.3-0.4 ms a tick |
| `MSC_SNAP_ONECOPY=0\|1\|2\|3` | `1` on the Vita (plan; `0` in the code and on the host) | Wave 11 (gt16a). `DrawableCharacter::Grab` copied all seven arrays of the simulation's `cPoseAccumulator` into the snapshot slot, among them `m_PrevNodeMatrices` (28 % of the bytes, about 14 KiB a character, ten characters every simulation step). The previous node matrices are read only by the simulation's own accumulator (`Character.cpp` joint velocity, `PhysicsPose.cpp`); no reader of a snapshot's accumulator uses them (the blend builds its own pose from `m_rot` / `m_trans` / `m_scale`, the recorder writes the quaternions / translations / scales, cull and prop code read `m_NodeMatrices`, which is still copied). `1`: `cPoseAccumulator::CopyForSnapshot` without that array (the allocation is unchanged, so the heap layout is too). `2` (proof): the array is filled with 0xFF instead; `3` (proof mutant): a recorded quaternion is nudged. `NP_REPLAY_HASH=1` prints an FNV hash of the whole recorder every 300 steps. Gain: estimate 0.1-0.15 ms a step (not the 0.3-0.45 of the plan: only the one array was provably dead); judged on the console by `gsplit` `snapshot` in the kit run `snap1off` |
| `MSC_RENDER_NEON=0\|1\|2` | `0` | Wave 11 (gt16a). NEON forms (`np/render_neon.h`) of the quaternion product, the quaternion nlerp (including its dot), the vector-matrix product and the vector lerp, used only by the blend of the drawable characters and the node-matrix build of their blended poses (`cPoseAccumulator::BuildNodeMatrices(.., true)`, `BlendRotRender`); the simulation's accumulators keep the scalar code. Same operations in the same order (multiply then add, sign flips for subtractions), so the same bits for every input except a subnormal operand or result, which NEON flushes and the VFP keeps while FPSCR.FZ is clear. `1`: on after a boot self-test (10,000 random inputs a kernel against the scalar functions: `[renderneon] self-test`; a difference on ordinary values turns it off for the run); `2`: verify, the scalar result is used and NEON mismatches are counted (`[renderneon] verify`). Proofs: `tools/gamefast/run_render_neon_test.py` (armhf under qemu-arm, 800,000 random cases with zeros, -0 and tiny values, FZ clear and set: ordinary differences 0, subnormal-case differences 21 with FZ clear and 0 with FZ set, the nudged mutant caught 1000 of 1000). There is no NEON on the host: the host builds run it as 0, so no host picture can show it. OFF until the console run `rneon1` and the emulator verify counters say the pictures do not change. Gain: estimate 0.4-0.8 ms a picture (not measured) |
| `MSC_FS_TLSFAST=0\|1` | `1` | The frame-send worker flag (`NpFsOnWorker`, read about 3,000 times a picture by `glplatFrameAlloc`, the heap hooks and the shadow-light lookups) was a `thread_local`; on the Vita (clang, emulated TLS) each read is `__emutls_get_address` -> `pthread_getspecific` -> `pte_osTlsGetValue` -> `__getreent_for_thread` -> `sceKernelGetTLSAddr`, about 90 instructions, seven calls and a `dmb ish`. Now a small table keyed by the thread's TLS slot address (one cheap call): same answers by construction (a thread that never ran a job reads 0). `MSC_FS_TLSVERIFY=1` compares each answer with the thread-local and prints `[fs] tlsfast MISMATCH` once. Unit test: `fscheck` in all modes with verify on, and a BREAK build (`-DNP_FS_TLSBREAK`) that must report. Cost on the game thread by estimate 1.0 to 1.5 ms a picture (host call counts times ARM instruction counts, not measured). |
| `MSC_PERSP_TAN=0\|1` | `1` | `C_MTXPerspective` called the double `tan()` 60 to 70 times a picture with a handful of distinct angles. A 16-entry cache keyed by the exact angle bits returns the stored float result (the same value the library gives; tested against the library on 20 million angles including denormals and -0). About 0.1 ms. |
| `MSC_TLS_FAST=0\|1` (+ `MSC_TLS_FAST_VERIFY=1`) | `1` | wave 13 cpuexact13. Every `thread_local`, `errno`, malloc lock and `pthread_getspecific` on the Vita went through `__emutls_get_address -> pthread_getspecific -> pte_osTlsGetValue -> vitasdk_get_tls_data -> __getreent_for_thread -> sceKernelGetTLSAddr(0x89)` (about 90 instructions, a 136-byte frame, a `dmb`). `np_fasttls.cpp` wraps `__getreent`, `pte_osTlsGetValue` and `__emutls_get_address` at link time (`-Wl,--wrap=`, vita.mk) and reads the slot as TPIDRURO + offset; the pthread-key list walk and the emutls array peek are the library's own logic (`np_fasttls.h`, host test `tests/run_fasttls_test.sh`: 16 M random cases equal to a transcription of the library code, mutant caught). Start-up self-check (`[tls]` line): offset from the real call, 10 more keys, the reent pointer, a pthread key on this and a second thread, the emutls key learned from one real call and both probe objects compared; any failure leaves the wraps forwarding. `np_framesend` thread token uses the same slot. Verify mode compares every call with the library's (`[tls] MISMATCH`). Console-only: the Vita path cannot run on the host. |
| `MSC_TICKS_FAST=0\|1` | `1` | wave 13 cpuexact13. `np_os_ticks_from_ns` (every OSGetTick/OSGetTime, ~115 a picture) did two 64-bit divides (software `__aeabi_uldivmod`). For the game's bus clock ticks = floor(ns*243/4000), done as a multiply, a shift and three 32-bit constant divides for ns < 2^50; other clocks or larger ns take the original. Host test `native/platform/vita/tests/run_ticks_fast_test.sh`: 430 M values (random, every step boundary, second boundaries, powers of two), 0 differences from the original formula and from exact 128-bit arithmetic; mutant caught. |
| `MSC_FPBITS=0\|1` | `1` | wave 13 cpuexact13. The pose code's hottest float compares (`nlQuatNLerp` dot>0, `BlendRot*` and `BuildNodeMatrices` weight tests) as integer tests on the bits (`np/fpbits.h`): no `vcmp`+`vmrs`. `native/tests/test_fpbits.cpp` runs every helper over all 2^32 bit patterns against the float expression: 0 mismatches (IEEE mode). With denormals flushed the only differences are denormal operands; the Vita runs FZ=1 and every operand is a computed value, so none occurs. |
| `MSC_VITA_FPSCR=0\|1\|2` | `0` | Only logs `[fpscr]` (what the main thread starts with) unless set: `1` flush-to-zero, `2` flush-to-zero and default NaN, applied to the main thread (the frame-send worker copies the game thread's control bits at each job). A denormal operand then reads as 0: normal values are untouched, so only a denormal-producing path changes; the host cannot model it (SSE keeps denormals), hence off until a console run shows a gain. |

Saving (callgrind, Vice pictures 3000-3060, plan env, all threads of the process): 425.4 M -> 402.5 M host instructions, -22.9 M = -382 k a picture (-5.4 %: `nlQuatNLerp` 23.2 -> 14.7 M, `nlQuatRenormalize` 8.5 -> 6.5 M, `PSQUATDotProduct` / `nlQuatDot` / `nlQuatScale` 19 M -> 0). The flat walk of `dxSimpleSpace::collide` also holds the Z extent keys, the category and collide bits and the body of each enabled geom (`NpBpExtra`, `NpBpNextFull` in `np_broadphase.h`: four more integer compares, two `vtst` and one `vceq` per four geoms on NEON), so it skips the pairs `collideAABBs` returns from before it calls anything: both geoms on one non-zero body (the 11 bone geoms of a character), `(cat1 & col2) || (cat2 & col1)` zero, or disjoint Z extents. Measured on the host (callgrind, a step): 884 `collideAABBs` calls for 71 callbacks, the other 800 or so are these. The survivors are the same pairs in the same order, so the callbacks, contacts and joints are the same. Same keyability rules as the X / Y keys (NaN and denormal extents: plain walk for that call); only where `unsigned long` is 32 bits. `MSC_ODE_BROADPHASE=2` checks every decision against the plain tests (`wrong=` must be 0); `MSC_ODE_BPFULL_BREAK=1` is the proof mutant. Tests: `native/tools/gamefast/np_bpfull_test.cpp` (NEON under qemu-arm and the C path against the reference predicate on 5 M random pairs, wrong 0, mutant caught), host game runs with `NP_DET_TRACE` and frame hashes. `2` (wave 11, gt16b): the geoms of one character are consecutive in the list, so the flat walk cuts the list into runs (three or more consecutive enabled geoms on one non-zero body, `NpBpSegs`, `NpBpBuildSegs`, `NpBpNextSeg` in `np_broadphase.h`) with the union of their integer keys (min of the lows, max of the highs, OR of the category and of the collide bits) and steps over a whole run when the geom's own body is the run's, its box misses the union box on any axis, or its category / collide bits can pass against no member; otherwise the members are walked with the same test as `1`. A member is skipped only where its own test would reject it, so the survivors are the same pairs in the same order (unit test: 15.4 M random pairs with bodies in runs, wrong 0, the union-box mutant gives wrong 16,348). `MSC_ODE_BPFULL_BREAK=2` is its proof mutant (every second run's union box is too small); `MSC_ODE_BROADPHASE=2` checks every decision of `1` and `2` (`wrong=` must be 0; `runskipped=` counts the geoms stepped over in the last call). Host proof (w8/gt16b.md): match60, The Dump (fast-boot route) and Lava Pit (items), 5004 pictures each, det trace and all frame hashes identical to both switches off; `BREAK=1` and `=2` change the trace (and are counted by the verify mode); on The Dump 68 % of the geom pairs of the flat walks are in stepped-over runs. For the first blend into a node `acc = 0 + w = w` exactly and `w / w = 1.0f` exactly (IEEE), so `1` uses `1.0f` when the accumulated weight was 0 and `w` is finite; the following arithmetic is unchanged. `2` verify: also divides and counts a difference (`[phys27div]`, wrong must be 0); `MSC_PHYS27_DIV_BREAK=1` is the proof mutant. Counters are plain integers (approximate beside the pose lanes). |
