From Galaxy, these do not apply here: the J3D material and display-list passes and the J3D skinning (Strikers uses NL/Render; its `nlMultMatrices` is already the NEON `PSMTX44Concat`,
and players are skinned on the GPU); the far-animation pass (players are already blended once per picture from two simulation ticks, so the nearest match is `MSC_POSE_CULL`); FIFO write
batching (records go to `rec::Reserve`, not to a write-gather pipe); and `GXV_AUDIO_HOLD` (audio runs on its own host thread and core, not on the game thread's core between its safe points).

### The freeze button (galaxyport18, from Galaxy c164a / NFS MW 1.6)

For a game that has stopped answering (a hung match, a goal cut that never ends, a load that does not finish) when the Vita has no PC to read the log from. Hold L + R + Select together for 3 seconds, continuously.
Rule (`native/platform/vita/src/np_freeze_button.h`, host test `tests/test_freeze_button.cpp` with mutants by `tests/run_freeze_button_test.sh`): polled every 100 ms by a thread of priority 191 that touches no game
state; the first 20 s of the process are ignored (that covers the settings screen's own L + R look at start-up, 160 ms right after the logo); a sample counts only with all three down and the settings screen closed
(`NpSettingsScreen::IsOpen()`); any sample without all three resets the hold; it fires at the first sample 3.0 s after the hold began (2.9 s of samples does not).
Accidental use: the pad maps L to the pass / lob button, R to the sprint (GC L) and Select to GC Z (`np_controls.cpp` Mapper::Map: Select is Z, which the game's controller statistics count as new presses: the Shakedown mash, `np_game_input.cpp:414`; the held-R sprint and a held L are the only buttons of the three that a
player keeps down); no action found in the code uses L + R + Select together, so the chord was kept as Galaxy has it (read from code only, not tried on a console).
What it does, in this order: (1) one line `[fatal +<ms> ms, frame <n>] FATAL: freeze button (L+R+Select held 3 s) at +<ms> ms, frame <n>, task state 0x.., task frame <n>` to `ux0:data/mscharged/log.txt` in every
build (the basic record: written straight to the card, not counted against the 64 fatal lines), followed by one `freeze thread <name> status 0x.. wait type 0x.. id 0x.. priority .. cores 0x.. cpu .. ms` line for every
thread of the process (status 0x4 waiting, 0x1 running, 0x2 ready; the wait id names the semaphore / event / mutex it sleeps on) and `freeze: N threads listed`. A logging build's log.txt has the last `[hitch]` / `[late]` /
`[stall]` lines just above (a quiet player build prints none of those lines, and the log's own lock is not taken here because the hung thread may hold it). (2) `FreezeTrap` (`np_vita_crash.cpp`): the kubridge exception
handlers are released (`kuKernelReleaseExceptionHandler`, which in kubridge's source clears the per-type handler so the exception goes to `defaultExceptionHandler`, i.e. the system) and `__builtin_trap()`
raises an undefined-instruction exception, which the system turns into its core dump `ux0:data/psp2core-<date>-<time>-eboot.bin-<title id>.psp2dmp` (`vita-parse-core` with the build's .elf names every thread's
stack). What the owner sees: the picture stops, the system's own "the application has unexpectedly quit" notice, the LiveArea; afterwards the log's FATAL lines and the psp2core file. If kubridge is absent the trap
goes to the system at once; if the release did not take, `CrashHandler` sees the freeze flag and does `FlushBlocking` + `sceKernelExitProcess(0)`: the FATAL lines but no dump and no fatal screen. The core plan guard is NOT
told (`VitaPlan::Failed` is not called): a button press is the owner's act, and the next start should run the same plan so that the freeze can be repeated. NOT run on a console: that the dump is written
is the kit17 check `freezebutton` (console-1.2 run line in w8/galaxyport18.md).

### Copy-record guard and the preset gate test (galaxyport18, Galaxy replay36 / c171-c172)

`renderer/src/vgx_copyguard.h`: every read of a `CMD_COPY_TEX` record on the render thread (`vgx_backend_gl.cpp`, 30 sites) goes through `vgx::copyguard::At(buf, arg)`: one compare when the record exists; an arg past the end of
`buf.copyTex` reads a dead record (skip set, no texture) instead of memory past the vector, is counted (`vgx::copyguard::Count()`) and logs `[copyguard] ...` for the first four. No switch, nothing seen when nothing dangles. Test:
`renderer/tests/copy_guard.cpp` + `run_copy_guard_test.sh` (mutants). The preset gate test: `np_picture_presets.h` `kGates` lists, for each ladder / child key, the gate key it needs on or off; `tests/test_settings.cpp`
fails for any picture mode (the presets of the screen, the `MSC_PICTURE` lists, every row's choice on its own unless the screen dims it) that sets a child whose gate is off in that column; `tests/run_preset_gate_mutants.sh` proves it
with ten mutants. A new ladder key goes into `kGates`.

### Feel (wave 6): pad to picture, the Mega Save hand, the camera

Analysis and the latency table: `/home/claude/s13/w6/feel.md` (a model; nothing has run on a console). All render-side or input-mapping-side; none changes a simulation step, its dt or a random
number. Host test: `native/platform/sdk/tests/test_feel.cpp` (`g++ -std=c++17 -I native/platform/sdk/input`, 28 checks).

| Line | Default | Effect and risk |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `MSC_MEGA_CURVE=<p>` | `1.5` | The Mega Save hand of a person's live pad: one radial dead zone (`MSC_MEGA_DEAD`, `0.04` of what the stick's own 16/102 zone leaves) and a power curve instead of the per-axis 0.2 zone on top of the stick's. Full deflection keeps the old top speed (3x glove: `MSC_MEGA_POINTER_SPEED`); a 0.3 stick moves at 0.14 of it (was 0.30), 0.5 at 0.33, 0.8 at 0.70. The right stick drives the hand from its first unit of deflection (it used to hand over to the left stick below 0.2). `1` linear, `0` the old formula. Scripts, menus and the picture harness keep the old formula. Console A/B `megacurve` |
| `MSC_MEGA_TRAP=0\|1` | `1` | The hand moves by the mean of the previous and current velocity times dt (trapezoid) instead of the current one (forward Euler, which leads the stick by half a picture of travel while it is held: 17 ms at 30, 8 ms at 60; 32 and 16 canvas px at 3x). Position at a sample = integral of the stick to it at any picture rate (host test: error 0.0003 against 0.034 at 15 Hz). A press or release edge inside one interval stays uncertain by up to a picture of travel |
| `MSC_CAM_INTERP=0\|1` | `1` | `GameplayCameraZoomLevel::CalcDesiredTarget`: with a player on the ball the camera aimed at the owner's SIMULATION position (steps every 20 ms), with a free ball at the picture's blended ball, while every player is drawn from the blended snapshot: a stepping aim point against interpolated players, and a jump when the ball changed hands. `1`: the owner's position from the same blended snapshot. The camera's one reach into the simulation, the remap angle, is captured once per step and sent with the input, so peers are unaffected. `0` the game's own |
| `MSC_REAR_LATCH=0\|1` | `1` | Wave 24 (feel24, X4). The back panel is read through its last four samples, not only the newest: a finger that lands and lifts wholly between two reads (so in no read's newest sample) is reported once, for one read (`NpControls::PickLostContact`, np_controls.h), the way the front panel's tap latch has done since 23 Oct. Only a NEW finger (its id not in the previous read) in a sample newer than the previous read's newest, with the newest sample empty, within `MSC_TAP_GAP_MS` of the previous read: a finger that was already reported and lifts is not shown twice, so no tap is delayed. The rear taps (switch player, cycle item) become input the same way as any tap, so a recorded or networked game carries them as input. Log line `[touch] rear: N short touches recovered` (first, then every 50th): a run with rear taps at 30 or 45 shows how often the old read lost one. `0`: the single newest sample. Also: the front panel's size is asked once, not at every poll (exact). |
| `MSC_STICK_DEAD=<4..32>`, `MSC_STICK_CURVE=<1..2>` | `16`, `1` (off) | Wave 24 (feel24, X10). The LEFT stick (movement, pass and shot aim) on the Vita: the radial dead zone in raw units (default 16, 12.6 % of travel) and a power curve on the deflection (1 = linear, the old mapping; 1.2 makes small deflections finer, full travel still gives 72). With neither set the mapping is the old one bit for bit (test: `native/platform/vita/tests/test_feel24_input.cpp`, all 65536 raw positions). The value is mapped before the game captures it, so a replay and a peer receive the result as the player's own input: multiplayer-safe; a feel change for the player who sets it. The right stick and the menus' stick thresholds are not changed except through the left stick. Not in the Settings screen. |
| `MSC_TAP_GAP_MS=<ms>` | `250` | `LatchTaps`: when the newest pad sample is more than this after the previous read's (pause, Home-button suspend, a loading stall) nothing older is OR-ed in, so a press from before the gap does not come back. `0` the old rule |
| `MSC_PAD_LATE=1` | off | The late latch (np_fps.cpp `NpFpsPadLateWait`, np_feel.h `PadLate`): under a held cadence (30, or 60) the read and the step that uses it are put off by the slack the picture would wait anyway (GPU done to refresh, and the game thread's own wait), at most `MSC_PAD_LATE_MAX_US` (12000), margin 3 ms, back to 0 for 120 pictures after a picture a period late. Single player only; never in a LAN or online session, in paced AUTO (swap mode 3) or in the game's own policy. Model: press to screen 66.7 -> 54.9 ms at held 30, 33.4 -> 21.6 at held 60. Not measured: console A/B `padlate` |
| `MSC_PHASE_SYNC=1` | off | (pace10) The late start of `MSC_PAD_LATE` with the Galaxy port's fault attribution (np_fps_phase.h `PhaseSync`, np_fps.cpp `NpFpsPadLateWait`; `MSC_PHASE_SYNC_MAX_US` 12000, `_MARGIN_US` 2500, `_WINDOW` 90). Same slack inputs (display slack and the game thread's own idle time, each without the delay in force); the differences: a late picture with no delay in force, or with a delay well inside the margin, is a spike and is not charged to the delay (PadLate zeroed and held off 120 pictures after ANY late picture, so it was off most of the time at the 1-3 % late pictures of the console), a late picture the delay may have caused halves it and holds the rise for 12 pictures instead of zeroing it, the margin widens by half a step after repeated charged lates and heals after 600 clean pictures. The picture's slot, the steps and every dt are untouched (the sleep is inside the frame, as with PadLate). Host model (tools/fps/pace10_unit.cpp, estimates): press-to-picture within -1 to +1 ms of the plan's PAD_LATE at Vice and The Dump, because the delay is quantized by whole refreshes; the log line `[fps] phase sync:` (every 1200 steps) shows the share of steps delayed, the mean delay and the late pictures charged. Console A/B first: `MSC_PHASE_SYNC=1` on d1 and worstgpu1. |
| `MSC_LAT_SAMPLE=1` | off | (press16, np_lat16.h, np_fps.cpp `Lat16Wait`) The pad read aligned to the controller's own samples, replacing the continuous delay of `MSC_PAD_LATE` / `MSC_PHASE_SYNC` while it is on: before the step that reads the pad the thread polls the controller (one-sample Peek, 0.3 ms steps) for the next sample and reads right after it, within the room the picture really has (smallest display-callback entry to screen time of the last 24 pictures, which includes the gate's own waits, less 3 ms, and the thread's idle time); no wait when the arrival does not fit. 30 pictures of back-off after a late picture. Single player only (refused in LAN/online, free runs, locked cut-scenes). Simulation untouched. Knobs: `MSC_LAT_SAMPLE_MARGIN_US` (3000), `_CAP_US` (18000), `_STEP_US` (300). Host test hooks `MSC_LAT_SAMPLE_SIM=1`, `_SIM_PHASE_US`, `_SIM_SLACK_US`. Log: `[lat16]` every 300 pad reads. |
| `MSC_LAT_PADAGE=1` | off | (press16) Only the `[lat16]` line: the age of the controller sample at the game's pad read (estimate from the stamps, a lower bound until a find is seen) and the controller period; no change of behaviour. |
| `MSC_LAT_SLACK_LB=1` | off | (press16) `MSC_PAD_LATE` / `MSC_PHASE_SYNC` take the room of a finished picture from the display callback's entry (np_latency.cpp `NpLatEntered`, `NpLatSlackLbTake`) instead of the gate's exit: the old figure never exceeds one refresh (the gate has waited by then), so the late latch is starved of the 2-3 refreshes the picture really sits finished. More delay under the same 3 ms margin and back-off: watch `[fps] late latch` and the late-picture share. |
| `MSC_LAT_DEPTH_PACED=0..3` | `1` in code, `2` in the Vita plan since wave 9 (an aligned free run keeps 4 frames in flight: 59.2 pictures a second against 52.3 at a GPU of 1.0 refresh, 4.3 ms more delay; `pace_mid_model depth`; A/B `latdepthp1`) | The frames more than `MSC_LAT_DEPTH` that paced AUTO (`MSC_FPS_BETWEEN=pace`) may keep on their way after the hand-over (was hard-coded 1): in a pipeline-bound scene every frame in flight is a frame of button delay. `0` is the A/B against the throughput the model found with 1 |
| `MSC_STICK_DEAD=<raw>`, `MSC_STICK_FULL=<raw>` | `16`, `102` | The stick's radial dead zone and full-deflection point (input_native.cpp `map_stick`, and its inverse for scripts). Defaults are the old values; for a console A/B of the Vita's small throw (14 / 96 is the one to try) |

### Cadence (wave 7): a steady picture rate in the scenes that cannot hold 60

Analysis: `/home/claude/s13/w7/cadence.md`. Render and pacing side only; no simulation step, random number or input record changes. Host tests: `native/tools/fps/run_fps_unit_test.sh` (`cadence_unit.cpp`, 37 checks, four deliberate breaks each caught), `native/tools/fps/run_pace_tests.sh` (`pace_mid_model.cpp`, the fast-start checks). Every millisecond is an estimate; nothing has run on a console.

| Line | Default | Effect and risk |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `MSC_CROWD_VISFAST=0\|1\|2` | Vita plan `1`, code `0` | `1`: UpdateCrowdVisibility appends, per layout and sprite, figure slot lists built once (rebuilt when a figure is placed or reset, or the sideline numbers change) instead of testing every figure; the filter's position tests and its camera test are split so the camera test runs once. The same lists in the same order: `2` runs the game's loop too and counts differences (0 in 1200 checks on the host, match60 to picture 4000; det trace and all 4005 frame hashes identical). About 0.3 M game-thread instructions a picture less in the goal celebration (3.12 to 2.83 M, measured with the other crowd switches). |
| `MSC_CROWD_GT=0..7` | Vita plan `7` (int26; was `5`), code `0` | Game-thread cost of the crowd's sprite refresh (w8/gt10.md; render only: no random number, nothing the simulation reads). Bit `1`: a sprite's view matrices (a perspective, a look-at and an inverse, 65 sprites a picture) are made where the sprite is captured, from the picture's camera, instead of for every sprite on every picture (a suspended sprite's view is not recorded; under MSC_CROWD_POS_PAR this also drops the lane call). Bit `4`: also no view for a captured sprite that has no figures to show. Both leave every picture identical (match60, 5004 frame hashes and det trace equal to MSC_CROWD_GT=0; the break mutant changes 957 hashes). Bit `2` (on in int26, console 6241/6273): the sprites of one texture (one model seen from 4 angles) are captured on the same picture, so the animation pose, node matrices and skin pose are made once per texture per refresh; a sprite is still captured once in the refresh period but its animation phase moves by up to one picture, so the crowd's pixels differ. `MSC_CROWD_GT_BREAK=1` is the proof mutant (lazy views from the previous picture's camera). The `[crowdgt]` line at exit counts views skipped/made, poses, sprites captured. |
| `MSC_POSE_JOBS=0..3` | Vita plan `1`, code `0` (wave 12, w8/crowd24.md; host proof on Vice and The Dump: det trace and every frame hash identical to off, with the job lanes and with `MSC_JOBS=1`; the break mutant `MSC_POSE_JOBS_BREAK=1` (host only: compiled out of the Vita build since 18 Nov) changes 1321 (bit 1) and 1488 (bit 2) hashes) | The poses of crowd and decorative-NPC models are made on the job lanes (`PortParallelFor`, the pool of `np_jobs.cpp`) before the game thread builds their packets. Bit `1`: in `ImpostorManager::UpdateSprites` the models the capture loop will draw (a model once, whatever its number of angle sprites) get the animation evaluate, `BuildNodeMatrices` and the skin mesh's `Pose`, and the captured sprites' view matrices (`UpdateView`) are made in the same fork; the loop then skips the evaluate and draws each sprite with `RenderSamePose` (a model with no pose job takes the old path). Bit `2`: the same for the NPCs `WorldNPCManager::Render` will draw (exact too, but 8 models are about 20 k instructions, less than a fork costs on the console: off in the plan). Each item writes only its own model's accumulator and skin mesh and reads constants, so the matrices are the same bit for bit; the impostor models' two-buffer previous-matrix swap only decides which buffer holds the pose (nothing but `Character.cpp`, with its own accumulator, reads the other). With no worker lane (`MSC_JOBS=1`) the old serial path runs. About 0.28 M instructions a picture of pose work (378 k a fork, 45 of 60 pictures) leave the game thread's own share on the host (callgrind, Vice, 60 pictures; the console splits the items about evenly over its 3 lanes, a fork costs ~80 us there). `[posejobs]` line counts models and forks. |
| `MSC_NPC_EVAL_LAZY=0\|1` | Vita plan `1`, code `0` (wave 12; exact on the host, same proof) | `WorldNPCManager::Update` (every picture, in `UpdateStadium`) does not evaluate an NPC the render filter hides (under the gameplay camera only the models above the ground are drawn: half of the 16 at Vice); nothing reads a hidden NPC's pose (`MSC_NPC_LAZY` rests on the same fact). If the filter lets it through at the draw (the camera type changed in between) the draw evaluates it. An evaluate at the draw for every model was tried first and was not exact: an animation change between the update and the draw (a goal cheer, a cut) showed one picture early (2-6 pictures of the goal routes differed). 0.08 M instructions a picture at Vice (host callgrind). |
| `MSC_CROWD_GOV=0\|1` (+ `MSC_CROWD_GOV_FORCE=0..5`, host proof only) | Vita plan `1`, code `0` (crowd25, w8/crowd25.md; owner 17 Nov 22:19 and 22:23: cheaper crowd animation is fine when needed, down to the bare minimum if subtle and seamless) | The adaptive crowd-cost governor (`np_crowdgov.h`, unit test `tools/fps/crowdgov_unit.cpp` with four mutants). In live play, from the pictures' game-thread busy time (frame length less waits), the cadence's period and the renderer's lanes: a picture is over when busy > 87 % of the period (14.5 ms at 60, 29 ms at 30) and the game thread is the longest lane (a GPU- or back-end-bound picture is never counted). 20 % of a 1 s window over: one level down (2 s apart); room (the window plus the LEARNED saving of the levels given back leaves under 3 % over): back at once, as many levels as fit; a give-back followed by a step down within 12 s doubles the hold (10, 20, 40, 60 s) and gives back one level at a time. Six levels (`np/crowdgov.h` table): each crowd MODEL (one pose clock for all the angles' sprites that show it) has a size class (mean of its sprites' nearest-figure heights, 8 samples, a quarter of the sprites a picture; classes 1/2/3 at 110/55 lines: at the gameplay camera the nearest figure of a sprite is 64-80 lines in 85-100 % of the sprites, so a split by the nearest figure of a model, or the 40/20 lines of `MSC_CROWD_SIZED`, would put every model in class 1) and its own level that follows the governor one notch at a time (one model in 12 a picture; far first going down, near first coming back); the model's sprites are redrawn together at the game's interval times 1.5 .. 10 (level 5 about 1.5 s for middle and far). The model's animation clock only moves when its sprites are redrawn, by at most 0.25 s a time (what is banked beyond 1.5 s is dropped: the animations are cycles), so a slowed or frozen model shows the pose its clock holds and resumes from it, with no snap to the live pose and no extra RNG draw; an animation change (`PlayAnimation`) lets the crowd run free for 48 pictures. The pose jobs (`MSC_POSE_JOBS`) only pose the models that are due. Render side only: `NP_DET_TRACE` identical. Counters: `[crowdgov]` level changes and seconds at each level. Kit run `crowdgov0` is the A/B. |
| `MSC_AI_AVOID=0..7` | Vita plan `3`, code `0` (wave 11, w8/gt16b.md: det trace and all frame hashes identical to off on match60, The Dump and Lava Pit; bit 1 avoids 19-25 % of the AVL lookups and its mutant `BREAK=2` changes the trace on all three; bit 2 is reached only with item objects (Lava Pit, `powerup=10`: 6,358 base `IsWithinRange` calls, 6,335 square roots avoided, verify wrong 0) and its mutant `BREAK=1` changes the trace there) | AvoidController::Update (the avoidance loop over about 14 avoidable objects for each of 8 fielders a step), simulation-exact (w8/gt10.md). Bit `1`: CanAddAvoidance's pure tests (not myself, does this fielder avoid that kind of thing) run before the AVL lookup of the tracked avoidances; the tests that can draw a random number (a polygon's IsWithinRange) are still reached only for an untracked object that passed every earlier test, in the old order. Bit `2`: the base `AvoidableObject::IsWithinRange` (powerups, patches, points; fielders and goalies use the cached pair-distance table and polygons their own override, both untouched) rejects a pair whose squared distance exceeds (radii + range)^2 * 1.000244 before the emulated square root (the root is within 2^-22 of the true one, so a pair that might be in range always takes the exact code). Bit `4` (with 2): also runs the exact code and counts a reject it would not have made (`verify wrong`, must be 0). `MSC_AI_AVOID_BREAK=1` is the proof mutant of bit 2 (reject at half the distance), `=2` the one of bit 1 (an already tracked object is added again). The `[aiavoid]` line at exit counts tests, lookups avoided, base calls, square roots avoided. |
| `MSC_CROWD_SIZED=0\|1`, `_SIZED_PX`, `_SIZED_MAX`, `_SIZED_CLUSTER`, `MSC_CROWD_LAZYVIEW` | `0` | Off: a crowd sprite is redrawn every 1st, 2nd or 3rd picture by the size of the nearest figure that shows it (steps in time: 1/2/3 at 60 pictures a second). In the goal celebration of match60 every sprite's nearest figure is over 80 lines, so it saves nothing there; wide replay shots untested. Needs strips of consecutive frames before it can be on (owner's rule: never seen engaging). `np/crowdcut.h` |
| `MSC_CADENCE=0\|1` | Vita plan `1`, code `0` | AUTO only, not LOCKED 60, not in a LAN or online session, and only with `MSC_FPS_REPLAY` at its default (`fit`). A replay and a cut-scene (the goal celebration, the other NIS) start held at a locked 30 from their cut (policy 30: every picture two refreshes, the display gate, the 50 Hz simulation blended to the picture's time as before) and stay so until they end; the verdict never changes inside a shot. The class verdict is learned: `MSC_CADENCE_VISITS` (2) consecutive visits whose frames (95 %) all fit 60 at `MSC_CADENCE_FIT` (0.85) of a refresh release it (60 from the cut), a released class that the controller then took to 30 is held again and needs twice as many visits (at most `MSC_CADENCE_VISITS_MAX`, 8). At the cut back to open play the policy that was in force before the scene (60, or AUTO's free run) is put back at once (`Auto::Resume`, `Between::Resume`): without it the controller's cool-down kept open play at 30 for 6 s or more after every replay. A goal in play makes the controller wait for its cut (4 s at most). Open play is never held by it. `[cadence]` lines at each cut and a summary line. Risk: a class that would hold 60 on the console stays at 30 until two fit visits |
| `MSC_PRESENT_SYNC_START=0\|1` | Vita plan `1`, code `0` | Picture alignment starts at 3 vblanks of latency instead of 4 and tightens in 32-picture windows (about a second a step) until the first raise or a window that did not lower it, instead of 180 pictures (3 s) a step: the start of play ran 4 vblanks (67 ms) behind for 6 to 9 s. A raise ends the quick descent for good. The settled value was already carried across scene changes (L is never reset by a scene) |
| `MSC_FX_ATTACH=0\|1` | Vita plan `1`, code `0` | The particles of an emitter whose position is the live simulated ball trail (`UpdateEmitterFromBallTrail`) are DRAWN offset by the difference between the trail's drawn (blended) position and its simulated one: weight 1 for the newest particle, none at `MSC_FX_ATTACH_TAPER_MS` (40) of age; a difference of 1.5 m or more is a reset and is ignored. Emission, counts and random rolls are not touched (the emitter keeps the simulated position). Character emitters already read the blended pose (ParticleUpdateTask runs after the snapshot blend; every character callback re-points the emitter at the drawn pose), so the ball trail is the one found reading the live state |
| `MSC_DL_WARM=0\|1` | off (code and plan) | Front end only. The lists a goal's scenes call for the first time (about 150 display lists, 120k vertices, the first picture of the celebration at about five times a normal picture) are remembered by identity (list, descriptor, array bindings, skin arrays) per scene class during the first 120 frames of a celebration and of a replay; at the next goal in play (`NpFpsNoteGoal`) the ones that have no entry are built again by the same code, at most `MSC_DL_WARM_VERTS` (8000) vertices a picture, recording nothing. The first goal of a session builds as before. `[dlwarm]` lines. Held off: on the host (match60, 6000 pictures, two goals) the det trace is byte-identical but two pictures (5388 and 5486, 65 and 163 pictures after the warm) differ from the run without it, with different draw and vertex counts (667 against 682 draws), and the cause is explained (below, MSC_DL_KEEP): a list that is already a static buffer draws through the static path, one built at its call through the arena merge, the same vertices; with static buffers off (`MSC_VGX_STATICBUF=0`) every hash is identical; the second goal's first picture built 168 lists without it, 39 with it | Not needed once `MSC_DL_KEEP` keeps the lists.
| `MSC_DL_KEEP=<MiB>` | Vita plan `24`, code `0` | Front end only. The display-list cache let go of every entry 600 use-frames (10 s) after its last use, so each goal built the celebration and replay lists again (host, 9000 pictures, three goals: 187 and 161 lists built in one picture at goals 2 and 3; the walk-out and kick-off lists of the first goal are swept just before it). With the value above 0 the due entries are kept while they add up to that many MiB (decoded vertices in the heap plus static buffers; oldest go first, at most 24 a sweep of 120 frames, never all at once); an entry kept is judged by its generations at its next call like any other. Measured on the host: the due entries peak at 19.4 MiB, 11.2 MiB of decoded vertices in the heap (the heap peaks at 67-71 of 96 MiB without it, so up to about 82 with it) and 8.3 MiB in static buffers; the cache of the entries in use is not counted (27-55 MiB on the host harness). With a 12 MiB cap goal 3 built 18 lists and goal 2 135 (the oldest were let go); the det trace is byte-identical. Frame hashes: identical with static buffers off; with them on, 78 of 8007 differ, all on pictures where a list is a static buffer (kept) against built at its call (the base promotes after a second use), the same vertices, one draw path (see `MSC_DL_WARM`). `[dlkeep]` lines with `MSC_DL_KEEP_LOG=1` |
| `MSC_CADENCE_FULL=0\|1` | Vita plan `1`, code `0` | With `MSC_CADENCE`. A scene the cadence policy holds at a locked 30 is drawn at the game's own size (960x544, the shape rules as they are, the game's own sampling) from the cut that holds it, instead of the smaller picture the 60 ladder had reached (a replay at that size is estimated at about 26 ms of the 33 ms). The size changes at that cut and at the cut that ends the hold (the ladder's size of before is put back) and at no other time; only when the target of the size is kept in the pool already or is the picture in use (never a target made at a cut: the two sizes' targets are pinned while a hold lasts); inside the scene the ladder's own rules for a scene held at 30 apply (a step down needs frames over the 30 budget, with its hysteresis; nothing goes above the game's own size). Not with a forced, cycling, fixed, LOCKED 60 or Mega Strike size. Host: `cadfull_unit.cpp` (21 checks, 4 breaks caught: only at cuts, never without the target, open play untouched). Not exercised in a picture run (the harness has no GL back end; dynamic resolution reads the wall clock): the pass objects of the native size are made by the first frame that draws them if the pool let them go, so the console check is `[cadfull]` lines and `[hitch]` at the cut |
| `MSC_CADENCE_MENUS=0\|1` | off (code and plan) | With `MSC_CADENCE`. The front-end menus, the stadium viewer and the pause menu are a class that is held at 30 like a replay (never loading, never open play; `ClassOfTaskState`, 55 checks in `cadence_unit.cpp`). Held off: in the AUTO host run it changes the random seed at the first simulation step and so the whole offline trace from step 27, because the front end draws from the same stream (`nlRandom` with `gNetworkRandomSeed`) once per picture and the picture count in the menus changes. The same is true of any change of the menu's rate (AUTO's own choice included), but this wave's rule is a byte-identical trace. The front end reads the pad once per picture (`FrontEndTask::Run(dt)`), so a held menu acts on a press 16.7 ms later on average than at 60 (model of feel.md: about 50 ms press to picture at 30, 42 at 60 for a light menu), the pad's 4-sample latch keeps a short press. A cursor at 60 over a held picture is not simple (the picture is presented whole) and was not done |
| `MSC_HELD_CADENCE=auto` | off (code and plan) | AUTO only (not LOCKED 60), single player, with `MSC_LATE_WAIT>=1`. A replay or cut-scene held at 30 in which more than a third (7 of 20) of the last 20 pictures took over 36 ms (after 4 skipped first pictures; loads over 134 ms not judged) is shown at a steady 3 refreshes (50 ms, 20 fps) instead of the 3/4/2 mix: the late wait (`np_fps.cpp` `NpFpsLateWait`) and the back-pressure gate use 3 instead of 2 (`HeldPeriod`). Back to 2 when 40 pictures in a row did their work (slowest of game thread, busiest core, the renderer's lower bound for its smallest picture) in under 28 ms; one entry and one exit a scene, never back and forth, and leaving the scene puts 2 back at once. Never in open play, menus, loads, a Mega Strike, a locked cut-scene (swap mode 2), a free run, LAN, online or a recorded or played-back game (`NpLanActive`, `IsNetworkOrRecordedGame`). Presentation only; a picture that needs over 50 ms is still late (the logs' 4+ refresh pictures stay). Decision: `np_fps_cad3.h` (pure); host: `tools/fps/cad3_unit.cpp` on the 51 held scenes of the r11c/r12/r13 console logs (`cad3_scenes.txt`, made by `cad3_extract.py`; 6 mutants must fail) and `cad3_wire_unit.cpp` (the real `np_fps.cpp`). Off because it is a deliberate drop to 20 fps where 30 is missed, the owner's call, and cannot be judged without console timing (kit runs `cad3`, `cad3d`). heldpipe23: the exit's "work" is also the largest of the front-end, back-end and GPU lanes (the `[late]` lanes, `NpLateLaneNote`), not only the game thread, the core forecast and the GPU floor; default unchanged. |

### More 1.2 Switches, By Area

Every switch below was added after 1.1 and is read once at start-up (from `np-player.txt`, `env.txt` or the environment of the
desktop harness). "Default" is what a build without the line does. A default of "on" changes nothing the player can see unless noted:
the pictures stay the same, and the switch exists so a console A/B can turn it off.

**Renderer front end (CPU work per draw)**

| Switch | Values | Default | What it does |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `MSC_VGX_GXD=<mask>` | bit mask; `0` = the 1.0/1.1 code paths | `0x3FFF5FFF` (bits 0-29 except 13 and 15) | The front-end savings, one bit each. Bits 0-12 are the 1.1 ones. 1.2 adds bit 13 `GXD_MERGESER` (merge a draw into the previous record by a state serial), 14 `GXD_DECFILL` (vertex default values written by a loop chosen once per call), 15 `GXD_RSFAST` (state resolution leaves after three word tests when nothing it builds from was written), 16 `GXD_XFWORD` (single-word XF writes inline), 17 `GXD_TEXNEON` (NEON decode of the I4, I8, IA4 and IA8 texture formats, whole tiles). Bit 30 `GXD_VERIFY` runs every shortcut and the plain code and reports a disagreement (diagnostic). Bits 13 and 15 are off: on the armhf instruction counts they did not pay (+0.01 % and +0.15 %). Console A/B for those two |
| `MSC_VGX_RSCACHE=0\|1\|2` | `0` the 1.1 code, `1` cached state resolution, `2` run both and count any difference (`rscache: MISMATCH`) | `1` | The register writers keep a 64-bit hash of the registers the shader keys are built from; a draw with the same hash reuses the keys, a 1024-entry memo answers the others, and render state, texture bindings and uniform blocks are rebuilt on what they are built from |
| `MSC_VGX_TEXMEMO=0\|1\|2` | `0` per-picture memo of 1.1, `1` persistent binding memo, `2` resolve both ways and count disagreements | `1` | A texture binding is kept across pictures, keyed by the texture registers, and reused while the copy generation, erase generation, HD state and write generation of the source memory all match |
| `MSC_VGX_REGSHADOW=0\|1` | `0` off | `1` | A vertex-format record that restates the descriptor and attribute-table words the player already holds is consumed without the per-register writers |
| `MSC_VGX_ATRIVIAL=0\|1` | `0` off | `1` | An alpha test that cannot fail for any 8-bit alpha emits no `discard` and uses the shader key of a draw without a test. Exact by construction |
| `MSC_VGX_COPYSHADERS=0\|1` | `0` off | `1` | Dedicated copy programs for the identity, identity-with-alpha-1, channel-broadcast and broadcast-with-alpha colour matrices; every other matrix keeps the generic program |
| `MSC_VGX_Q1=0\|1` | `1` on | `0` | A projective texture coordinate whose matrix makes q exactly 1 uses the shader key without the projective bit. A few pixels differ by one level, hence off |
| `MSC_VGX_DLCACHE_BOUNDS_GUARD=0\|1` | `0` off | `1` | A display list whose index reaches past its bound vertex array is not cached (its zero vertices used to be kept when the array was later bound with room). Only a failure path changes |
| `MSC_VGX_SKINPAL=0\|1` | `1` on | `0` | A GPU-skinned draw gets the smallest bone palette (4, 10, 16 or 32 bones) that holds its skin state: a smaller `u_bone` array and a smaller block in the uniform arena. Key value 1 stays the whole palette, so shader lists written before stay valid. Console A/B |
| `MSC_VGX_CULL=0\|1\|2` | `1` cull, `2` only count | Vita player build `1` (`vita_plan.cpp`), desktop `0` | A resident (static) triangle draw whose stored-position box lies wholly outside the scissor rectangle of its own state, with 4 pixels of margin, is not recorded. Skinned, per-vertex-matrix and Z-frozen draws are never culled (proof: `renderer/src/vgx_cull.h`: exact in any target, so shadow casters and reflections are covered by their own scissor). Below the game's per-object frustum tests this acts per packet. Census (`MSC_VGX_CULL=2`, `MSC_VGX_GEOMCENSUS=1`) on the Pipeline Central route in the emulator: 27-31 of 172 static draws a frame, 12 % of their triangles. Galaxy's `GXV_STATICCULL` measured 1.5 ms of backend time. Not measured on a Strikers console |
| `MSC_VGX_VGL_FRAGMEMO=0\|1` | `1` on | Vita player build `1`, desktop `0` | vitaGL patch 0018: each program keeps what the shader patcher answered for a blend function, target format and sample count (8 entries), so a change of blend asks the patcher once. The same pointers (host test `renderer/tests/run_vitagl_draw_caches_test.sh`). Idea from Galaxy's `GXV_VGL_FRAGMEMO`; written anew, its source was not at hand |
| `MSC_VGX_VGL_UNIREUSE=0\|1` | `1` on | Vita player build `1`, desktop `0` | vitaGL patch 0018: a program switched to whose uniforms did not change since its last copy in this scene names that copy again instead of reserving and copying. A copy never outlives its scene or a turn of the uniform ring. The same bytes at every draw in the host test |
| `MSC_VGX_UNICMP=0\|1\|2` | `1` on (Vita plan) | desktop `0` (the host has no vitaGL buffers: nothing changes there) | be26: a draw's uniform block whose uniforms all have a place in vitaGL's buffers (MSC_VGX_UNI_SHADOW) is compared with that place and no second copy (the program's shadow) is kept or read for it; any other block keeps the shadow. Same answer by construction; `2` makes both answers and counts every disagreement in the `[fastcmp]` line (must be 0; `MSC_VGX_UNICMP_BREAK=1` is the test mutant: mode 2 must then count some) |
| `MSC_VGX_RTSCAN_NOFILL=0\|1\|2` (`MSC_VGX_RTSCAN_BREAK=1` test only) | `1` on (Vita plan), host `0` | wave 13 (render13 R5, from the Galaxy port's C184 "skip zeroing scratch that is overwritten before use"). `RtScan` (the per-picture scan of the command buffer for the EFB's depth) sized `rt.live` and `rt.nextBreak` with a fill (n + 1 and n entries) that its two backward loops overwrite entry by entry (all but `nextBreak[n - 1]`, which is now set directly). Exact: nothing reads them in between. `2`: poison, scan, count entries still poison (`[rtscan]` line, must be 0); the mutant leaves `nextBreak[n - 1]` unset and is counted. About 5 bytes a command (some KiB a picture): negligible; kept because it is free. |
| `MSC_VGX_VGL_UNIHIST=0\|1\|2` | `1` on | Vita player build `1`, desktop `0` | be26, vitaGL patch 0046 (needs UNIREUSE=1): a program's uniform buffer whose bytes equal one of its last four copies of this scene (kept in main memory, up to 1.5 MiB in all, compared word by word, never read back from the ring) names that ring copy again instead of reserving and copying. Same bytes at every draw (host model test with a half-compare mutant); `2` also reads every reused copy back and counts a difference (test mode). Counters: the `[vgx] vitaGL uniform buffers` line (copies, last-copy and history reuses, KiB not copied a frame) |
| `MSC_VGX_RINGHIST=0\|1` | `0` | desktop and host only | be26: counts how many of the uniform-buffer copies a draw makes vitaGL write to its ring repeat the program's last copy, or one of its last 16, in the same scene (`[ringhist]` lines every 300 pictures, top programs by ring bytes every 1500). No effect on the picture |

**GPU passes and render targets**

| Switch | Values | Default | What it does |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `MSC_VGX_PASSMEMO=0\|1\|2` | `0` off, `1` on, `2` verify (desktop: draws anyway, reads both back, counts `memoVerified` / `memoMismatch`) | `1` | The glow chain after each 3D frame is 12 GXM scenes (6 blur passes, 6 down-size copies). A pass and its copy that would draw exactly what they drew last time into a texture nobody has written since are not issued; the copy's clear still runs. The key covers the pass, the copy, each draw's state, textures with write versions, the uniform values the program takes, geometry, render size and pass scale |
| `MSC_VGX_PASSMEMO_LOG=1` | `1` on | `0` | Log the reason a unit was drawn again. The skip counts (`memoRuns`, `memoSkips`, `memoVerified`, `memoMismatch`, `clearDefers`, `clearFlushes`, `efbSwaps`) are columns of the desktop `MSC_VGX_GLSTATS=<csv>` census |
| `MSC_VGX_CLEARDEFER=0\|1` | `0` off | `1` | A clear that follows a copy is held back and runs before the next use of the EFB, so the copy's scene does not end with a clear |
| `MSC_VGX_CELLCLEAR=0\|1` | `0` off (`MSC_VGX_CELLCLEAR_MIN=n`, default `3`: runs of fewer cells keep the glClear) | Vita player build `1` (`vita_plan.cpp`), desktop `0` | The cells of a pass atlas (the crowd impostors: 32 cells of 64 x 64 at pitch 66 in a 660 x 660 atlas, and 4 of 144 x 144) are each cleared with a scissored `glClear` before their pass draws: 35 to 38 clears a frame in open play, and on vitaGL each one is a scene reset, a scissor-mask rebuild, the clear quad and a burst of `sceGxm` state calls that put everything back. With the switch on, the first cell of a run of cells of one atlas draws the rectangles (cell plus gutter) of all the cells of the run in one `glDrawElements` of quads with a solid-colour program (scissor off, viewport the whole target, colour mask all, blend off, depth test ALWAYS with depth writes on, z = the clear depth 1, stencil untouched as the clear leaves it); the cells after it find their clear done and only set their scissor. Exact where it is used: it needs the pass values to be 0 or 1 in every channel and depth 1 (else the glClear stays), rectangles inside the atlas and pairwise disjoint (a cell's draws are scissored to its own rectangle, so a later cell's clear made earlier changes no pixel), and the same scene on the same pooled object when the later cell arrives (the depth buffer belongs to the object, and a scene on a pass target loads no depth); otherwise that cell does its own glClear. Only a cell whose pass would not have run (its target could not be made, `MSC_VGX_ALLOC_GUARD`) differs: it has been cleared all the same. Host, `MSC_DYNRES=off`, frames 1000, 1800 (menus), 2300, 2450, 2600, 2750 (open play): every dumped PNG identical with the switch off and on (also with `MSC_VGX_RT=1 MSC_VGX_RTSIM=1`, the hostile-depth simulation). Per open-play frame: `glClear` 35 to 38 -> 1, 2 to 4 batch draws (34 to 37 cells), scissor calls and mask rebuilds +2 (the batch draw runs with the scissor off), draws unchanged. Vita3K navigation run (100-frame groups of the sceGxm call counters, the two runs not in the same game phase): open play issued 1320 to 1575 state calls a frame with the switch off and 1142 to 1213 on (depth function, depth write, polygon mode and depth bias calls fall by 35 to 45 %, 19 to 39 calls a frame each); no crash, no `corrupt record stream`. A first use compiles one more small program (the shader cache has to be seeded with the switch on; if the compile fails the glClear stays) |
| `MSC_VGX_COPYINPLACE=0\|1` | `1` on | Vita player build `1` (`vita_plan.cpp`), desktop `0` | 1.3. A copy of the whole EFB 1:1 whose alpha row is the identity and that has no clear (the shadow volumes' copy into `target/shadowvolume`, every 3D frame) is not made when the draws that read it (up to the last one) read only its alpha (no TEXC input, alpha swap `a`, no indirect or Z-texture use, clamped linear sampling) and nothing between the copy and the last of them writes alpha, is an effect or crowd draw, or another command. The EFB's colour buffer is re-attached at the copy (the EFB's scene still ends there, as before) and those draws sample it in the next scene: its alpha bytes are what the copy would have held, since only tile stores change that memory and every store carries the loaded alpha unchanged. One 960x544 copy scene fewer per 3D frame (`inplaceCopies`, `inplaceDraws` in the counts; `inplaceStale` counts a later frame's draw that sampled the left-out texture before any write, which none of the routes did). Desktop GL calls sampling an attached texture a feedback loop, so it is not used there. `vgx_backend_gl.cpp` InplaceScan. Console A/B `copyinplace0` |
| `MSC_VGX_EFBSWAP=0\|1` | `1` on | `0` | Hand the EFB colour buffer to a full 1:1 copy. No copy in the game qualifies (all convert or shrink), so it stays off |
| `MSC_VGX_ALLOC_GUARD=0\|1` | `0` = the 1.1 behaviour | `1` | Names and storage of render targets, copy textures and uploads are checked: a pass whose target could not be made is skipped (its copy's clear still happens), an upload without memory samples as white, the reduced-size effects are drawn at full size when their target cannot be made, and a short bounded line (4 per kind, 24 in all) goes to the log. Only failure paths differ |
| `MSC_VGX_TEXCENSUS=0\|1` | `1` (code and Vita player build; counts only) | `1` | A census of draws that sample a texture with no GPU storage (it samples the 1x1 white texture): kind 0 never uploaded, 1 slot empty (evicted by the texture budget's trim, freed, or left empty for want of memory), 2 a copy texture sampled before any copy wrote it. Counted per scene kind (menu, play, replay, cut-scene, mega, heavy-stadium); one `[texcensus]` line per new (texture, kind, scene), at most 80, and a summary line every 600 pictures while the count moves. No line in a run means no such draw. Render only, no change to any picture (wave 10, light10). |
| `MSC_VITA_ROBUST=0\|1` | `0` = scene entry and shader patching as in 1.1 | `1` | vitaGL: a failed depth, stencil or render-target allocation, a refused `sceGxmBeginScene`, or a fragment or vertex program that could not be patched skips the draw, clear or blit that asked, instead of using a null pointer or the previous layout's program. Not retried for that framebuffer before the next frame |
| `MSC_VITA_RT_RING_GUARD=0\|1` | `0` = as 1.1 | `1` | vitaGL's GPU timer ring is never recorded over the record the watcher still holds; frames that lost a record are published untimed |
| `MSC_VGX_ONESTREAM=0\|1` | `1` on | `0` | vitaGL `vglSetOneStream`: a `glDrawElements` draw whose active attributes come from one buffer with one stride gets one hardware vertex stream (plus one per disabled attribute) instead of one per attribute. Needs vitaGL built with `DRAW_SPEEDHACK` (patch 0010). Console A/B |
| `MSC_VGX_GXM_DIRECT=0\|1` | `1` on | `0` | 1.3. A triangle draw (warm-up draws too) goes to vitaGL as one call, `vglDrawElementsDirect` (patch 0017), with one hardware vertex stream and its patched vertex program kept per program and layout, instead of the attribute calls and `glDrawElements`. Lines, points and everything without the call in vitaGL keep the ordinary path, which `MSC_VGX_ONESTREAM` still governs; both make the same vertex program. Log: `[gxm-direct]`. Console A/B: Galaxy measured +1 fps and -1 ms in the back end; Strikers has no measurement |
| `MSC_LOAD_FPS=60` | `60`, else off | off | 1.3. A picture per refresh in the task states FE_TO_GAME and GAME_TO_FE, as `MSC_BOOT_FPS` does at the start. About 85 frames pass in a match load and read completions are taken once a frame, so a finished slice waits out a 33 ms period. No simulation runs there. Not in a LAN or online session. Off until a console A/B (`loadfps60`) |
| `MSC_VITA_SWEEP_BG=0\|1` | `0` = as 1.2 | `1` in the Vita plan (wave 9), `0` in code | 1.3. The thread sweeps made every 600 frames (`KeepCoreZeroForGame` on the game thread, `PinLibraryThreads` on the render thread, at least 500 system calls each) go to the `np_stallwatch` thread, every 30 s. Logging builds print `[vita] thread sweep ... took <us>` for the first ones |
| `MSC_GL_TEXMEMO=0\|1\|2` | `0` a walk of the inventories for every bind, `2` memo and walk, a difference logs `[gl] texture memo MISMATCH` | `1` | 1.3. Handle to texture index, kept for the bind (`glTextureMemo.h`). Most binds of a send carry no resolved index, and each walked every pool's level trees (the largest render-side item of the PGO list). Every writer of the trees, of the pool ring and of the indices empties the memo. Exact: host test `renderer/tests/run_gl_render_fast_test.sh`, which also runs the deliberate break |
| `MSC_VGX_RECLAIM_QUIET=0\|1` | `0` = as 1.2 | `1` | 1.3. When more than `MSC_VGX_MAXPROGRAMS` programs are linked, the deletes of stale programs wait for a frame outside match play, replays and cut-scenes, because each `glDeleteProgram` waits for the GPU. More than 64 over the limit trims in a match as well; a draw that finds no free slot makes room at once either way. Only reachable with a console list near the limit (the package's holds 448 programs) |
| `MSC_FX_LOWRES_RUNS=n` | `0` = no limit | `0` | 1.3. At most `n` effect runs of a frame go to the reduced target, the ones covering the most pixels, Mega Strikes included; the others stay in the scene at full size. Each run costs two more EFB scenes and a composite. Off until `MSC_FX_STAT=1` shows how many runs a Strikers frame has |
| `MSC_MEGA_WARM=0\|1\|2` | `0` = the 1.1 / 1.2 rules, `1` keep, `2` keep and make ahead | `2` | The pauses at the cuts into and out of a Mega Strike were the making of render targets and pass objects for a picture size the session had not used for a while (1.1 console: 80-190 ms). `1`: the render target a Mega Strike leaves, and the pass objects the play before it was using, stay while the Mega Strike lasts (however long) and while the video memory allows; the targets and pass objects a Mega Strike scene used stay for the next one. `2`: also, when a Mega Strike begins (its meter starts, seconds before the cut) the render target of the size it starts at is made then, in play. Pictures are the same; only when things are made and how long they are kept changes |
| `MSC_MEGA_WARM_KIB=n` (`28672` with LOCKED 60, `vita_plan.cpp` kLock60), `MSC_MEGA_WARM_FLOOR_KIB=n`, `MSC_MEGA_WARM_MAKE_KIB=n`, `MSC_MEGA_WARM_KEEP=n` | KiB, KiB, KiB, presents | `16384`, `16384`, `24576`, `36000` | The video-memory rules of `MSC_MEGA_WARM`: the most the held targets and pass objects may take together; held things are let go (the old rules) when less than the floor is free; a target is made ahead only when this much plus its own size is free; how long a Mega Strike scene's pass objects are kept after their last use |
| `MSC_DISCARD_FAST=0\|1` | | `1` in the Vita plan, `0` in code | Stall10 (w8/stall10.md). The game throws frames away on purpose (`glDiscardFrame`): the front end at the end of loading until its seven overlay scenes are valid (FrontEndTask.cpp), then the walk-out's presentation script (`GameBegin`, Presentation.cpp) until the NIS is ready. Each discarded frame waited for one retrace (`glplatAbortFrame`), so the loader's fixed count of frames (about 71 and 32 on the host) was 1.2 s and 0.5 s on the 60 Hz screen (1.9 s and 1.2 s on the console, r11). With the switch a discarded frame skips the retrace wait (`glplatAbortFrameFast`); the count of frames is unchanged. Not in a LAN, online or recorded game and not before the session objects exist. `[discard] run N: ...` says frames, ms a frame, how many were not paced and who asked (lab builds and when the switch is on). |
| `MSC_DISCARD_REHEARSE=N` | | `0` in the Vita plan and in code (stall10 commit da9e6376 turned the plan's `6` off; the r17 console logs read "0 sent hidden"; kit runs `rehearse6` / `loadhold1` are the console test, not yet run) | Stall10. Every Nth frame the walk-out wait discards (Presentation.cpp, "GameBegin", until `NisPlayer::IsReadyToPlay`) is sent to the renderer as a hidden frame (`np_gx_hidden`, EXT_HIDDEN, `CmdBuf::hidden`): executed (display-list conversion, texture decode and uploads, shader links, render targets) but never presented, so that work is done under the loading picture, not on the first intro picture. Single player only (as `MSC_DISCARD_FAST`). Counter: `[discard]` lines (`sent hidden`) and `[hidden]` lines from the back end. |
| `MSC_LOADHOLD=N` | | `0` in the Vita plan and in code (off with `MSC_DISCARD_REHEARSE`; kit run `loadhold1`) | Stall10. With `MSC_DISCARD_REHEARSE` on, the first N (at most 3) frames of the walk-out (NIS, Presentation.cpp "GameBegin") are also sent hidden: the loading picture stays up until the walk-out's own first frame has been made. Counter: `[discard]` lines (asked by "GameBegin hold"). |

Changes nothing. |
| `MSC_PIC_PREWARM=0\|1` | | `1` in the Vita plan, `0` in code | Smooth (w6/smooth.md). The picture program of the present's mode (SMOOTH: both of its programs) is built after the back end starts, not at the first present that needs it. At the screen's size the picture is a plain copy with no program, so the first program was built at the first step to a smaller picture: a shader compile and a link in play. Same programs, built earlier; nothing is drawn. `[vgx] picture programs ready ahead of play` in the log. Host: not run (the host harness has no such call); console: the `[hitch]` summary's `shader` count in play
| `MSC_POOL_KEEP=n` | presents | `18000` in the Vita plan (5 min at 60), `0`/unset the old 600 | Smooth. Memory pressure is judged as before (60 presents, or 2 after a size change, when not roomy) |

| `MSC_HDTEX_FADE=n`, `MSC_HDTEX_FADE_RAW=r` | pictures, 1/8 levels | `8` in the Vita plan (r 8 = one level), `0` off | An HD replacement texture arrives a few pictures after its object is first drawn with the original (a one-time sharpness pop). Console only: the host's GLES2 has no LOD bias, host pictures are unchanged by construction |
| `MSC_STILL_TOL=0\|1`, `MSC_STILL_REFRESH=n` | | TOL `1` in the Vita plan, REFRESH `0` | Only with `MSC_MENU_STILL`/`MSC_PAUSE_STILL`. Measured (`MSC_STILL_DIAG=1`): on a settled menu only the camera's stamp changed, by float noise (1e-7 on rotation, 1e-5 on a translation of 100), so the exact comparison never settled. TOL compares with a quarter-pixel tolerance against the matrix at the last move (`NpStillCameraMoved`). REFRESH=n makes every n-th held picture a live copy so the world's own movement goes on at 1/n rate. Captains, cursor, glow and menu stay live every picture either way |

On: pass objects drawn into only by copy targets and by passes whose `PassCmd::planes` has no depth bit are made with a depth/stencil surface that has no memory (tile memory only, nothing loaded or stored); an object with no depth serves only such acquires (`vgx_nodepth.h`, host test `renderer/tests/run_nodepth_test.sh`). With this on a draw in a match marks its program in the list (record type 5, appended once) and the next start links the marked programs and their variants first, so what the limit leaves unlinked is menu filler. No marks are written and none read with it off (a list with marks read with it off is written anew without them). Not in the pack or the shaders. Today one task delta is clamped at 0.1 s (`g_fTaskTimeUpperBound`, `nlTask.cpp`), five 20 ms steps in one frame after a hitch, and `MSC_SLIP=20` cuts a late frame to at least frame/1.2. With this a late frame advances the game by at most `n` steps (never less than the slip limit of one period); the rest of the lateness is not made up, the game clock runs behind real time by it. The same fixed steps and inputs; needs `MSC_SLIP` and has its conditions (single player, in the match, a picture policy with a period; never in a LAN or online session). Model (`native/tools/fps/slip_unit.cpp`, estimate): a 70 ms hitch every 150 pictures, holds of 3+ refreshes 0.6-0.8 % of pictures with slip only, 0 % with the cap; at a steady 20 fps the game runs at 80 % instead of 84 %. With a budget a late frame is cut only from a bucket of that many ms that fills at `MSC_SLIP_REFILL_PCT` (default 2) percent of real time (`np_slip.h`): a single hitch is cut as before (70 ms: 30 ms), a sustained overload runs the game at 98 % or better (worst 2 s window 3.5 %: refill x 2 s + the bucket), an on-time frame is never touched. The decision is made once per frame (`NpFpsBeginFrame`) and every task of the frame gets the same fraction. Single player only; needs `MSC_SLIP`. `[slip] match #n so far / end` lines: game time lost to slip of the match's play time, the stateless rule's figure beside it, the worst 2 s window, fixed steps a second of play (50.00 is exact). Counts the fixed 20 ms steps the simulation ran (`NpFpsSimStep`) against the unclamped wall time between frames of the gameplay task state; it does not depend on `MSC_SLIP` and sees what the `[slip]` ledger cannot (frames over 100 ms, steps not run). COOL s before the next try, doubling for a try that ended within FAIL s (at most COOLMAX). Code values `26.7`, `30`, `6`, `96`, `30`, `20`; the Vita plan sets `20`, `25`, `45`, `240`, `90`, `60`. Why: free running at 1.3-1.6 refreshes of work gives 36-44 pictures a second in a 1/2 refresh mix (64/36 to 34/65 in `pace_mid_model.cpp between`) that the clean 30 does not have; 20 ms is 1.2 refreshes, 7 pictures in 10 one refresh long. Host test: `between_sticky_unit.cpp` (a 22 +- 4 ms scene over five minutes: 4 switches, 16 without the cool-down and hysteresis; mutants caught). Console A/B: `betweenold` |
| `MSC_LAT_BP=0\|1` | `1` | Back-pressure before the pad (pacing11, `np_fps_bp.h`): under a held 30 or 60 (single player) the game thread starts a picture, and so reads the pad, only when at most `depth` earlier pictures are unshown; in a heavy moment it waits there (one refresh at a time, at most 6 a picture) instead of standing aside. Pacing12: the first refresh over the depth is waited for only while it FITS in the picture's period (period less the thread's own busy time less 3 ms): r10 showed that a wait after the cadence wait with 22 ms of work behind it made the picture miss its flip (3-refresh holds, [hitch] counts +40 %); a backlog of more than depth + 1 pictures is always waited for. `depth` starts at 2 at 30 (pacing12; r10 kick-off with depth 1 from the start ran slower) and goes down to 1 after calm; it goes up (to 4) when late pictures follow a wait at the gate and the raise brings the mean gap down, and back down when it does not (a GPU-bound stretch) or after 45 clean pictures (the wait doubles for a descent that failed, up to 16 times). Standing aside only when the display reports nothing for 8 pictures running. Free runs keep the wave 10 limit. Model: `tools/fps/bp_unit.cpp` (30 after a cut 100 -> 67 ms pad to picture, 60 held, GPU 40 and 70 ms no frame rate lost); console A/B: kit run `bp0`. |
| `MSC_CROWD_SEED60=0\|1` | `0` (why60, not in the plan) | A live picture at 60 that follows live pictures at another cadence (the p98 trial of 60 after the relaxed 30 gave the crowd back to level 0) starts the crowd governor (`MSC_CROWD_GOV`) at the level the last 60 reached (`np_crowdgov.h` `Seed`, unit test `crowdgov_unit` 10) instead of walking down one level a second; console line `[crowdgov] MSC_CROWD_SEED60: a 60 begins at level a -> b`. Render only (crowd sprite refresh interval): the simulation does not read it. Console run line why60-seed in w8/why60.md. |

Needs MSC_CROWD_GOV (the plan's). Log: `[crowdgov] MSC_CROWD_FLOOR60=n: live play at 60 holds the crowd at level n or more`, `... released`. Render only. |
| `MSC_VGX_FEPAR_KEEP=0..7` | **ON in every mode since 26 Nov** (the plan's quality list, value `7`; before that LOCKED 60 only, wave 25; code default `0`; 25 Nov console int8 Vice player-like A/B: 60 for 19-25 s against 13-19 s, and the strict 50 holds with it) | Keeps the front end's decode worker on the Vita's system core (MSC_VITA_CORE3_WORK bit 2). Bit 1: on core 3 it runs at priority 0x10000100-48, above the core watch's 3 ms spell (216). Bit 2: the cost rule replaces '3 late joins in 48': retire when the mean wait of 128 joins that waited is over 900 us or 12 of them over 3 ms. Bit 4: a retired worker is put back after `MSC_VGX_FEPAR_KEEP_RETRY_S` s (20, doubling, at most `MSC_VGX_FEPAR_KEEP_TRIES` returns, 4), only while the core is open (the online guard still moves it). | `MSC_FPS_PROBE_EVERY=N` | `1` (every picture, as before; not in the plan) | probe18: with `MSC_FPS=auto` the per-core probe (`np_fps_probe.cpp`: one `sceKernelGetThreadInfo` per registered thread, 15-20 on the console, estimate 0.3-0.6 ms of the begin-frame "pace" zone) is read once in N pictures (1..8). It feeds only the controller's cost figures (`Auto::Predict`: the 60 Hz cost, the free-run cost, "busiest core over budget") and the free-run gate; never the simulation, a picture, the pad or what a LAN peer is sent. The pictures between reads hear the last window's per-picture mean (the controller's frame counters step as before). A read is forced on: a LAN/online/recorded game (always every picture), no valid window yet, a scene change, a locked cut-scene frame, a policy or free-run change on the picture before, a picture whose length differs from the one before by more than half a refresh, N pictures since the last read; a forced read before the window is full hears the picture just finished as the window's total less the cached mean of the pictures before it (never less than the mean). Bound: a policy decision moves by at most N pictures up and 2N down (unit test: `tools/fps/run_fps_unit_test.sh`, N=4: 60 at picture 102 against 101, back to 30 at 226 against 223, 92 reads in 330 pictures; mutants: no skipping, no forced reads). The `[fps]` 120-picture line says how many pictures were read. Host runs never have the probe (Vita only), so the det trace cannot see it. A console A/B (`price17b`-style) decides the default. |

No overlap, so no gain (a few tenths of a ms lost, estimate): it proves what the step needs of its thread before anything runs beside it: game-heap identity (`NpMemSetGameThread(1)`), the caller's FPSCR (adopted at every kick), the frame-send gates (`NpFsSwapGameThread`: np_sim is the thread that joins while the game thread waits), a painted stack with high-water mark. **Crowd and fill options (W12 CROWD; the first two change the picture a little)**

The crowd (draw layer 10) is 26 to 28 alpha-tested, blended, depth-writing draws of impostor sprites, about 690 000 fragments over 96 000 pixels in open play
(7.1 per covered pixel), all from one 660 x 660 impostor texture. The sprites are drawn in no depth order: a near and a far row alternate between draws and the quads
of one draw run both ways. The host model of the census (below) finds that only about 10 % of those fragments pass the alpha test (68 000 of 687 000; 12 % of the used
half of the texture is not transparent), that the game's order already lets the depth test reject 17 % (570 000 fragments are still shaded), and that sorting therefore
gains little: 6 to 7 % fewer shaded fragments with 8 depth bands (about 100 more draws), 14 to 15 % in the ideal order of 1 000 pieces. The waste is the transparent
margin of every sprite, which no order can remove, but a cut can (`MSC_VGX_CROWDTRIM`, on in the player build, below). The two ordering options are off by default; they are here for a console A/B.

| Switch | Values | Default | What it does |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `MSC_VGX_CROWDSORT=<n>` | `0` off, `1` the crowd draws of a run go near to far by their mean depth and the quads of each draw near to far, `2` every quad near to far (about 1000 pieces: many more draw calls), `4`..`64` that many depth bands, near to far, with the quads of a draw together inside a band | `0` | The depth test then rejects what a nearer sprite already covers. Needs the layer marks, which the switch turns on by itself. Model, 8 bands: 6.3 to 7.3 % fewer shaded fragments, 123 to 139 pieces instead of 26 draws, about 0.2 ms of CPU per frame on the desktop to measure and order (more on the console). Mode 1 shades 1 to 2 % more (the draws overlap in depth). Picture: only the soft rim of a sprite changes (alpha above 128 but not opaque, blended with a farther sprite drawn first): 1 400 to 1 950 of 286 720 pixels at the open-play frames of `nav_match60` (0.5 to 0.7 %), at most 100 levels, one pixel wide; mode 2 gives the "nearest alpha-passing fragment wins" picture exactly (identical to the pre-pass) |
| `MSC_VGX_CROWDPREZ=1` | `1` on | `0` | An alpha-tested depth-only pass of the run (colour writes off, no blend), then the colour pass with the depth test `<=` and no depth write. The same picture as `CROWDSORT=2`. On a tile-based GPU the alpha test already keeps the hidden-surface removal off for these draws, so the pre-pass costs a second texture fetch for every fragment (the model: pass 1 about 570 000 fragments, pass 2 about 490 000, against 570 000 now) and doubles the draw calls: expected to lose |
| `MSC_VGX_CROWDFTB=1`, `MSC_VGX_CROWDFTB_MIN=n`, `MSC_VGX_CROWDFTB_PROBE=n` | OFF (interim 13 Nov after review9; it was `1` in the plan; kit run `crowdftb1` turns it on) | `0` in code; MIN `1500` (hundredths of a 640x448 screen), PROBE `4` | The crowd drawn near to far (the mode-1 order of `MSC_VGX_CROWDSORT`) only in the buffers where it pays: a buffer whose sorted crowd quads add up to MIN or more (a goal celebration: 40 to 50 screens of nominal quad area against about 7 in open play) keeps the order on; otherwise one buffer in PROBE is tried. Model (FILLCENSUS, celebration close-ups): 25 to 31 % fewer shaded crowd fragments, so about 1.7 to 2.1 of the 6.8 screens the crowd costs there (host estimate, not timed on a console). Picture: the soft rim of a sprite (blended over a farther one that is now rejected by the depth test): about 15 000 of 286 720 pixels at a celebration frame, thin one-pixel edges, at most 46 levels; open-play frames are identical. Simulation: `NP_DET_TRACE` checksums equal over 3 734 frames. Not applied while the effects pass is open. `MSC_VGX_CROWDSTAT=1` prints a `[crowdftb]` line every 600 frames. A host run that renders only the dumped frames (`MSC_VGX_LEAD` small) never lets the probe see a crowd: use `LEAD=12` to check it |
| `MSC_VGX_ATLATE=0\|1\|2`, `MSC_VGX_ATLATE_LAYERS`, `MSC_VGX_ATLATE_STAT`, `MSC_VGX_ATLATE_BREAK` (test only), `MSC_VGX_ATCENSUS=<frames>` | `1` in the Vita plan (interim 13 Nov after review9: level 2 proven on one stadium only; kit runs `atlate0` and `atlate2`) | `0` in code | The tile GPU loses hidden-surface removal for an alpha-tested draw and for the opaque draws drawn after it (Galaxy: 30.2 to 23.6 ms by sinking them). The alpha-tested, SRC_ALPHA / INV_SRC_ALPHA, depth-writing draws (the crowd, nets, fences) are held to the end of their window of simple draws (a pass, a copy, a target change or a conflicting upload ends the window), where the order does not change the picture: `vgx_atlate.h` plans it, `renderer/tests/run_atlate_test.sh` tests it with a mutant that must fail. | `MSC_FX_CROWD=0\|1`, `MSC_FX_CROWD_MIN=n`, `MSC_FX_CROWD_TRIPX=n` | | `0` | Experiment: the crowd draw as a member of the reduced effects target (half or third size). Measured on the host at 3000, 3120, 5040, 5280: the compositor's divisor stays 0 and the pictures are identical, so it does nothing there (the crowd is a depth-writing alpha-tested draw, not a soft member, and 64-texel sprites with no mips would shimmer below about 55 px). | `MSC_VGX_CROWDTRIM=0\|1\|2` | `0` off, `1` on, `2` on and the host reads the pictures back and checks every cut | Vita player build `1` (`vita_plan.cpp`), desktop `0` | Each crowd sprite quad is cut to the part of its picture that a figure was drawn in. The back end follows the offscreen passes of the frame (the crowd's 64 x 64 captures into cells of the 660 x 660 atlas): for each pass it takes the pixel box of everything its draws can have put in the picture, from the draws' own matrices and vertices (a rigid mesh: the box of its vertices; a skinned mesh: the box of the vertices of each bone through that bone's matrix, valid because a skinned vertex lies in the hull of its bones' transformed boxes; a matrix-per-vertex mesh: one box per palette row), widened by a pixel of the pass. The pass clears its picture to alpha 0, so outside that box every texel of the cell has alpha 0. A later draw of arena quads whose alpha test fails at 0 and whose final alpha is the texture's (the crowd's stage is texture alpha times vertex alpha) gets each quad whose four corners are one cell (a parallelogram in position and in texture coordinates) cut down to the box widened by 2.5 texels (the linear filter and the raster's rounding): position and texture coordinate of the four corners are interpolated, so the texture coordinate of every pixel stays where it was. A pixel outside the cut would have sampled alpha 0 only, failed the test and been discarded. The vertex arena is changed before it is uploaded, nothing else is. A cell is measured again whenever a pass or copy writes it (the crowd's cells are captured every second frame); anything not as described (a pass that is not measurable, a texture written another way, a mipmapped texture, a frame whose passes are not drawn, a buffer that is executed more than once as frame interpolation does) leaves the quads whole and forgets the cells. Open play on the host (nav route, frames 2300 and 2600): 686 873 -> 342 938 fragments (-50.1 %) and 703 108 -> 359 557 (-48.9 %), 568 386 -> 274 646 and 580 409 -> 290 146 shaded; the fragments that write depth are the same 68 050 / 72 744; 52 % of the cut quads' area is kept (the box of each bone is bounded per clip coordinate and the perspective division is bounded on those ranges, which is a little looser than the hull of the eight projected corners: that kept 45 %). The pictures are not bit-identical: any change of a triangle's vertices moves its interpolation planes by a rounding step (the raster snaps vertices to 1/256 pixel), so a bilinear weight of a few soft-edge pixels changes by one step: at the four open-play frames 2300, 2450, 2600 and 2750, 79, 63, 99 and 28 of 286 720 pixels differ, by at most 2 of 255 levels. The check (`=2` with `MSC_VGX_CROWDTRIM_CHECK`) read back every picture the cut quads sampled: no texel with alpha above 0 lies outside its measured box |

| `MSC_VGX_FEPAR=0\|1\|2` | fepar25: the front end's vertex decode of plain stream draws on a worker thread. `0` the player decodes every draw (as before), `1` a stream draw of 8 or more vertices whose format has no matrix-index attribute (particles, HUD, flat crowd quads, replay overlays: every DrawStream decode of the Vice tour, 72,000 calls, none with a matrix index; the display-list cache and skinned characters keep their path) is not decoded when the stream reaches it: the player carries on with state resolution, the record merge and the index buffer, and hands the decode (and the CPU transform of a soft draw) to a worker as a job (copy of the plan and array bindings, source, arena place, two matrices); it joins the worker before the end of the picture (before the game is told its guest memory may change), before a record chunk is handed back, and before the arena may move; a draw that needs its vertices at once (Z-freeze, trace, soft-instanced replay lists) or a culled-away draw is decoded in the player / not at all, `2` verify: the player decodes as with `0` and the worker's copy is compared byte for byte at the join. `MSC_VGX_FEPAR_MIN`, `_WAKE`, `_SPIN_US`, `_CORE`, `_PRIO` tune it; `MSC_VGX_FEPAR_BREAK=1\|2` host mutants | Vita `1` (host proof; console runs `fepar0` / `fepar0d` are the off side), code and desktop `0` | Exact: the vertex bytes are what the player's decode gives (same code over a snapshot of the bindings; host: per-picture back-end hashes, frame hashes and det trace identical off / on / verify at Vice goal + replay + walk-out, The Dump and the tour; the BREAK mutants change the hash / are reported). Memory: a ring of 128 jobs (about 1 KB each) made when the worker starts, no allocation per draw; a full ring decodes inline. Core: the worker is placed by `PinFeparThread` (renderer/vita/vgx_host_vita.cpp): core 1 beside the back end, priority like the job pool's. Estimate (x86 callgrind, then the console's own lane scopes): about 13 % of the front end's instructions leave the player thread in live play (decode 1.2 M + soft transform 0.2 M of 10.1 M per picture, 0.4 M of staging stay); the s28i-fe lanes put the decode scope at 1.9-2.7 ms and the soft-transform scope at 0.3 ms of a 12-13.5 ms front end: 1.7-2.3 ms off the player thread per picture, not measured on a console. A worker that is silent for 200 ms is given up (a line says so, the player decodes, FEPAR is off for the rest of the run: mutant `MSC_VGX_FEPAR_BREAK=3`). Counters: `[fepar]` line every 300 pictures (jobs, vertices, worker busy ms, join wait ms per picture), `front end decode worker` line of `vgx_stats.txt` fepar26: every job carries a claim word (open, claimed, done); the worker, the join and the chunk hand-back run only jobs they claimed (compare-exchange), so no job runs twice and none is written after the join returned (`RUN TWICE` in the [fepar] report must stay 0). The join runs unclaimed jobs itself and waits without timeout for the one the worker holds; a worker holding a job for 200 ms is logged once and gets no new claim (inline for the rest of the run). FEPAR is valid only in the thread record mode (`MSC_VGX_PLAY=thread`, the default); in another mode it is off with a log line. Verify mode treats a full ring (128 jobs unverified) as inline and counts it. Host mutant `MSC_VGX_FEPAR_BREAK=4`: the worker sleeps 300 ms in every 64th claimed job (pictures unchanged, no double run). `_MIN`, `_WAKE`, `_SPIN_US` are clamped. |
| `MSC_JOBS_C3FIRST=0\|1\|2` (`MSC_JOBS_ORDER=core3first` is the same as `1`) | nxcpu25 (25 Nov), order changed by nxr6a (NEON round 6 #4, verified: NEON was right). Code default `1` = **core 2 lane, then core 3, then core 1** (a call for 2 lanes stays on the spinning core 2 lane: no kernel wake of the parked core 3 lane, no `LaneGuard` exposure; a call for 3 lanes uses core 2 + core 3 and still keeps off core 1; 4 lanes all). `2` = nxcpu25's front order (core 3 FIRST: a 2-lane call moved from core 2 to core 3; 6027.log retired the core 3 lane with 20 late calls, longest 2820 us), kept for the A/B. `0` = the old order. Test (`run_jobs_tests.sh`, 21 configurations; mutants `-DNP_JOBS_C3FIRST_BREAK=1..3`, 3 = the front place) shows 2-lane calls never reach lane 3 under `1`. Old text, still true for `2`: Code default `1`, only when the pool has its lane on the system core (`MSC_VITA_CORE3_WORK` bit 1, proven core 3; no such lane: nothing changes, no line). A call that wakes fewer workers than the pool has now wakes the lane on core 3 FIRST, then the core 2 lane, the core 1 lane last (before: core 2, core 1, and core 3 only for a call with work for every lane). Same worker, same rules (never spins, registered with the online guard, given up by the `LaneGuard` after 3 late calls in 48, withdrawn / held: left out). Not with `MSC_JOBS_ORDER=interleave`. `0` is the old order | `1` | Thread placement, exact: every item runs once and the output slots do not depend on the lane (host: `run_jobs_tests.sh` 19 configurations, 3 mutants caught: `-DNP_JOBS_C3FIRST_BREAK=1` order not changed, `=2` a given-up lane still woken; fb-tour 6000 pictures det trace md5 `7f8ee01a5edb` equal with it off, on and on with `MSC_JOBS_VERIFY=1` under `NP_CORE3_FAKE=1`; seed `1234abce` differs). Measured basis (6122, 6000 pictures): lane 2 on core 1 ran 4.58 s (0.76 ms a picture, the ceiling of what this can take off core 1), lane 3 on core 3 0.99 s. | `MSC_STDIO_LOCK=0\|1` | crash26b: the log printing of logging builds goes around stdout / stderr's `FILE`. The SDK's newlib is built without `_RETARGETABLE_LOCKING` (`flockfile` is empty): with the shared line-buffered streams two threads printing at once (the game thread's `[nav]` / `[jobs]` / stat lines, the back end's `[hitch]` / `[lock60]` lines, `fflush(nullptr)`) lost updates of `_p` / `_w` and `__sfvwrite_r` called `memmove(buf, fmt, (size_t)-1)` through the heap (both 18 Nov console dumps: `pte_osTlsGetValue` data abort, the copying thread still in `memmove`). crash26's fix (unbuffered streams, `MSC_STDIO_NBF`, withdrawn) stalled the console at the end of start-up in 6 of 6 runs: every printf then carried newlib's 1 KiB `__sbprintf` buffer, and the 4 KiB `SceGxmDisplayQueue` thread prints from the display callback (static frame sums ~1.7 KiB before, ~3.2-3.6 KiB after, before the SDK's own frames). Now vita.mk wraps `printf vprintf fprintf vfprintf puts putchar fputs fputc putc fwrite` in logging builds too (`vita_silent_stdio.cpp`): on stdout / stderr the text calls hand their bytes to `VitaLog::Write` (its own mutex), the printf calls `vsnprintf` into one 4 KiB buffer under a bounded spin lock (`np_log_stdio.h`: 64 spins, then 50 us naps, 1 s at most; a thread that holds it or timed out formats into 256 bytes of its own; longer texts a heap copy outside the lock) and hand the whole text over; nothing inside the lock prints; the console refresh runs after the release. The streams stay line-buffered (1.2) for anything the link cannot wrap (none in the image: `__swbuf_r` is called only by newlib's own `_putc_r`). `NpVitaLogFlush` flushes stdout and stderr only (not every `FILE`: a read stream's flush seeks and resets it without a lock while dvd-io may read it). `0`: the real functions, the 1.2 path (A/B only: it can crash). Counter: `[stdio] alive at frame N: ... wrapped C calls B bytes, format-lock waits W timeouts T nested N long L` every 600 frames and `[stdio] the end of the run: ...` | Vita `1` (`vita_plan.cpp`), desktop not used | Host stress test `native/platform/vita/tests/run_log_stdio_test.sh` (the header's own code, host threads, the Vita's wraps): see w8/crash26b.md. The race itself: the SDK's own `fvwrite.o` / `fflush.o` / `wbuf.o` / `wsetup.o` under qemu-arm (w8/crash26/repro) fail in 11 of 11 line-buffered runs. No picture or simulation change |
