**Movies**

| Switch | Values | Default | What it does |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `MSC_THP_HWJPEG=0\|1\|fake` | `0` software, `1` hardware, `fake` = a host stand-in codec for tests | Vita: `1`; desktop: `0` | Each THP movie frame is a JPEG without byte stuffing and with its own tables. The decode lanes restuff the scan, hand a standard JPEG to `sceJpegDecodeMJpegYCbCr` and retile the Y/Cb/Cr planes into the GX I8 layout the software decoder writes. A frame the engine refuses is decoded in software. The library is linked weak: without it every call reports unavailable |
| `MSC_THP_HWJPEG_VERIFY=N` | frames | `2` | The first N frames of each movie are also decoded in software and compared; a codec whose picture differs switches itself off for the run |
| `MSC_THP_HWJPEG_MEM=cdram\|phycont\|auto` | | `auto` (CDRAM first, physically contiguous main memory second) | Where the codec's stream, planes and coefficient block live |
| (link) `NP_JPEG_REQUIRE` | | the game image | Wave 9: image r7 linked `-lSceJpeg_stub_weak` but nothing pulled its members (a weak reference never pulls an archive member), so the four `sceJpeg` calls were null and the default-on codec never opened (`[thp-hw] hardware JPEG decoding switched off for this run: the codec could not be opened`). `Makefile.vpk` now requires them (`-Wl,--require-defined=`) and checks the image after the link (`jpeg guard`); the stub library stays weak, so a firmware without SceJpeg still loads the game. Every failure of the codec (open, decode, a picture that differs from software in the verify frames) gives a software decode, never a stop; `MSC_THP_HWJPEG=0` is the off switch. `tools/check-image-weak.py --strict IMAGE` lists the weak-null symbols left. kubridge's `kuKernel*` stay weak on purpose (an optional plug-in). Kit pair `n-hwjpeg1` / `n-hwjpeg0` (phase p5) |
| (link) `__env_lock` / `__env_unlock` | | the game image | Galaxy's finding, confirmed in image r9: the SDK's libc defines both as an EMPTY function (2 bytes), so `getenv` / `setenv` / `unsetenv` are not thread-safe; `setenv` reallocates the environment array for every new name and a thread inside `getenv` walks the freed block (a data abort; Galaxy: about 1 boot in 10). `native/platform/vita/src/np_vita_envlock.cpp` defines them: recursive, owner = the thread's TLS register (no system call), a waiter sleeps 100 us, 2 s then goes on without the lock. Linked directly, so libc's member is never pulled. `Makefile.vpk` checks the symbol sizes after the link (`env lock guard`, > 8 bytes) and `tools/check-image-weak.py` reports `ENV LOCK NOT REAL` for an image with the empty ones. Log: `[env] self-test` once at start (a reader thread against 400 setenv + 400 unsetenv) and `[env] N pictures: ... environment lock takes (X per picture)` every `MSC_VITA_RT_LOG` presents: a getenv on a hot path shows as a large X. Host test `native/platform/vita/tests/run_envlock_test.sh` (control: the empty lock fails 7 checks) |
| (exit) `VgxQuiesceForExit(ms)` | | the Vita player | Galaxy-style fix for the r10 console crash at exit (1 in about 12: a data abort in sceGxm on the back-end thread, `glDrawElements <- vglDrawElementsDirect <- DirectDrawElements`, right after `[plat] script exit at frame N, exiting`): `NpHarness::Finish` ended with `sceKernelExitProcess` (no destructors) while the back end was drawing. `renderer/src/vgx_play.cpp`: the call raises a flag (`HandOver` then drops every frame, before and after its wait for the slot), waits up to `ms` (200 at every caller) until nothing is on its way into the slot, in it, or executing, then `VgxHostQuiesceGpu` (vgx_host_vita.cpp) waits for the scenes vitaGL has submitted (its scene counters; needs the GPU timers the player turns on); log line `[vgx] exit quiesce: back end idle after N ms` (`STILL BUSY` when the bound ran out). Callers: `NpHarness::Finish`, `NpPlatformExit`, the end of `main`, the Settings restart (`VgxQuiesceCancel` re-opens the flow if the system refuses it), the game's quit hook in `os_core.cpp`. Not routed: the crash handler (the back end may be what crashed), the start-up failure exits, the lab's watchdog reload of a hung process, the system's own kill. A no-op with no back-end thread (host, `MSC_VGX_BACKEND_THREAD=0`). Both functions are on `vita_require.mk`. Host model test `tests/test_exit_quiesce.py` (6 mutants) |
| `MSC_THP_NOWAIT=0\|1` | `0` off | Vita: `1`; desktop: `0` | The game thread never waits for a decode lane in the stadium preview: it shows the newest finished frame that is not older than the one on the screen, otherwise it keeps the picture. Audio is decoded before the video and the clock is the retrace, so sync is unaffected |
| `MSC_THP_HWJPEG_FAKE_FAIL=N`, `MSC_THP_HWJPEG_FAKE_BAD=1`, `MSC_THP_HWJPEG_FAKE_DELAY_MS=ms` | | off | Host tests with `MSC_THP_HWJPEG=fake`: every Nth frame refused, wrong pictures returned (the first-frames check must catch them), an artificial decode time |
| `MSC_THP_DELAY=<calls>` | `0..4` | `0` (off, exactly the rules before it); needs `MSC_THP_NOWAIT=1` | Wave 14, ported from Galaxy's movie36 (`thp_pacing.h`, `thp_native.cpp`). A delay line for the movie pictures: the call that asks for frame N shows frame N - delay, so a frame has `delay` more calls (33 ms each) to be decoded in. The lanes become a pool of `MSC_THP_DEPTH` workers over `delay + 4` slots; a worker takes the queued frame that is due first and can still be finished (earliest deadline first), drops frames nobody will show, and a frame that misses its call is passed over for the one due next (no picture twice for one slow decode, no drift, never a step back). The picture runs `delay` frames (66 ms at 2) behind its sound; the last `delay` pictures of a movie are not shown. Timing change: OFF until the console decides (run lines in `docs/notes`: the `[thp-pace]` line says whether repeats exist). Host test `tests/movie/run_delay.sh` (model decoder + the real lanes, 11 mutants) |
| `MSC_THP_RECOVER=<calls>` | `0` off, `2..120` | `8` (on; changes only the failure path) | Wave 14: a movie whose picture froze while the sound played on. After `calls` consecutive calls with a due frame no lane has finished (every lane stuck, frames never queued, a codec call that never returns) the asked frame is decoded on the game thread in software; a codec call that has not returned after `MSC_THP_HWSTUCK_MS` (default `700`) switches the codec off for the run and gives that lane up (another is started); a lane waits at most 250 ms for the codec's lock; the first picture of a movie is waited for at most 1.5 s. Counted in the `[thp-pace]` line (`recovered on the game thread`, `first picture timeouts`, `lanes given up`, `codec lock timeouts`) |
| `[thp-pace]` line | | always on, one per movie | Galaxy's movie33 statistics: calls and their rate, new pictures (on time / passed over / early), `repeated` (calls with no new picture although one was due), gaps between new pictures `late` (over 1.5 call periods) and `doubled` (under half a period), longest gap; plus the recovery counters above. `[movie-fix] ... read_wait_run_max=N` is the longest run of render ticks that found the next frame still being read from the card |
| `MSC_THP_HWJPEG_FAKE_SLOW_EVERY=N`, `_SLOW_MS=ms`, `MSC_THP_HWJPEG_FAKE_HANG_AT=N`, `_HANG_MS=ms` | | off | Host tests with `MSC_THP_HWJPEG=fake`: every Nth codec call takes `ms` longer; the Nth call does not return for `ms` (a hung codec, `test_thp_delay`) |
| `MSC_THP_DUMP_DIR=<dir>` | | off | Test only (`test_thp_jpeg`): writes the first frames as `.jpg` files for `tests/movie/check_bridge.py` |

**Audio, threads and file layer**

| Switch | Values | Default | What it does |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `MSC_AUDIO_PRIO=<n>` | sceKernel priority, e.g. `0x10000100 - 48` = 112 | 128 (`0x10000100 - 32`, level with the SDK's mixer and timer threads on core 2) | Priority of the audio output thread. 112 puts the device feed ahead of the mixer, the timers and the job and frame-send workers (144). Console A/B |
| `MSC_AUDIO_NEON=0\|1` | `0` or `off` the VFP loop | `1` | 1.3. The stereo reverb (`AXFXReverbHiExpCallback`) in NEON, four samples at a time (`native/platform/sdk/src/axfx_reverb_neon.c`). Same output integers as the reference's loop; delay-line words can differ only below 2^-96 (NEON flushes denormals). It needs `MSC_AX_FAST=1`. The AX voice and bus kernels were already NEON (`MSC_AX_FAST`). Audio only: no game state. Reverb runs in the AI callback under the interrupt lock, so its time also counts against a game thread waiting for that lock. Instruction count per frame under an emulator: 41,835 to 16,948; cycles and console time not measured. Test: `native/platform/sdk/tests/axfx_reverb_neon/run_test.py` (needs `pip install unicorn`) |
| `MSC_VITA_CORE_GC=<cores>` | `1`, `2`, a set like `12` | unset (core 2) | 1.3 A/B. vitaGL's collector, and with it the mixer unless `NP_VITA_CORE_AUDIO` is set. The mixer follows only in plans C and Cn (`-1`, threads set by hand, too), never to core 0 or `any`: in plans A and S the player and the back end are on core 1. Galaxy moved both to core 1 and measured a benefit there, with the player as its tightest stage; Strikers' core 1 also carries the frame send, so nothing here is on until the console A/B. `NP_VITA_CORE_AUDIO=2` with `MSC_VITA_CORE_GC=1` moves the collector alone |
| `MSC_VITA_CORE3=1` | anything else is off | **ON in the plan since c3def18** (with `MSC_VITA_CORE3_WORK=3`; 23 Nov console, s41, CapUnlocker: Vice late gameplay pictures 13.0 -> 2.2-4.1 %, 60 fps pictures 4.0 -> 13.4 % with the decode worker; acts only where the probe proves core 3; `=0` written out turns every use of the core off, WORK and SEND included: c3kill18) | 1.3. Asks for the fourth core, which needs the owner's CapUnlocker kernel plug-in; the game neither needs nor touches it. A thread created with mask 0x80000 (or moved to it) must run, end within 200 ms and report CPU 3 and the mask (`np_vita_core3.h`); any other outcome means nothing moves, and `log.txt` has `[vita] fourth core: ...` either way. Then audio output, the mixer, `dvd-io`, `nand-io` and the log writer run there; a thread the system refuses goes back to its plan core. Named lines (`MSC_VITA_CORE_AUDIO`, `NP_VITA_CORE_AUDIO`, `NP_VITA_CORE_IO`) win. Timers and the game's threads do not move. Risk: part of the audio callback holds the interrupt lock, and on this core the system can preempt the mixer while it holds it, which a game thread would feel; `NP_VITA_CORE_AUDIO=2` keeps the mixer on core 2. Read once at start. Not tried on a console |
| `MSC_VITA_CORE3_SEND=1\|2` | anything else is off | off | 1.3 (Advanced > Fourth Core: **ON + FRAME SEND** writes `MSC_VITA_CORE3=1` and `2`). `1`: the frame-send worker (the End Frame task's send of the finished picture, plans C and A) runs on the fourth core, where the strikersVita port puts its send thread, so core 1 keeps the GL back end and the display queue. Needs the same proof as `MSC_VITA_CORE3` (the probe thread; the key alone is enough to run it, it does not move audio, mixer, disc and save work or the log writer: those stay on `MSC_VITA_CORE3=1`) and wins over `MSC_VITA_CORE_FRAMESEND`, which stays the fallback core. Without the plug-in, or if the system refuses the pin, the worker stays on the plan's core (`[vita] thread "frame send": the fourth core was refused ..., back to the plan's core`). Only placement: no tick, random number or sync record depends on a core. Not measured. Core 3 is shared with the system's own tasks and the worker's priority (`MSC_VITA_PRIO_FRAMESEND`) is above most of them: the A/B decides whether the send gets late or jittery there `2`: the same, and vitaGL's collector too (`np_vita_core3.h` `CollectorMask`), leaving core 2 to the record player in plan C as Galaxy did for its front end (a measured gain there). The collector is created on its plan core and moved once the renderer pins its library threads (`vgx_host_vita.cpp` `PinLibraryThreads`); a refusal sends it back to that core for good and logs `the fourth core was refused for "vitaGL Garbage Collector"`. A named `MSC_VITA_CORE_GC` stays the fallback core. Test: `native/platform/vita/tests/test_core3.cpp` |
| `MSC_AUDIO_RESUME=0` | | on | Do not reopen the audio output after the console slept: by default the output thread closes and opens its port again when the power callback says the console woke up, or when `sceAudioOutOutput` starts failing (the Home menu, standby), and sets the volume again. While the port is down it drops periods at the real-time pace |
| `MSC_AUDIO_STREAM_KEEP=0` | | on | 1.3. A streamed sound (music) whose reads fell behind (more than two pending: reads are issued and completed once per game frame, so a long frame or a late disc read causes it) used to be stopped for good (`STARVE` in the log). Now its voices pause where they are and resume from the same place once every read has completed; after about 6 s without that, the old stop applies. No fade on the voice itself. Reads still follow the game thread's frame (the lists are the game thread's). Host test: `make skcheck` (stalls 100/250/1000 ms, a 1 s late read; the old stop fails the 1 s cases) |
| `MSC_AUDIO_NODEV=0` | | on | 1.3. When the sound output could not be opened (the port was refused at start), the SDK's audio thread used to mix with no clock at all, a core flat out for nothing, the game's voices and streams running ahead of real time. Now the sink keeps the 3 ms pace on the system clock and tries to open the output again every 2 s (`[audio] the output device is open now: sound is back`). Host test: `make aocheck` (open failure and retry) |
| `MSC_AUDIO_PACE_SLACK=<0..5>` | periods of 16 ms | `3` (as 1.2) | 1.3. How much sound the mixer keeps queued above the queue's start level. Each period less is 16 ms less between an on-screen kick and its sound, and 16 ms less to ride out a stall of the mixer's core. Host model (`make aqcheck`, simulated clock, mixer 3x real time): queue length / shortest stall that runs it dry: 0: 23 ms / 40 ms, 1: 39 / 56, 2: 55 / 72, 3: 71 / 88, 5: 103 / 120. A dry queue is a faded gap, never a click. Console A/B: 1 or 2 against 3, listening for dropouts in a Mega Strike and with the crowd |
| `MSC_VITA_STALL_NOTICE=<s>` | seconds, `0` off | `20` | When no picture was presented for that long (not in Settings), a plain message is written onto the picture the display shows ("Still working: no new picture for N seconds", and at three times the delay what to do). Nothing is stopped; the next picture the game presents removes it. The 15 s log line and the 30 s safe-start record are separate (`MSC_VITA_STALLWATCH=0` stops the whole watch thread) |
| `MSC_TEX_PRETOUCH=0\|1\|2` | Vita plan `2` (quality), code `0` | Textures shown to the renderer ahead of their first draw. A texture is uploaded when it is first drawn, so the textures of a goal, a Mega Strike or a replay (and of the kick-off after the walk-out: 92 uploads on the host) were uploaded in play. `NpTexPretouchStep` (`NL/glx/glxTexture.cpp`; the loading scene's frames call it from `LoadingTask::Run` with a count that follows the real frame time, and the loading scene waits for it (`NpTexPretouchHold`, 3 s at most); later it is called once per rendered frame from `GameRenderTask` outside the front end) walks the texture manager's table and hands each registered texture that is not a render target's or a Mii icon's memory (`m_NpNoTouch`), not a palette texture and not shown yet to `np_gx_touch_tex` (`platform/gx/src/gx_texture.cpp`), which records `EXT_TOUCH_TEX` (kind 7, `renderer/src/vgx_ext.h`); the renderer's `TexTouch` (`vgx_texcache.cpp`) resolves it without a draw and without changing any texmap's binding (texmap 7 is lent for the call). `1`: upload only. `2`: the textures are also pinned: the budget's trim (`TexCacheEndFrame`, also the HD free pass) leaves them until the front end is back (mode 2 record, every pin released). `MSC_TEX_PRETOUCH_N=<n>` (default 4) textures a frame, `MSC_TEX_PRETOUCH_KIB=<k>` (default 0 = count only; the Galaxy port measured 2.7-5.3 ms a texture whatever its size) KiB of RGBA8 a frame (one texture at least), `MSC_TEX_PRETOUCH_MIB=<m>` (default 0 = no limit) in all, `MSC_TEX_PRETOUCH_LOG=1` a `[pretouch]` line every 64 touches. Host figures (full 90 s match, `MSC_VGX_TEXBUDGET=32`): uploads after kick-off 92 -> 0; held at the end 20.0 -> 31.5 MB; det trace and pictures unchanged. `MSC_CREATE_LOG=1` (renderer, diagnostic): a `[created]` line for every frame that makes a render target, pass object or copy texture, with the size of each. Console A/B: tools/console-1.2 run `pretouchoff`. |
| `MSC_DVD_KEEP_KIB=<KiB>` | Vita plan `8192` (quality), code `0` | Keep cache in the disc layer. The cut-scene files of a goal or a Mega Strike (`art/nis/*`, the sidekicks' `art/animation/<sidekick>/*.sanim`, `art/fe/defensive_play.fen`) were read from the card again on every goal. When the match is loading (`CharacterLoader::BuildCharacterList`, a loading screen is up; the walk-out's first cut-scene is a second chance) `NisPlayer::PrefetchMatchFiles` hands the files of the two line-ups to the background thread `dvd-keep`, in the order play needs them (goal and Mega Strike scenes, the defence HUD, the sidekicks' animation folders, the losers' reactions, the end of the game), and it reads them once into memory; the game's own asynchronous read is then answered from memory. A file that play asks for and the list missed is kept from the bytes of the game's own read (read-through: a file read front to back is put together as its pieces arrive; there is no second read of the file, so the card is not asked twice). A file is held only if it fits `MSC_DVD_KEEP_FILE_KIB` and the free heap stays above `MSC_DVD_KEEP_HEAP_MIB`; the total never passes this budget. A line-up needs about 8120 KiB (host count: 75 goal and Mega Strike scenes 3280, the defence HUD 13, six sidekick folders 3354, reactions 1354, the end of the game 117), so 4096 held only about half. The log says `[dvdkeep] queue empty: ...` with the reasons anything was left out. Answers are byte for byte what the file holds, so no step of the simulation changes. `0` turns it off. |
| `MSC_DVD_KEEP_FILE_KIB=<KiB>` | `256` | Largest single file the keep cache holds. |
| `MSC_DVD_KEEP_HEAP_MIB=<MiB>` | `12` | The keep cache stops filling when the C heap has less than this free. |
| `MSC_DVD_KEEP_RELEASE_MIB=<MiB>` | `6` | The cache gives memory back: when the C heap has less than this free (looked at twice a second by the keep thread) cached files are dropped, an earlier match's and then the longest unused first, and `[dvdkeep] released ...` is logged. A failed C++ allocation does the same through the new-handler and the allocation tries again. A plain `malloc` failure in other code is not caught. `0` turns the polling off. |
| `MSC_NIS_PREFETCH=0\|1` | `1` | Hand the cut-scene files of the match to the keep cache at the start of a match (needs `MSC_DVD_KEEP_KIB`). `[nisprefetch]` in the log says how many files were handed over and how many fit. |
| `MSC_VITA_STALL_GUARD=0\|1` | `1` | The stall notice waits at least 10 seconds, needs two looks in a row with no picture, and starts over after a wake from sleep, so a sub-second or short hitch never shows the message. `0` returns to the earlier timing. |
| `MSC_DVD_DIRECT=0\|1` | `0`, `off` | `1` on the Vita, `0` on the host | 1.3. Disc files are read with `sceIoOpen` and one `sceIoPread` per chunk straight into the game's buffer. The 1.2 path was `fopen`/`fseek`/`fread`: the SDK's libc `fread` has no direct path (its object code only copies out of the stream buffer and calls `__srefill_r`), so a read of N KiB was N/B system calls of one stream buffer B each (BUFSIZ = 1 KiB; `makebuf` takes `st_blksize` from `fstat` when that gives one, which was not checked) and a long `fseek` discarded the buffer. Same bytes (host test `test_dvd_direct`, deliberate break checked). If a raw open fails where `fopen` works, direct mode switches itself off for the run and says so. Not measured on a console |
| `MSC_DVD_CHUNK_KIB=<n>` | `0` = whole request | `512` | 1.3. The most one `sceIoPread` asks for. Console A/B: 128, 512, 0 |
| `MSC_DVD_RETRY=<n>\|off` | `0` or `off` none (1.2), `1` a single retry, `n` up to 200 | `60` | 1.3. A disc read or open that fails is tried again on a freshly opened handle (a card's handles can be invalid after the console slept; before, the first error was the fatal disc error): the first retry at once, the next ones 50 ms apart, about three seconds in all (one second until 11 Nov; the Galaxy port's bound for files the game cannot start without) (`np/dvd_retry.h`, `dvd_native.cpp` `do_read`). Nothing changes when no read fails. Tests: `test_dvd_direct` (one injected error, `MSC_DVD_FAULT=<n>`, tests only; with the retry off it is the old fatal error) and `test_dvd_retry` (the policy with fakes; a handle that stays dead for five raw calls, `MSC_DVD_FAULT_RUN=5`, tests only, recovers with the default and fails with `MSC_DVD_RETRY=1` or `0`, the deliberate breaks) |
| `MSC_DVD_RETRY_RESUME=<n>` | `0`..`400`; `0` off | `100` | Wave 14 (Galaxy 20 Nov, "music after sleep / Home"). Within 20 s of the console waking up (the power callback's wake counter, `np_vita_wake_epoch`, weak) a failing disc read or open keeps trying `n` more attempts (50 ms apart, about 5 s) before the fatal disc error: the card can take seconds to come back. Elsewhere `MSC_DVD_RETRY` alone decides; nothing changes when no read fails. Test: `test_dvd_retry --resume` (a handle dead for 80 raw calls recovers) and `MSC_DVD_RETRY_RESUME=0 test_dvd_retry --resume --expect-fail` (fails) |
| `MSC_SAFE_FILE_RETRY=<n>` | `0`..`5` | `2` | Wave 14 (Galaxy savelife review). `np_safe_file.h`: a stat or read that fails is asked again (`n` times, 30 ms apart) before a file is called missing or damaged, so a card that hiccups after a wake-up cannot make the newest save be set aside and the older `.bak` put in its place; a stat that still cannot tell makes `Recover` answer `Found::Undecided`, and `Replace` / `BeginWrite` then write nothing (the file is left exactly as it is). `ENOENT` is answered at once. Test: `test_safe_file` (a stat that errs twice / always, a read that errs twice / always); mutants `-DNP_SAFEFILE_BREAK_UNKNOWN` (7 failures) and `-DNP_SAFEFILE_BREAK_RETRY` (3) |
| `MSC_FD_GUARD=0` | `0` off | on | Wave 14 (Galaxy `fd_guard.cpp`). `-Wl,--wrap=_lseek_r` (`np_stdio_lock.cpp`): a seek on a file handle with no record answers `EBADF` instead of the data abort at address 4 in the C library's `_lseek_r` (a start-up crash on Galaxy); logged as `[io] a seek on file handle N ...` (three times). Every other seek goes on unchanged |
| `MSC_LOG_DISPLAY_DROP=0` | `0` off | on | Wave 14 (Galaxy `DisplayDropRule`). The display-queue callback thread (4 KiB stack) never writes the log to the memory card: when the log buffer is full a line from that thread is dropped and counted (`[log] N display-thread line(s) dropped (buffer full)`, written once by the next ordinary line); other threads still flush the buffer themselves. `vgx_host_vita.cpp` `DisplayCallback` names the thread once (`NpLogMarkNoCardWrite`, weak) |
| `MSC_BOOTIO_INJECT=<n>` | `0`.. | `0` | 1.3 tests only. The first n attempts of `np_boot_fopen` / the disc table's open fail with EIO, which proves the start-up retry (`platform/vita/src/np_stdio_lock.cpp`, `tests/run_stdio_lock_test.sh`). Not a switch for players. |
| `MSC_BOOTIO_MS=<ms>` | `0`.. | `3000` | 1.3. The bound of the start-up retry of a file the game cannot start without (50 ms apart; each failure is logged as `[bootio]`); the loading screen shows "retrying the game files" meanwhile. The fopen lock itself has no switch: it is a fix (`-Wl,--wrap=fopen,freopen,fdopen,tmpfile,fclose` in `vita.mk`). |
| `MSC_BOOT_ART=0` | `0` = off, anything else on | ON (a change to the boot screen's picture only; no simulation, no gameplay file) | art25. A package without `boot/loading.bin` (the public one) shows the game's own art on the loading screen, made on the console from the player's own files (`vita_bootart.cpp`, a port of the loading picture of `tools/brand-from-disc.py`: the start-screen picture of `files/Art/fe/mainui.dmn` and the logo of `files/opening.bnr`, cropped, scaled, blurred and composited with Pillow's own arithmetic; byte for byte the tool's `boot/loading.bin`, proved on the disc copy and on made-up files by `tests/test_bootart_cpp.py`). Order (`vita_bootscreen.cpp`): the package's picture if it has one (unchanged), else `ux0:data/mscharged/cache/loading.bin` when its 16-byte trailer (format version, sizes of `mainui.dmn` and `opening.bnr`) matches the files now, else the plain screen at once and a thread `np_bootart` (core 2, priority 190, 96 KiB stack, behind the screen's own and the game's) builds the picture, writes the cache (tmp + rename) and hands it over; the screen fades to it over 0.5 s (at once when vitaGL owns the display or the end of the loading screen has begun) and the picture becomes the background. The boot never waits for it. Memory: the 1 MiB picture plus a bounded temporary (host measure: 4.74 MB peak, all of it freed when the build ends; the screen's own 4 MB buffers are unchanged). Time: host 60-90 ms, an estimate of 0.6-1.5 s on the Vita's 444 MHz core. A missing, cut or damaged file means the plain screen, as before, with one `[boot] picture: plain, <why>` line; success logs `[boot] picture: made from the game files in N ms, cache written, fading in`, and a later start `[boot] loading screen: the picture made from the game files (cache)`. `0` skips the cache and the build (the plain screen, as before art25). A change to the picture the code makes bumps `kFormatVersion` in `vita_bootart.h`, which makes old caches rebuild. Nothing of the game's art is ever in the package, the repository or a log |
| `MSC_LIVEAREA_ART=0\|1\|force` | `0` = off, `1` = default on in player builds, `force` = rebuild even over custom art | ON in player builds (`NP_PLAYER`), off in lab builds unless `force` | v2.0 (`vita_livearea_art.cpp`, C++17 port of `tools/brand-from-disc.py` `art33`, tested by `tests/test_livearea_art_cpp.py` and verified on physical PS Vita hardware). On first boot of the plain public VPK (and after any unbranded VPK update), runs sequentially after `vita_bootart` on the low-priority core-2 `np_bootart` thread to generate SceShell-compatible 8-bit palette-indexed PNGs (`icon0.png` 128x128, `bg.png` 840x500, `startup.png` 280x158, `pic0.png` 960x544) directly from the player's own extracted disc (`opening.bnr`, `Art/fe/mainui.dmn`, `Art/fe/superabilityui.res`, `Art/fe/fonts/engfontheading36.res`), writes them atomically to `ur0:appmeta/<TITLE_ID>/` (4/4 verified writable on-device; `ux0:app/<TITLE_ID>/` is read-only while running), and records `ux0:data/mscharged/cache/livearea_art.stamp` with the generated icon's CRC-32 so subsequent boots skip in <1 ms. Only replaces the shipped generic CBOC-Vita icon (CRC-32 guarded) or a prior auto-generated icon whose stamp matches, so custom user/installer artwork is never overwritten. Zero game artwork is shipped in the VPK or repository |
| `MSC_DVD_PREOPEN=0\|1` | `1` | off | 1.3 A/B. A helper thread (`dvd-open`) opens the file of each request as it is queued, so the worker finds the handle ready (eight slots; a file already open is not asked for). Hides the open time (the code's own comment measured 4-8 ms for a stat on the memory card) behind the previous read. Needs `MSC_DVD_DIRECT` |
| `MSC_DVD_TRACE=1` | | off | One log line per disc read (clock, file, offset, size) and, in the file layer, how long a synchronous read spun until the disc delivered and how many yields a request waited for one of the 64 queue entries |

**Local multiplayer (LAN lockstep)**

| Switch | Values | Default | What it does |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `MSC_LAN_EXACT_BUILD=0\|1` | `0` = the fingerprint without the build | `1` | 1.3: the multiplayer fingerprint (and `X-Msc-Build`) carries the build: `-DNP_BUILD_ID="<source hash>"` when the build passes one, else the game version, so two builds never pair and meet a sync error mid-match; they get the "different version" sentence instead. `0` on both consoles is for mixed-build A/B runs. |
| `MSC_LAN_DIGEST_EXT=0\|1` | `0` = the game's own checksum only | `1` | In LAN sessions the lockstep checksum also folds in score, ball owner and last touch, clock, power-ups, tangibility, pick-up timers and the random state. Both consoles run this build (the fingerprint says so), so equal states still compare equal. The last ticks' fields are dumped on a DESYNC |
| `MSC_LAN_INPUT_DELAY=<1..20>\|auto` | ticks | unset = the game's own constants (start 4.0, floor 2.0: settles at 2 after about 17 calm ticks) | The host chooses the input delay and sends it in the game-start message; every console stores it and uses it as the START VALUE AND FLOOR of the router's congestion multiplier (delay19, `np_delay19.h`), so the chosen delay holds for the match and only congestion raises it (+0.3 per congested message, -0.06 per calm one, down to the choice, ceiling 10 or the choice). It is not a fixed delay. LAN protocol `MSCLAN/4`. `auto`: 1 + ceil((round trip / 2 + margin) / tick), at least 2 |
| `MSC_LAN_DRAIN=0\|1` | `1` = read the datagrams that already arrived before the step loop | `0` | delay19: `NetworkSocket::ReceiveOnly` once a frame (the receive loop only, never the transport's `Update`, which has frame-counted state and sends an ack per call); LAN / online only. Changes when a step runs, not what it computes. Off until the two-console runs; the `[mp-lan] net` line shows `drain=on/runs/datagrams` and `starved=` |
| `MSC_LAN_RETRY_US=<0..8000>` | microseconds | `0` (off) | delay19: a step that would starve waits up to N us a frame, receiving only (`NpLanRetryWait`), before it is dropped; refused with `MSC_SIMT=1`. The congestion flag is evaluated before and after the wait, so the multiplier still sees the starve. `[mp-lan] net`: `retry=<N>us/tries/ok/us` |
| `MSC_LAN_EXCLUSIVE=0\|1` | `0` = the old behaviour | `1` | One radio mode at a time: a Same-Wi-Fi session does not start while a Nearby session's ad hoc radio is still up or closing, and the other way round |
| `MSC_LAN_INPUT_TRACE=1`, `MSC_LAN_WIRE_SELFTEST=1`, `MSC_LAN_DIGEST_TRACE=<tick>`, `MSC_LAN_TEST_DIGEST=1`, `MSC_LAN_TEST_MUTATE=<tick>:<field>`, `MSC_LAN_TEST_NOFLOOR=1` (delay19 negative control: one console keeps the game's own start and floor, the pair must desync), `MSC_LAN_TEST_SUSPEND_NOTICE=1` | | off | Test hooks for the pair harness and the unit tests, never set on a console: captured against applied input records (`tools/mp/input_compare.py`); a byte-exact write/read, truncation and garbage test of the game-start message; the values folded into the checksum at one tick; changing one field of one console's state (`score`, `owner`, `clock`, `powerup`, `tangible`, `megastrike`, `rng`, `timer`) so the other must report DESYNC; and a suspend notice that arrives in time |

**Online play**

The transport lives in `native/platform/sdk/net/online/` and `vlink_online.cpp`, the signaling client in `HttpSignal`, the HTTPS client in
`np_https*` ([ONLINE-TLS.md](ONLINE-TLS.md)); the service is in `server/` ([README](../server/README.md), [PROTOCOL](../server/PROTOCOL.md)).
MULTIPLAYER opens the host/join flow; Online Central uses the account, ranking and leaderboard APIs.
Vita builds default to `https://strikers.psvita.dev` and fall back to `https://msc-online.cboc-vita.workers.dev` (the same Worker; see `MSC_ONLINE_HOST` and docs/ONLINE-TLS.md "Service hosts"), which also serves the
[web leaderboard](https://strikers.psvita.dev/leaderboard). Desktop builds have no default service URL, so tests cannot contact it accidentally.
These switches are for development; times are milliseconds on the transport's clock (0 = no limit unless noted).

| Switch | Values | Default | What it does |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `MSC_NET_MODE=host\|join` | | unset | Start an online session without the menu (a host makes a room, a joiner needs the code) |
| `MSC_NET_INVITE=<code>`, `MSC_NET_INVITE_FILE=<file>` | | unset | The room code a joining test process uses, or (desktop only) a file the host's process writes it to |
| `MSC_ONLINE_URL=<url>` | | Vita: live service; desktop: unset | Service URL used by the game's account and menu flow; an explicit setting overrides `servers.json` and the compiled default |
| `MSC_ONLINE_HOST=auto\|new\|old` | | `auto` | Which built-in service address: `auto` = strikers.psvita.dev first, and the old workers.dev address when the new one does not resolve, connect or handshake (never on a certificate error), and also when the new name answers with something that is not our service (an HTML / non-JSON challenge, rate-limit or block page from the shared Cloudflare zone: the body must start with `{`; our own JSON errors, 429 and 503 included, are final); unset or unknown = `auto`; `new` = only the new one; `old` = only the old one. Testing aid; an `MSC_ONLINE_URL` is used as written |
| `MSC_NET_REQLOG=1` | | off | One `[np-net] req N T+s.s METHOD /path host=new\|old\|other -> STATUS ms reused=R` line per request of the online service (the HTTP client and the DWC transport); path ids print as `:id`; pollScale, and code / level / retryAfterSec of an error answer, are listed when present; no body, query or token is ever logged. For the online runs of the console kit (`online_check.py`) |
| `MSC_NET_SERVER=<url>`, `MSC_NET_TOKEN=<token>`, `MSC_NET_APP=<ver>`, `MSC_NET_BUILD=<text>` | | unset; app `1.2.0` | Desktop stand-in for the service account set by the menu: base URL (plain `http://` to the local fake is allowed there), sign-in token, app version and build text sent with each call |
| `MSC_NET_SIGNAL_DIR=<dir>` | | unset | Desktop signaling through files in a folder instead of the service (two test processes on one machine) |
| `MSC_NET_STUN=host:port,host:port` | | `stun.cloudflare.com:3478,stun.cloudflare.com:53` | The STUN servers asked for this console's public address |
| `MSC_NET_PORT=<n>` | | `25110` | First local UDP port of the online socket. If it is refused or taken, the next 7 are tried, then port 0 (the system chooses; STUN learns the public mapping and the local candidate reads the real port back). The log says `bind to UDP port N failed (0x...)` for each refusal and `UDP port N bound (asked A, K binds tried)` for the one that worked. Why not 41000: the Vita refuses chosen UDP ports from about 32768 up with `0x8041010D` (`SCE_NET_ERROR_EACCES`), measured on a console by the Galaxy port (23 Nov); the old default could never bind there. The server never sees or needs this port (it takes whatever STUN reports). |
| `MSC_LINK_PORT=<n>` | | `25100` | First UDP port of the Same Wi-Fi link (console and desktop). On the console, a refused or taken port falls back to the next 3 (25101..25103), then port 0; every console broadcasts to the first port (`MSC_LINK_BCAST` lists `ip:port` targets and overrides) and replies go to the sender's real port, so a console on a fallback port is still found. Both consoles of a pair must use the same first port: builds that disagree on it (a desktop partner still on 41000) do not hear each other; set `MSC_LINK_PORT=25100` on an older desktop build. Clear of Galaxy's 24100-24110. |
| `MSC_NET_PUNCH_INTERVAL_MS`, `MSC_NET_PUNCH_TIMEOUT_MS` | ms | 500, 8000 | Hole-punch probe interval and the time after which a pair gives up |
| `MSC_NET_HOPELESS_MS`, `MSC_NET_SYM_RELAY=0\|1` | ms; flag | 1200; `0` | When two symmetric NATs make a direct path impossible, give up after this long; `1` = go straight to "relay needed" instead of trying |
| `MSC_NET_KEEPALIVE_MS`, `MSC_NET_STALE_MS`, `MSC_NET_PEER_LOST_MS` | ms | 2000, 3000, 25000 | Idle send interval; silence after which every candidate is probed again; silence after which the peer is lost (never before the game's own keep-alive limit of `MSC_LAN_KEEPALIVE_MS`) |
| `MSC_NET_POLL_BACKOFF=0\|1` | flag | `1` | A failed room poll waits 2, 4, 8, 16 s as before, then 15, 30 and 60 s (was 15 s for ever), with +-12.5 percent jitter from the third failure on, so a service that is down is not asked every 15 s by every console and consoles that lost it together do not return in step (Galaxy port's rule). `0` = the old 15 s ceiling and no jitter. The retryAfterSec in an error answer is clamped where it is read (0 to 86400) and a retry wait it causes is at most 60 s: no undefined `int(double)`, no wrapped `uint32 * 1000`. Host test `native/tools/net/retry_after_test.cpp` |
| `MSC_NET_POOLS_AT_BOOT=0\|1` | flag | `0` | The two network memory pools of the retail game (700 KB for DWC, 200 KB for the socket library) are made when something first asks for them, which this port never does (its DWC and socket layers ignore the allocator they are given), so single player holds 0.9 MB more of the virtual arena. `1` makes both at boot as the retail game did (the A/B switch; the log prints `[netpool] ... at boot` or `at its first use` when one is made) |
| `MSC_ONLINE_SAVE_ALWAYS=0\|1` | flag | `0` | Leaving internet play saves the single-player card only when this session changed something (the win / loss counters the stats manager marks as changed and has not yet saved) and never after an error (link lost, desync, certificate or clock refusal, stats service failure). `1` is the retail rule: always save. LAN never saves. The log prints `[onlinesave] leaving online: changed=.. error=.. always=.. -> save / no save` |
| `MSC_NET_TRY_LOCAL=0\|1` | | `0` | Probe the peers' private addresses even when the public addresses differ |
| `MSC_NET_RESOLVE_LIMIT_MS`, `MSC_NET_ROOM_LIMIT_MS`, `MSC_NET_HOST_WAIT_MS`, `MSC_NET_REBUILD_LIMIT_MS` | ms | 8000, 15000, 600000, 45000 | Stage limits: DNS of the STUN servers (the session goes on without STUN), the room must exist, the host waiting alone, back to ready after a lost link. Each ends with a sentence for the player |
| `MSC_NET_RELAY_WAIT_MS` | ms | 28000 | After direct traversal requests a relay, keep pumping while the other relay address, channel binding or encrypted confirmation is pending. Either address-publication order works. `0` disables this grace; it never extends the credential, DNS, rebuild or menu deadlines |
| `MSC_NET_STATS_MS=<ms>`, `MSC_NET_MAX_PACKETS=<n>` | | 5000 (0 = off), 64 | Link-quality log line interval (round trip, jitter and loss from an authenticated once-a-second ping); datagrams handled per pump The line ends with `longest send gap N ms` (netclient13, O5: the longest time between two datagrams on the connected path, i.e. the quietest stretch the Wi-Fi radio saw; 25 ms in a running match, about 1000 ms in a lobby on the host). |
| `MSC_NET_DROP=<%>`, `MSC_NET_DUP=<%>`, `MSC_NET_DELAY_MS=<ms>`, `MSC_NET_JITTER_MS=<ms>` | | off | Fault shim on this console's sends: loss, duplicates, delay, jitter. Works on the console and on the desktop |
| `MSC_NET_LOG_ADDRS=1` | | off | Log full public addresses; by default the last two parts of a public IPv4 address are hidden on the Vita |
| `MSC_NET_SIM=<ip:port>`, `MSC_NET_SIM_ID=<n>`, `MSC_NET_SIM_PRIV=<ip>`, `MSC_NET_LOCAL_IP=<ip>` | | off | Desktop only: route every datagram through the NAT simulator in `native/tools/net`, with a client id and a private address in the simulated LAN; or set the local candidate without it |
| `MSC_NET_SELFTEST=https://host/path` | | off | Four seconds after start-up one diagnostic HTTPS `GET`, with timings, cipher and certificate details in the log (see [ONLINE-TLS.md](ONLINE-TLS.md)); independent of normal online API requests |
| `MSC_TEST_SERVER=<url>`, `MSC_TEST_TOKEN0`, `MSC_TEST_TOKEN1` | | unset | Test only: `tools/net/online_test` runs the signaling flow against the local fake of the service (`run_signal_http_test.sh` sets them) |

**Diagnostics (change no picture and no result)**

| Switch | Values | Default | What it does |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `MSC_VGX_GENSTAT=1` | | off | At exit: how many shaders the generator wrote, their bytes, lines, operators, texture reads and discards |
| `MSC_VGX_GEOMCENSUS=1` | | off | Every 300 frames: skinned draws, bone counts and palette bytes, static and arena draws, and the cull test's counts |
| `MSC_VGX_MERGECENSUS=1` | | off | A count of draw records that differ only in their uniform blocks |
| `MSC_VGX_WAITSTAT=1` | | off | Every 600 presents: how often the idle player went to sleep and how many sleeps ended by the 1 ms time-out |
| `MSC_IRQ_WAITSTAT=1` | | off | 1.3. Every 5 s an `[irq]` line: how often and how long guest threads (the game thread, the network threads) waited in `OSDisableInterrupts` for the interrupt lock, how long the thread giving it up spun for a waiting handler, and how long the AX frame callback (every 3 ms, on the audio thread) held it. The game takes the lock around its audio calls; Strikers has no guest sound thread, so this is all the game thread pays for audio. Console run: `irqwait` |
| `MSC_NETCENSUS=1` | | off | Every 300 frames: vertices rebuilt per call of the two net meshes (goal nets) |
| `MSC_STADIUM_CENSUS=1` | | off | One block in the log at each stadium load: the world's objects by type (drawables, effects, lights, shadow volumes, high-range objects and so on), its packets and vertices. Compares stadiums without profiling; draws nothing differently |
| `MSC_BOOT_MATCH=<stadium>[:<captainA>:<captainB>][:<mode>]` | | off (TEST ONLY: no player default, no Settings row; `0` / `off` = off) | Fast boot for emulator and console test runs (wave 10, fastboot10). From the title screen it starts a match the way the `launch` script command does (`GameInfoManager::SetMode` / `SetStadium` / `SetTeam` / `ResetPlayingSides` / `SetPlayingSide`, then `PushLoadingScene(true)`, `np_harness.cpp` `BootMatchTick`, `LaunchMatch`): the title is not left through the main menu, the captain, sidekick, side and stadium screens, so a tour run saves about 2,200 of its 4,100 pictures (the menus were 12 of its 19 emulator minutes; measured on the emulator, an estimate: title at f=160, play at f=307 after 60.7 s, where the menu route reached play at f=2633 after about 800 s, so about 12 minutes saved per run). Stadium 0..17 or a name prefix (`vice`, `dump`, `lava`, `pipe`, `sand`), captains 0..11 (default 3 v 6, Donkey Kong v Waluigi), mode `friendly` (default) / `demo` / `cup` / `challenge` / 0..4. `MSC_BOOT_MATCH_OPTS="time=180 skill=0 powerup=10 hsk=0,1,2 ask=5,6,7"` adds `launch` keys (without them the game's own defaults, as the menus leave them); `MSC_BOOT_MATCH_SEED=<hex>|off` the simulation seed written at the launch (default `12345678`, the game's start value; a menu-started match reaches kick-off with another seed, because menu frames and the longer load draw from it, so the two do not give the same match; `MSC_SIM_SEED=<hex>` rewrites the seed until the first step in either start so they can be compared, and even then the later steps differ, as the sim also draws from frame timing). Until the title it presses A (wrist-strap screen, intro movie); the Vita env file did not deliver this variable, so a route's first line `bootmatch <spec> [launch keys]` sets it (and `MSC_BOOT_MATCH_OPTS`) instead; an `MSC_INPUT_SCRIPT` route then starts at the launch (write it without the title and menu lines: `tools/gen_fb_routes.py` makes `tools/console-1.2/fastboot/routes/fb-*.txt` and `env/fb-*.txt` from the console routes); without a route the pad is the player's after the launch. **No save is ever written while it is on**: `nand_native.cpp` drops every NAND create / delete / move / write-open / write (reports success, nothing reaches the files, `boot: NAND ... dropped` lines). Log lines `boot: ...`, `boot: play began in <stadium> after N s`; off prints no `boot:` line and changes nothing (one flag test per NAND call, one pointer test per picture). Test: `tests/test_fastboot.py` |
| `MSC_DOF_LOG=1` | | off | One line each time the depth-of-field effect turns on or off |
| `MSC_CLOCK_STATS=1` | | off | Desktop harness: counts every read of the port's clocks per thread and caller (audit of what depends on time) |
| `MSC_SAMPLER=1` (`MSC_SAMPLER_US=<us>` 1000, `MSC_SAMPLER_SECS=<s>` 10, `MSC_SAMPLER_THREADS=<name part>[,..]`) | `0`, off in code and plan | diag13 diagnostic, changes no picture or simulation. An on-console pc sampling profiler (`native/platform/vita/src/vita_sampler.cpp`, pure parts `np_sampler_core.h`, test `tests/test_sampler_core.cpp`; the method is the NFS Underground 2 Vita port's sampler, MIT, see `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md`). A thread on cores 1-2 (never the system core), priority 140, wakes every `MSC_SAMPLER_US` (at least 500) and, for one target per tick round robin (the game thread, plus up to 3 found by a part of their name), reads the thread's state: waiting threads are counted by (wait type, wait id) and left alone; a running one is suspended with `sceKernelSuspendThreadForVM`, its pc and lr read with `sceKernelGetThreadContextForVM`, and resumed at once (nothing else happens while it is stopped). The register layout of the raw context is undocumented: at start a thread spinning in `msc_sampler_spin` with a sentinel in lr is suspended once, the word inside the function is the pc, the sentinel word the lr, and the buffer sizes are probed (logged: `[sampler] context layout: pc word N, lr word M`). If the suspend is refused or the layout is not found the sampler goes on with waits only and never suspends a game thread. Tables are fixed (4096 pcs, 2048 lrs a target, 64 waits), no allocation after start. Every window: `[sampler] window N target T "name" S s: ...` then the top 40 `pc 0xADDR count percent`, top 12 `lr`, top 8 `wait type id count`, then `[sampler] cost:` (ticks a second, how many wake-ups came 3 intervals late, mean and longest suspend-to-resume time, the share of the target's time stopped, the sampler thread's own CPU share). The import stubs are strong: `-lSceLibKernel_stub` (GetThreadContextForVM) and the existing ThreadMgr stub (Suspend / Resume) are in `vita.mk`. Host tool: `tools/console-1.2/symbolize_samples.py --log console.log --elf mscharged-np.elf` (llvm-symbolizer / addr2line, load base from the logged `msc_sampler_anchor` address). gxp17 (23 Nov): `MSC_SAMPLER_BIN=0\|1` (default `1` once the sampler is on) also keeps a histogram of every (target, pc, lr) pair and waits (`np_sampler_bin.h`, 32768 entries, 512 KiB, allocated at start) and writes it at the end of each window to `ux0:data/mscharged/sampler-<n>.bin` (the first free number: a new run never overwrites an old one), with the layout, run and wait counts, per-target suspend hold times and the sampler thread's CPU; `MSC_SAMPLER_TAG=<text>` labels the run in the header. `tools/console-1.2/sampler_report.py ELF sampler-*.bin --top 40` merges the files and prints, per thread, the run / wait split (wait by object type), subsystems by pc and by pc plus the caller found in lr, the top functions self and inclusive-by-lr, and the sampler's own cost (load offset from `msc_sampler_anchor`). Host tests: `tests/run_sampler_bin_test.sh` (histogram, writer, report on a synthetic .bin and ELF, 5 mutants). Console runs: see the gxp17 note. |
| `MSC_DQ_TIMING=1` | `0`, off in code and plan | diag13 diagnostic (R1), changes no picture. Two clock reads a picture on the render thread around `vglSwapBuffers` alone (the display queue taking the buffer; it blocks while the queue is full; one read is shared with `MSC_HITCH_MS` / `MSC_PACE_LOG` / `MSC_VGX_BEPH` when one of them is on). Adds: ` dq <ms>` at the end of the split of each `[hitch] gap=` line (the frame before the gap; with `MSC_HITCH_MS`), a `[hitch] window: display queue (vglSwapBuffers alone, MSC_DQ_TIMING): n swaps, mean, longest, k at 8 ms or more, j at 33 ms or more` line with each 1800-frame summary, and ` | display queue n swaps mean m max x ms, k at 8 ms or more, j at 33 ms or more` at the end of each `[late]` line. `swap=` (swap plus the GPU-queue limiter) less `dq` is the GPU-queue wait. Counters are cumulative atomics read as windows (`vgx_host_dq_window`, one cursor for `[hitch]` and one for `[late]`). |
| `MSC_HITCH_MS=<ms>` | ms, `0` off | `25` in the Vita plan (smooth, 11 Nov), off in code | 1.3 diagnostic, changes no picture. A line `[hitch]   alloc: n allocator walks over 1000 free blocks around that frame, longest m` follows when the game thread's first-fit scan (`MemoryAllocator::AllocateFromStart`) was long in that frame (counters in `MemAlloc.cpp`, no allocation changes). A `[hitch] gap=<ms> <cause> swap=<ms> scene=<n>` line, followed by the `[mega-cut]` fields, for each frame gap of that length or more (three budgets of 240: menus and loading; live play, marked ` live`; held scenes, cut-scenes and replays, marked ` held`, of which a quarter may be used before the first live-play picture so goal cuts and replays always find lines; `MSC_HITCH_LINES=<n>` sets all three caps). Wave 11 appends ` | split pmax v<ms> f<ms> rt<ms> gc <n>/<ms> pool <vram>/<ram>/<cdram>K pt-var <n> other <ms>` to the same line (no new lines): the longest single vitaGL patcher vertex-program create, fragment-program create and render-target create of the picture (patch 0035), GPU memory recoveries (0036) and their time, the change of free KiB in each vitaGL pool since the previous picture (which pool the picture's new pass surfaces and textures came from), patcher creates less new vertex+fragment variants (0: each create has its variant) and the back-end time that nothing above names (back end less swap, shader work and the three patcher timers), charged to the frame before the gap, and a summary by cause every 1800 frames. Causes, first match wins: shader, target, texture, wait (the game thread waited for the disc), display (the swap), backend, upstream (the frame came late from the game thread or the front end). `vgx_hitch.h`. The console kit uses 45 | Wave 9: a gap is a hitch only against the cadence in force (the larger of `MSC_HITCH_MS` and 1.45 x the picture period of the policy: a held 30's 33.4 ms gaps are not hitches; they used up the 240 live lines in 8 s), the summary counts the gaps that were only the held cadence and folds the hitches by frame % 600 in 60-frame bins.
| `MSC_LOAD_TRACE=1` | | off | 1.3 diagnostic. One `[load]` line per step of the game's loader: ms, frames, disc reads, the game thread's time in zlib, byte-order conversion. One clock read per step and per chunk inflated |
| `MSC_MEGA_CUTLOG=1\|2` | `1` the frames around the cuts, `2` every frame | off | `[mega-cut]` lines: for the 12 frames before a cut into or out of a Mega Strike scene, the cut and the 40 after it, and a sum over the cut's nine frames: the back end's time (`be`), the gap since the previous frame (`gap`), a size change's CPU time (`rz`, the target's making in brackets), render targets made / taken back / kept / dropped, pass objects made and deleted, copy textures resized or made, texture uploads, shader compiles, links and variants, disc reads and the ones the game thread waited for, audio sources made, free video memory. Real milliseconds on the console. Counting costs nothing when off |

## Clocks And Memory: What Is Used (1.3 audit)

Clocks. The game requests CPU 444, bus 222, GPU 222, GPU crossbar 166 MHz once, in `SetUpCpu` before any game thread (`np_vita_clock.h`). These are the top of every stock table: VitaSDK's `psp2kern/power.h` (FW 3.60: GPU 1-222, quantised to 41, 55, 83, 111, 166, 222, core and MP together; xbar 1-166; bus 1-222; ARM 1-444) and PSVshell's own step lists, which also stop at GPU 222, bus 222, xbar 166. Nothing is left on the GPU side. The system default is 333/111/222/111, so a game that did not ask would run at half the fill. Galaxy logs from this console: rc 0 on all three and reports of 222/222/166. A report is not a measurement of the GPU clock; kit run `gpu166` measures it.

500 MHz. An application's own request fails (Galaxy: `0x802B0000`). PSVshell or PSVshellPlus reaches it in the kernel; a profile changes the CPU by about 12 % and nothing for fill. `session/psvshell/gen_profiles.py` writes lock `0x0b` (CPU, GPU, xbar): then the game's requests answer 0 and change nothing, so `MSC_VITA_GPU_MHZ` runs need a title without that profile. A profile applies per title id or through the global profile. With a profile the getter can read 333 while the core runs 498: `measure` policy and both watches are built on that.

Suspend. No source says outright that a stock system restores a process's requests. PSVshell reads them from per-process storage, which suggests it does; PSVshellPlus 1.1 fixed a reset of its own. `MSC_VITA_CLOCK_KEEP` looks and logs instead of assuming.

Power. Forum figures only, none measured here: 300 mA to 400 mA at 444/222 with low brightness, the GPU step about 30 mA; "5 to 6 hours" against "3.5 to 4.5". 444 MHz was the retail mode with Wi-Fi off, so online play at 444 is outside what retail games did, though homebrew does it routinely. A `dmid` reference in the kit shows drift from heat.

Memory. CDRAM is the GPU's fast path (a wide I/O die on the chip package, behind the crossbar clock); main memory (LPDDR2) is behind the bus clock. In vitaGL's terms `for_gpu` tries CDRAM first, `for_cpu` main memory first, uncached.

| What | Where | Note |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Display colour, framebuffer object colour, depth, stencil | CDRAM | Right |
| Textures | CDRAM, then main memory when the pool is full | Right. Render targets and uncompressed textures linear; DXT swizzled |
| Static vertex and index buffers (`g_staticUsage`) | CDRAM | Right |
| Parameter buffer | CDRAM (no `PB_LPDDR` flag) | 16 MiB, `MSC_VITA_PB_MIB` |
| VDM, vertex, fragment rings; patcher's program buffer (8 MiB) | CDRAM | Right. The comment at the patcher sizes calls this main memory; it is not |
| Render target driver data | Uncached main memory | sceGxm's own |
| Frame vertex and index data | Uncached main memory, circular pool | CPU writes about 0.25 GB/s (5 Oct). `streamcdram`, `streamcached` |
| Uniform ring | Uncached main memory | `ringcdram` |
| Shader code | Uncached main memory | `MSC_VITA_USSE_CDRAM`, `ussecdram` |

Tiler. Tile size is fixed (32 x 32 in sceGxm's surface alignment). A partial render has no counter on a retail console; `pb32` and `pb8` are the test. Framebuffer object targets are created for 8 scenes a frame, the display for 1 (`dscn2`). The textures' caches are small (the SGX543MP4+'s TCU: 512 B first level, 8 KiB second, 256 KiB shared system level cache; 32-byte lines).

Sources: [VitaSDK power header](https://github.com/vitasdk/vita-headers/blob/master/include/psp2kern/power.h), [PSVshell README](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/Electry/PSVshell/master/README.md), [PSVshell oc.c](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/Electry/PSVshell/master/src/oc.c), [PSVshellPlus kernel](https://github.com/GrapheneCt/PSVshellPlus/blob/main/PSVshellPlus_Kernel/main.c), [PSVshellPlus notes](https://gamebrew.org/wiki/PSVshellPlus_Vita), [Vita GPU overview](https://playstationdev.wiki/psvitadevwiki/Graphics), [package teardown](https://semiconductor-digest.com/sonys-ps-vita-uses-chip-on-chip-sip-3d-but-not-3d/amp), [Series 5 guide on partial renders](https://ftp.radix-linux.su/3pp/Imagination/doc/SGX544/PowerVR/PowerVR%20Series%205.Architecture%20Guide%20for%20Developers.pdf), [twiddled layout, Imagination forum](https://forums.imgtec.com/t/twiddling-algorithm-complexity/853), [forum power figures](https://lr.in.psf.lt/r/vitahacks/comments/1e5l6at/overclocking).

## Settings Screen (Performance And Graphics)

**Where the settings are (set18, owner 23 Nov: "the settings menu is native in game and no longer on the home live screen").** Every player setting is in the game's own menu:
OPTIONS > VITA SETTINGS in the front end and VITA SETTINGS in the pause menu (not in a LAN or online session). The model of that screen is
`native/platform/sdk/net/np_native_settings.cpp` (five pages of at most five rows, most used first; the scene is `SHVitaSettings.cpp`). The rows, their choices and what each
writes are `kTable` in `native/platform/vita/src/np_settings.cpp`, which also reads and writes `np-player.txt` for both screens (byte for byte the same file).
The LiveArea Settings tile is gone (`template.xml` content-rev 5). A console that still shows the old tile starts the game with `-settings`: the game starts normally
(a log line says where the settings are) and a press of the old tile while the game runs is logged and dropped. The port's own pixel screen is left with two pages only:
**SAFE START**, opened by holding L + R while the game starts (the fatal screen says "Hold L + R at the next start to reset the settings."): CORE PLAN, RESET TO DEFAULTS,
VERSION, BUILD, SETTINGS FILE, GAME FILES, then SAVE AND START / SAVE AND EXIT. It is the way out when a setting stops the game from starting, not a settings menu.
**ONLINE**, opened from the game's menu (VITA SETTINGS > ONLINE ACCOUNT): ONLINE NAME, SEND A REPORT, SAVE A REPORT, DELETE MY ONLINE ACCOUNT (these need the pixel screen's own dialogs).

The game's menu (each row is "NAME: VALUE", at most 24 characters in English, Spanish and French; each has one line of help, `NpNativeSettings::Help`):

| Page | Row (name in the menu) | Choices (first is the default and writes nothing) | Lines |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| MAIN | Unlock All Characters (ALL CHARACTERS) | ON, OFF | `MSC_UNLOCK_CAPTAINS=0` |
| MAIN | Unlock All Stadiums (ALL STADIUMS) | OFF, ON | `MSC_UNLOCK_STADIUMS=1` |
| MAIN | Frame Rate | SMOOTH 60 (DEFAULT), 30 LOCKED | `MSC_FPS=30`. SMOOTH 60 is `MSC_LOCK60=1`, which the plan sets by itself (`kLock60Default` in `vita_plan.cpp`, 23 Nov console A/B); the row follows `VitaPlanDefaults::kLock60` (`vita_plan_defaults.h`, which must say the same; `audit_settings_keys.py` checks it). While that switch is false the row is AUTO / SMOOTH 60 / 30 LOCKED and SMOOTH 60 writes `MSC_LOCK60=1`. A file's `MSC_LOCK60=1` reads as the default; `MSC_LOCK60=0` by hand reads CUSTOM |
| MAIN | Controls | MODERN, CLASSIC | `MSC_CONTROLS=classic` |
| MAIN | Language | AUTOMATIC, ENGLISH, SPANISH, FRENCH | `MSC_LANGUAGE=en\|es\|fr` (read once at start-up) |
| PICTURE | Stadium Crowd (CROWD; Performance Mode in 1.1) | SHOW, HIDE | `MSC_PERFORMANCE_MODE=1` (applies at once) |
| PICTURE | Sharpness | SMOOTH, OFF, CRISP | `MSC_VITA_UPSCALE=off\|sharp` |
| PICTURE | Depth Of Field (BLUR) | OFF (DEFAULT), ON | `MSC_DOF=lazy` when ON; the Vita plan default is `0` (`ship26`) |
| PICTURE | HD Characters | ON, OFF | `MSC_HDTEX=0` (no effect without `hdtex.pack`) |
| TOUCH | Rear Touch | WITH MODERN, ON, OFF | `MSC_REAR_TOUCH=1\|0` (applies at once) |
| TOUCH | Mega Save Aim (MEGA SAVE) | FRONT TOUCH, REAR + CROSS, STICK ONLY | `MSC_MEGA_AIM=rear`, `MSC_TOUCH_SAVE=0` (STICK ONLY is the old TOUCH MEGA SAVE OFF; a file holding it reads STICK ONLY) |
| TOUCH | Mega Save Speed (SAVE SPEED) | 3x, 4x | `MSC_MEGA_POINTER_SPEED=4` |
| TOUCH | Touch Mega Strike (TOUCH STRIKE) | ON, OFF | `MSC_TOUCH_METER=0` |
| TOUCH | Touch Shakedown | ON, OFF | `MSC_TOUCH_SHAKE=0` |
| MATCH | Reduced Camera Shake (LESS SHAKE) | OFF, ON (QUARTER SHAKE) | `MSC_REDUCED_MOTION=1` (applies at once) |
| MATCH | Mega Strikes | ON (STANDARD), OFF (BOTH MUST MATCH) | `MSC_RULE_NOMEGA=1` |
| MATCH | Original Game Fixes (GAME FIXES) | DRAWING ONLY, ALL (WITH BOO FIX) | `MSC_GAME_FIXES=all` (ALL changes the match: both Vitas must match) |
| MATCH | Pointer Speed | 1-10 (5 NORMAL) | `NP_POINTER_SPEED=0.5..2` |
| ONLINE | Connection | AUTO, RELAY ONLY | `MSC_NET_RELAY_ONLY=1` |
| ONLINE | ONLINE ACCOUNT | (opens the pixel screen's ONLINE page) | |
| ONLINE | RESET TO DEFAULTS | (every row to its default; the leave popup saves) | drops the retired keys wherever they stand |
| (SAFE START) | Core Plan | AUTO, C NO OVERLAP, PLAN A, PLAN S | `MSC_PLAN=Cn\|A\|S` (a manual `MSC_FRAMESEND`, `MSC_VGX_BACKEND_THREAD`, `MSC_VITA_CORE_FRAMESEND` or `MSC_FS_PIPE` line reads CUSTOM) |
| (no row) Start check | automatic; `MSC_CHANGE_GUARD=0` turns it off | `np-change.txt` beside `np-plan.txt`: after two failed starts of a changed Picture or Language line the standard one is used for that start |

Each row's one line of help is shown on the steel foot of the panel, under DONE, while the cursor is on the row (`NpNativeSettings::HelpLine`, drawn by
`np_notice.cpp` `RenderSettingsHelp` with the font and colour of the bars' own lettering: the pause panel package has no description text of its own, see `np_settings_help.h`).
Nothing is shown over MORE or DONE, while the panel slides or while a popup is up.

Crowd, Less Shake and Rear Touch apply at once when saved in a match; every other row applies at the next start (the leave popup says so; from OPTIONS it offers SAVE AND START AGAIN).

**set18 (owner, 23 Nov: "Remove any settings that aren't needed or are just pure negative to the experience. Simplify where able").** 29 rows became 20 (19 in the game's
menu, CORE PLAN on SAFE START). Went: RESOLUTION (a fixed size never adapts and turns the dynamic resolution, its smoothing and SMOOTH 60 off), ANTI-ALIASING (OFF only made
edges worse: the picture already gives the samples up first under load), INPUT DELAY (a tuning knob: the game's own delay adapts), TOUCH POINTER (not needed), LIVE SHADOWS (FULL only cost a pass), PICTURE FORMAT (4:3 only adds bars), MULTIPLAYER LINK
(the game's menu asks ONLINE / LOCAL and NEARBY / SAME WI-FI every time; its HOST / JOIN choice, NEARBY PAIRING, went in mp24: a hand line `MSC_LAN_DIALOG=create|join` still works), SMOOTH 60 MODE (a choice of FRAME RATE), TOUCH
MEGA SAVE (a choice of MEGA SAVE AIM), the choices FRAME RATE AUTO / UP TO 60 (SMOOTH 60 is the mode), ORIGINAL GAME FIXES OFF (the retail drawing bugs) and MEGA SAVE SPEED
ORIGINAL and 2x (the glove did not reach the far side). Nothing was removed from the game: every key keeps its reader. Old files:
- the keys of the removed rows (`MSC_VITA_RENDER`, `MSC_VGX_MSAA`, `MSC_LAN_INPUT_DELAY`, `NP_TOUCH_POINTER`, `MSC_LAN_MODE`, `MSC_SHADOW_LIVE`, `NP_WIDESCREEN`, with the 1.3 prune's) are `kRetiredKeys`: Load ignores them,
  a line inside the screen's block goes at the next save, a line outside it (the kit's, a player's own) stays byte for byte, RESET TO DEFAULTS drops it anywhere;
- the purely worse values an earlier 1.3 screen wrote into its own block (`kObsolete`: `MSC_VITA_RENDER=*`, `MSC_VGX_MSAA=0`, `MSC_GAME_FIXES=none`, `MSC_MEGA_POINTER_SPEED=1|2`,
  `MSC_FPS=auto`, `MSC_FPS_BETWEEN=pace`) are skipped by the loader at start-up with a log line (`NpSettings::WithoutObsolete`, `LoadEnvFile`), read as the row's default, and dropped
  by the next save; outside the block they are applied as written;
- a 1.0-1.2 file (no stamp or an older one) is first migrated by `np_settings_guard.cpp` as before (`np-player.before-1.3.txt` keeps the original).

**1.3 prune (owner, 15 Nov).** The Picture Mode row and its presets went; a selector `MSC_PICTURE=performance|quality` in a hand file still expands in the game (`np_picture_presets.h`);
the screen's save writes it out as the rows' lines and leaves the obsolete fixed size out. The list of rows that went and the rule for retiring one: `docs/SETTINGS.md`.

`native/platform/vita/tests/audit_settings_keys.py` checks that every key a choice writes has a reader in the code and a value that reader accepts, that the retired and obsolete keys
keep their readers, and that the FRAME RATE row's default and the plan's agree; `check_settings_effective.py` starts the desktop harness with the file each choice writes and reads the
effective setting back from the `[run]` header lines. The rows that only exist in the Vita build (Core Plan, Sharpness) are covered by
the first script and by reading the code. The menu itself: `run_native_settings_tests.py` (rows, labels in three languages, help, old files, RESET, mutants).

## Dynamic Resolution And Frame Rate

`renderer/src/vgx_backend_gl.cpp` ("DYNRES") reads the GPU time of each finished
frame from vitaGL's GPU timers (`vglRtLastGpuFrame`: one fragment notification
per render scene, a watcher thread on core 2). It moves along a ladder of
picture levels so that the GPU stays inside the frame budget of the frame rate
in force (16.7 ms at 60, 33.4 ms at 30):

| Levels | Scene scale | Large passes | When |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 0-2 | 1.20, 1.12, 1.06 | 1.0 | The GPU has had room for a full second: every frame's time projected to the next level within 80 % of the budget (10 % of the frames may exceed it, none 92 %), not within 90 presents of another change nor 5 s of a 30/60 change. Two frames over 92 % send it back to level 3 and lock these levels for 20 s (doubling within two minutes). 1.1 rule (`MSC_DYNRES_RULES=1`): under 72 % for 45 frames |
| 3 | 1.0 | 1.0 | The game's own size (the start) |
| 4-5 | 1.0 | 0.75, 0.5 | The first saving: blur and glow chains, full-screen filters |
| 6-7 | 0.93, 0.86 | 0.5 | The floor of menus and matches |
| 8-12 | 0.78 to 0.50 | 0.5 | Mega Strike scenes only (they start at level 5: the scene at full size, the effect sprites at a quarter of the pixels) |

"Large passes" are offscreen passes of at least an eighth of the frame buffer
that are not drawn into an atlas. Over 90 % of the budget it jumps straight to
the best level predicted to fit in 82 %. Copy textures and the frame buffer keep
their picture, scaled, when their size changes, so a change shows no flash.
`VgxGpuMinMs` gives the frame-rate controller (`native/platform/compat/np_fps_ctl.h`)
the GPU time predicted at the scene's floor: AUTO does not go to 60 when that is
over 80 % of a refresh, and goes back to 30 when it stays over 92 % for 4 frames
(1.1: 85 % and 97 % for 8 frames). `VgxDynResGrace` tells AUTO that the renderer
changed its picture size in the last 24 presents: the late frames around a resize are
not held against 60. `VgxPacedSceneHint` tells the renderer that the policy holds the
scene (replay, celebration, cut-scene) at 30, where it may go down to 0.70 per side.
The host's sharpening follows the scale (`VgxRenderScale`).

Effect sprites (FX): the game marks its additive sprite meshes (`np/fx.h`;
`ParticleSystem` and `BuildParticleQuads` in `main.cpp`, the path the game
actually uses), `glxSend` wraps their draws in an `EXT_FX` record, and the
back end draws a run of them into a half-size target that starts with the
scene's depth (a depth-copy pass) and adds it to the frame buffer in one
full-screen pass. Runs under 400 vertices outside Mega Strikes are drawn as
before, since a run costs two scene changes and about 1.25 screens of pixels.

With `MSC_VGX_MSAA=2|4` the three levels above the game's own size are replaced
by multisampled levels at that size (4x then 2x, or 2x alone). The scene target
is the only multisampled one: its render target and hidden depth buffer carry
the samples, the hardware averages them into the colour texture when a scene
ends, so every copy, pass and the present only ever see the resolved picture.
Multisampled levels are never used in Mega Strikes (those start at the game's
own size), and the effects pass that reads the scene's depth is not used while
the target is multisampled (that depth is not a linear texture).

Scene classes: the game reports once per frame what kind of scene it draws
(`NpFxSetScene`, an extension record `EXT_SCENE`): 1 match play, 2 replay,
3 cut-scene (NIS), 0 other (menus). The renderer uses it for
`MSC_FX_LOWRES_SCENES`, for the effects statistics and for the census.

Overdraw census (`MSC_VGX_SHCENSUS=1`, with `MSC_VGX_LEAD=1000000` on the
host so every frame is drawn): per window of `MSC_VGX_SHCENSUS_WINDOW` frames,
the screen pixels of every draw class, polygon-clipped to its scissor box,
overall and per scene class; the additive draws split by whether the game marked
them and by the length of the run they sit in; draws and triangles that cannot
change the picture; triangles outside the scissor box or under a quarter pixel.
`MSC_VGX_SHCENSUS_DRAWLIST=f1,f2` lists every draw of those frames.

On the desktop harness, `MSC_DYNRES_FAKE=a,b` simulates a GPU time and
`MSC_DYNRES_CYCLE=N` changes the level every N frames (a stress test of the
size changes); `MSC_DYNRES_FAKE=a,b,period,mult` makes the second half of every `period`
frames cost `mult` times as much (a test of the up/down rules).

### Foreground first (MSC_DYNRES_FG, W12 FG)

The game's render code draws in layer views (`RLView`, `eCLV_*`), in this order each frame: offscreen passes (the crowd impostor captures), the
stadium's opaque world objects (`Shadowed`), the crowd sprites (`ImpostorOut`), the players and goalies (`Characters`, `MoreCharacters`), far
scenery, the shadow volumes, particles, then the 2D layers (HUD). `RLView::BeginRender` tells the renderer which layer the send is about to draw
(`NpFxSetLayer`, an extension record `EXT_LAYER`, only recorded when `MSC_DYNRES_FG` or `MSC_VGX_FGCENSUS` is on); `DrawRec::cls` carries the draw
class (`np/fx.h` `NPFX_*`): players, items, effects, HUD, decorative NPCs and the cut-scene characters count as foreground, the stadium, its blended
objects, the crowd, no-fog sky and far scenery as background, and anything not listed (render-to-texture, shadow maps, movies) is unknown, which is
foreground. In Mega Strikes the far-scenery layer also holds the Mega characters, so the renderer counts it as foreground there.

With `MSC_DYNRES_FG=1` the ladder gets background steps at its last large-pass level (level 5, the game's own size with the passes at half), taken one
by one before the whole scene shrinks (levels 6 and 7), and given back after it: the whole scene first (7 -> 6 -> 5), then the steps. Steps (`MSC_DYNRES_FG_STEPS`):
`l` the stadium, crowd and sky draws sample their mipmapped textures with a LOD bias (vitaGL `GL_TEXTURE_LOD_BIAS`, raw units, console only; offered
only when `MSC_FG_LODBIAS` is set), `c` the crowd impostor sprites are refreshed half as often (`ImpostorManager::SetUpdateThrottle`; the game
already does it at half rate in play, this makes it a quarter, and in replays and cut-scenes a half). The model's guess of what a step saves is
corrected by the next measured frames (`[vgx] background step n ...` in the log). A half-resolution background layer is not built: see the notes
in `MSC_VGX_FGCENSUS`' evidence (the crowd run is one contiguous run, but its composite costs most of what it saves, and a depth upscale would put
the ground shadows, which test against the pitch's depth, at risk).

## LOCKED 60 (MSC_LOCK60, 1.3)

One switch, `MSC_LOCK60=1` (VITA SETTINGS > FRAME RATE > SMOOTH 60, the picture mode LOCKED 60; `kLock60Default` in `vita_plan.cpp` makes it the build's default, which the 23 Nov console A/B decided, and `VitaPlanDefaults::kLock60` makes FRAME RATE's first choice follow it). Render only: the simulation, random numbers and input timing are untouched, so online lockstep is not affected. Needs dynamic resolution on and no fixed `MSC_VITA_RENDER` size (otherwise it stays off and the log says so).

**What the game simulates.** The game steps its simulation in fixed 20 ms steps (50 Hz) and the renderer interpolates them to picture time. At 60 Hz presentation 5 of 6 pictures hold one step. "60 fps" here means 60 pictures, not 60 steps.

**Policy.** Every scene starts at 60 (`np_fps_ctl.h` `Config::lock60`: 60-first controller, replays and Mega Strikes on AUTO, no between-scene 30). The renderer governor (`vgx_lock60.h`, pure, tested on the host) works against the 16.7 ms budget in this order: the effects governor first, then resolution rungs down to a per-class floor, then lighter shadows (`VgxLock60ShadowStep`, read by `nVisibleInterval`) and crowd refresh (`VgxFgCrowdThrottle`). Only when the ladder is used up and frames still miss does the controller relieve to 30, and outside play the relief lasts to the end of the scene. Steps up need proof: headroom held for `proofFrames` (150) at the model's fit, a probe at the higher rung, and a block that grows (120 to 3600 frames) after a step is taken back. Rungs are made ahead (`EfbPrewarm`, during the stadium intro); a rung not ready is never used and no target is made in play.

| Scene class | Preset (start / floor, side scale) | Why |
|---|---|---|
| open play | 0.93 / 0.70 | size-independent part (scenes plus draws) about 10.7 ms at mid; resolution buys a few ms |
| heavy stadium (Pipeline) | 0.78 / 0.62 | fill plus draws |
| replay | 0.78 / 0.62 | extra scenes |
| cut-scene / goal celebration | 0.86 / 0.70 | |
| Mega Strike | 0.78 / 0.56 | largest fill of the game; effects governor first |
| menus / stadium intro | 0.78 / 0.62 | |

All of this is a model from the Galaxy console costs (25-35 us a draw, about 1 ms a scene), not a Vita measurement. Resolution alone cannot hold 60 in replay, items, menus or the unthinned Mega Strike; relief to 30 is the honest fallback there. Vita3K timings mean nothing.

| Switch | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `MSC_LOCK60=1` | **`1` in the Vita plan since l60def18** (`kLock60Default = true`; 23 Nov console s49: live-play fps 30.1 -> 37.8 Vice, 30.0 -> 36.9 The Dump against UP TO 60; judder 0.22-0.26 -> 0.19-0.20; walk-outs late 61 -> 41 %); code `0` | The policy. Also sets `MSC_AMORT_60`, `MSC_FX_GOV`, `MSC_FX_GOV_MEGA`, `MSC_MEGA_WARM_KIB=28672` and (look, 11 Nov; not run on a console) `MSC_VGX_HUDNATIVE=1` unless set |
| `MSC_LOCK60_PIXSHARE=f` | `0.30` | Share of GPU time the model takes as following the pixel count |
| `MSC_LOCK60_DOWN=f` | `0.90` | Step down when the GPU mean passes this share of 16.7 ms |
| `MSC_LOCK60_UPFIT=f` | code `0.80`, **`0.88` in the Vita plan under LOCKED 60** (`vita_plan.cpp` kLock60, int32) | Predicted share a higher rung must fit under (with actual clamped target pixel area in `L60Pix`) |
| `MSC_LOCK60_UPHARD=f` | code `0.90`, **`0.97` in the Vita plan under LOCKED 60** (`vita_plan.cpp` kLock60, int32) | Single-frame predicted-share ceiling during step-up proof before resetting the proof window |
| `MSC_LOCK60_PROOF=n` | code `150`, **`90` in the Vita plan under LOCKED 60** (`vita_plan.cpp` kLock60, int32) | Frames of headroom before a step up (1.5 s at 60 Hz) |
| `MSC_LOCK60_PLAY_START=n` | code `-1` (preset), **`2` in the Vita plan under LOCKED 60** (`vita_plan.cpp` kLock60, int32/int33) | Starting rung index for live play (`kPlay`) under Smooth floors (`2` = `768x544` 1x; The Dump starts at its floor `728x512` 1x to avoid a kickoff resize hitch; climbs to `960x544` 1x on proof, steps down to `728x512` 1x on spikes/heavy stadiums) |
| `MSC_LOCK60_PROBEAT=f` | code `0.95`, **`1.06` in the Vita plan under LOCKED 60** (`vita_plan.cpp` kLock60, int33) | Single-frame budget share ceiling during post-climb probation before taking a step-up back (`1.06` prevents single sub-16.7 ms frames from aborting `768x544 -> 960x544` climbs while sustained overloads still fail probation via `downAt`) |
| `MSC_LOCK60_LATEAT=f` | code `1.00`, **`1.08` in the Vita plan under LOCKED 60** (`vita_plan.cpp` kLock60, int33) | Single-frame budget share ceiling for immediate step-down (`1.08` avoids 60-85 ms resize hitches on isolated 16.8 ms blips while 2 consecutive hot frames or big spikes still step down immediately) |
| `MSC_CROWD_FLOOR60_LIVE=0\|1` | code `0`, **`1` in the Vita plan under LOCKED 60** (`vita_plan.cpp` kLock60, int33) | Keeps `MSC_CROWD_FLOOR60` and `MSC_AMORT_60` crowd amortization active during Smooth 60 live open play (never Mega Strikes, Quality 30, walk-outs, replays, cut-scenes, or menus) across temporary 30-fps relief so GPU time does not jump +4 ms and trap `hold60` at 30 |
| `MSC_LOCK60_HELD_FULL=0\|1` | code `0`, **`1` in the Vita plan under LOCKED 60** (`vita_plan.cpp` kLock60, int34) | Starts Smooth 60's 30-Hz non-gameplay scenes at full native resolution (`kMenu` and `kMega` at `960x544` 1x; `kCut` and `kReplay` at `768x544` 1x) and evaluates them against the 33.3 ms 30-Hz budget so the game-start giant ball, Mega Strike goalie gloves/balls, and cut-scenes stay crisp while live gameplay keeps the strict 16.683 ms 60-Hz governor |
| `MSC_LOCK60_KIB=n`, `MSC_LOCK60_FREE_KIB=n` | `24576`, `12288` | KiB of targets made ahead, and the video memory kept free |
| `MSC_LOCK60_AA=0\|1\|2` | `1` | Rungs above the game's size: none, the 2x rung, also the 4x rung |

**Log lines to read.** `[fps] LOCKED 60` (policy at start); `[lock60] scene <class>` (preset, floor, ready rungs); `[lock60] step down` / `step up` (levels, size, reason, GPU ms); `[lock60] steps after the floor`, `no ready rung`; `[lock60] 30 s` (steps, takebacks, relief, GPU mean); `[lock60] <class> ended` (measured frames, share over the 60 budget); `[lock60] the step to level ... made N object(s)` (must not appear in play); `[lane]` (game, send, back end, GPU ms); `[frame-timing]` p50 and late frames; `[fps] auto: 60 -> 30` (relief). Console kit: `lock60pipe`, `lock60tour`, `lock60mega`, `lock60replay`, `lock60pix`. Tests: `renderer/tests/run_lock60_test.sh` (35 checks, scene-class traces: no flapping, no allocating steps, recovery after a spike; `BREAK=1..4` each fail) and `native/tools/fps/run_fps_unit_test.sh` (9 checks, `BREAK=1..3`). Not verified: any console figure, the budget table, the feel at 0.70.

## Reuse from frame to frame: what was rejected (1.3)

Emulator counts (Vita3K, `MSC_VIEW_LAYERS=1`, frames 600..900 of open play; counts, not times). Bowser and Crystal Canyon gave no play window: both runs ended in a guest fault in `_lseek_r` before play, the same fault as another branch's `upres-r1`.

| Open play, per frame | Pipeline Central | Wastelands |
| --- | --- | --- |
| All EFB draws, triangles | 191, 55.4k | 198, 74.2k |
| Players | 79, 38.5k | 83, 50.1k |
| Stadium layer, pitch included | 66, 13.4k | 63, 18.5k |
| Crowd sprites | 1, 2 | 4, 1.4k |
| Sky and far scenery | 2, 0.8k | 5, 0.9k |
| Scenes, offscreen pass draws | 6.2, 17 | 7.0, 36 |

- **Far stadium and crowd panorama: rejected.** The crowd and sky it would replace are 3 to 9 draws. The rest would have to be cut out of the stadium layer, which holds the pitch; open-play pictures show the far band in about the top 10 to 25 % of the screen. The camera is not steady: on the Pipeline route a picture of the plane at height 5 is off by a mean of 19 pixels of 960x544 after one frame and 56 after three, with two thirds of frames over 8 pixels. Parallax between the near and far stands needs a refresh close to every frame, each a new scene, and a panorama holds no depth, as `MSC_MENU_STILL` showed: without the world's depth, sprites are no longer hidden. Still's rule (any change of camera goes live) would never hold a frame in play. Animated machinery (71 drawables in Pipeline Central) would freeze too.
- **Shadow pictures left out for players out of view: built, counted, removed.** On the Pipeline route 4.46 pictures a frame were in view and 0.03 could be left out: the test's sphere (twice the radius) keeps nearly all ten players in view.
- **Skipping crowd captures while the crowd is out of view: nothing to gain.** `ImpostorCharacter::UpdateSprites` already captures only sprites with render slots.
- **Reflections: no such pass.** The floor's reflection is a scrolled texture read (`MSC_FLOOR_BAKE`), not a render target.
- **Effects update on alternate frames at 60: rejected.** Particle update is 1.0 % and quad building 0.5 % of the PGO profile's counted blocks. Particles on 30 updates beside players on 60 would judder for less than the crowd and shadows give (`MSC_AMORT_60`).

## Mega Saves Rendering

The save scene is graphics-bound by blending. It draws about 40 screens' worth
of overlapping additive particle quads per frame, and one faint layer (alpha
