0-11 of 255) covers about 28 of them. `ParticleSystem::RenderAllParticles`
writes a zero-area quad for any particle whose alpha is 0, because such
particles fail the alpha test everywhere. While the Mega Strike background is
up, it keeps one in four faint additive particles at four times the alpha.
The choice follows the particle's pool slot, so a particle does not flicker.
Between alpha 12 and 32 the thinning fades out to the original. Simulation,
emission and timing are unchanged. Since version 1.1 this thinning applies
only at the smallest Mega Strike picture size; the low-resolution effects pass
below does the heavy lifting.

## Version 1.3: Languages, Versions, One VPK

**The port's own texts in Spanish and French.** `native/platform/sdk/include/np/np_text.h` (header only: the Settings screen links without the
game) holds every player-facing text the port added, keyed by its English wording, with Spanish and French: the table is
`np_text_table.h`, generated by `native/platform/vita/tools/gen_text.py` from `np_text_strings.tsv` (English, Spanish, French by tabs,
UTF-8). The language is the game's own (`MSC_LANGUAGE`, else `NP_LANG`, read once like the game reads it); the Settings screen uses its
LANGUAGE row instead, so a change shows at once. A text with no translation is shown in English. Nothing that is compared, hashed, logged
for tools, written to a file or sent over the network is translated: the Settings model, `np-player.txt`, the online protocol, the
rule/version fingerprints and the log lines stay English (two formatted failure sentences of the online menu are logged as shown), so the simulation and lockstep online play cannot see the language.

| Where | How |
|---|---|
| Settings screen and control guide (`np_settings.cpp`) | every text goes through `Text()`, which looks it up and draws UTF-8; page widths in characters; the control guide translates each part of a line, then joins them |
| Game popups of the multiplayer menus (`np_menu_ui.cpp`), the in-match banner (`np_notice.cpp`) | the whole text, else each sentence, then UTF-8 to the game's UTF-16 (`NpText::Sentences`, `ToUtf16`): a transport reason followed by "The match goes on." reads whole |
| Formatted texts (`np_menu_flow.cpp`, `np_lan.cpp`) | the format is translated before `snprintf` (the version to update to, the invite code, the rule names) |
| Loading screen, waiting notice, error screen (`vita_bootscreen.cpp`, `vita_notice.h`, `vita_console.cpp`) | looked up, drawn as UTF-8; the error screen's details (file names, codes) stay as written |

The Settings font (`vita_font.h`, `gen_font.py`) gained the Latin-1 letters U+00A0..U+00FF (accents, ñ, ¿, ¡). The game's heading lettering
used for the Settings titles has ASCII only, so a translated heading drops its accents there (`Folded`). `tests/test_text_table.py` checks
every line: three columns, the same `%s`/`%d`, characters the font has, the Settings widths (76 characters for a help line, 29 for a row
name, choice or table cell, every control-guide line whole), that every text of the Settings tables has a line, that `np_text_table.h` is current,
and runs `native/platform/sdk/tests/test_text.cpp` (lookup, sentences, UTF-16). Not translated yet: the game's own tutorial and help texts keep
the Wii's button names in Spanish and French (`np_prompts.cpp` rewrites the English tables only), and the pause menu's controller picture
(`np_prompt_art.cpp`) is English.

**Plain versions and one VPK.** `APP_VER` is `01.30` (`NP_APP_VER`, default in `vita.mk`; the release package gets it through
`tools/repack-release.py --app-version 01.30`), `cboc-release.json` says `"version": "1.3"`, the tag is `v1.3`. 1.2 was distributed briefly and then withdrawn, so 1.3
replaces it as the release after 1.1 and its notes carry the 1.2 work. A release is one VPK: Settings are in the game (`psla:-settings` from the
LiveArea's Settings button starts the same app on its Settings screen); no separate settings package is made or documented.

## Version 1.2 Changes

The engineering list behind the 1.2 notes in the [changelog](../CHANGELOG.md). Every behaviour change sits behind a switch (listed in
[Runtime Switches](#runtime-switches)); the console decides the defaults that are still open ([Console tests](CONSOLE-TESTS.md)).
Nothing here changes the simulation: replays and LAN play depend on that, and the scripted-match digest and picture hashes are the
gate for every renderer change that is meant to leave the picture alone.

### vitaGL base and patches

- `dependencies/vitaGL/source` is upstream vitaGL at `9bf3e3f` (`vitagl_base` in `dependencies/lock.json`) with the fifteen patches in
  `native/platform/vita/vitagl/` applied in order (`patch -p1`). 1.0 and 1.1 built on `cdbba42`, upstream `36f512e` plus one fix that is not in the public history
  (the source region of FBO-to-FBO blits, now patch 0008). The tree is verified identical to the patches applied in order.
- Taken from upstream: matrix and uniform fast paths, small-matrix transposes without the circular pool, faster 16 to 8 bit index conversion, `glUniform4iv`
  arrays, aligned `vglForceAlloc`, GLSL translator fixes (`mod`, `lessThan`, `lessThanEqual`, boolean `equal`), custom splash support, the `HAVE_PTHREAD`
  typo fix, and shader cache version 3 (`<cache>/v3`, file names of 16 hex digits). Dropped as covered by ours: "do not cache failed shader compilations"
  (the candidate/commit scheme stores nothing for a failed compile) and the save-time bucket `mkdir` (our cache I/O makes the bucket itself).
- The local series now describes the whole tree: 0001 GLB pool, statistics, cached stream arena and skip-sets; 0002 seed read-through; 0003 scene depth
  load/store hints and GPU timers; 0004 boot shader pack; 0005 GPU memory recovery and display trace hooks; 0006 validated shader loading and library-owned cache I/O;
  0007 anisotropic minification, framebuffer depth binding and GPU frame progress; 0008 FBO blit source region.
- New in 1.2: **0009** failures that must not take the game down (`init_depth_stencil_buffer` returns false and keeps nothing when the surface cannot be
  allocated; `scene_reset` returns `GLboolean`, leaves no open scene on failure, is not retried for that framebuffer before the next frame and makes the draw, clear or
  blit that asked skip itself; a draw is submitted only if its program exists; patch functions return the `sceGxm` result and count failures; a GPU timer scene never
  overwrites a record the watcher still holds, `vglRtSetRingGuard`). `MSC_VITA_ROBUST=0` and `MSC_VITA_RT_RING_GUARD=0` restore the 1.1 behaviour.
  **0010** `vglSetOneStream` (`MSC_VGX_ONESTREAM`). **0011** per-framebuffer multisampling, `vglFramebufferMultisample` (`MSC_VGX_MSAA`): a framebuffer object
  can have its own 1x/2x/4x mode; its render target, hidden depth buffer and colour surface follow it and every fragment program used with it is patched for that
  mode. The display and new framebuffer objects keep the global mode, so a build that never calls it is unchanged.
- New in 1.3: **0017** `vglDrawElementsDirect` (`MSC_VGX_GXM_DIRECT`), ported from the Galaxy port's 0013-vgx-direct-draw. It changes no compile option and no shader compilation, so the shader cache
  key, the pack and the warm-up list stay valid; the library has to be rebuilt from the patched source.
- New in 1.3: **0024** `vglGetUniformShadow` / `vglUniformShadowWritten` (`MSC_VGX_UNI_SHADOW`), ported from the Galaxy port's 0015-vgx-uniform-shadow. Nothing changes unless a
  caller uses them; no compile option and no shader text changes, so the shader cache key, the pack and the warm-up list stay valid. Host test `renderer/tests/run_vitagl_uniform_shadow_test.sh`.
- `vglStrikersShaderCacheVersion()` (weak; no function means the old layout) tells the host the cache folder version before `vglInit`.

### Shader forms (`MSC_VGX_FS_HALF`, `MSC_VGX_TEX2D`)

Both change the text of generated shaders and nothing else. The bits are in the keys' former padding (`FsKey::prec`, `VsKey::chan[0].pad`) and are set only on the copy the generator gets (`ShaderSwitchKey`, called from `Lookup`). With both off every key, text and text hash is the 1.2 one (`tests/shader_forms.cpp` pins it), so the pack, the console's cache and `kShaderGenVersion` do not change.

- **Caches cannot mix.** vitaGL finds a compiled shader by a hash of its text, in the pack and in the folder. A text of another form has another hash: the pack misses, the folder misses, and the shader compiles and is kept. Switching back finds the first form again; both stay on the card.
- **The warm-up list stays plain.** It holds keys without the bits, in every run, so it does not grow or change with a switch; a run with one on generates each listed shader in its form during the warm-up. So the compiling happens once, at the start of the first run with the switch, behind the progress bar, and a match meets the same compiles as in 1.2 (keys no list has). The cost of that start: for N shaders of the new form (the `[vgx] shader forms:` line gives N), about N x (one compile, 100 ms or more) plus 310 ms for each of the cache's 512 folders not there yet; later starts open one file per shader instead of the pack, about 7 ms each. All figures from earlier console notes, none timed with these switches.
- **What half leaves in float.** vitaGL's translator keeps `half` as it is, but rewrites every `*` into `vglMul`, which has no half forms; so the text has no `*` on a half operand (a scale is an addition or a half `mix`). Every change of type is written out (`half3(x)`, `vec3(h)`), the form the picture shaders brought. Texture reads, uniforms and `gl_FragColor` are float in the translator's header: a shader pays their conversions, so fewer instructions are not certain.
- **Refused text.** If the console's compiler refuses a half text the float text of the key is compiled at once, and after three refusals no half text is tried in that run (a `MSC_VGX_FS_HALF:` note in `log.txt`). A `half4` varying that compiles but fails when the program is patched has no such fallback: that is why it is mode 2.
- **Not proven without the console's compiler** (`tests/run_shader_forms_test.sh` covers Mesa, vitaGL's real translator on the host and an emulation of half's rounding): `clamp` and `lerp` on half types and `half4(half3, half)` (the picture shaders only add); `half4` varyings on a float vertex output; a `vec2` read of a colour texgen's `vec3` output. Mesa refuses that last pair, so a desktop run with `MSC_VGX_TEX2D=1` fails to link draws with colour texgens. Whether either switch saves GPU time is a console question.

`MSC_VGX_FOGVTX` (`renderer/src/vgx_fogvtx.h`, proof in the header comment). GX's perspective fog is `a / (b - z_w) - c`; with the projection `BuildProj` builds, `z_w = c0 + c1 / ze` (ze the eye-space depth), so when `b = c0` the factor is linear in `ze`, which a varying interpolates exactly. The vertex shader of every fogged draw writes `v_fz = eye.z` (vertex key bit `VSK_FOGZ`, `tg[7].pad[1]`, set from BP 0xF1 so it is a register-derived key bit) and, when `FogResolve` finds the draw's fog linear (`fogvtx::LinearOf` on the current registers, decided where the fog registers, projection, viewport or key change), the draw uses the base fragment key with `FOG_LINEAR` (`fog` bit 16): `clamp(fa * v_fz + fb, 0, 1)` from the TEV block, the start offset in `fb`. Orthographic fog with an orthographic projection is affine in `ze` and always taken. Perspective: libogc's `GXSetFog` (and this renderer's) stores b with the constant 8388638, so b is off from c0 by about 3.6e-6 of B and the old form is a Moebius function of `ze`, not a line; the linear form is fitted exactly through the two edges of the ramp (factor 0 and 1) and the largest difference over the ramp is measured (64 points). Mode 1 takes it when that is at most 2^-17 (float rounding; in practice b exactly c0: the host test finds such draws only with short ramps), mode 2 at most 2^-11 (NOT exact; the old form's own float error at the far end of a ramp is larger). Refused (old text): fog kind different from the projection kind, c1 <= 0, a reversed or empty viewport depth range, a pole of the old form in view, non-finite values. The fog range adjustment (BP 0xE8..0xED) is not implemented in this renderer, so there is nothing to handle. Z-freeze is unaffected (fog reads the rasterised depth, not the frozen one). `MSC_VGX_FOGLOG=1` logs each distinct fog set-up with its decision and error. Tests: `renderer/tests/run_fogvtx_test.sh` (random GX fog and triangles both ways, a mutated coefficient is caught, and the generated texts). With the switch on, fogged draws use vertex keys and linear fragment keys that are not in the 1.3 pack: regenerate it first; `MSC_VGX_Q1=1` turns it off.

`MSC_VGX_SBATCH` (`renderer/src/vgx_sbatch.inc`, included by `vgx_draw.cpp`; cache side in `vgx_dlcache.cpp`). GX has no view matrix: a model-view matrix is V x M_i, so MV_A^-1 x MV_i = M_A^-1 x M_i is the same in every frame and every pass for two rigid objects. `EmitStaticDraw` notes each static record that may join (triangles, no palette, no GPU skin, no matrix index per vertex, no Z-freeze, a background class, the list's decoded vertices kept: the cache keeps them for rigid lists promoted while a background class draws, up to `MSC_VGX_SBATCH_MB`). At the end of the frame (`FrontPresent`, after the palette runs) the command list is walked: a run is adjacent noted records of one material with equal projection, light, TEV and other texture-matrix blocks. A material has up to 8 islands; an island holds members (geometry: buffer id + serial + range; R, Q = N_A^-1 N_i; the box of the moved positions). The run's frame matrix F = MV_i x R_i^-1 comes from an admitted member (preferably one that makes a second record fit); each record takes an unused member of its geometry for which F x R equals its model-view matrix within the tolerance (and the normal matrix within 10x, and every position / normal texture matrix likewise); a record with no member becomes one (R = F^-1 MV), admitted after a second frame in which it fits (moving objects never are; a geometry with an unused member makes no new one). When members are admitted the island's buffers are rebuilt (vertices moved by R, normals by Q, 65535 vertices per buffer, in the order of the latest run; uploads in front of the run; old buffers freed at the end of the frame). A stretch of two or more records whose members are consecutive in one buffer becomes the first record with the batch buffer's range and new model-view (F) and texture-matrix blocks; the others are dropped (their records are left with no vertices). A single record stays exactly as it was. Off-screen geometry: only the records the frame made are drawn (`MSC_VGX_CULL` has already dropped the rest). The W12 FILL box of a batched record is the union of its members' moved boxes. Islands go when one of their source buffers is released, or after 600 frames unused. Pixels: not bit-exact (MVrec x (R p) against MV x p: float rounding within the tolerance); members with the same model-view (most of a stadium section) get R = identity and draw the same. `MSC_VGX_SBATCH_ORDER`: within a stretch with nothing but draws and shader declarations between, an opaque noted record is inserted right after the last earlier record of its material when it commutes with every draw it passes: their pixel rectangles (8 box corners through model-view, projection and viewport, 2 pixels of margin, mirrored in the viewport, clipped to the scissor) do not meet, or both are opaque depth writers (no blend, LESS / LEQUAL with depth write, no alpha pass, no shader depth) whose window depth ranges are more than 2^-15 apart. A record with an unknown box (palette, a corner behind the camera, arena draws over 512 vertices) commutes with nothing. The result is the same picture as the original order (each move is a sequence of transpositions of commuting operations).

`MSC_VGX_PALMERGE` (`renderer/src/vgx_draw.cpp`, "palette runs"; cache side in `vgx_dlcache.cpp`). A skinned mesh is drawn the GX way: up to ten matrices are loaded, a display list whose vertices name one each (PNMTXIDX, kept in `a_pos.w`) is called, the next set is loaded, the next list called. Each cached list is a static buffer of its own, so each is a draw (about 50 adjacent pairs per open-play frame differ only in `u_pal`: `MSC_VGX_DRAWCENSUS` `s-pal`). A run joins a static draw when it has the short palette (`perVtxPos` 2: no row above 27, no texture matrix from the palette), no GPU skin, triangles, no Z-freeze, a list whose decoded vertices the cache kept (it keeps them, while this is on, for lists with a matrix index per vertex), and the run's shaders, render state, textures and every uniform block except the palette and the model-view one (a palette vertex shader declares no `u_mv`); the run must be the newest record and command. When the run closes (a draw that does not join, any arena draw, the end of the frame) and has two or more members: the combined buffer holds the members' vertices in draw order, member i's matrix rows moved up by base_i (the rows of the members before it: highest row + 3 each), and their indices in order; the record's palette holds member i's position rows at base_i and its normal rows at P + base_i, P = 30 when the spans add up to 30 or less (the members' own vertex shader) else 60 (the same key with `palRows` 60). Each vertex reads the same rows by value from a larger table through the same text (row = the whole number in `a_pos.w`, below the clamp 57; three `dot` per position, three per normal), and the triangles keep their order, so every pixel is the same. Buffers are keyed by the members (buffer id + a serial that changes when an id is reused, offset, count, base, primitive); one goes when any member's buffer is released, or after 600 frames unused (memory: on the host match60 route with the Vita plan up to about 2.7 MB of combined buffers and 2.7-8 MB of kept copies at their peak before the cap); ids for them are never ones freed in the same frame (their upload is put in front of draws already recorded). When the cap would be passed, or a member's vertices are gone, the run's members are drawn one by one as without the switch. Test: `renderer/tests/run_palmerge_test.sh` (the real front end with fake static buffers; every recorded draw executed on the CPU as the vertex shader reads its rows: off, on, 30 rows and no room are byte-identical, and two deliberate breaks are caught) and the 60-row form in `tests/palbucket.cpp` and the forms dump of `run_shader_forms_test.sh` (Mesa and vitaGL's translator). **New shader forms: every short-palette vertex key (`perVtxPos` 2, `palRows` 0) that draws in a run of more than 30 rows, with `palRows` 60 (vertex only; match60 with the Vita plan: 11 keys, all twins of pack keys; at most 127, the pack's short-palette keys).** They are not in the 1.3 pack, hence the auto default; to put them in, record keys with `MSC_VGX_PALMERGE=1` over the routes, or add every twin with `shl.py pal60` (+127 vertex shaders, +511 programs, +676 variants: a longer warm-up).

`MSC_VGX_FS_TRIM` (key bit `FSP_TRIM`, on the generator's copy like the two above). The console's compiler keeps every clamp of the text, each a VMIN and a VMAX. `FsTrimAnalyze` follows the range of each TEV value: texels, konst colours, vertex colours and registers are 0..1, so a stage clamp whose range is inside its bounds goes and one with a single open side becomes `min` or `max`. The final clamp of the output (`vec4 fin = clamp(...)`) stays in every text: the Galaxy port dropped it where the ranges allowed and its console drew shadows and decals as static (6 Nov); `run_fs_trim_test.sh` checks every trimmed text for it (`BREAK=1`, a generator that drops it, must fail). Shader pack 15 (`final/pack15`) holds the new texts: pack 14's fragment forms of the trimmed keys miss. A register outside 0..255 (only `GXSetTevColorS10`, the movie shader) sets `FSK_WIDE` in the key's `pad`, with the switch on only, and the analysis takes the full range. `floor(x)` against a whole number is `x` against it, so `<`, `<=`, `>`, `>=` tests lose the floor. `renderer/tests/run_fs_trim_test.sh` runs random keys both ways (float differences under 1e-6, no 8-bit change, a mutated analysis is caught); `run_shader_forms_test.sh` draws both texts on Mesa. A multisampled edge can extrapolate a vertex colour past 0..1 at a pixel outside its triangle: the old clamp caught that, the new text may not, a single edge pixel by a small amount. The pack is keyed by text, so all fragment entries miss until it is regenerated ([Building](BUILDING.md#making-the-shader-seed)): keep the switch off until then. `native/tools/gxpcount/` compiles texts with the console's real compiler under Vita3K (`shc.c`) and ranks them (`rank.py`).

`MSC_VGX_VS_TRIM` differs from the forms above: its facts depend on the state, so they are part of `VsKey` (padding bytes, 0 with the switch off; list in `vgx_shadergen.h`) and reach the key memos as a small id (`LightFactId`). The game's lights have cos attenuation `(1, 0, 0)`, and its directional lights distance attenuation `(1, 0, 0)` too (`GameObjectLighting.cpp`, `glxLight.cpp`), compared here bit for bit: `max(0, 1 + 0 ca + 0 ca ca) / (1 + 0 d1 + 0 d2)` is exactly 1. With the switch on the registry holds other keys than the warm-up list, so a run compiles them when first drawn, in play, until the list and the pack are recorded with the switch on. `run_shader_forms_test.sh` draws the lit colours of both texts on Mesa with the same light values and checks that the trimmed text differs when the facts do not hold.

### Shader cache key and migration

vitaGL keeps compiled shaders in a folder named from a key (8 hex digits) made from its id string, the host's shader ABI and the shader generator version
(`ShaderCacheFolder`, `renderer/vita/vgx_host_vita.cpp`). The earlier move to the `9a14d17-glb` cache identity changed the key and layout
(`v2` with short names to `v3` with 16-digit names). The current `9bf3e3f` source base retains that cache identity deliberately; it is not the source pin.
So that an update does not recompile hundreds of shaders:

- The host accepts the key of the 1.0 and 1.1 builds (`bc447993`, `LegacyCacheKey`) for the package's pack and seed folder, but only while the build options
  in the id string, the host ABI and the generator version are unchanged. The renderer's generated shader text avoids every GLSL built-in whose translation
  upstream corrected between the two vitaGL versions, and the compiler, serialisation and bindings are unchanged, so the old compiled shaders are good.
- Files that an older layout or the older key left on the console are **moved once** into the new folder (a rename, no copy; files the pack already holds are
  left where they are, a file that cannot be renamed is copied and removed). At most 3000 files per start; the marker `<key>/cache-moved` is written only when the
  pass ran to the end, so a larger cache finishes over the next start. The old folders are left (deleting hundreds of empty buckets costs more than it saves).
- `tools/make_shader_pack.py`, `make_seed.sh` and `seedcheck` read both layouts. `renderer/tools/seedtest/run_migrate.sh` tests keys, names and the move.
- Any later change to the build options, the ABI or the generator makes the old key stop matching and needs a new pack ([Building](BUILDING.md)).

### Frame pacing and AUTO

- **30 fps lock (STR-022).** The 1.1 frames-in-flight gate waited one refresh when too many frames were on their way, and the late-wait anchor of the 30 fps
  cadence was taken *after* that wait, so the next send target slid by a refresh per gated frame and the gate's backoff only saw the usual two refreshes. In a
  model that includes the real `np_fps.cpp` it ran at 23.98 or 20.00 simulated fps (29.97 with the gate off). `MSC_LAT_ANCHOR=1` (default) takes the anchor before
  the gate's own wait (29.87, the remaining cost is the bounded probes). A gate that switched itself off tries again after 900, 1800, 3600 and 7200 frames
  (`MSC_LAT_RETRY`). Test: `native/tools/fps/fps_gate_unit.cpp`.
- **AUTO 30/60.** Asymmetric hysteresis (down after 4 frames over 92 % of a refresh, up after 2 s within 80 % with 10 % outliers tolerated), a cool-down of
  6 s that doubles with every failed try of the same scene, no judgement of the late frames around a renderer size change (`VgxDynResGrace`), and a replay class:
  a replay, celebration or cut-scene that failed a tried 60 stays at 30 until it ends, `MSC_FPS_REPLAY=30` holds the class at 30. `MSC_FPS_HYST=1` is the 1.1 set.
- **Larger pictures** (`MSC_DYNRES_RULES=2`): a full second of per-frame headroom, back in two frames over 92 %, then locked out for 20 s (doubling), a distance
  between size changes, none right after a frame-rate change. Replay-class scenes held at 30 may go deeper (to 0.70) to keep the 30 even (`MSC_DYNRES_PACED_MIN`).
- Galaxy-port items, each a switch, on in the Vita build ([1.2 defaults](#12-defaults-the-best-experience-is-on)): `MSC_VITA_QUEUE_FIXED30`, `MSC_PACE_VBLANK`, `MSC_VITA_PRIO_VBLANK`, `MSC_VITA_CORE0_ALONE`; bounded slip `MSC_SLIP=20` likewise.
- Latency diagnostics (STR-102): a latency record belongs to one frame tag (stale stages are ignored), the stamps are relaxed atomics, the renderer's frame to tag
  map is one release/acquire word, and `np_display_last_vblank` reads its (count, time) pair through a sequence lock (the old pair returned the old count with the
  new time in 14 % of reads in the fixture). `tools/fps/lat_race_unit.cpp`: four threads, clean under the thread sanitizer.
- The Vita log starts with `[run]` lines (build, ARM/GPU/bus clocks, frame-rate mode and thresholds, picture size range, picture path, sharpening, queue, buffers, and one line each for the
  `gfx:`, `settings:` and `controls:` rows) in every build, and repeats them every 6000 frames in logging builds.

### Dynamic resolution and effects

- A scene class per frame (`EXT_SCENE`: play, replay, cut-scene, other) drives the effects-pass policy: `MSC_FX_LOWRES_SCENES=nis` (a hand line) uses it for cut-scenes
  (`MSC_FX_LOWRES_SCENES=nis` with a pixel threshold, `MSC_FX_LOWRES_MINPX`). Draws that provably change nothing are skipped (`MSC_VGX_DEADDRAW`).
- Foreground first (`MSC_DYNRES_FG`, on in the Vita build): see [Foreground first](#foreground-first-msc_dynres_fg-w12-fg).
- Multisampled rungs (`MSC_VGX_MSAA`, 4x then 2x in the Vita build when the ladder runs) replace the larger-than-native sizes: see [Dynamic Resolution And Frame Rate](#dynamic-resolution-and-frame-rate).
- The heat-distortion copy can be made at half size (`MSC_FX_WARBLE=half`, off), and a torn-down stadium's display-list cache entries are freed 240 frames later
  (`MSC_VGX_DLFLUSH_STADIUM`, on; a list that is called again is built again, so pictures cannot change).

### Renderer speed (CPU and GPU passes)

All on by default unless the table says otherwise, all picture-neutral except `MSC_VGX_Q1`:

- Cached state resolution (`MSC_VGX_RSCACHE`), persistent texture binding memo (`MSC_VGX_TEXMEMO`), vertex-format register shadow (`MSC_VGX_REGSHADOW`),
  exact trivial alpha tests (`MSC_VGX_ATRIVIAL`), dedicated copy programs (`MSC_VGX_COPYSHADERS`), and the Galaxy front-end bits of `MSC_VGX_GXD` (DECFILL, XFWORD and the NEON
  decode of the luminance-alpha texture formats on; MERGESER and RSFAST off because the instruction counts did not pay). Each has a verify mode (`=2`, or
  `GXD_VERIFY`) that runs both ways and counts disagreements.
- GPU-pass memo and deferred copy clears (`MSC_VGX_PASSMEMO`, `MSC_VGX_CLEARDEFER`): unchanged glow-chain passes are not issued again.
- Atlas-cell clears as one quad draw (`MSC_VGX_CELLCLEAR`, on in the player build, identical pictures on the host): the crowd-impostor cells' 35 to 38 scissored `glClear` calls a frame become 1 clear and 2 to 4 draws.
- No `discard` on blended, depth-write-off draws whose alpha test rejects alpha 0 only (`MSC_VGX_BLENDNODISCARD`, on in the player build, identical pictures on the host). The crowd's sprite quads are cut to the box a figure was drawn in (`MSC_VGX_CROWDTRIM`, on in the player build: 50 % fewer crowd fragments on the host, pictures equal to within 2 of 255 levels on 0.01 to 0.03 % of the pixels). The ordering options (`MSC_VGX_CROWDSORT`, `MSC_VGX_CROWDPREZ`) are off: see "Crowd and fill options" above.
- Off by default, console A/B: smaller bone palettes (`MSC_VGX_SKINPAL`), one hardware vertex
  stream (`MSC_VGX_ONESTREAM`).
- Failure paths: an allocation guard for render targets, copy textures and uploads (`MSC_VGX_ALLOC_GUARD`), a bounds guard for display-list decodes
  (`MSC_VGX_DLCACHE_BOUNDS_GUARD`), and survived failures are written to the log as short bounded `[note]` lines (`NpPlatformNote`, 24 per run, in every build).
- Measurement before change: the shader-economy and overdraw censuses (`MSC_VGX_SHCENSUS`, `MSC_VGX_GENSTAT`, `MSC_VGX_FGCENSUS`, `MSC_VGX_GEOMCENSUS`,
  `MSC_VGX_MERGECENSUS`, `MSC_NETCENSUS`) are diagnostics only.
- The renderer library and the GX glue can be built with `-O3` and as ThinLTO bitcode joined to the game's LTO (`VGX_OPT`, `VGX_LTO`), the LTO code generation at
  `-O3` (`NP_LTO_OLEVEL`), and the vitaGL build with extra options (`VGL_EXTRA_OPTS`); all off by default. A profile-guided build of the game objects
  (`NP_CLANG_PGO`) is described in [Building](BUILDING.md#profile-guided-build). The Vita glue and the vgx library are compiled with `-fno-math-errno` like the game.

### Movies

- Hardware JPEG decode of THP frames (`MSC_THP_HWJPEG`, Vita default on) with the first frames of every movie checked against the software decoder; a frame
  the engine refuses is decoded in software. The stadium preview never makes the game thread wait for a decode lane (`MSC_THP_NOWAIT`, Vita default on).
- Truncated movies: the software decoder takes an end pointer and reports a damaged frame instead of reading past it, the player compares every read (offset plus size) with the length of the file and ends the movie at the last
  complete frame, logging the file, the frame and the lengths. `Open` refuses a file whose first frame is not there.
- A rejected asynchronous frame request no longer leaves the reader waiting for a callback that will never come (STR-023).
- Tests: `test_thp_jpeg`, `test_thp_lanes`, `test_thp_movies` (frames and decoded audio against what the THP file authors), `thp_skip`, `tests/movie/check_bridge.py`.

### Game data, errors and Settings recovery

- A read of a missing or truncated extracted file keeps its exact path and reason; at the game's fatal-status boundary the player shows repair instructions
  and returns to the LiveArea instead of waiting for a disc-drive state that cannot change (STR-024, STR-025). Nothing scans every file at start-up.
- A split asynchronous read (aligned head plus 32-byte tail) is admitted as a whole or refused as a whole, so a refused tail cannot leave the head queued for good.
- `PREPARE-GAME.py` checks the complete file table, regular-file sizes and disc extents, and pins the supported USA Rev 1 table by SHA-256 before trusting its inventory
  (STR-026 to STR-028). It is a structure check, not a hash of every asset.
- Every abort path (`nlBreak`, `OSPanic`, `OSFatal`, `abort`, assert, `std::terminate`, the fatal screen) writes its reason to the quiet build's `log.txt`.
- The Settings model expands legacy picture presets, shows CUSTOM for manual thread overrides, leaves the old `MSC_VITA_MSAA` line as written (set18: no row owns it), skips the purely worse values of an earlier 1.3 screen's block (`kObsolete`), refuses a save beyond the
  runtime's 96 assignments, tracks derived picture and core keys across rereads, and the restart guard is serialised with the watchdog and cancelled before a
  successful restart (STR-013 to STR-021). Tests: `native/platform/vita/tests/` (settings model, runtime and key audit).

### Local multiplayer

- The link fingerprint is the protocol string (`MSCLAN/4 R4QE01 native full-records`) plus a **rule byte** (bit 0 the NK/Boo fix of `MSC_GAME_FIXES`, bit 1
  No Mega Strikes; render-only fix bits stay out). A console with another fingerprint is refused and recorded (`Link::GetIncompatible`); a join that finds no game says in the
  log whether the refused console runs a different version or different rules. 1.0 and 1.1 are `MSCLAN/2` and are ignored.
- Received messages never read past their buffer (serializer `Fits`/`mFailed`; counts and sizes are bounds-checked; a malformed datagram is dropped and logged); the
  draft takes sides from a deterministic rule when the message carries none, and an invalid roster ends the session; fragment reassembly checks that the pieces tile
  the frame exactly, keeps the first copy of a duplicate, expires stale slots on every pump and counts frames for another console; frames queued from an earlier session
  are dropped at start.
- The lockstep checksum in LAN sessions also folds in score, ball owner and last touch, clock, power-ups, tangibility, pick-up timers and the random state
  (`MSC_LAN_DIGEST_EXT`); the end-of-match digest is logged and compared by the pair harness. The host can choose the input delay (`MSC_LAN_INPUT_DELAY`): the start value and floor of the adapting delay, identical on every console (the multiplier is driven only by flags of popped messages).
- A suspend notice makes the session say goodbye (`NpLanOnSuspend`); a resume ends the session within about half a second instead of after the 20 s keep-alive.
- Tests: `tools/net/net_test.cpp` (fingerprint over all fix masks, incompatible peers, fragment fuzz), `tools/mp/input_compare.py`, the pair scripts under `native/tools/mp/`.

### Online Play And Leaderboards

- `native/platform/sdk/net/online/`: STUN client, hole punching, encrypted per-peer sessions (XChaCha20-Poly1305 with a replay window, vendored Monocypher), link quality
  measurement, a fault shim, stage limits, port fallback and the VLink backend `vlink_online.cpp`; `HttpSignal` speaks `server/PROTOCOL.md` section 4 over the asynchronous
  HTTP client; the HTTPS client `np_https*` runs on Mbed TLS 3.6.7 pinned in `dependencies/lock.json` ([ONLINE-TLS.md](ONLINE-TLS.md)).
- `server/` is the live service (a Cloudflare Worker and one Durable Object: sign-in, invite rooms, ranked queue, result checks, leaderboard, TURN credentials).
  The game's host/join flow uses short invite codes; Online Central and the web page show the season and daily tables.
- The client supports TURN allocation, permission and channel setup, then the same encrypted peer handshake and lockstep packets as direct traversal.
  A symmetric-NAT direct timeout does not end the menu flow while relay setup is still pending. The bounded relay wait covers either order of published
  relay addresses, including an allocated relay waiting for its peer address or confirmation; existing outer deadlines remain in force.
- Tests: `tools/net` (NAT simulator and unit tests), `native/tools/https/run_https_test.sh`, `tools/net/run_signal_http_test.sh`, `server/test`.
- `MSC_ONLINE_RETAIL_TZ=0|1` (default `0`, 1.3): the hour shift the retail game applies to the online clock before it works out the weekly season and the day (`g_nAddHoursTime`: US -8, Japan +9; `np/online_clock.h`). The service's windows are UTC (`server/src/core/calendar.js`), so the port applies no shift; `1` keeps the retail one (which loses points for 8 or 9 hours around every boundary). `tests/test_online_calendar.py` runs the game's `NetworkSeasonCalendar.cpp` against `calendar.js`.
- **Ranked matches (1.3).** `MSC_ONLINE_RANKED=0|1` (default `1`): RANKED MATCH is the third button of MULTIPLAYER -> ONLINE (`np_menu_flow.cpp`; Circle cancels)
  and opens Online Central (`np_online_flow.cpp` `NpOnlineCentralBegin`: the public name, the virtual profile slot, the game's login and hub). The hub's RANKED
  MATCH is the game's own flow (SHOnlinePlayerCount -> NetworkLobby::StartMatchmaking); `platform/sdk/dwc/np_dwc_match.cpp` implements the DWC matching
  and transport calls it makes: `DWC_ConnectToAnybodyAsync` = `POST /v1/ranked/queue` then `GET` every 2 s (bounded 6 min, cancel = `DELETE`, a stale
  ranked room from a crash is left first), the paired room is entered with the code-room link (`NpNetJoin("@CODE")`: signaling by `GET /v1/rooms/{code}`,
  STUN, punching, relay), AID = room slot, `DWC_SendUnreliable` / the receive callback = the link's frames, a lost link = the connection-closed callback.
  The result: `cGame::ChangeGameState(GS_END_GAME)` records the step, the game's checksum and seed (`NpRankedEndOfMatch`; read only), and
  `NetworkStatsManager::CalculateAndReportGameResult` of a ranked match queues `POST /v1/matches/{id}/result` with the score of each slot and that digest
  (`NpRankedMatchResult`, the persistent retry queue); the ranking client holds the game's score puts until the service calls the match final.
  One game per ranked match: the service keeps one match record per pairing and closes the room at the result, so `ContinuePostGame`
  (`SHPausePostGame.cpp`, `NpRankedSingleGame`) ends a queue-paired session after its game, a draw included, instead of starting the series' next
  game on the link (which the service could not rate). The players are back in Online Central and may queue again.
  `MSC_ONLINE=1` keeps its old meaning: a developer shortcut that puts Online Central on the main menu's old Wi-Fi entry. It stays off: the narrower
  `MSC_ONLINE_RANKED` gates what players see, and the main-menu entry keeps opening the multiplayer chooser that holds both kinds of online play.
  Desktop test builds may point the DWC client at the local fake over plain HTTP (`MSC_ONLINE_URL=http://...`; never on the console).
  Test: `tools/mp/run_online_menu_test.sh` with `CASES=ranked` (two consoles, the fake service, the NAT simulator), `server/test/core/ranked-flow.test.js`.

- **Always-on multiplayer diagnostics and SAVE A REPORT (wave 11, mpdiag18; no switch, release builds included).** `native/platform/sdk/net/np_mpdiag.{h,cpp}`: a ring of 256 fixed
  80-byte records in static memory (20 KiB; no allocation, no file, no per-frame or per-packet call; a spin lock around a few stores) of the events that matter: session start (link
  kind), the draft's machine count, the input delay (auto from a round trip, or the host's choice), `DESYNC ... at network tick N` (frame / checksum / seed), the game's "connection lost"
  with its reason code, the transport's link-lost reason, the match's totals with the last digest each console reported, session end, sleep and wake, the online menu's failure
  sentences, and each connection test's verdict. At most 12 records of one kind are kept per session (the rest are counted), so a desync that repeats every step cannot push the start of
  the session out. The hooks are one line each in `np_lan.cpp` (`NpLanLog` feeds the ring before its quiet-log guard) and `np_menu_flow.cpp`. Nothing is sent, no step is moved.
  SETTINGS > MULTIPLAYER > SAVE A REPORT (one action row, `kRowAction`, run by its title in `np_vita_settings.cpp`; not offered during a session, so it is for the session that has just
  ended) writes `ux0:data/mscharged/mp-report.txt` through `NpSafeFile`: build, version, fingerprint, the six multiplayer keys that are set (an allow-list: never a name, code or address),
  the last connection test, and the events, all through `NpMpDiag::Scrub` (Galaxy's `mp_scrub` is the model: addresses incl. shortened ones and hex `host:port`, IPv6, names, ids, tokens,
  keys, invite and friend codes, URL and host names, user folders). `tests/test_mpdiag.py`: scrubber (an IP, a name, an id and a token come out clean; fuzz under ASan; mutant "scrubber
  off" is caught), ring (bounded, no allocation), report (bounds, order, truncation).
- **SEND A REPORT, the offer, and DELETE MY ONLINE ACCOUNT (public beta, report22; no switch).** Client: `np_mpreport.{h,cpp}` (payload builder, texts, `Op`, the one-shot operation:
  Ask -> Consent -> Start -> Step; `Start()` refuses unless the player consented, so no request exists without SEND / YES), `np_mpreport_net.cpp` (the production `Env`: `NpNet::Http*`,
  `NpMenuReportAccount` / `NpMenuForgetAccount` in `np_menu_flow.cpp`, which reads and removes `online.dat`, `online_name.txt`, `online_code.txt`). `np_mpdiag` gains the log tail
  (`NoteLogLine`, 8 KiB, scrubbed on the way out; `np_lan.cpp`'s `LAN_LOG` and `NpLanLog` feed it), `SessionNumbers` / `SessionHeader` (the numbers of the last session: `d` seconds,
  `dl` delay ticks, `rs` link rebuilds, `dr` loss per mille, `sv` starved steps, `e` ended with an error) and the offer-once state (`OfferWanted`, `MarkOffered`; an error kind sets it,
  the session start clears it). The payload (`BuildPayload`) scrubs again, escapes to ASCII JSON, caps fields at 24000/16000/1200 characters and the whole at 48 KiB by dropping the oldest
  log lines first. Screens: `np_menu_flow.cpp` (`S_RPT_OFFER/ASK/SEND`, the game's popups, translated sentence by sentence) and the settings glue (`np_vita_settings.cpp`,
  `NpSettings::DrawReportDialog`: a drawn consent / confirm / waiting screen; the settings screen cannot use the game's popups). `POST /v1/report` and `POST /v1/account` carry the
  account token (`Authorization: Bearer`); the leave request of an online session carries `X-Msc-Stats: d=..;dl=..;rs=..;dr=..;sv=..;e=..` (`np_signal_http.cpp`, built by
  `NpMpDiagSessionHeader`, no name or address). Server: `server/src/core/reports.js` and migration 1003 (docs: server/PROTOCOL.md section 14). Tests: `tests/test_mpreport.py`
  (`native/platform/sdk/tests/test_mpreport.cpp`; mutants: scrubber off, no size cap, no consent, forget always, limit as failure, 401 as failure, offer always),
  `server/test/core/reports.test.js` (+ 34 mutants in `server/scripts/mutants.mjs`). LAN protocol and single player are untouched (determinism proof: pinned seed 1234abcd identical, 1234abce differs).
- **Connection Test depth (wave 11, mpdiag18; wording and classification only).** `np_conntest.{h,cpp}` borrows Galaxy's five budgeted checks (Wi-Fi, same Wi-Fi, the service, hosting,
  delay: 8 s in all, 1.5 / 3 / 2.2 / 1.2 s a step), its delay classes (fast under 90 ms, fine, choppy from 220 ms), and a plain sentence for a Vita clock that is wrong (a year off breaks TLS:
  "Set it in Settings > Date & Time"). The engine is pure (probes are function pointers; `tests/test_mpdiag.py` drives it with fakes and a clock it advances). The menu flow keeps its
  room-based probe and feeds the facts it has (Wi-Fi up, clock, room made, NAT class, a partner's round trip when one is in the room) through the same classifier; checks it cannot
  make (same Wi-Fi, a delay with nobody in the room) are skipped and said nothing about.

### Other

- Spanish and French menus and texts (`MSC_LANGUAGE`): the language of the Wii system menu as the game reads it (`SCGetLanguage`). Texts and fonts only.
- No Mega Strikes (`MSC_RULE_NOMEGA`): a charged captain shot takes the game's own no-Mega branch (the one the Glass Jaw cheat takes) and skips the wind-up cue.
  Challenges, Strikers 101 and ranked online ignore it. It is part of the LAN rule byte.
- Depth of field (`MSC_DOF`): `0` never turns the cut-scene blur on (no focus quad, no half-size capture); `lazy` keeps the picture but makes the capture only on frames that use it.
- Threads: the audio output thread's priority is a switch (`MSC_AUDIO_PRIO`); `pthread_cancel` is forced into the Vita link so libstdc++ keeps `std::mutex` and `std::thread`
  live (and the log says so if it does not); the Vita log lists the live thread table (priority, cores, stack, CPU) twice per session in logging builds.

## Version 1.1 Changes

The full engineering list behind the [1.1 release notes](../CHANGELOG.md).

## 2026-10-23: Mega Saves, Touch And Zoom Update

### Gameplay, rendering and controls
- Faster, sharper Mega Strikes: glowing effect sprites (the Mega Strike haze,
  item and super-ability glows) are drawn at a quarter of the pixels into a
  separate image, still hidden behind what is in front of them, and added to
  the scene in one pass. The scene itself stays at full size. Used in Mega
  Strikes, and for heavy effects elsewhere when the graphics chip is short.
  `MSC_FX_LOWRES=0` turns it off. Fully transparent sprites are skipped. The
  haze thinning of earlier builds now only applies at the smallest Mega Strike
  picture (it was never reached before: the game draws its sprites through a
  second path, which now has it as well).
- 60 fps by default where it holds: Settings > Frame Rate is now AUTO by
  default (30 FPS is still there). AUTO also watches the graphics chip now,
  so it does not try 60 in a scene the GPU cannot draw that fast. Mega
  Strikes stay at 30 with the full-size picture (`MSC_FPS_MEGA=auto` lets
  them try 60).
- Less input delay in slow scenes: when the graphics chip needs most of the
  frame time, only one frame may wait on it instead of two (the change made
  for Super Mario Galaxy). `MSC_VITA_QUEUE=off|1|2` overrides it.
- Dynamic resolution: the renderer measures the graphics chip's time every
  frame and adjusts the picture to stay inside the frame budget (60 or 30).
  When it is short it first draws the large blur and glow effects at a
  smaller size, then the scene at 93 % and at most 86 % of each side; menus
  and matches never go lower. With time to spare it draws the scene larger
  than the game's own size (up to 1.2 times, 768x544), for a sharper
  picture. Mega Strike scenes may go down to 50 % if still short, with
  stronger haze thinning at the bottom. Settings: `MSC_DYNRES=off`,
  `MSC_DYNRES_MAX=1.0..1.2`, `MSC_DYNRES_MIN=0.75..1.0`,
  `MSC_MEGA_RENDER=off|WxH` in `np-player.txt`.
- Sharper picture everywhere: the picture is now scaled to the screen with a
  light sharpening filter (Settings > Sharpness: SMOOTH, the new default). It
  sharpens more when the scene is drawn smaller. OFF restores the plain
  scaling; CRISP and CRISP+ are still there.
- Mega Save touch aim uses the position sampled for that press.
- Optional rear-touch aim and Mega stick speed (2x-4x) in LiveArea Settings.
- Tap anywhere on the title screen to start. A finger already down does not
  count, and the tap does not press anything in the main menu.
- Short front-panel taps between reads are no longer lost.
- Button taps are no longer swallowed by the debug poll or replayed after a
  system dialog.
- Options > Visual shows all seven zoom levels: 1-5, or 3-7 from level 4 up.
- From zoom level 4 up, the CPU judges "on screen" from about the TV-default
  view, so close cameras no longer make the AI play worse.

### Performance and reliability
- Shader cache: entries are written whole and replaced when invalid. Compile
  and link failures are bounded, and a uniform-buffer leak is fixed.
- Graphics memory recovery waits for the collector instead of sleeping.
- DXT1 mip chains upload without repeated storage growth.
- Background threads wait without 500 us polling.
- Characters hidden during Mega Strikes are not prepared for drawing.

### Diagnostics
- `MSC_VITA_DISPLAY_BUFFERS=2` (test switch): double buffering, for a console
  A/B of input latency. [Console tests](docs/CONSOLE-TESTS.md) lists the
  measurements that decide the next optimisations.
- vitaGL is built with `-ffile-prefix-map`, so no local paths end up in the
  executable.
- Development builds write the log from a background thread, so card writes
  no longer stall the game or change the timings being measured.
- Performance recorder: its memory is allocated only when it is turned on, the
  bank title follows the build, the CRC table is built once, and GPU-call
  timing is an explicit build switch (`NP_DIAG_GPU=1`).
- New `tools/diag/perf-report.py` reads the recorder's bank files (display
  cadence, handover to display, back-end and GPU-call times, tasks) and flags
  damaged or incomplete banks. A host round-trip test checks it against the
  real recorder. Guide: `docs/DIAGNOSTICS.md`.

### Repository
- Stable asset names (`Strikers-Charged-Vita.vpk`, `-install.zip`), so
  "latest" download links keep working.
- Developer docs moved to `docs/`, and a reproducible repack tool was added
  (`tools/repack-release.py`).
- Release-metadata tests, run with the host tests in GitHub Actions.
- Developer files moved out of the top folder: `tools/build-deps.py`,
  `tools/check-files.py`, `docs/RELEASE.json`, `docs/SOURCE-MANIFEST.json`,
  `docs/CHECK-DOWNLOAD.md` and `store/artwork.json`.

### Console fixes after the first hardware test
- The framebuffer-object pool kept every pass size the dynamic resolution had
  used, with its colour and depth textures, for the whole session; after a few
  level changes the video memory ran out and creating a target crashed the GPU
  set-up (`sceGxmBeginScene`, at the start of a match). Pool objects unused for
  60 presents are now deleted, and the controller only steps up when vitaGL's
  VRAM pool has room: 32 MiB free for levels above the game's own size, 16 MiB
  for any step up, and below 12 MiB a larger picture drops back to native.
  `MSC_DYNRES_VRAM=KiB` simulates the free memory on the desktop.
- Every fresh run-time shader compile failed on the console ("fatal internal
  error on line -1", Cg and GLSL alike). The shader-cache hardening in the
  patched vitaGL (`custom_shaders.c`, `compile_shader`) passed vitaShaRK a
  size of 0, and vitaShaRK reads that argument as the length of the source
  text, so the compiler saw an empty file. Only packaged or cached shaders
  worked, so the sharpening fell back to the plain picture and the
  low-resolution effects to full size. The size now goes in as the source
  length. The sharpening shader is also written one sample per statement.

- The lighter effects never ran on the console: vitaGL has no depth textures
  (no `GL_OES_depth_texture`, colour attachments only), so the effects pass
  had no scene depth and stayed off, and Mega Strikes started at 70 % size.
  The patched vitaGL now has `vglStrikersBindFramebufferDepth`, which shows a
  framebuffer's stored depth buffer (linear DF32M, rows aligned to 32 samples)
  as the bound texture; the effects pass reads the EFB's depth through it.
  `MSC_FX_VITA_DEPTH=0` turns that path off.
- Size changes are spaced at least 20 frames apart (the start and end of a
  Mega Strike excepted), need the free GPU memory for the new targets plus
  8 MiB (vitaGL's VRAM and RAM pools together), and are followed by a quick
  trim of the framebuffer pool. If the console still cannot allocate the new
  render target, the old one is kept and the level goes back.
- Copy textures that keep their picture over a size change fall back to the
  plain resize when there is no memory for a second copy. Deleted textures are
  detached from pooled framebuffer objects first, so vitaGL frees them at once.
- The pool is never compacted while a pass is open; a frame-rate interval of
  0 (no policy yet) uses the 30 fps budget; with a fixed render size
  (`MSC_VITA_RENDER`) Mega Strikes are still held at 30.
- Second console test: in a Mega Save the GPU time stayed at the frame budget
  while the picture went down to 0.50 (the effects are already at a quarter of
  the pixels). A step down is now judged after about 20 measured frames and
  undone if the GPU time fell by less than 8 %; the scene then stays at that
  level (until the Mega Strike ends, or 900 frames in ordinary scenes). Mega
  Strikes start with the scene at the game's own size when the effects pass
  is available, also from a match's 0.86.
- Mega Save stick speed: 3x by default (`MSC_MEGA_POINTER_SPEED=1` is the
  original speed).
- The package's shader pack and warm-up list include the shaders the console
  compiled in a first match (768 programs' shaders, the warm-up list of that
  console), so the first goal replay at close zoom no longer compiles.
- Input delay (third console session): every stage after the game thread is
  faster than a frame, but the display shows a picture every second refresh,
  so each hand-off held a queue and a button took about 150 ms to reach the
  screen at 30 fps. Frames now carry their number to the display also without
  `MSC_LAT`, and the game thread starts the next frame only when at most
  `MSC_LAT_DEPTH` (default 2) of its frames are still on their way (one refresh
  of waiting at most; it stands aside for 150 frames after any late frame so
  it never costs frame rate). Scripted match on the console: 150 ms -> 99 ms.
- The no-gain revert of the dynamic resolution only runs in Mega Strikes, and
  only when the GPU time stayed about the same (a rise is a scene change).
- Pool trimming and texture deletion never touch the framebuffer object of
  the open scene (a console crash in `scene_end`). The effects pass binds the
  console depth buffer after the EFB's scene has ended and leaves its store
  mode alone.
- Larger front caches for texture, shader and program lookups (1024 entries,
  from the Galaxy port).
- Display-list cache, from the Galaxy port: a list built while the game
  thread wrote to memory is only distrusted when a write overlapped the list,
  its skinning data or an array range it read (a log of the last 2048 write
  notifications), and entries age by frames that call cached lists, so a
  loading screen or pause does not throw the match's lists away.
- Size changes (fourth console session, after the Galaxy port's finding):
  on the console each change stalled the picture for 80-190 ms. A Mega Strike
  that learned a smaller picture saves nothing keeps that for the session's
  next Mega Strikes (no step down and back each time), the end of a Mega
  Strike returns to the level of the play before it at the cut (no change a
  few seconds into play), and with 24 MiB of video memory free the pass
  objects of the sizes used in the last 600 presents stay pooled
  (`MSC_VGX_KEEP_SIZES=0`: the 60-present age). The log reports each change's
  cost (`size change to WxH`).
- Edge smoothing in the SMOOTH picture filter: where the four neighbours it
  already reads show a contrast edge, the pixel is taken a fraction of a
  texel across the edge (FXAA's sub-pixel step) and the unsharp mask is
  mostly left out there. One more texture read. `MSC_VITA_EDGE_AA=0` turns it
  off.
- HD textures (from the Galaxy port, `renderer/src/vgx_hdtex.cpp`,
  `tools/hdtex`): an optional pack of 2x versions of the character and ball
  textures (278, DXT1 with their own mip chains; 1-bit alpha where the
  original's alpha is on/off), keyed by a hash of the original texels and
  looked up only when a texture is uploaded. The pack (37 MiB) is too big to
  preload, and a read on the player thread took 5-51 ms on the console, so a
  reader thread loads each replacement and the texture is uploaded again
  once it is in. At most 8 MiB alive (`MSC_HDTEX_BUDGET`); replacements
  unused for 600 frames are freed past half of that; a typical match uses
  about 2.6 MiB. Sampled with mips, so ordinary play reads the
  original-sized level. `MSC_HDTEX=0` turns it off.
- Face morphs on the GPU (`ShaderSkinMesh::GpuSkinPacket`): packets with an
  active morph target were CPU-skinned into a new array every frame, so the
  renderer also decoded their lists again every frame (about 12 bypassed
  lists a frame in celebrations and replays). They are now GPU-skinned from a
  morphed bind pose kept per packet in a pool of its own (outside the game's
  heaps, which the simulation's layout depends on; registered with the
  renderer as tracked memory) and remade only when the weights change.
  ARM instruction counts in replays: renderer thread 8.32 M -> 7.00 M a
  frame, game thread 5.08 M -> 4.95 M; open play unchanged; pictures equal.
  `MSC_SKIN_GPU_MORPH=0` restores the CPU path. `MSC_VGX_BYPASSLOG=1` logs
  why a list is decoded every frame.

| `MSC_VGX_DRAWCENSUS=<n>` | Diagnostics, default off, changes nothing. Every n frames (1 = 600) and per scene class and draw class (`MSC_DYNRES_FG=1` for the classes): draw records per frame, static ones, vertices, triangles, cull-none records, and the first test that kept a record from joining the one before it (`nonadj tag static layout vs fs state skin mvonly texmonly tevonly blocks`). `MSC_VGX_DRAWCENSUS_ORDER=<frame>` lists that frame's records in order. Works in the null and GL back ends (armhf harness) |
| `MSC_VGX_DRAWCENSUS_RICH=1` with `MSC_VGX_DRAWCENSUS_FRAMES=a,b-c`, `MSC_VGX_DRAWCENSUS_SHADERS=<file>` | Diagnostics, default off, host/harness only. One machine-readable `[dcr]` line per draw record of the listed frames (or `_ORDER=<frame>`): shader ids, layout, buffer, textures with wrap and filter, render state, a hash of each uniform block, the bone-matrix row hashes of a GPU-skinned draw, the position rows a palette draw fills, the TEV block floats and the model-view matrix. `_SHADERS` writes every shader text that arrives (`#### F<id>` / `#### V<id>`) to the file. Read by `/home/claude/s13/w7/players/census.py` (wave 7 player-merging census) |

## Logging added for the first console night (Wave 7, galren; no switch)
- `MSC_VGX_RESTRACE=1` (diagnostic, off, changes nothing; wave 9): a `[restrace]` line for every render-target, copy-texture and pass-object creation or respecification and every new program link: picture serial, scene class, logical id (copy slot / framebuffer / program key), old -> new size and samples, a generation per id. Shows whether a respecification changed anything, two users alternating in one slot, and what is first made after kick-off or a Mega Strike. `MSC_VGX_RESTRACE_MAX` caps the lines (20000).
- `MSC_VGX_HSRCENSUS=<seconds>` (diagnostic, off; `MSC_VGX_HSRCENSUS_FRAMES=<n>` counts frames instead): per scene kind, how many alpha-tested draws sit before the last opaque depth-writing draw of a scene (`vgx_hsrcensus.h`). Nothing is reordered.
- `[acts]` (every 3600 pictures from picture 600): what the default-on skip and trim options of the Vita plan did in this run (uniform duplicates not made, crowd cell batches, copies not made, half-size pass steps, direct draws, crowd quads cut). A 0 beside an option that is on means it did nothing here.
- `shader warm-up queue ended at the slot limit with N not linked`: once, when the warm-up stopped linking at `MSC_VGX_MAXPROGRAMS`.
- `[vgx-vita] shader pack MISS #n`: one line for each distinct shader text (vitaGL's hash) the pack did not answer, at most 256; the periodic `shader pack:` line adds asks not answered, distinct texts, those first asked in play, and compiles in all.
- Per match (wave 8, lessons from the Galaxy console runs; no switch): `[match] #n start` (picture; programs resident against `MSC_VGX_MAXPROGRAMS` and vitaGL's 1,024 slots; shaders resident against its 2,048; the warm-up queue's unlinked count; programs marked by `MSC_VGX_WARM_ORDER`), `[match] #n so far` every 3,600 pictures, and `[match] #n end` (programs linked and deleted during the match, shader compiles asked, slow ones of 3 ms and over with total and longest ms, pack misses and those first asked in play, background-compiler skips, pass objects and kept targets let go, spared by `MSC_POOL_THRASH_GUARD`). A match runs from its first picture with a scene class to 1,800 pictures of menus, or to a new stadium. `[acts-match] #n` gives each default-on option's count for the match and `DID NOTHING` names the ones that are on with 0. `[shaderplay]` is one line for each compile of 3 ms and over made on the render thread during a match (at most 200). `[present]` ends with the hold mix: percent of pictures shown 1 / 2 / 3+ refreshes, in live play and in all scenes.
